| `SetData` / `GetData` round-trip | `EasyGL_VbSetData`, `_VertexBufferIndexBufferGetData` | `Vulkan_VertexBufferIndexBufferGetData` and `Vulkan_VbSetData` — both EasyGL's own sources | `PARITY` (was `TEST_GAP` for `VbSetData`; closed by `VULKAN-135`, re-checked 2026-09-07) | `VULKAN-135` ✅ |
| `SetDataOptions` (`Discard`/`NoOverwrite`) under a deferred draw model | `EasyGL_DynamicBufferStress` (orphaning) | `Vulkan_DynamicBufferUpdate`, `Vulkan_DynamicBufferStress` — a recorded draw copies out of the mapping at record time, so the deferred model does not make the options observable | `PARITY`, measured rather than suspected (was `SEMANTIC_DIVERGENCE` (suspected) when this matrix was written; `VULKAN-132` measured the two renderers to agree and `VULKAN-133` pinned it, re-checked 2026-09-07) | `VULKAN-132` ✅, `VULKAN-133` ✅ |
| `BufferUsage::WriteOnly` `GetData` refusal | `EasyGL_BufferUsage` | `Vulkan_BufferUsage` — the same source, registered on both | `PARITY` (was `TEST_GAP` when this matrix was written; closed and re-checked 2026-09-07 by `VULKAN-473`). **The trailing “Vulkan integration unproven” was removed 2026-09-07 (`VULKAN-221`)**: it described the state `VULKAN-136` was opened to fix — a `WriteOnly` buffer must refuse `GetData` even though this renderer's persistently-mapped buffers physically permit the read — and that row registered the assertion and it passed. Left in place it read as a live caveat on a closed row | `VULKAN-136` ✅ |
| Empty (0-vertex/0-index) buffers | `EasyGL_ZeroLengthBuffers` | `Vulkan_ZeroLengthBuffers` — the same source, registered on both; the explicit `max(1, …)` allocation is this renderer's own | **`PARITY`, re-derived 2026-09-07 (`VULKAN-221`).** It read `VULKAN_STRONGER`, **untested** — and its own owner row had already made that false twice over. `VULKAN-137` registered one renderer-agnostic source on **both** renderers and got **4/4 identical**, which is the definition of `PARITY`, not of one renderer being stronger; and “untested” survived a row whose whole content is the test. Re-run 2026-09-07: `Vulkan_ZeroLengthBuffers` and `EasyGL_ZeroLengthBuffers` both green. The `max(1, …)` padding stays a real implementation difference — `vkCreateBuffer` rejects a size of 0 where `glBufferData` accepts it — and the point of running one source on both is that the difference is invisible from the public API | `VULKAN-137` ✅ |

| `VertexDeclaration` → native input layout | declaration-driven (`ApplyLayout`); arbitrary declarations bind | **declaration-driven** since `VULKAN-144`: the attribute set is built from the declaration itself, for stock programs, custom effects and per-instance streams alike | `PARITY` (was `IMPLEMENTATION_GAP` — stride-driven — when this matrix was written; closed by `VULKAN-144` and its eleven children, re-checked 2026-09-07 — `Vulkan_DeclaredVertexLayout`, `_DeclaredEffectLayout`, `_DeclaredInstancedLayout`) | `VULKAN-144` ✅ |
| Supported strides, unknown-stride rejection, custom declarations | `EasyGL_VertexFormats_AllStrides`, `_UnknownStride_Rejected`, `_DrawUserPrimitives_CustomVD` | `Vulkan_VertexFormat_AllStrides`, `Vulkan_DrawUserPrimitives_CustomVertex`, and since `VULKAN-165` `Vulkan_UnknownStride_Rejected` — a declaration-less buffer whose stride the canonical table does not list is refused by name on the routes whose fallback invents a colour attribute, where `VULKAN-139` measured it accepted at upload, drawn and presented in silence | `PARITY` | `VULKAN-139` ✅ (audit), `VULKAN-165` ✅ (fix), `VULKAN-140` ✅ |
| `DrawPrimitives` / `DrawIndexedPrimitives` | covered on both | covered on both | `PARITY` | — |
| `DrawUserPrimitives` / `DrawUserIndexedPrimitives` | four dedicated tests | the **same four**, registered here by `VULKAN-134`: ten pixel-verified sub-tests including non-zero vertex and index offsets | `PARITY` | `VULKAN-134` ✅, `VULKAN-140` |
| Instancing | `EasyGL_InstancedModel_Shader` (GLSL), `EasyGL_InstancedTexturedDraw` | `Vulkan_DrawInstanced_3Instances`, `_InstancedVertexColor_Cardinality`, `_DeclaredInstancedLayout`, `_InstancedTexturedDraw` | `PARITY` **since 2026-09-08, and the verdict has now been wrong in both directions — which is the whole history of this row.** It read `VULKAN_STRONGER` until 2026-09-07, on a true count of tests and a question neither renderer had been asked; F-41 re-derived it as a gap, and eight rows closed it. EasyGL adds an optional per-instance matrix to **every** stock program, so instancing composes with the whole effect set — and **this renderer now does the same thing by the same means**: one source per family, compiled twice, with the four columns injected at locations 12..15. `useInstanced` selects no program at all any more, and `instanced3d.vert.glsl`, `GetOrCreatePipelineInstanced3D`, `pipelinesInstanced3D_` and `pipelineLayoutExt3D_` no longer exist. Measured on the shared corpus rather than counted: **37/37 on each renderer**, from 17/17 when the gap was found and 2/5 when the file was first run | `VULKAN-217` ✅, `VULKAN-218` ✅ (and its eight children `VULKAN-227`–`VULKAN-234`) |
| `baseVertex` / `startIndex` / `minVertexIndex` semantics | — | `vertexOffset`/`firstIndex` map one-to-one onto XNA's `baseVertex`/`startIndex`; the two hints change no pixel | `PARITY` — measured with four quadrant-separated quads, each parameter moved alone | `VULKAN-141` ✅ |
| Range and primitive-type validation | `EasyGL_DrawRangeValidation`, `_PrimitiveTypeValidation`, `_DrawNoVertexBuffer`, `_DrawNoIndexBuffer` | `Vulkan_DrawRangeValidation`, `_PrimitiveTypeValidation`, `_DrawNoVertexBuffer`, `_DrawNoIndexBuffer` — the same sources, registered on both | `PARITY` (was `TEST_GAP` when this matrix was written; closed and re-checked 2026-09-07 by `VULKAN-473`) | `VULKAN-143` |
| Multi-stream vertex input | `OrdinaryDrawMultiStreamTest`, `InstancedDrawMultiStreamTest` | the **same sources**, all **48/48** legs: non-indexed/indexed, UInt16/UInt32, `vertexStart`/`startIndex`/`baseVertex`/per-binding offsets, mixed instance frequencies, deferred lifetime and range failures; `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_3D` adds split-stream custom draws | `PARITY` since 2026-09-08. Vulkan packs one immutable interleaved snapshot per input rate before its deferred draw is queued; the public limit remains 16 | `REMED-GFX-203` ✅ |

One-time commands and off-screen readback wait a fence attached to their exact submission, never the queue/device | `PARITY`: 120 disposal cycles cost 0 stalls; 48 uploads remain exactly one submission each but now wait their own fences; ordinary target sampling costs 0 synchronous submits; `GetData` records producer closure + transitions + copy once and adds exactly 1 fence wait | `PARITY` | `VULKAN-392` ✅, `VULKAN-396` ✅, `VULKAN-399` ✅, `VULKAN-401` ✅, `MOD-2253` ✅ |

---

## 11. Task status legend

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| ✅ | complete **and verified against the row's own acceptance criterion** |
| 🟨 | partial — code or evidence exists but the criterion is not met; the row says what is missing |
| ⬜ | not started |
| ⛔ | deliberately not done, or blocked; the reason is written in the row itself, never only in a commit message |

Task IDs are `VULKAN-###`, unique and stable forever. Numbering is **sparse inside each phase on
purpose** so work discovered later takes a free neighbouring number instead of forcing a renumbering
pass (`plan_csl.md`'s convention; see also `feedback: renumbering breaks external citations`).

**One task = one commit.** Every row below is sized to be independently verifiable and independently
committable. A row that turns out to be larger than that is split into new rows before it is
started, not silently widened.

---

## 12. Phase 0 — Baseline and audit infrastructure (`VULKAN-001`–`VULKAN-019`)

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-001 | Record the implementation baseline commit and both build configurations | ✅ | **§7.2 re-confirmed against the commit the work has actually reached, not the one it started from** -- branch `vulkan`, **1055** commits ahead of its fork point `1bb2145d9` on `develop`. Both `cmake` command lines are written out in §7.2, **read back from the two `CMakeCache.txt` files** rather than retyped from memory, and `cmake --build` returns **0** for both directories. **The row's real question is answered by doing it, not by reading:** a configure needing a fourth fix would have had to be added to §7.2. None was -- the three recorded fixes (submodule init, `-DCNA_ENABLE_DRACO=OFF`, and the `sharp-runtimenext` root) are still the whole list, and both configurations still configure and build clean from them. **One thing §7.2 did not say and a reader will hit within minutes:** both caches carry `CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER_LAUNCHER` as a **list** -- `cmake;-E;env;CCACHE_BASEDIR=…;ccache` -- rather than a bare `ccache`. Re-passing it unquoted through a shell splits it and the configure fails; a `-C` preload cache is how to carry it into a new build directory. That is now in §7.2, because a baseline that cannot be reproduced is not a baseline. |
| VULKAN-002 | Establish a green **EasyGL** test baseline and record it | ✅ | **Established, and it is not green -- which is the finding, and the reason the row said "a pre-existing EasyGL failure is named, not rounded away".** Recorded as new §7.5b. `ctest --test-dir cmake-build-easygl -j2` → **9175/9198**, 23 failed; `--rerun-failed -j1` → **6 pass, 17 do not**, which is what separates a flake from a failure and is why the row asks for it. **The six flakes are the known parallel-contention set** -- one `WaveBankTest` and five ENet cases -- and none is renderer-related. **Of the seventeen that reproduce serially, four are `EasyGL_` tests: `EasyGL_HandleRelease` and `EasyGL_DeviceDisposeOrder` both SEGFAULT, plus `EasyGL_StockEffectSamplerContract` and `EasyGL_SamplerComponentIsolation`.** The other thirteen are the shared content/lifecycle/network/C-API set that fails identically in the Vulkan configuration. **Consequence for this plan, written into §7.5b rather than left implicit:** the maturity reference is not green on its own renderer set while `^Vulkan_` is **338/338**. That does not make Vulkan "ahead" -- four specific defects are not a coverage measure -- but `VULKAN-487` must not assume "EasyGL passes it" for any of those four when it recomputes the delta, and must not import them as Vulkan work either. They are EasyGL defects on EasyGL's own branch, and §5's rule is that EasyGL is the maturity reference and never the semantic authority. |
| VULKAN-003 | Establish a green **Vulkan** test baseline and record it | ✅ | **Recorded as new §7.5c, by the same method as `VULKAN-002`'s so the two are comparable.** `ctest --test-dir cmake-build-vulkan -j2` → **9187/9199**, 12 failed; `--rerun-failed -j1` → **0 of the 12 pass**, so every one is deterministic and there are no flakes in this run at all. Separately, `-R '^Vulkan_'` → **338/338**. **All twelve classified, and none called "accepted" without naming what accepts it:** every one is a **CNA defect** in shared code outside this renderer, pre-existing on this branch, and **not a `Vulkan_*` test** -- one content-pipeline case whose fixture another session is mid-change on, six `GraphicsDeviceSubsystemLifecycle`, one `PacketReaderTest`, one `GameWindowPlatformTest`, and the three C-ABI generated-artefact gates. **Neither "environment" nor "driver" appears in that column, and that is a result rather than an omission.** The one failure this plan long carried as a driver artefact, `Vulkan_DepthBias`, was reclassified by §7.6 as a **test** written against OpenGL's depth range; every `Vulkan_*` test now passes. The environment facts that remain are §7.1's, and they are about which device runs, not about a failing test. **One methodological note the numbers make necessary:** §7.5b's EasyGL run reported six flakes and this one reported none, from the same ENet/audio set. A run that lists them is not a regression and a run that does not is not an improvement -- which is precisely why the row asks for the serial re-run rather than the headline count. |
| VULKAN-004 | Fix or quarantine the pre-existing non-Vulkan build break so the suite can be built at all | ✅ | **Fixed at the call site.** `CnbTextureCodecTests.cpp:475` passed one argument to `CnbDocument::Parse(std::vector<std::uint8_t>, const std::string& origin, const CnbReadLimits&)`; `origin` has no default and every one of the file's other eleven `Parse` calls supplies one (`:190`, `:210`, `:217`, …). The asset is encoded as `EncodeTexture2DToCnb(data, "mipped")`, so the origin is `"mipped.cnb"`. Introduced by `347139500` (`feat(cnb): generate mip chains when compiling a source image`); not another plan's in-flight work, so it is repaired rather than quarantined. **Evidence:** `cmake --build cmake-build-vulkan -j16` exits **0**; `ctest -R CnbTextureCodecTest` is 15/15 including `GeneratedMipChainSurvivesAnEncodeDecodeRoundTrip`. **Discovered while closing it:** §7.3's `Total Tests: 302` / `407` were measured with this break present. `gtest_discover_tests` runs each test binary *after* it links, so the unbuildable `cna_content_test_objects` suppressed every test it and its dependents own. With the build green the same command reports **9071** registered CTests for the Vulkan configuration. The `215 ^Vulkan_` count is unchanged and is the number this plan's parity arithmetic actually uses. `VULKAN-006` owns re-stating §7.3 from a green build. |
| VULKAN-005 | Record the Vulkan physical device, driver, extensions and layer environment | ✅ | **§7.1 regenerated from a fresh `vulkaninfo --summary` and from the renderer's own startup dump, with the selected device named rather than assumed.** Instance API **1.4.309**, eight instance layers including `VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation` **1.4.309**, and the same two devices as before: GPU0 `AMD Radeon 780M (RADV PHOENIX)` (`DRIVER_ID_MESA_RADV`, apiVersion 1.4.305, conformance 1.4.0.0) and GPU1 `llvmpipe (LLVM 19.1.7, 256 bits)` (`DRIVER_ID_MESA_LLVMPIPE`, conformance 1.3.1.1). **The selection is measured, not inferred:** the renderer's startup dump under Xvfb `:99` prints four repetitions of `vulkan: No DRI3 support detected - required for presentation` as `PickPhysicalDevice` rejects RADV, then `[Vulkan] GPU: llvmpipe (LLVM 19.1.7, 256 bits)`. So §7.1's existing account of why the physical GPU is unreachable still holds today, and every number in this plan is llvmpipe's. **One correction to the row above it rather than a rewrite:** it recorded "Mesa 25.0.7-2+deb13u1" for GPU0, which is `driverInfo`; the numeric `driverVersion` is **25.0.7** (GPU1's is 0.0.1). A bug report wants the numeric one, so both are now stated. **And one addition the section did not have:** the device limits, because `VULKAN-024` had to go and get them and nothing recorded them -- `maxImageDimension2D` 16384, `maxImageDimensionCube` 32768, `maxImageDimension3D` **4096**, `maxColorAttachments` 8, `maxVertexInputBindings` 32. The 3D limit is four times smaller than the 2D one, and it is the number `VULKAN-180` was found through. |
| VULKAN-006 | Enumerate both renderers' registered CTests mechanically and check the numbers in §7.3 | ✅ | **All four counts re-measured from a green build of both configurations and all four corrected.** `ctest -N` gives **9199** total / **338** `^Vulkan_` for `cmake-build-vulkan` and **9198** total / **322** `^EasyGL_` for `cmake-build-easygl`, against the `302 / 215` and `407 / 317` the section carried. Taken **before** any other `ctest` invocation, as the row requires, because `ctest -N` overwrites `LastTest.log` -- a trap this project has recorded before and one that would have destroyed the failure list a baseline row needs. **Recorded as a second dated correction rather than by editing the first**, so §7.3 now shows the original numbers, `VULKAN-004`'s explanation of why the totals under-counted by an order of magnitude (an unbuildable test object registers nothing under `gtest_discover_tests`), and today's figures in sequence. **The deltas are stated as arithmetic and deliberately not attributed:** +123 `^Vulkan_` and +5 `^EasyGL_`. This plan's registrations account for most of the first and for four of the second -- each of those four a control for a source that also runs on Vulkan -- and the remainder is not traced, because nothing here depends on tracing it. Claiming the whole delta would have been the easy sentence and the wrong one. |
| VULKAN-007 | Script the EasyGL↔Vulkan coverage comparison and keep it re-runnable | ✅ | **`tools/vulkan/renderer_coverage_delta.py`** -- Python, no build system, no build directory, not a CTest, exactly as the row scopes it. It parses both `examples/CMakeLists.txt` files and emits §7.4's three sets, with `--list shared|easygl-only|vulkan-only` and a `--json` mode for `VULKAN-487` to consume. **Equivalence is by SOURCE FILE, not by test name, and that decision is the whole tool.** This campaign's method is to register EasyGL's own source on Vulkan wherever the behaviour is portable, so two tests sharing a source are one measurement under two renderers whatever they are called. Comparing names would treat `EasyGL_Foo` and `Vulkan_Foo` as unrelated and would miss every registration made that way -- which is most of them. **The row's acceptance is stale and the script is what proves it.** It asks the tool to "reproduce §9.3's 143/51 numbers on this commit", and on this commit it cannot: the current figures are **EasyGL 320 / Vulkan 337 registered, 199 sources on both, 121 EasyGL-only, 136 Vulkan-only**. The 143 → **121** difference is **22 EasyGL sources this campaign has since registered on Vulkan** -- §7.5's own baseline rows record a 23-registration batch among them -- so the tool disagreeing with the number is the tool working. §9.3's 143 stays as the dated Phase-0 figure it is; this script is how the current one is obtained, and `VULKAN-487` should quote the script rather than the section. **Three registrations are counted separately rather than silently dropped:** `EasyGL_Demo2D_SmokeTest`, `EasyGL_House3D_SmokeTest` and `Vulkan_Demo2D_SmokeTest` name demo executables built outside `cna_<renderer>_test`, so no source can be traced for them. The script prints them under a heading that says exactly that. Left unreported they would have quietly understated every set, and the totals would not have reconciled with `ctest -N`'s 322/338 -- which is how the gap was noticed. |
| VULKAN-008 | Capture Vulkan's current capability reporting as a checked-in snapshot | ✅ | **Captured: 93 entries, all read through the `GraphicsDevice` seam.** New CTest `Vulkan_CapabilitySnapshot` (`vulkan_capability_snapshot_test.cpp`), **9/9**. It covers all **19** `GraphicsCapability` members, all **30** `RendererFeature` rows, all **10** `RendererLimit` rows, `SupportsSurfaceFormatAsRenderTargetEXT` for all **27** `SurfaceFormat` values, and the scalar queries — `GetShaderDialectEXT`, `ExecutesShaderEffectSourceEXT`, `SupportsShadowSamplingEXT`, `SupportsImageBasedLightingEXT`, `GetMaxTextureDimension`, `GetMaxComputeWorkGroupInvocationsEXT`. **The seam matters and the row said so:** six members are resolved by `GraphicsDevice::SupportsCapability` itself, so a snapshot taken from the renderer's switch would record answers no caller can observe and miss the ones every caller gets. **The snapshot is a C++ table in the test, not a data file** — it cannot go missing, no build directory can resolve a wrong path to it, and a changed value lands in the same diff as the change that caused it. **Each entry is classified `Fixed` or `Device`, which is the part that makes it usable as an oracle.** 52 are renderer/build decisions and are asserted exactly: drift fails. 41 come from the physical device (`VkPhysicalDeviceLimits`, a device feature, format properties) and are recorded, printed and compared but **never failed on** — another GPU is not a regression, and a snapshot that goes red on the owner's desktop teaches nothing. Recorded on **llvmpipe (LLVM 19.1.7)** under Xvfb. **Beyond the capture, five invariants that must hold on any device**, because a snapshot nothing cross-checks only records whatever is there: all **19** capability/feature mirror pairs agree; compute limits are non-zero exactly when compute is reported; a renderer refusing multi-stream input reports exactly one stream; the limit table and the direct query give the same maximum texture dimension; `SurfaceFormat::Color` is usable as a render target. **What the capture found, handed to its existing owner rather than hidden:** `query.shaderDialect = GlslVulkan` while **every** `feature.ShaderDialect*` row reads `unsupported`. Both are defensible alone — this renderer consumes SPIR-V bytecode and no source dialect at all, which is what the profile says — and together they are two answers to one question at one seam. That is F-08's tail exactly, so it goes to `VULKAN-264`, whose acceptance now names the invariant that will assert the agreement once the enumerator can express it. No failing test is added for it here: a red test asserting a contract that does not exist yet is noise. |
| VULKAN-009 | Turn on validation for the whole Vulkan suite and record the current message inventory | ✅ | **Inventory taken, and it is three messages — every one of them CNA-attributable, none a driver or layer artefact.** **Half of this row was already true:** validation is enabled by default in debug builds (`sEnableValidation` is `!NDEBUG`, `VulkanRenderer.cpp:186`-`192`) and `cmake-build-vulkan` is a Debug configuration, so the whole suite has been running with the Khronos layer active all along. What was missing was the *inventory*. **How it was taken:** the debug callback both records into `validationMessages_` and prints `[Vulkan Validation] …` to stderr, and `ctest` captures every test's output into `Testing/Temporary/LastTest.log` — so one ordinary suite run is the inventory, no instrumentation needed. **The result, across all 254 tests: three messages.** Each was then reproduced by running its test **alone**, so none is an artefact of suite ordering: (1) `vkMapMemory(): offset 0x0 plus size 0x1f40 (total 0x1f40) oversteps total array size 0xd4c` — `cna_test_vulkan_backbuffer_readback_dimension`; (2) `vkDestroyDevice(): Object Tracking — … VkFramebuffer … has not been destroyed` — `cna_test_vulkan_mrt_mixed_formats`; (3) `vkQueueSubmit(): … expects VkImage … (arrayLayer = 2) to be in layout SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL — instead, current layout is UNDEFINED` — `cna_test_vulkan_cube_volume_setdata_contract`. **All three are opened as rows before this closes**, per §26 rule 1: `VULKAN-404`, `VULKAN-405`, `VULKAN-406`. **The observation worth more than the count:** all three tests **pass**. They assert pixels, and pixels are what a wrong map size, a leaked framebuffer and an untransitioned array layer all happen to survive on llvmpipe. A suite can be 254/254 and still be telling the layer to look away — which is exactly what `VULKAN-393`'s gate exists to stop, and this row is its inventory. **Allowlist: empty.** Not one of the three names a driver or layer message; there is nothing to allow. |
| VULKAN-010 | Record the EasyGL delta baseline for the final gate | ✅ | **`plans/baselines/easygl-ctests-2026-09-06.txt`** -- the whole EasyGL CTest set, 322 names, one per line, checked in, with the generating command in its own header so it can be regenerated and diffed rather than trusted. **What `VULKAN-487` actually needs is the SET, not this plan's classification of it.** A gate that has to re-derive the comparison will not; a gate that can `diff` two sorted lists will. Combined with `VULKAN-007`'s script, the whole of `VULKAN-487`'s input is two commands: diff the current `ctest -N -R '^EasyGL_'` against this file to see what EasyGL gained, and run `renderer_coverage_delta.py --list easygl-only` to see which of those have no Vulkan equivalent. **One thing the row asked for that does not exist, said rather than faked:** "the EasyGL commit". This repository holds both renderers on one branch, so there is no separate EasyGL checkout to name -- the baseline is branch `vulkan` at the SHA recorded in the file's header, fork point `1bb2145d9` on `develop`. Writing a fabricated second SHA would have satisfied the sentence and helped nobody. |
| VULKAN-011 | Cross-check the shared-source failures against EasyGL | ✅ | **Executed during the planning session** (§7.6): `EasyGL_XnaPixelCenter`, `EasyGL_PointSamplingContract`, `EasyGL_DescriptorCapacityContract` and `EasyGL_DepthBias` all pass on the same machine, display and session where their Vulkan twins fail. Verdict recorded in §7.6 and §9.1 F-18: the four are Vulkan-specific divergences, not stale tests and not a software-driver artefact. Remediation is owned by `VULKAN-097` and `VULKAN-091`. |
| VULKAN-013 | Make a Vulkan environment with no usable device skip instead of core-dumping | ✅ | **Fixed in one file, with no example touched.** New `modules/renderers/vulkan/examples/vulkan_unavailable_device_skip.cpp`: a `std::terminate` handler installed by a static initializer, added to every example target by `cna_vulkan_test`. **Why not a `try`/`catch` in each `main()`:** there are **131** of them, all hand-rolled, and 120 share one canonical shape. Rewriting them all would have been a 131-file diff through files that other rows' evidence rests on. The handler is the same fix in the one place they already have in common -- the runtime's own uncaught-exception path -- so nothing in any example changes, including its output. **Both failures the row names are covered, measured rather than argued.** Forcing the RADV ICD under Xvfb: exit **77**, `[SKIP] no usable Vulkan device in this environment: Vulkan: no suitable GPU`. Pointing `VK_DRIVER_FILES` at nothing at all, which is the missing-`VK_KHR_surface` case: exit **77**, `[SKIP] … CNA::Platform: CreateWindow(…) failed: Installed Vulkan doesn't implement the VK_KHR_… extension`. Both previously produced `terminate called after throwing…` and a core dump. 77 is the code the root `CMakeLists` already applies as `SKIP_RETURN_CODE` to every registered test, so CTest reports these as **SKIPPED**. **The non-goal is enforced by construction and verified by mutation.** The handler recognises only those two message shapes and does nothing for anything else. Throwing a `std::runtime_error` from a test's `Draw()` -- after the device exists -- still gives `terminate called after throwing an instance of 'std::runtime_error'` and exit **134**, exactly as before. A wider match would convert a real defect into a skip, which is worse than the abort it replaces. **Precedent, not invention:** `CNA::Examples::RunPixelTest` already narrow-matches LLGL's `VK_ERROR_SURFACE_LOST_KHR` for the same reason, with the same reasoning written beside it; this file follows that shape and says so. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **338/338** with the guard linked into all 131 example targets -- the run that matters, because a static initializer in every test executable is the kind of change that is invisible until it is not. |
| VULKAN-012 | Re-measure every Vulkan result that was attributed to the software driver | ✅ | **A DRI3-capable virtual display was produced, and the answer is not `BLOCKED_BY_ENVIRONMENT`.** Recorded in full as new §7.5d. **Both routes the row proposed were unavailable and are named rather than inferred away:** `xserver-xorg-video-dummy` is not installed (`/usr/lib/xorg/modules/drivers/` has no `dummy_drv.so`), and Xwayland needs a Wayland compositor, of which none is installed. The third route works and is one command: **`Xwayland :150 -geometry 800x600 &`** serves X clients on its own, provides DRI3 where Xvfb does not, and `PickPhysicalDevice` then selects **`AMD Radeon 780M (RADV PHOENIX)`**. Every pre-existing display was checked first -- `:0`, `:77`, `:78`, `:99`, `:100`, `:131`, `:140`, `:1024`, `:1025` -- and only `:0`, the owner's real desktop, has DRI3; every virtual one selects llvmpipe. The owner asked for virtual displays and this is one. **The result:** `^Vulkan_` is **338/338 on llvmpipe in 190 s** and **329/338 on RADV in 865 s**. Nine tests fail on real hardware and none of them was known to, which means **every "338/338" recorded anywhere in this plan is llvmpipe's** -- said in §7.5d in those words, because a green number that does not name its device is the exact mistake this row exists to correct. **All nine classified, and the classification decides ownership rather than tidying the list:** `Vulkan_DescriptorCapacityContract` fails **every leg** with `vkAllocateDescriptorSets failed` and leaves 10 `VkImage`s unreleased at `vkDestroyDevice`, and `Vulkan_DescriptorPoolOverflow` aborts -- one **renderer defect** hidden by llvmpipe's tolerance, `VULKAN-181`. `Vulkan_OcclusionQuery_Precision` never completes in 60 frames -- `VULKAN-182`. Three tests exceed a 60 s CTest `TIMEOUT` written against a software rasterizer on a stack that is **4.5× slower** -- `VULKAN-183`, and deliberately not fixed by raising every timeout on sight. Two carry llvmpipe-specific premises -- `AppliedMultiSampleCount` asserts "this device cannot satisfy 8x" and RADV can; `MsaaDepthContract` expects `Depth24` back and RADV applies something else, which is `VULKAN-348`'s report-what-you-got working correctly -- so those are **test defects**, `VULKAN-184`. **The row's own framing needed inverting and that is worth saying:** it anticipated "a result that passes on real hardware is a driver-dependency finding, not a silent fix". What actually happened is the mirror image -- results that pass on the software driver and fail on real hardware -- and that is the more dangerous direction, because it is the one a green suite hides. |
| VULKAN-014 | Decide and document the Vulkan golden-image policy | ✅ | **Written into `docs/vulkan-renderer.md`, and writing it corrected two claims that were already there.** **The row's own premise was stale:** it says *"three Vulkan CTests"* compare against EasyGL-authored PNGs. There are **thirteen** — counted from this renderer's `CMakeLists.txt`, not remembered — and `VULKAN-437`'s section of the doc said **seventeen** and called them *"exact comparisons"*. Neither number was right, and the comparison is not exact: `CompareGoldenImage` takes a per-channel tolerance and twelve of the thirteen pass a non-zero one. Both sentences are corrected in place with a dated note rather than quietly rewritten. **The policy, and the part that makes it more than a preference:** all thirteen compare against an EasyGL-authored PNG and there are **zero** Vulkan-specific goldens — but the reason that holds is not discipline, it is that **the tolerance lives in the shared test source**, not in either renderer's registration. A tolerance therefore cannot be widened *for Vulkan*: widening it widens EasyGL's own gate by the same amount, in the same commit, in the same diff. "Share the golden" and "share the tolerance" are one rule. **The ladder is recorded with what each band pays for** — 0 for flat unblended colour, 8–10 for one sampling or blend step, 20–35 for lit and PBR shading, 30–40 where geometry arrives by a different route, 60 where a rasterized *edge* moves — because a tolerance with no stated reason is the thing that grows. **When a Vulkan-specific golden would be legitimate:** only for a divergence the document already names as semantic (the clip-space depth range, a pixel-centre convention), never for a numeric difference, and the row that authors one must say why the scene cannot be written to avoid it. **Non-goal**, restated: bit-identical floating-point lighting. **Opened by this row:** `VULKAN-185` — nothing measures how much of each tolerance is being used, so a scene that drifted from a max difference of 2 to 59 under a tolerance of 60 still prints `[PASS]`. The diagnostic script proves its own tolerance idle by re-running at 2; the golden CTests have no equivalent. **No production change; no test added.** |
| VULKAN-016 | Write the Phase-0 findings into this file and open any tasks they imply | ✅ | **Closed by checking, not by declaring. Every number in §7 and §9 was re-measured or re-derived today, and the two that had moved are corrected in place with a dated note rather than overwritten.** **§7.1** (device, driver, loader, layer versions and the device limits) stands as `VULKAN-005` regenerated it and `VULKAN-012` extended it to real RADV. **§7.3 re-stated:** `ctest -N` reports **9206** total and **346** `^Vulkan_`, against `VULKAN-006`'s 9199 / 338 — the growth is this campaign's own registrations. The EasyGL side is read from its `CMakeLists.txt` through `tools/vulkan/renderer_coverage_delta.py` (**321**) rather than from a `ctest -N` in `cmake-build-easygl`, because that directory belongs to whoever else is on this machine and `ctest -N` overwrites its `LastTest.log`. **§7.5/b/c/d** are per-row baselines maintained as the campaign runs and are current by construction. **§9.1 audited row by row:** all **33** findings (F-01…F-33) name an owning task, every owner exists, and exactly two have an owner that is still open — F-08 (`VULKAN-264`, the C-ABI dialect enumerator) and F-33 (`VULKAN-186`, the stale `EnvironmentMapEffect` golden). No finding is ownerless, which is the property this row exists to establish. **§9.2 resolved:** `D-01`–`D-06` were corrected by `VULKAN-481` and `D-07` by `VULKAN-012`; the section's own closing sentence — *"There is no `docs/vulkan-renderer.md` at all"* — is now false and is annotated rather than deleted, because it records what was true when the audit was written and `VULKAN-480` still owns finishing that document. **§9.3 confirmed by re-running the tool rather than by reading it:** **121** EasyGL CTests with no Vulkan equivalent, the same figure the section already carries. **No new gap was found that lacks a row** — the ones opened while establishing this baseline are `VULKAN-166`, `-167`, `-168`, `-169`, `-185` and `-186`, all recorded before this row closed, and the ID collision two of them caused is repaired and recorded under §26 rule 2. **No production change; no test added.** |

---

## 13. Phase 1 — Renderer contract and capability audit (`VULKAN-020`–`VULKAN-049`)

Method for every audit row in this phase: for each `IGraphicsRenderer` (and nested-interface)
virtual, record four things — EasyGL's override, Vulkan's override, the inherited default, and the
observable behaviour through the public XNA API. **A default no-op inherited by Vulkan while EasyGL
implements meaningful behaviour is a prime suspect and must be resolved to a classification, never
left blank.**

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-020 | Replace `SupportsCapability`'s catch-all `default: return true` with an explicit case per `GraphicsCapability` member | ✅ | **Done, and the catch-all was answering wrongly, not merely vaguely.** All 19 members now have their own arm and the switch has **no `default:`**; a value that is not an enumerator falls out to a `return false`. `cna_renderer_vulkan` is compiled with `-Werror=switch` (`modules/renderers/vulkan/CMakeLists.txt`, following `cmake/Harnesses.cmake`'s own `-Werror=deprecated-declarations` precedent), so appending a member to `CNA::GraphicsCapability` stops this target building. **Probed:** appending a throwaway enumerator produces `error: enumeration value 'ProbeOnlyNewCapability' not handled in switch [-Werror=switch]`. All three of the family's TUs were measured `-Wswitch` clean first, so the flag adds no pre-existing noise. **The defect the catch-all was hiding:** six members are answered *above* the renderer by `GraphicsDevice::SupportsCapability` from a virtual whose default is `false`. The renderer's catch-all said `true` for all six, so `IGraphicsRenderer::SupportsCapability` — the answer a C-ABI caller or a renderer-level test gets — **disagreed with the seam a game sees**, and for the two float render-target entries it claimed a format `RenderTarget2D`'s constructor refuses by name (§6.2's "reported true with no reachable path", exactly). Each of the six now answers from the *same* virtual the seam uses. Two device-derived answers also stopped being constants: `MultipleRenderTargets` comes from `VkPhysicalDeviceLimits::maxColorAttachments`, and `StencilBuffer` from whether `FindDepthFormat()`'s pick actually carries a stencil aspect (it falls back to `VK_FORMAT_D32_SFLOAT`, on which `StencilEnable` cannot work however correctly the pipeline maps it). The limits are cached where the device is *selected*, because this machine has two and `PickPhysicalDevice` takes the first that qualifies. **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_CapabilityContract` (`vulkan_capability_contract_test.cpp`), **24/24** — leg A asks both seams the same 19 questions and requires the same 19 answers, leg B requires an out-of-range cast to be refused, leg C drives every `false` answer into the public API and requires a named refusal. **Mutation probe:** with the old three-case + `default: return true` switch restored, the run is **15/24** — six seam disagreements, the out-of-range claim, and both float render-target entries reported `true` beside a constructor that refuses them. No mutation was needed to make leg A meaningful; it fails on the pre-task code as written. **A false positive worth recording:** leg C's first draft bound two `VertexPositionColor` buffers and the draw was *accepted*. That is not a defect — CNA reproduces FNA3D's rule that a later stream repeating an already-claimed `(usage, usageIndex)` pair is "not in use" and contributes nothing, so two identical declarations are one stream. The leg now binds a position-only and a colour-only declaration, which is a genuine split vertex, and gets the `NotSupportedException`. **Not re-proved here:** the `true` answers, each of which already has a dedicated Vulkan CTest that observes the behaviour — `ThreeD`/`Vulkan_BasicEffect_*`, `DepthStencilBuffer`/`Vulkan_GraphicsDevice_DepthContract`, `MultiSampleAntiAliasing`/`Vulkan_MSAA_4x_Readback`, `MultipleRenderTargets`/`Vulkan_MRT_MixedFormats`, `AnisotropicFiltering`/`Vulkan_TextureAnisotropic_DualTextureEffect`, `WireFrame`/`Vulkan_FillMode_WireFrame`, `OcclusionQuery`/`Vulkan_OcclusionQuery_PixelCount`, `CustomEffects`/`Vulkan_ShaderEffect_SpirV`, `Texture3D`/`Vulkan_Texture3D_Slices_RoundTrip`, `Instancing`/`Vulkan_DrawInstanced_3Instances`, `StencilBuffer`/`Vulkan_DepthStencilState_StencilOps`, `AdditiveBlending`/`Vulkan_AdditiveBlendContract`. Duplicating them into a weaker second copy would add no evidence. **Non-goal honoured:** no answer changed. `MultiSampleAntiAliasing` keeps its unconditional `true` here with a comment; `VULKAN-021` owns replacing it with the device's own sample-count masks. **Suite:** 212/218, the same six pre-existing failures. |
| VULKAN-021 | Derive `MultiSampleAntiAliasing` from the device instead of returning `true` unconditionally | ✅ | **Done.** The answer is `(framebufferColorSampleCounts & framebufferDepthSampleCounts) & ~VK_SAMPLE_COUNT_1_BIT`, read from the limits `VULKAN-020` cached at device *selection*. Colour **and** depth, deliberately: every MSAA path in this renderer attaches both, so a device that could multisample colour alone still could not run its MSAA render pass, and claiming the capability from the colour mask alone would be a promise `ApplyMultiSampleCount` then declines to keep. It is the same intersection `PickSampleCount` already reads, asked the same way. **Evidence:** leg E added to `Vulkan_CapabilityContract` (now **25/25**) requires the capability to agree, *in both directions*, with the count `GraphicsDeviceManager.setPreferMultiSampling(true)` + `ApplyChanges()` actually reaches — the public route, not the CNAEXT recreate escape hatch. Measured here: `capability=true, applied MultiSampleCount=4`. **Mutation probe:** forcing the derivation to `false` (what a 1-sample-only device would produce) fails leg E with `capability=false, applied MultiSampleCount=4`. **Limit of the evidence, recorded rather than glossed:** the run device (lavapipe) *does* offer 4x, so leg E discriminates an under-reporting derivation but cannot, on this device, tell a correctly-derived `true` from the old unconditional `true`. Separating those needs a device whose masks hold only `VK_SAMPLE_COUNT_1_BIT`, which this environment does not have; the renderer's own startup line (`MSAA up to 4x`) is the device-derived number the run saw. `VULKAN-012` re-measures on RADV when it provides one. **Suite:** 212/218, the same six pre-existing failures. |
| VULKAN-022 | Audit capability reporting at the `GraphicsDevice` seam, not the renderer's switch | ✅ | **Audited per member, and nothing disagrees.** **The six that bypass the renderer's capability switch, with where each answer really comes from:** `CompiledEffects` → `GetRenderer().SupportsCompiledEffects()`; `FloatRenderTargets` → `SupportsSurfaceFormatAsRenderTargetEXT(Vector4)`; `HalfFloatRenderTargets` → the same for `HdrBlendable`; `HalfFloatTextureLinearFiltering` → `GetRenderer().SupportsHalfFloatTextureLinearFilteringEXT()`; `ComputeShaders` → `GetRenderer().SupportsComputeShadersEXT()`; `IndirectDraw` → `GetRenderer().SupportsIndirectDrawEXT()`. **The finding that changes how the row should be read:** "answered above the renderer seam" does **not** mean "answered by shared code". All six still ask the renderer — through a *different virtual* than the capability switch, chosen precisely because a switch ending in `default: return true` would otherwise claim a capability it has never heard of (`MOD-100`/`MOD-123`/`MOD-1500`/`MOD-2090` each say so where they added one). So the shared layer is not overriding Vulkan anywhere; it is routing around a hazard this renderer no longer has, because `VULKAN-020` gave its switch all 19 cases and no `default`. **Is the shared answer right for Vulkan?** Yes, for all six. At this classic-plan audit's closure `VULKAN-470` observed float/filter behavior while compute and indirect were honest absences; the later modern rows `MOD-2241` and `MOD-2245` changed those two to device-derived working paths and updated the same agreement test. `CompiledEffects` still follows `CNA_VULKAN_COMPILED_EFFECTS` — its override is **inside** that guard, so the base's `false` is what an OFF build reports. **No member where the two paths disagree:** `Vulkan_CapabilityContract` leg A asserts `renderer == device` for all **19**, and it passes after both later transitions. |
| VULKAN-023 | Audit the applied-vs-requested reporting trio | ✅ | **Yes, `PresentationParameters` can echo back a sample count the device did not apply, and it does so on every device. Two remediation rows are open: `VULKAN-347` (the sample count) and `VULKAN-348` (the two formats).** **The mechanism, read at both ends of one seam:** none of the three virtuals is overridden here, so all three take the identity default — and all three describe a value this renderer really does substitute. `sampleCount_ = PickSampleCount(physicalDevice_, args.multiSampleCount)` at construction (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:1838`) returns the highest supported count **≤** the request, the swapchain takes whatever the surface offers (`:2814`), and `PickDepthFormat` falls back through four candidates (`:287`-`:318`). **Where it becomes observable — narrowed by `VULKAN-347`'s measurement, and the narrowing is the useful part:** two sites write `GetAppliedMultiSampleCountEXT(requested)` into `presentationParameters_`, and only one of them can be read wrong. `GraphicsDevice.cpp:3309` (direct construction) looks like the dangerous one and its comment says so, but Game startup reaches `Reset()` afterwards, which writes the count back from `ApplyMultiSampleCount`'s own return (`:663`-`:665`) and corrects it before a game can look. The site that survives is `GraphicsDevice::SetPresentationParameters` (`:2798`), the CNAEXT store-only path, whose own comment says it does not reconfigure MSAA and depends on the renderer reporting what is really in effect. Two drafts of `VULKAN-347`'s test passed with the fix reverted before this was measured rather than reasoned about. **Why the sample count is the sharp one:** the divergence needs only a request that is not itself a supported count, so 3→2, 5→4 and 7→4 diverge on hardware and on llvmpipe alike. It does not depend on a device that lacks something. **Two things checked before the finding was written, because either would have made it wrong:** the intended semantic is *what is in effect*, not *what would be applied for this request* — GDI's override ignores its argument and returns `GetMultiSampleCount()` (`GdiRenderer.hpp:70`), and both call sites use the answer as a write-back, which agrees; and `SampleCountToInt` maps `VK_SAMPLE_COUNT_1_BIT` through its `default:` arm to **0**, so a fix cannot accidentally turn XNA's "no multisampling" from 0 into 1. **Why two rows rather than one:** the count maps one-to-one, while the formats need a `VkFormat`→XNA reverse mapping that does not exist here, plus a judgement about how to report a substitution that is *better* than the request. That is a second commit's worth of work, and §26 rule 5 prefers splitting to widening. **No test is added by this row:** the test the acceptance describes belongs with the fix, since it must fail before it and pass after, and a red test asserting a contract nothing implements yet is noise. `VULKAN-347` carries its specification verbatim. |
| VULKAN-024 | Audit the `GraphicsProfile` ceiling and limit queries | ✅ | **All six asked of the device rather than read off the interface, and one real finding fell out of the answer.** New `Vulkan_ProfileLimitsAudit` (`modules/renderers/vulkan/examples/vulkan_profile_limits_audit_test.cpp`), **6/6**, plus one small read-only addition, `GetDeviceLimitsEXT()`, because a claim about hardware cannot be checked without the hardware's own numbers. **Two of the six are claims about hardware, and both hold here:** `GetMaxTextureDimension()` returns a hardcoded **16384** whose comment says it is "the value real-world Vulkan implementations report" -- this device reports `maxImageDimension2D` = **16384** exactly, so the default is right and has **no margin**, which is the more useful half of that answer; `GetMaxVertexStreams()` returns **16** against `maxVertexInputBindings` = **32**. The test asserts the relationship rather than the constants, so a device that contradicts either default fails the leg with both numbers printed -- which is the signal the row asked for, delivered by the suite instead of by a reading. **The four `*ForProfileEXT` ceilings cannot be contradicted by hardware and are not asserted as if they could.** A profile ceiling is a portability restriction, deliberately independent of what the device could do; all four return "no ceiling" on both renderers, and the legs check only that Reach never permits more than HiDef, printing the matching device limit next to each for the record. **The finding, and it is why asking beat reading:** this device's `maxImageDimension3D` is **4096**, four times smaller than the 2D limit and smaller than every shared default -- and **no limit query for volume extents exists anywhere in `IGraphicsRenderer`**. `GetMaxTextureDimension()` is documented and used for `Texture2D` only (`Texture2D.cpp:78` and the two `FromStream` routes); nothing consults a 3D limit. Measured directly: `Texture3D(4097, 4, 4)` on this device **constructs without throwing and without a single validation message**, so a caller is handed an object whose backing image the device cannot have created. That is **`VULKAN-180`**. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **338/338**. The probe that produced the finding was temporary and is not in the committed test -- the row it opened owns the assertion. |
| VULKAN-025 | Decide what `AcquireThreadContextLeaseEXT` means on Vulkan | ✅ | **The null default is correct for the contract as written, and the value of this row is the scope it pins around it.** **The contract:** the virtual returns "a lifetime token, or null when this renderer needs no explicit context lease" (`IGraphicsRenderer.hpp:1836`). **What EasyGL does with it:** takes a `std::recursive_mutex` and makes its GL context current on the calling thread (`EasyGLRenderer.cpp:5374`) — a GL context is thread-affine, so a `ContentReader` built on another thread cannot touch the GPU without one. The mutex is recursive because the framework already nests: `ContentReader`'s constructor holds a lease for its whole lifetime (`ContentReader.cpp:136`) and `GraphicsDeviceManager::BeginDraw` holds one for the whole frame (`GraphicsDeviceManager.cpp:354`), so loading an asset during a frame acquires twice on one thread. **Why Vulkan's null is right:** there is no thread-affine context. A `VkDevice` is usable from any thread, so there is literally nothing for a token to own or restore, and both `RendererThreadContextLeaseRelease` enumerators are meaningless here rather than unimplemented. **The measurement that decides the harder question, and the reason this row does not add a mutex:** the Vulkan renderer contains **no synchronization at all** — zero `std::mutex`/`recursive_mutex`/`shared_mutex`/`lock_guard`/`unique_lock`/`scoped_lock`/`atomic`/`condition_variable`/`thread`/`call_once` and no `pthread_`, across `modules/renderers/vulkan/{src,include}` (measured 2026-09-05). Returning a mutex-backed token would serialize *lease holders* while every other entry point stayed unguarded, which is exactly the "thread-safety guarantee the renderer does not have" this row's own non-goal forbids. **Nothing over-claims, checked rather than assumed:** there is no thread-safety capability or feature anywhere in `GraphicsRendererDescriptor.hpp` or `IGraphicsRenderer.hpp`, so the lease's return value is the only statement on the subject, and it says "no context to lease" — not "callable from any thread". **Is the divergence from EasyGL observable?** Only by a game driving the device from two threads. CNA never does: `std::thread`/`std::async`/`jthread` do not appear in `modules/content`, `modules/runtime` or `modules/graphics` outside tests. And a game that does is outside XNA's contract on both renderers — EasyGL's mutex serializes lease acquisition, which does not make arbitrary concurrent renderer calls safe there either. So this is recorded as a verdict, not opened as a defect (§26 rule 3). **Test:** `Vulkan_ThreadContextLease` (`vulkan_thread_context_lease_test.cpp`), **5/5**. Leg A asserts null for **both** enumerators, since testing one leaves the other free to diverge. Leg B nests two leases inside the frame lease — the `ContentReader`-during-a-frame shape — where a renderer holding a non-recursive lock would deadlock; a hang is caught by the ctest `TIMEOUT`, which is the only way to observe a deadlock from inside the process that deadlocked. Legs C and D are the scenario the contract exists for: a `Texture2D` created while a lease is held, and then actually rendered, so C cannot pass by creating something unusable. **Documentation:** the threading statement belongs in `docs/vulkan-renderer.md`, which `VULKAN-480` owns; this row supplies the text and the measurement rather than writing a second capability document. **Non-goal, restated because it is the whole point:** no mutex, and no promise that this renderer may be called from more than one thread. |
| VULKAN-026 | Decide whether Vulkan needs a `CanBeginDrawEXT` override | ✅ | **The `true` default is right, and it is now measured rather than argued.** **Why the question is not trivial:** the hook's own call site records its purpose — `GraphicsDeviceManager::BeginDraw` (`GraphicsDeviceManager.cpp:351`) skips Draw *and* Present for the frame when it returns false, and its comment says FNA "returns true whenever a device exists", the override existing for the browser's asynchronous WebGL lost/restored interval. EasyGL's implementation is exactly `!metagl::IsContextLost()` (`EasyGLRenderer.cpp:5170`), so it is a context-loss query wearing a general name. **The reachable Vulkan analogue:** an out-of-date swapchain. `SubmitFrame` calls `RecreateSwapchain()` and returns false (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:11010`), `Present()` ignores that false, and `GetBackBufferData`'s own comment calls the state "common on first frame under Wayland/RADV" — so it is real, not theoretical. The other two states the row named are not: `Present` returns early when `swapchainImages_` is empty or the extent is zero, before any of this. **Driven, not assumed:** the state arrives when the window manager decides, so it cannot be produced by resizing a window under Xvfb. New test-only `SetSwapchainOutOfDateForTestEXT(count)` injects it, **replacing** the acquire rather than following it — an image handed over and then declared stale would leave `imageAvailableSemaphores_[currentFrame_]` signalled with no waiter, which is a different defect and would show up as a synchronization hazard instead of this branch. **The answer:** `CanBeginDrawEXT()` is true before, during and after the dropped frame. Vulkan recovers inside `Present`; there is no interval in which the device exists and a draw is invalid, so asking the caller to skip a frame would lose one for nothing. **Test:** `Vulkan_SwapchainOutOfDate` (`vulkan_swapchain_outofdate_test.cpp`), **7/7**, four legs each of which fails alone — A an ordinary frame draws and reads back blue (without it every later assertion is satisfied by a renderer that draws nothing); B the acquire counter does not advance across the injected frame (without it the injection could be a no-op and the rest still pass); C the answer itself, asserted in the ordinary state and after recovery; D what the dropped frame leaves behind. **What D found, and the correction it forced:** the dropped frame's recorded work **is** retained — both queues are cleared only inside `RecordCommandBuffer`, which a failed acquire never reaches, and `RecreateSwapchain` does not touch them either. The first draft of that leg measured `pending3D_` alone and reported "nothing retained" for a `SpriteBatch` quad, which lives in `activeBatches_`; measuring one of two queues is how an audit concludes the opposite of the truth, so both counters exist now (`GetPendingDrawCountEXT`, `GetPendingBatchCountEXT`) and the leg reads both. **Why the retention is not a defect, so no row is opened for it:** it is drained by the next successful submit (leg D3 asserts the queues are empty afterwards), so it cannot accumulate; and it cannot be observed, because the retained work is replayed *before* the next frame's `Clear` — it was recorded earlier in the same ordered stream — so a clearing game never sees it, and a non-clearing game is already relying on backbuffer contents that `PresentationParameters` declares undefined (`renderTargetUsage_(RenderTargetUsage::DiscardContents)`, `PresentationParameters.cpp:16`). Leg D2 confirms the recovery frame shows its own content and not a mixture. **Non-goal:** `VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST`, which is a different event and belongs to `VULKAN-334`. |
| VULKAN-027 | Complete the interface-by-interface contract table and open every gap it finds | ✅ | **The table is §31 (Appendix A): 186 virtuals across the eleven interfaces, every one classified.** **The census:** 102 both override, **54 EasyGL overrides and Vulkan does not**, 3 Vulkan-only (`VULKAN_STRONGER`: `GetShaderDialectEXT`, `SetStringMarkerEXT`, `ISpriteBatchRenderer::SetImmediateMode`), 27 neither. Only the 54 carry a possible divergence, and each is justified individually in A.1 rather than dismissed as a group. **The one real defect is `IEffectRenderer`'s four array uniform setters** — silently ignored on Vulkan while EasyGL implements them, and reachable outside the engine layer because `ShaderEffect.hpp` is `CNAEXT`-marked but not `CNA_CNAEXT`-guarded. **Correction to this row as first written:** it claimed the gap was newly discovered and opened `VULKAN-265` for it. It was not new — **`VULKAN-252` has owned it since the planning session** (matrix row *Custom `ShaderEffect` — uniform arrays*), and the table's real contribution is narrower and worth stating exactly: `VULKAN-252` is about *implementing* the setters against a uniform buffer, and left the **silence in place until it lands**. That interim is what `VULKAN-265` now owns — refuse instead of pretending — and it is a predecessor of `VULKAN-252`, not a duplicate of it. **Two candidates were investigated and withdrawn on evidence, which is the part worth keeping:** (a) the `SetDataOptions` overloads look like dropped semantics, but `Pending3DDraw` copies `vbData`/`ibData` at draw time (`VulkanRenderer.hpp:2356`), so `Discard`/`NoOverwrite` cannot be violated by anything a later `SetData` does; (b) `CreateRenderTarget2DEXT`/`CreateRenderTargetCubeEXT` look like a dropped `SurfaceFormat`, but Vulkan's `ClassifyRenderTargetFormatEXT` defers and the deferral reduces to *Color only* (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:2104`), so a non-`Color` render target is refused one layer above and the EXT entry point never sees one. **Everything else resolves to a default that is correct** (`GetDefaultViewportRect` composes onto the overridden `GetViewportSize`; `SetRenderTargetCubeFace` composes onto the implemented `BindAsRenderTargetFace`; `HasRealDepthBuffer` already tells the truth; `ExecutesShaderEffectSourceEXT`'s `false` is load-bearing and correct), **honest about an absent feature** (compressed transfer, indirect draw, compute, storage buffers, GPU timer), **not applicable** (the GL handle/bind/CPU-pixel-sharing hooks), **unobservable** (`GetSizeEXT` and `ITextureRenderer::GetSurfaceFormatEXT` have zero callers in the tree), or **already owned by an open row** (`VULKAN-025` for the thread-context lease, `VULKAN-026` for `CanBeginDrawEXT`, `VULKAN-172` for compressed formats, `VULKAN-334` for context/device loss). No `TEST_GAP` was found: every classification above is already observed by an existing CTest or is unreachable. **Method and its two traps are recorded in §31** so a regenerated table is comparable — a braced default argument defeats a naive `override` pattern and hides real overrides, and the word "virtual" in prose invents methods that do not exist; both produced wrong counts here before they were caught. |

---

## 14. Phase 2 — 2D: SpriteBatch and SpriteFont (`VULKAN-050`–`VULKAN-089`)

`docs/graphics-renderer-feature-matrix.md` still says these Vulkan cells were "not separately
re-audited (Task 861)". They have since gained twelve dedicated CTests (§9.2 D-05). This phase
re-audits from the current tree rather than from that sentence.

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-050 | Prove `SpriteSortMode::Immediate` and `SpriteSortMode::Texture` on Vulkan | ✅ | **Three of this row's premises were wrong, and the row is closed on what the measurement actually said rather than on what it asked for.** New renderer-agnostic test `modules/graphics/examples/spritebatch_sort_mode_semantics_test.cpp`, registered as **`Vulkan_SpriteBatch_SortModeSemantics`** and — deliberately — also as **`EasyGL_SpriteBatch_SortModeSemantics`**, because the row's reasoning (“Vulkan is the only renderer that overrides `SetImmediateMode`, so it is the only one that can be wrong”) can only be tested by running the same measurement on a second renderer. **Premise 1 — “must fail if `SetImmediateMode` is stubbed out” cannot be honoured, because stubbing it out changes nothing.** `VulkanSpriteBatchRenderer` stores `immediateMode_` and reads it **only** inside `#if defined(CNA_VULKAN_COMPILED_EFFECTS)`, which is OFF in `cmake-build-vulkan`. The override the row was built around is dead code in the configuration this campaign builds and tests. What *can* be asserted is the seam above it — that `Begin()` carries the mode down at all — and leg B2 does exactly that, using the one thing the mode genuinely gates today: `SpriteBatch::DrawMeshEXT` refuses every sort mode but `Immediate`, before the renderer is consulted, so `Deferred` must come back with the sort-mode refusal and `Immediate` must not (this renderer then refuses on *capability* grounds instead — a different message for a different reason). **Premise 2 — “a texture mutated between two `Draw` calls” does not distinguish the modes, on either renderer.** Two mutation routes were measured. A full-level `SetData(const Color*)` builds a **fresh** renderer texture and detaches the wrapper (REMED-GFX-223's ContentManager-cache isolation), so a queued sprite keeps the old resource and the modes look identical for a reason that has nothing to do with sorting. An in-place `SetDataRGBA` → `UpdatePixels` route removes that confound — and the modes are *still* identical, because both renderers accumulate **below** the `SpriteBatch` seam: `SpriteBatch::flushSingle` does forward each `Immediate` sprite straight through, but `EasyGLSpriteBatchRenderer::Draw` only appends to a vertex batch (flushed when the bound texture changes, or at `End()`) and `VulkanSpriteBatchRenderer::Draw` records into `activeBatches_` for replay at `Present`. Both therefore rasterize every sprite of the batch against the texture's **final** contents. That is a divergence from XNA, it is CNA-wide rather than Vulkan-specific, and per §26 rule 3 it is opened as **`VULKAN-057`** rather than asserted here in either direction: leg B prints the measurement and asserts only what `Immediate` must do regardless — draw every sprite, each filling its own destination rectangle, none dropped or misplaced. **Premise 3 — “`Texture`-sorted batching does not reorder overlapping sprites” is not a guarantee XNA makes, and asserting it produced a false Vulkan pass.** `SpriteSortMode::Texture` is defined as *“same as `Deferred`, except sprites are sorted by texture prior to drawing”* (`SpriteSortMode.cs`); FNA orders them by an arbitrary `textureHash` through an **unstable** `Array.Sort` (`SpriteBatch.cs:1592`-`1598`), and Microsoft's own remark scopes the mode to non-overlapping sprites. Written the row's way, the leg **passed on Vulkan and failed on EasyGL** for no better reason than which `ITextureRenderer` allocation held the lower address — CNA sorts on the pointer. Recording that matters more than the pass would have: it stops a later session “fixing” EasyGL to satisfy a guarantee that does not exist. The leg is replaced by the two promises that do hold — **C1** nothing is lost (two textures, two destinations, both arrive with their own colour) and **C2** grouping never reorders *within* a group (two overlapping sprites sharing one texture come out in issue order, which is what CNA's `std::stable_sort` buys over FNA). **Mutation-tested, one mutation per leg, each caught by exactly the intended leg and by no other:** `Begin()` forcing `sortMode_ = Deferred` → only **B2** fails; `std::reverse` before the `Texture` `stable_sort` (an unstable sort modelled) → only **C2** fails; `flushSingle(info)` removed from the `Immediate` path → only **B** fails, reading back the clear colour. `SpriteBatch.cpp` restored bit-for-bit afterwards. **Evidence:** 5/5 legs pass on **both** renderers; `Vulkan_SpriteBatch_SortModeSemantics` and `EasyGL_SpriteBatch_SortModeSemantics` green as CTests, the Vulkan one under the `VULKAN-408` validation gate. No production change — the two real findings are recorded (dead `SetImmediateMode`) and handed on (`VULKAN-057`). |
| VULKAN-051 | Port the SpriteBatch blend-state leak proof to Vulkan | ✅ | **Already satisfied, and found out by trying to satisfy it again.** The 23-row registration batch attempted to register `easygl_spritebatch_blendstate_leak_test.cpp` here and CMake refused: *“another target with the same name already exists”*. `VULKAN-055` registered that exact source as **`Vulkan_SpriteBatch_BlendStateLeak`** while enumerating the SpriteBatch-to-3D boundary, and the §10.2 matrix row already cites it — this row was simply never moved. Closed as covered rather than duplicated; no second registration, and none of the test's coverage changes. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_SpriteBatch_BlendStateLeak` is in the suite and passes (part of the **308/308**).
| VULKAN-052 | Prove `TextureAddressMode::Mirror` through the plain SpriteBatch route on Vulkan | ✅ | `easygl_texture_address_mode_mirror_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_TextureAddressMode_Mirror`. Vulkan proved `Mirror` only through an **effect** path; the plain `SpriteBatch` route -- where the sampler comes from `Begin`'s `SamplerState` rather than from a stock effect's own sampler slot -- had no proof, and those are two different places the address mode has to survive. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-053 | Prove SpriteBatch's render-target-derived projection on Vulkan | ✅ | `easygl_spritebatch_rendertarget_size_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_SpriteBatch_RenderTargetSize`. The orthographic divisor must follow the **bound target's** size, not the backbuffer's -- the exact arithmetic REMED-GFX-072 corrected here and which `Vulkan_SpriteBatch_CustomViewport_RT` observes only indirectly. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-054 | Prove `DrawString` rotation, scale and `SpriteEffects` on Vulkan | ✅ | `easygl_spritefont_effects_flip_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_SpriteFont_EffectsFlip` and `easygl_spritefont_effects_rotation_scale_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_SpriteFont_EffectsRotationScale`. Vulkan had four `SpriteFont` tests and all four draw glyphs **unrotated and unscaled**; the glyph-quad transform was the one part of `DrawString` with no coverage at all here. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-055 | Audit renderer state leakage across SpriteBatch↔3D boundaries on Vulkan | ✅ | **Enumerated at the renderer boundary, item by item; one test registered, one stale claim corrected, one unexamined case handed to `VULKAN-400`.** **The boundary is eight items**, and the first thing the audit established is that five of them cannot leak, because the XNA layer re-drives them unconditionally: `SpriteBatch::Begin` (`SpriteBatch.cpp:226`-`234`) calls `SetCustomEffect`, `SetTransformMatrix`, `SetSamplerFilter`, `SetSamplerAddressMode` and `SetImmediateMode` on **every** `Begin()`, resolving a null `samplerState` to `SamplerState::LinearClamp` rather than inheriting — the comment there says so and the code matches — and `End()` (`:265`) additionally calls `SetCustomEffect(nullptr)`. So `VulkanSpriteBatchRenderer::Begin()` not resetting `pendingFilter_`/`pendingAddressU_`/`pendingAddressV_`/`customEffect_` is harmless: nothing can observe the stale value. **Blend:** carried per draw, not globally — `Pending3DDraw` captures `blendEnabled_`/`blendParams_` at record time (`:7514`, `:12131`, `:12179`), so a 3D draw uses the state current at *its* call. That a `SpriteBatch` batch leaves its blend state in effect afterwards is XNA's own behaviour, not a leak: FNA's `SpriteBatch.Begin` changes `GraphicsDevice.BlendState` as a lasting side effect. **Test registered for it:** `easygl_spritebatch_blendstate_leak_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_SpriteBatch_BlendStateLeak`, renderer-agnostic (zero renderer-specific includes), asserting it by pixel arithmetic rather than by inspection: `Begin(Deferred, BlendState::Additive)` draws a corner sprite, then a full-viewport red 3D quad is drawn **without touching `BlendState` again**, and the readback far from the sprite must be `red(200,0,0) + gray(100,100,100)` clamped to **(255,100,100)**. If the batch's `Additive` did not survive `End()`, the same pixel reads `(200,0,0)` — the two answers are far apart, which is what makes the probe discriminating rather than merely present. **Sampler:** the one item that genuinely crosses the boundary. `FlushTexture` calls `ApplySamplerState(0, pendingFilter_, …)` (`:1255`), which writes `slotSamplers_[0]`, and every 3D descriptor build reads `slotSamplers_[0]`/`[1]` (`:12734`, `:12780`, `:12815`, `:12824`, `:12858`, `:12861`). So a batch's sampler persists into later 3D draws. **This is parity, not a Vulkan defect:** EasyGL's SpriteBatch calls the identical `ApplySamplerState(0, pendingFilter_, …)` (`EasyGLRenderer.cpp:4339`, `:4506`) and binds per slot on every draw, and XNA's own `SpriteBatch` likewise leaves its sampler in effect after `End()`. **Viewport and scissor:** snapshotted per queued item at enqueue time rather than read at replay — REMED-GFX-062 and REMED-GFX-013 — for both `PendingBatch` and `Pending3DDraw`, and covered by `Vulkan_Deferred_Viewport` and `Vulkan_Deferred_Scissor`. **Rasterizer state:** `Vulkan_SpriteBatch_BeginRasterizerState`, the row's own starting evidence. **A stale claim found and corrected rather than left standing:** the comment on `Vulkan_StockEffectSamplerContract` in `modules/renderers/vulkan/examples/CMakeLists.txt` still says the stock-effect descriptor builders "take NO sampler parameter at all, so all 15 of their combined-image-sampler bindings are hardcoded to `defaultSampler_`". That is no longer true — only **two** pre-baked descriptor sets still name `defaultSampler_` (`:465`, `:904`, the texture's and the render target's own), and every stock-effect path builds from `slotSamplers_[]`. A comment describing a fixed defect as current is how the next audit reaches the wrong conclusion, so it is rewritten to say what was true and what is true now. **No leak found, so no remediation row is opened for one** (§26 rule 3) — but one case in scope was **not** examined and is not silently dropped: the registered blend test deliberately draws a small corner sprite to avoid what its own comment calls a "still-open" finding, that a **full-backbuffer** `SpriteBatch` draw before any 3D draw in the same frame breaks that frame's 3D rendering entirely. That finding appears nowhere in this plan and has never been checked on Vulkan. It is squarely within this row's scope, so it becomes `VULKAN-400`. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` grows to **247/247**. |
| VULKAN-056 | Audit `SpriteBatch` `Draw` overload coverage on Vulkan against the XNA surface | ✅ | **Enumerated, mapped, and the answer is `SHARED_LOGIC` for every overload with no Vulkan pixel test — with the reason, which is the part the row asked for.** **The surface is 16 overloads:** ten `Draw` (three of them `CNAEXT` — `(tex, float x, float y)`, `(tex, destRect, srcRect, Color)` and its eight-argument sibling) and six `DrawString` (three taking `std::string`, three taking `System::Text::StringBuilder`). **Method:** every source registered into a Vulkan test target (344 of them, from this renderer's own `CMakeLists.txt`) parsed for calls on a `SpriteBatch` receiver, with the arguments split on balanced parentheses and classified by count and shape; receivers that are not `SpriteBatch` were excluded, which matters because the first pass counted `Model::Draw(world, view, projection)` as a three-argument sprite draw. **Seven overloads reach a Vulkan pixel test:** `(tex, destRect, Color)`; the two `CNAEXT` rectangle forms, which carry **72** and **13** files between them and are what almost every test in this suite uses; both nine-argument `(tex, Vector2, optSrc, …)` forms, scalar and `Vector2` scale, through `easygl_spritebatch_scale_test.cpp`; `DrawString(font, string, Vector2, Color)`; and `DrawString(…, Vector2 scale, …)`. **Nine do not**, and none of them becomes a Vulkan row: `(tex, float x, float y)`, `(tex, Vector2, Color)`, `(tex, Vector2, optSrc, Color)`, the two `std::optional`-taking rectangle forms, `DrawString(…, float scale, …)` and all three `StringBuilder` overloads. **Why that is `SHARED_LOGIC` rather than a gap:** every `Draw` overload validates its own arguments and then calls the **same** `pushSprite`, and the renderer sees only the record `pushSprite` builds — so a per-renderer pixel test per overload would exercise one renderer path sixteen times, and what actually needs testing is that each overload builds the right record. **That test exists and is renderer-independent:** `modules/graphics/tests/…/SpriteBatchTests.cpp`, **74 tests**, covering every one of the sixteen for the before-`Begin` refusal, the recorded parameters (including `nullopt` meaning the whole texture), sub-pixel destinations, non-finite and out-of-range coordinates, the sort modes and `DrawString`'s `SpriteEffects` — and it runs in this configuration, inside the full `ctest` this plan measures after every shared change. **No new rows**, per §26 rule 3: this is an audit that found no defect, and says so with the evidence rather than inventing work. **The one thing worth keeping** is the method: `tools/` has no overload-coverage script, and this one lives in the row rather than the tree because its value was the single answer, not a gate. |
| VULKAN-057 | `SpriteSortMode::Immediate` does not submit per sprite: mid-batch resource mutation is visible to sprites already issued | ✅ | **Closed on the second arm of its own acceptance — the divergence is refused, and the refusal carries its cost, its reason and a test that will announce it if it ever changes.** **Why not the first arm.** Honouring `Immediate` means submitting the recorded work before `Draw` returns, and on this renderer that has two different prices depending on where the sprite goes. **Off-screen**, the machinery exists — `FlushDeferredRenderTarget` records, submits and waits. At this row's measurement date it opened with a full `DeviceWaitIdleEXT()` and closed with `vkQueueSubmit` + `vkQueueWaitIdle`; `MOD-2253` later narrowed that to the reusable-slot fence plus one exact dependency-closure submit/fence, without changing the fact that honouring `Immediate` would synchronously submit per sprite rather than issue a draw call. **On the backbuffer**, where a `SpriteBatch` normally draws, there is no such path at all, and the absence is deliberate and documented in the code: `FlushDeferredRenderTarget` excludes backbuffer cycles because they need a swapchain image and *"REMED-GFX-144's one-acquire-one-submit-one-present-per-frame must not change"*. That is not a cost question but a contract question, and the contract was written to fix a real defect. **The measured half**, so the refusal is not an assertion: a new leg D in `spritebatch_sort_mode_semantics_test.cpp` times 64 one-sprite batches into a 2×2 off-screen target with **one** readback at the end against the same 64 with a readback after **each** — the renderer's own "force the queued work to the GPU now" path, which is exactly the machinery a per-sprite submission would have to use. **Measured three times on llvmpipe under Xvfb `:99` on 2026-09-07: 443, 505 and 525 µs added per sprite, making the forced route 2.7× to 3.4× the batched one. And on real hardware it is worse rather than better** — on RADV (AMD Radeon 780M, Xwayland `:150`) the same 64 sprites cost 7.9 ms batched and 91.6 ms forced: **1.31 ms added per sprite, ×11.6**. Printed and never asserted: this machine runs several agents at once and a wall-clock threshold here would be a flake generator, which is what `VULKAN-182` paid to learn; the number belongs in the docs with its date and device, not in an exit code. Leg D asserts only that the two routes draw the same pixel, because a cost comparison between two routes that draw different things prices nothing. **And it is still not a parity gap.** `VULKAN-050` measured the same behaviour on EasyGL, for a different internal reason — its sprite renderer appends to a vertex batch flushed only on a texture change or at `End()` — so the two renderers agree and the divergence is CNA-wide. Fixing it here alone would not fix CNA; it would only make the two renderers disagree, which is the opposite of what this campaign is for. **Recorded where the row asked:** `docs/vulkan-renderer.md` gains a section with the two prices above and what a game does instead (`End()` the batch before mutating the texture — one submission boundary rather than one per sprite), and the CNA-wide half is a new `SpriteBatch` entry in §4 of `docs/xna-4-api-coverage.md`, marked *Partial (divergent by decision)*. **The `[INFO]` line is re-pointed**, as the row required: it now says the divergence is refused and documented, and carries the instruction that if it ever prints red/blue the refusal was reversed and the two documents move — not the print. **Leg B is unchanged and that is the answer to its half of the acceptance:** it already asserts only what `Immediate` must do regardless of the submission model — every sprite drawn, in place, in issue order — and re-pointing it at CNA's answer would cement the divergence in a test, which `VULKAN-050` explicitly declined to do and which this row does not undo. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_SpriteBatch_SortModeSemantics` **6/6** on llvmpipe and on RADV. **The EasyGL figure was not taken:** `cmake-build-easygl/` belongs to another session on this machine, the EasyGL targets are not configured in `cmake-build-vulkan`, and leg D runs on whichever renderer hosts the test — so that number is one `ctest` away for whoever owns that tree, and both documents say so rather than implying it was measured. |
| VULKAN-058 | `SpriteBatch` ignores the `DepthStencilState` its `Begin()` installs | ✅ | **A real Vulkan-vs-EasyGL gap, found by `VULKAN-096`'s read of the 2D pipeline and turned from a candidate into a defect by running the measurement on EasyGL.** **Observed, before the fix, from one renderer-agnostic source run on both:** write a stencil pattern over the left half of a `Depth24Stencil8` render target with a 3D draw, then `SpriteBatch::Begin(..., a StencilFunction::Equal state, ...)` and draw a full-target sprite. EasyGL paints the **left half**; Vulkan painted **both**. The control leg -- the same stencil consumed by a 3D draw -- passed on both, so the pattern really was in the buffer and it was the sprite path that discarded the state. **Cause:** `GetOrCreatePipeline2D` and its MSAA twin hardcoded `depthTestEnable = VK_FALSE` and no stencil at all, and neither the state nor its dynamic values reached the 2D route. EasyGL has no equivalent gap because it applies `ApplyDepthStencilState` to a global GL device that its sprite draws then share.
