XNA agrees with EasyGL -- stencil-masked sprites are an ordinary XNA idiom -- so `§5`'s authority order settles it without needing FNA. **Fixed along the whole path rather than at the pipeline alone:** `BatchSnapshot` now captures the `DepthStencilState` at `End()` **by value**, exactly as it already captured the `BlendState`, so a state object changed or destroyed before the deferred Present record cannot be read then; both 2D factories build their depth/stencil from the shared `FillDepthStencilState` the 3D route uses, so there is one XNA→Vulkan mapping and not two; the state is folded into the 2D pipeline key (bit 4 depth test, bit 5 depth write, bits 12–40 `PackDepthStencilBits`, clear of `FoldDepthFormatIntoKey`'s bits 45+); and `RecordCommandBuffer` sets the three stencil dynamic values before every sprite draw, the same always-set convention the 3D route follows. A target with **no** depth attachment keeps the old answer -- there is nothing to test against, and enabling the test there would be a render-pass mismatch rather than a masked sprite. **A second defect the first fix exposed, fixed rather than worked around:** four tests then failed the `VULKAN-408` gate with *“VkPipeline … doesn't set up VK_DYNAMIC_STATE_STENCIL_* , but since the vkCmdBindPipeline the related dynamic state commands have been called”*. A `SpriteBatch` driven by a custom `Effect` swaps in `VulkanEffectRenderer`'s own pipeline, which declared no stencil dynamic states. The narrow fix would have been to skip the `vkCmdSetStencil*` calls for that route -- which would have left a custom-effect batch silently ignoring its `DepthStencilState`, the very defect this row is about. So the effect pipeline got the same treatment: the three dynamic states, the real depth/stencil block, and the state in its `PipelineVariantKey`. `DepthStencilKeyParams` moved up in the header because two declarations now need the complete type. **Mutation-tested, one mutation per layer:** hardcoding the 2D pipeline's depth/stencil off again fails legs B and C; making the snapshot stop carrying `dsParams` fails B and C too, by a different route (the default `depthFunc` is `LessEqual`, so the sprite passes at `0.0 <= 0.0`). The control leg A stays green under both, which is what proves it is a control. **Evidence:** new `Vulkan_SpriteBatch_DepthStencilState` and `EasyGL_SpriteBatch_DepthStencilState` from one source, **3/3 identical on both**. `^Vulkan_` **334/334**, no validation output. Full `ctest` re-run, because this changes the pipeline every sprite in the suite is drawn with. |

---

## 15. Phase 3 — Graphics state (`VULKAN-090`–`VULKAN-129`)

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-090 | Prove per-MRT `ColorWriteChannels1/2/3` and `MultiSampleMask` on Vulkan | ✅ | **Already proved, and the row was written from incomplete knowledge of this plan's own suite. No test was added: a duplicate is worse than nothing.** The row's premise — "`Vulkan_ColorWriteChannels` covers slot 0 only" — is true of *that* file and false of the suite. `Vulkan_MRT_MsaaResolve` (`vulkan_mrt_msaa_test.cpp`) already carries both halves. **Per-slot write masks:** `RunFourTargetColorWriteCase` binds **four** 8×-requested targets, sets `ColorWriteChannels0/1/2/3` to Red/Green/Blue/Alpha, draws once through the four-output SPIR-V effect, and asserts a *different expected colour per target* — `(200,20,30,40)`, `(10,50,30,40)`, `(10,20,100,40)`, `(10,20,30,220)`. It meets the row's own bar: **collapsing the masks onto slot 0's fails it**, because target 1 would then keep its RED (100) instead of its green, and 100 is neither the expected 10 nor within tolerance of it. **Partial sample mask:** `RunTwoTargetCases` sets `MultiSampleMask = 1` — one sample of N, genuinely partial rather than the boolean 0-versus-all — and asserts **both** outputs resolve at exactly `1/appliedSamples` coverage via `ResolveOneSample`, alongside a `MultiSampleMask = 0` case that must preserve the clear. **What this row cost, honestly:** a `Vulkan_MRT_ColorWrite` was written before the existing coverage was found, then deleted. It also recorded one thing worth knowing for whoever writes the next MRT test: binding a **two**-output fragment shader to a **single** render target makes the validation layer complain that location 1 has no attachment — the shader's output count must match the bindings, and the existing test's two-output/four-output effect pair exists for exactly that reason. |
| VULKAN-091 | Root-cause and fix `Vulkan_DepthBias` | ✅ | **Root cause: the test's depth convention was OpenGL's, and the renderer was right.** `vulkan_depth_bias_test.cpp` placed its flat triangles at `z = 0` under an identity projection and called that "depth 0.5" -- the GL mapping, which clips `z` to `[-1,1]`. XNA uses Direct3D 9's convention (`z` in `[0,w]` -> depth `[0,1]`) and so does Vulkan, so `z = 0` is the **near plane**: the viewport depth range clamps at 0, the biased redraw stays at exactly 0, `LESS` fails, and the strip stays red. Nothing can be biased in front of the near plane, and that clamp is not a dropped bias. **Proof it is not the driver, which the row demanded before this could be said:** `spikes/vulkan-depth-bias-spike/` runs the same coplanar experiment **off screen** -- no surface, no swapchain -- on every device the loader offers, and `vkCmdSetDepthBias`'s constant factor behaves exactly as Vulkan specifies on **both** drivers: llvmpipe with the very `D24_UNORM_S8_UINT` format this renderer picks, and AMD RADV, which the renderer itself cannot reach under Xvfb. **That off-screen route is the finding worth reusing**: RADV is unreachable here only because PRESENTATION needs DRI3, so any Vulkan question that does not need a swapchain can be asked on real hardware today, without waiting for `VULKAN-012`. **Proof it is not the renderer:** moving the flat triangles to `z = 0.5` makes CNA pass 4/4 with no production change at all. **Fix:** the test's flat scenarios sit at `z = 0.5`, the tilted ones span 0.2..0.8 instead of straddling (and being clipped by) the near plane, and a **fifth leg** is added that pins the convention -- a flat triangle *at* `z = 0` with the same `-1e6` bias must stay RED. That leg is the one whose absence let the whole file be written against OpenGL's mapping and read a correct renderer as broken. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_DepthBias` **5/5**. **Mutation check** (the row's own requirement): forcing the constant factor to 0 at `vkCmdSetDepthBias` gives 4/5, failing exactly the `-1e6` leg. **No tolerance was widened and no driver was blamed.** **Discovered while closing it:** `EasyGL_DepthBias` passes from the identical premise only because EasyGL never remaps GL's `[-1,1]` clip depth, so the same XNA `z = 0` lands at depth 0.5 there. That is a real divergence from XNA in the *reference* renderer -- recorded as **F-19** and owned by **`VULKAN-098`**. |
| VULKAN-092 | Prove the public `Viewport` contract on Vulkan | ✅ | `easygl_viewport_state_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_ViewportState`. Vulkan had four viewport tests and every one observes the viewport's **effect** on pixels; none asserted the public round-trip and survival contract -- that what you set is what `GetViewport` returns, and that it survives the operations that are entitled to change it. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-093 | Prove sampler component isolation and single-level anisotropy on Vulkan | ✅ | `sampler_component_isolation_contract_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_SamplerComponentIsolation` and `easygl_texture2d_anisotropic_singlelevel_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_Texture2D_AnisotropicSingleLevel`. The first is the contract that setting one sampler field does not disturb the others -- on this renderer a `SamplerState` is a **cache key**, so a field that leaks into a neighbouring key is a wrong cached `VkSampler` rather than a wrong GL call. The second pins anisotropy on a texture with **one** mip level, where there is nothing to filter between. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-094 | Prove `SamplerState.AddressW` on Vulkan | ✅ | **Proved as far as it can be proved, and the reason it stops there is the finding.** The row proposed sampling a `Texture3D` with W wrap versus W clamp; that test **cannot be written on this renderer**, because Vulkan has no `Texture3D` sampling path at all — which the row anticipated as "a finding with its own row", and it now has two (F-31, `VULKAN-163`/`VULKAN-164`). New CTest `Vulkan_SamplerAddressW` (`vulkan_sampler_addressw_test.cpp`), **6/6**, pins what is true instead of leaving "could not be written" as the record. **`AddressW` reaches the sampler intact** (leg A): two states differing *only* in W build two distinct `VkSampler`s and repeating one builds no further. **And it changes no pixel** (leg B), because 2D sampling never consults the W axis. **Two legs exist purely so a negative result could be attributed**, and both earned their place: leg A0 changes `AddressU` — unquestionably in the key — to prove the harness applies sampler states at all, and leg A1 calls the renderer's own `ApplySamplerAddressW` through the interface to locate the layer. **The mistake they caught is worth keeping:** this file first concluded that `AddressW` was *dropped*, because its second state used `W = Clamp` — and `ApplySamplerState` sets `addressW = addressU` and **rebuilds** before `ApplySamplerAddressW` overwrites it, so the `(Clamp,Clamp,Clamp)` sampler is already cached as a side effect of every apply. The leg now uses Mirror and Wrap, neither of which collides with U. That transient sampler is real waste — one extra `VkSampler` per applied slot state — bounded since `VULKAN-160` and not worth its own row, but recorded here so the next reader of that function knows the rebuild is not free. |
| VULKAN-095 | Prove the runtime MSAA sample-count change on Vulkan | ✅ | **Done, and the row's *“register `easygl_msaa_change_test.cpp`”* is the one instruction that had to be refused — its own header explains why.** **Why not that source.** It asserts that toggling `preferMultiSampling` through `ApplyChanges()` on a live device leaves `MultiSampleCount` at **0**, *“since EasyGL cannot actually engage MSAA post-construction and now honestly reports that back”*. That is a true statement about EasyGL and a false one here: registering it verbatim would have asserted **EasyGL's limit as if it were the contract**, which §5 forbids in the other direction too. **Measured on Vulkan instead:** `MultiSampleCount` goes **0 → 4 → 0** on an already-constructed device, so `ApplyMultiSampleCount` really does the teardown-and-rebuild the matrix calls *“the riskier of the two implementations”*. **New CTest `Vulkan_MsaaRuntimeChange`, 12/12** (`vulkan_msaa_runtime_change_test.cpp`). A `Texture2D` and a `RenderTarget2D` are created **before** the first change and re-drawn after every one of four, because the render passes, framebuffers and pipelines they depend on are destroyed and rebuilt underneath them. Both directions are asserted, not assumed symmetric, and a third rebuild is driven deliberately — that is where anything the first two failed to release shows up. **The correction that came out of building it, and it is the useful part of this row.** The first version drew only into a NON-multisampled `RenderTarget2D`, and a mutation that kept the stale MSAA sprite pipelines across the change passed it **8/8** — because that path never touches `renderPassMsaa_` or `pipelines2DMsaaByDepthFmt_` at all. So legs B3, B4, C3 and D2 were added: the **backbuffer** (multisampled while the toggle is on) and a **multisampled `RenderTarget2D`** (a different rebuilt pass). Without them this row would have reported the highest-risk untested path in Phase 3 as covered while testing none of it. **Mutation-checked, and it names where the discriminator lives.** Leaving `renderPassMsaa_` neither destroyed nor forgotten in `ApplyMultiSampleCount` leaks a `VkRenderPass` per rebuild. The test's own twelve legs stay **12/12** — the leak is reported at `vkDestroyDevice`, after the last line a test can execute — and `ctest -R '^Vulkan_MsaaRuntimeChange$'` **fails** on `VULKAN-393`'s output gate with `VkRenderPass … has not been destroyed`, twice. Same shape as `VULKAN-405` and `VULKAN-407`: in-process assertions cannot see teardown, and the gate can. **Also recorded: the mutation that was NOT a defect.** Removing `clearPipelineCache(pipelines2DMsaaByDepthFmt_)` is harmless in the reachable state space — `PickSampleCount` returns either 1 or the device maximum, so a surviving MSAA pipeline is only ever reused at the same sample count it was built for. It would become a defect the day a third distinct count is reachable, and the line stays for that reason rather than because a test caught it. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **266/266**. Full `ctest` not re-run: one new test source and its registration; no production code changed. **Dependencies:** none.
| VULKAN-097 | Root-cause and fix the shared pixel-centre / magnifying-filter divergence (F-18) | ✅ | **Fixed, and F-18's "likely one root cause" hypothesis is confirmed: one change turned all three green.** **Root cause:** XNA 4.0 addresses pixel *centres* with integer coordinates (Direct3D 9); Vulkan addresses pixel corners, like OpenGL. This renderer applied no correction, so the screen-space triangle `(x,y),(x+1,y),(x,y+1)` landed entirely on an excluded fill edge and vanished, and every magnifying filter sampled half a texel off. **Fix:** `XnaPixelCenterCorrectionEXT()` returns the same slightly-under-half-pixel clip-space translation EasyGL and Wine use (63/64 of a clip unit = 63/128 of a viewport pixel), post-multiplied into `world * view * projection` in XNA row-vector order at all four WVP sites, and onto the projection half of the instanced route's view-projection product. **The one thing that looks like a bug and is not:** the Y sign is EasyGL's, unchanged. This renderer's vertex shaders negate `clip.y` themselves for Vulkan NDC, and that negation cancels the NDC-direction difference, so the identical offset produces the identical half-pixel shift down-and-right on screen. Working that out is the whole of the port. **Two inherited rules, both with their reasons:** the correction is suppressed when the destination is multisampled (REMED-GFX-235 — it is a *geometry* translation, equivalent to a pixel-centre rule only at one sample; at four, the outer sample positions sit inside the sub-half-pixel margin and it starts removing coverage), and the scale is clamped by the device's `subPixelPrecisionBits` so it can never round back UP to exactly half a pixel, which is the trap EasyGL hit on WebGL's four subpixel bits. **Authority:** XNA, not EasyGL. `xna_pixel_center_contract_test.cpp` records `covered=1` at pixel (16,16) measured on the real XNA 4.0 runtime (`spikes/xna-pixel-center-spike/`), and that is what the fix targets. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_XnaPixelCenter`, `Vulkan_PointSamplingContract` and `Vulkan_DescriptorCapacityContract` all pass, with **no tolerance widened and no expected value changed** — the acceptance criterion as written. The two strong ones are positional, not count-based: `PointSamplingContract` U2 magnifies 3x3 onto 10x10 at a non-integer scale and required 19 wrong texels to become 0, and `DescriptorCapacityContract` B1/C1 required 15/27 and 142/256 mag-linear texel reproductions to become exact. **Regression evidence:** the correction moves *every* 3D draw by ~0.49 px, and the full suite went from 212/218 to **215/218** — three fixed, nothing else moved. The three remaining failures are `Vulkan_DepthBias` (`VULKAN-091`, confirmed separate) and the two `VULKAN-144`/`VULKAN-346` stride refusals, re-checked to be failing on the same text as the baseline. **Split rule not needed:** the rasterisation rule and the sampler coordinate turned out to be the same offset, so the row was not split. |
| VULKAN-098 | Prove Vulkan's clip-space depth range is XNA's, and record the EasyGL divergence (F-19) | ✅ | **Proved positively, across the range, and cross-checked on the renderer that differs.** New CTest `Vulkan_DepthRangeContract` (`vulkan_depth_range_contract_test.cpp`), **6/6**. The substance is a **5×4 truth table**: five `z` values against four *cleared depth* values under an identity projection and `DepthStencilState::Default`, asserting the quad survives exactly when `z <= clearedDepth`, which is what "depth is `z` over `[0,1]`" means. **20/20 cells.** Plus both planes stated as facts about geometry rather than comparisons: `z = -0.5` is **clipped** at every cleared depth, and `z = 1.5` is clipped. **Why the table and not an ordering test:** both ranges are monotonic, so "does 0.25 occlude 0.75" passes on either. Only an ABSOLUTE comparison — a cleared depth the two ranges fall on opposite sides of — discriminates, and the row is explicit about which cell does it: at cleared depth `0.4`, `z = 0` must be drawn; under OpenGL's range it is depth 0.5 and is rejected. **F-19 is now measured rather than inferred, and on both renderers.** The identical source compiled against `cmake-build-easygl` scores **15/20** and fails exactly where the ranges differ — `z = 0` rejected at cleared depths 0.1 and 0.4, and `z = -0.5` **drawn**, as an ordinary depth of 0.25. The EasyGL build was a temporary registration, measured and reverted; nothing in that renderer's tree changed. **Documented:** `docs/vulkan-renderer.md` now exists and its first section is this divergence, with the two consequences spelled out — an XNA scene loses half its depth precision on EasyGL, and a shared fixture that *encodes* a depth value cannot be run on both. The file opens by saying it is incomplete and that `VULKAN-480` owns the rest, so it cannot be mistaken for the finished capability boundary. **The EasyGL half has an owner, named as this row required:** `plans/plan_graphics.md`, its Phase 71 "EasyGL final gap closure". This row does not open a task on another plan's behalf; it makes sure the divergence is not left implicitly nobody's. **Non-goal honoured:** EasyGL and the shared layer are untouched. |
| VULKAN-096 | Audit the `BlendState`/`DepthStencilState`/`RasterizerState` → pipeline-key mapping for dropped fields | ✅ | **All 34 public fields of the three state objects measured, one at a time, and the result is 30 `IN KEY`, 7 `DYNAMIC`, **one** `DROPPED`.** New `Vulkan_PipelineKeyStateCoverage` (`modules/renderers/vulkan/examples/vulkan_pipeline_key_state_coverage_test.cpp`), **35/35**, built on the row's own method note: cardinality, because a key that omits a field is invisible to a pixel test that only ever sets one value of it. Each field goes through the same three steps -- draw the baseline so its pipeline exists, change **exactly one** field and draw, then draw the changed state again and require the count to hold still. The third step is what makes the second mean anything: a count that keeps climbing on repetition is measuring frame count, not the key. **`IN KEY` (30), each verified to fragment the cache by exactly one entry:** `BlendState`'s `ColorSourceBlend`, `ColorDestinationBlend`, `AlphaSourceBlend`, `AlphaDestinationBlend`, `ColorBlendFunction`, `AlphaBlendFunction`, `ColorWriteChannels`, `ColorWriteChannels1/2/3` and `MultiSampleMask`; `DepthStencilState`'s `DepthBufferEnable`, `DepthBufferWriteEnable`, `DepthBufferFunction`, `StencilEnable`, `StencilFunction`, `StencilPass`, `StencilFail`, `StencilDepthBufferFail`, `TwoSidedStencilMode` and all four `CounterClockwise*`; `RasterizerState`'s `CullMode` and `FillMode`. **`DYNAMIC` (7), each verified NOT to fragment it -- absence from the key is correctness here, not an omission:** `BlendState.BlendFactor` (`vkCmdSetBlendConstants`, and the dynamic state is appended only when the equation consumes it), `DepthStencilState.StencilMask`/`StencilWriteMask`/`ReferenceStencil` (`vkCmdSetStencilCompareMask`/`WriteMask`/`Reference`), `RasterizerState.DepthBias`/`SlopeScaleDepthBias` (`vkCmdSetDepthBias`) and `ScissorTestEnable` (`vkCmdSetScissor`). **`DROPPED` (1): `RasterizerState.MultiSampleAntiAlias`.** It reaches no renderer at all -- `IGraphicsRenderer::ApplyRasterizerState(cullMode, fillMode, scissorTestEnable, depthBias, slopeScaleDepthBias)` has no parameter for it, which the D3D11 state cache already says in its own comment (`D3D11StateObjectCache.cpp:164`). **This is `PARITY` with EasyGL rather than a Vulkan gap** -- the field stops at the shared interface, so no renderer sees it -- and it becomes **`VULKAN-099`** rather than closing silently. **A limit of the instrument, stated because it changes what the numbers prove:** `DYNAMIC` and `DROPPED` produce the *same* cardinality signal, so the run-time measurement can confirm a claim but can never tell those two apart. The separation is made at compile time instead, by a `void_t` detector asserting that `ApplyRasterizerState` takes five arguments and not six — **with a positive control**, because without one a renamed method would make both detectors false and the audit would report a boundary it never looked at. Adding the parameter now breaks this file rather than quietly invalidating the row. **Mutation-tested against the production key, not against the test's own literals:** deleting `bp.alphaFunc` from `PackBlendBits` fails exactly `BlendState.AlphaBlendFunction`; deleting `ds.twoSidedStencilMode` from `PackDepthStencilBits` fails exactly `DepthStencilState.TwoSidedStencilMode`. Both leave the other 34 green, and `VulkanRenderer.cpp` was restored bit-for-bit. **One finding recorded but out of this row's scope, because it is about the 2D key and not a dropped field:** `GetOrCreatePipeline2D` hardcodes `depthTestEnable = VK_FALSE` and no stencil at all, so a `SpriteBatch::Begin(..., DepthStencilState, ...)` cannot depth-test or stencil-mask sprites on this renderer, while EasyGL applies the same state to a global GL device that its sprite draws then share. **Measured rather than left as a suspicion, and it was a defect:** the same source run on both renderers masked the sprite correctly on EasyGL and painted the whole target on Vulkan. Fixed by **`VULKAN-058`**. |
| VULKAN-099 | `RasterizerState.MultiSampleAntiAlias` reaches no renderer | ✅ | **Closed on arm (b) — the refusal is recorded, and the XNA measurement the row demanded before anyone touched the interface was actually taken.** **The measurement.** New spike `spikes/xna-multisample-antialias-spike/`, on the XNA 4.0 runtime in `~/.wine-cna-xna40` (D3D9 through DXVK, AMD Radeon 780M / RADV PHOENIX, `GraphicsProfile.HiDef`). One triangle with a shallow diagonal edge and one line along the same edge, each rendered three ways — no multisampling, 4× with `MultiSampleAntiAlias = true`, 4× with it `false` — scored by counting the pixels that come back as a *blend* of the fill and clear colours. **XNA does not drop the property.** Setting it `false` makes the D3D9 layer write render state **161**, which DXVK logs as *"Unhandled render state 161"* and which `d3d9types.h:1008` names **`D3DRS_MULTISAMPLEANTIALIAS`**. **The attribution is measured, not inferred:** re-running with `PROBE_SKIP_FALSE=1`, so that no leg ever sets the flag to `false`, produces that log line **zero** times, while the leg that sets it produces it exactly once. So the property is live upstream and CNA is where it stops. **What it does to pixels is unmeasured, and the probe reports that rather than guessing.** DXVK calls the state unhandled, so the edge counts are identical either way: triangle **151** blended pixels with the flag true and **151** with it false, against **0** on an unmultisampled target — which is the leg that rules out the "multisampling never happened" reading and without which nothing else could be concluded. The line leg agrees (**96 / 96 / 0**). Answering the behavioural half needs a native D3D9 stack; this machine has none, and the probe prints `INCONCLUSIVE` on its own if the control leg ever stops separating. **Why the refusal, given XNA carries it.** A Vulkan graphics pipeline's `rasterizationSamples` must equal the render pass attachment's sample count, so *"do not multisample this draw"* is not expressible as a per-draw property here: honouring it would mean changing the attachment, and `pSampleMask` restricts which samples are **written** rather than reproducing a D3D9 rasterizer mode. The alternative is a sixth parameter on an interface **twelve** renderer families implement, so that all twelve can refuse it — cost with no behaviour behind it. **And it is still not a parity gap**, which is what keeps it out of this campaign's gate: no renderer receives the field, so no two renderers disagree, and `D3D11StateObjectCache.cpp:164` already documents its own `MultisampleEnable = FALSE` on the same grounds. **The verdict is flipped where the row asked, in meaning rather than in letter.** `Vulkan_PipelineKeyStateCoverage` still measures `DROPPED`, because that is still what happens; what changed is that the file now says it is a **decision** and names the evidence, and its `static_assert` message now says that a sixth argument invalidates `VULKAN-096`'s verdict **and** `VULKAN-099`'s refusal and must move `docs/vulkan-renderer.md` and `docs/rasterizerstate-support.md` with it. Turning the leg into `IN KEY` would have asserted a behaviour that does not exist. **Docs:** a new section in `docs/vulkan-renderer.md` and §8 of `docs/rasterizerstate-support.md`, the shared graphics doc that owns this state class — both carrying the measurement, the DXVK caveat and the Vulkan reason. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_PipelineKeyStateCoverage` **35/35** on llvmpipe and on RADV; the probe's two runs are reproducible from the spike's README. **Depends on:** `VULKAN-096` ✅. |

---

## 16. Phase 4 — Vertex/index buffers and draw semantics (`VULKAN-130`–`VULKAN-169`)

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-130 | Fix the unchecked `VertexBuffer` upload (F-01) | ✅ | **Fixed.** `VulkanVertexBufferRenderer` now records the bytes it has mapped (`allocatedBytes_`) and `SetData` calls `EnsureByteCapacity(max(vertex_count, capacity_) * stride)` before its `memcpy`. The reservation is the whole logical capacity at the new stride, not the bytes this call writes, because the draw routes copy out of the mapping at the caller's own `vertexStart`/`vertexCount`, which the shared layer bounds by the buffer's capacity rather than by the last upload -- a mapping sized to one short upload would still be read past its end by a legal draw. **What the fix rests on, recorded because it is not obvious and constrains any future change:** this `VkBuffer` is never bound to a command buffer. `GetBuffer()` has no caller anywhere in the renderer; every draw route (`DrawColoredPrimitives`, `DrawPrimitivesEx`, `DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx`, `DrawInstancedPrimitivesEx`) `memcpy`s the vertices out of `GetMappedPtr()` into its own deferred record, and `liveVertexBuffers_` is a teardown list only. Reallocating therefore needs no fence, no `vkDeviceWaitIdle` and no retirement-queue entry -- and a comment on `EnsureByteCapacity` says so, because the day something binds the handle that stops being true. The already-uploaded bytes are carried across a grow. **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_VertexBuffer_WideStrideBounds` (`modules/renderers/vulkan/examples/vulkan_vertexbuffer_wide_stride_bounds_test.cpp`), **10/10**. Leg A reads the renderer's own new `GetLiveVertexBufferBytesEXT()` counter as a before/after delta for a 512-vertex buffer at each of the three wide strides the pipeline-key table recognises; leg B round-trips every uploaded byte through `GetDataRawEXT`; leg C draws two triangles from `vertexStart=506` of a 512-vertex stride-68 buffer -- byte 34,408 of a mapping the old code sized at 32,768 -- with a same-frame head-of-buffer control proving the tail is what coloured the pixel; leg D asserts the validation layer stayed silent. **Mutation probe:** with `EnsureByteCapacity` short-circuited the run is 7/10 -- leg A fails at all three strides with the exact shortfall (`mapped=32768, upload needs 34816 / 38912 / 40960`). Legs B and C pass under the mutation, which is recorded rather than tuned away: B measures the shared CPU shadow and C survives because lavapipe's allocation rounding happened to leave the overrun pages present. **That is the finding's true severity** -- the pre-fix code was latent undefined behaviour whose observable damage depends on the allocator, not a reliable crash, which is exactly why leg A asserts the allocation and not the symptom. Leg A is the discriminator; B and C exist to stop a truncating "fix" and to prove the read side. **Suite:** `ctest -R '^Vulkan_'` re-run after the change (see §7.5's updated baseline). |
| VULKAN-131 | Fix the unchecked `IndexBuffer` upload (F-02) | ✅ | **Fixed, and the finding is narrower than F-02 stated.** `SetData16`/`SetData32` now call `RequireByteCapacity` before their `memcpy` and refuse an over-long upload by name (`The Vulkan renderer: a 16-bit index upload would write 6146 bytes into an index buffer mapped at 6144 bytes (3072 indices of 2 bytes)...`). This buffer **grows nothing**, unlike `VULKAN-130`'s twin, and the asymmetry is the point: a vertex buffer's allocation comes from a stride guess and a wider real stride is a legal thing for a caller to have, whereas an index buffer's comes from the element width it was constructed with, so the only routes past its end are more indices than its capacity or a width that is not this buffer's -- both caller errors, and widening for the second would make it draw from misread bytes instead of failing. **Reachability, measured rather than assumed:** every public path is already bounded above the renderer. `IndexBuffer::SetDataInternal` throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` on `elementCount > indexCount_` and `ArgumentException` on a width mismatch; `SetDataAtInternal` bounds the window against the same capacity and always uploads exactly `capacity` indices; and the two internal creators (`GraphicsDevice::DrawUserIndexedPrimitives` and its typed siblings) size the buffer to the index count they then upload. So F-02's overrun is **not reachable through the public API today** and this is defence in depth -- the invariant moved to the object that owns the allocation. That is recorded rather than used to close the row as a non-finding, because the shared guard is not the allocation's owner and nothing binds the two together. **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_IndexBuffer_UploadBounds` (`modules/renderers/vulkan/examples/vulkan_indexbuffer_upload_bounds_test.cpp`), **9/9** -- the allocation is exactly `capacity x 2` and `capacity x 4` (read from the new `GetLiveIndexBufferBytesEXT()` counter as a before/after delta), a full-capacity upload of each width round-trips every index, an over-capacity upload is refused by name *and leaves the existing contents intact*, a 32-bit source into a 16-bit buffer is refused by name, and a full-capacity 32-bit buffer still draws from its very last six indices (`startIndex = 3066`, the boundary case where the draw route's own copy reads the last byte of the mapping). **Mutation probe** -- the one that proves the *renderer's* guard rather than the shared layer's: with `IndexBuffer::SetDataInternal`'s capacity throw short-circuited, leg C fails as `wrong type: The Vulkan renderer: a 16-bit index upload would write 6146 bytes into an index buffer mapped at 6144 bytes` -- the renderer catching what the shared layer no longer does, where before this task the same mutation wrote two bytes past the mapping in silence. **Suite:** 211/217, the same six pre-existing failures. |
| VULKAN-132 | Determine whether the deferred draw model makes dynamic-buffer updates observable (F-12) | ✅ | **Measured: it does not. Vulkan and EasyGL agree, so this closes with no production change — the outcome the row itself names as valid.** **What F-12 predicted:** two draws in one frame from one `DynamicVertexBuffer` with a `SetData` between them would both replay against the **last** upload, because Vulkan overrides none of the `*WithOptions` setters, its buffers are single persistently-mapped allocations, and draws are recorded for replay at `Present()`. Every step of that premise is true. **Why the conclusion does not follow, established by reading and then confirmed by measurement:** a recorded draw does not reference the buffer. `Pending3DDraw` holds **no `VkBuffer` at all**, and every draw entry point `memcpy`s the vertex bytes out of the mapped allocation **at its own call** — `VulkanRenderer.cpp:12233`, `:12276`, `:12393`, `:12740`, `:13105`, with the index twins at `:12280`, `:12744`, `:13111`. `SetData` writes synchronously into `mappedPtr_` (`:1757`), so each draw snapshots the state current at its call, which is exactly EasyGL's immediate semantics. **Measured:** `Vulkan_DynamicBufferUpdate` (`vulkan_dynamic_buffer_update_test.cpp`), **3/3** — draw a red quad on the left, `SetData(..., Discard)` new geometry, draw a blue quad on the right, then read **both** regions in the same frame. Left is `(255,0,0)`, right is `(0,0,255)`. **Two probes, not one:** reading only the right region cannot tell "both draws are correct" from "the first draw was overwritten", since the right one is right either way. Leg C is the control, proving the readback can see a quad in the left region at all, so an empty left region for some unrelated reason is not mistaken for F-12 confirmed. **Leg A's sensitivity is demonstrated rather than asserted:** giving the first draw the second's data makes it fail with `(0,255,0)` — green, the exact reading that would mean the first draw was replayed with the second upload. **What that does NOT demonstrate, stated so the evidence is not overclaimed:** it exercises the probe, not a production mutation. Reproducing the aliasing itself would mean rebuilding the deferred model to reference buffers instead of snapshotting them, which is not a change worth making to test a defect the code does not have. **Consequences recorded rather than left implied:** F-12's row keeps its factual premise and loses its conclusion, and `VULKAN-133` — which was to port the stress proof and "depends on `VULKAN-132`'s verdict for what the expected values are" — now has its verdict: the expected values are EasyGL's. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **251/251**. |
| VULKAN-133 | Port the dynamic-buffer stress proof to Vulkan | ✅ | **Registered, green on the first run — 36/36 across 12 frames — and closing with no production change, which §26 rule 4 names as a real result rather than a shortfall.** `easygl_dynamic_buffer_stress_test.cpp` runs unchanged as `Vulkan_DynamicBufferStress`: 12 frames cycling `None`/`Discard`/`NoOverwrite` on a `DynamicVertexBuffer` and a `DynamicIndexBuffer`, each frame uploading a new solid colour, drawing a full-screen quad and reading the centre pixel back, plus a capacity check on both buffers every frame. **The expected values are EasyGL's, unchanged, and that is `VULKAN-132`'s verdict rather than an assumption:** each draw copies its buffer's bytes at its own call, so the three `SetDataOptions` are indistinguishable here and the source needed no Vulkan-specific expectations. **What this adds beyond `VULKAN-132`:** that row proved the semantics once, with two draws in one frame; this one exercises **36 consecutive uploads without a recreate**, which is where an orphaning or sub-data strategy would drift — the capacity assertions in particular would catch a buffer being silently reallocated per update, which is EasyGL's `Discard` strategy and would be a real difference if Vulkan adopted it. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **252/252**. Full `ctest` not re-run: only this file's registration changed. |
| VULKAN-134 | Prove `DrawUserPrimitives` and `DrawUserIndexedPrimitives` on Vulkan | ✅ | **Four sources registered, ten pixel-verified sub-tests, all green on the first run — which is the result, not a shortfall (§26 rule 4): the evidence exists where it did not, and the routes were never covered here before.** **Registered** (renderer-agnostic, zero renderer-specific includes in any of the four, so they run unchanged — the same reuse the other 58 EasyGL sources in this file use): `easygl_draw_user_primitives_vpc_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_DrawUserPrimitives_VPC`, `easygl_draw_user_primitives_custom_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_DrawUserPrimitives_CustomVertex`, `easygl_draw_user_indexed_primitives_vpc_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_DrawUserIndexedPrimitives_VPC`, `easygl_draw_user_indexed_primitives_32_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_DrawUserIndexedPrimitives_32`. **What the ten sub-tests actually observe**, each by reading back the centre pixel rather than by completing without error: `DrawUserPrimitives<VertexPositionColor>` at vertex offsets 0 and 1; `DrawUserPrimitives` with a caller-supplied `VertexDeclaration` at offsets 0 and 1; `DrawUserIndexedPrimitives` with a caller-supplied declaration for both 16-bit and 32-bit indices; `DrawUserIndexedPrimitives<VertexPositionColor>` at offsets 0 and 1; and 32-bit indexed at offsets 0 and 1. The non-zero offsets are the part that matters — a route that ignores `vertexOffset`/`indexOffset` draws the dummy first vertex and shows it in the pixel. **Why these four rather than three:** the row named three; `easygl_draw_user_primitives_custom_test.cpp` sits beside them, exercises the caller-supplied-declaration form of the same routes, and is the one that covers 32-bit indices *with* a custom declaration. Registering it costs nothing and closes the same seam more completely. **No production change was needed**, which is itself worth recording given `VULKAN-144`'s declaration-driven layout work: these routes hand the renderer a declaration per call, and they already bind what the caller declared. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` grows 241 → **245/245**. Full `ctest` not re-run — nothing but this file's registrations changed, so no test outside this renderer's own set can move. **Unblocks `VULKAN-392`**, which needs exactly this scene: each of these calls allocates a throwaway `VertexBuffer`, so on Vulkan each is a `vkCreateBuffer`/`vkAllocateMemory` and, until that row lands, a `vkDeviceWaitIdle` on destruction. |
| VULKAN-135 | Prove `VertexBuffer::SetData` overloads on Vulkan | ✅ | `easygl_vertexbuffer_setdata_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_VbSetData`. Vulkan proved the `SetData`/`GetData` **round trip**; the overload matrix -- offsets, element counts, strides -- is what `VULKAN-130`'s unchecked `memcpy` lived in, so it is the one that had to be pinned. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-136 | Prove `BufferUsage::WriteOnly` refusal on Vulkan | ✅ | `easygl_buffer_usage_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_BufferUsage`. Shared-layer behaviour whose integration with this renderer's persistently-mapped buffers was unproven: a `WriteOnly` buffer must refuse `GetData` even though the mapping physically permits the read. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-137 | Prove zero-length buffers on Vulkan | ✅ | **Proved, and proved against EasyGL rather than against itself.** New renderer-agnostic `modules/graphics/examples/zero_length_buffer_test.cpp`, registered as **`Vulkan_ZeroLengthBuffers`** and **`EasyGL_ZeroLengthBuffers`**, **4/4 identical on both**. The divergence the row names is real -- `vkCreateBuffer` rejects a size of 0, so `VulkanVertexBufferRenderer` and `VulkanIndexBufferRenderer` allocate `std::max<VkDeviceSize>(1, ...)`, where EasyGL hands the size straight to `glBufferData` -- and a divergence in the implementation is only legitimate if it is invisible from the public API. That is what running the same source on both renderers establishes; a Vulkan-only test could have shown the padding does no harm here without saying anything about whether the two agree. **Four legs:** a zero-element `VertexBuffer` constructs and reports `VertexCount == 0` (the count it was given, not the 1 the allocation pads to); binding and unbinding it leaves the device able to draw an ordinary quad -- the leg that would catch the padded allocation being bound as if it held a vertex; the same for a zero-element `IndexBuffer`, whose bind is a separate command; and both dispose cleanly with the device still alive. **The interesting half is deliberately not asserted in-process.** A validation message about a zero-sized allocation can arrive at buffer destruction, after this program's last statement, so it belongs to the `VULKAN-408` CTest output gate rather than to a `check()`. **Mutation-tested exactly there:** removing the `max(1, ...)` from both buffer kinds produces **six** `[Vulkan Validation]` messages -- `vkCreateBuffer(): pCreateInfo->size is zero`, two `vkAllocateMemory(): allocationSize is 0`, and three from the resulting `vkBindBufferMemory` with a null `VkDeviceMemory` -- and the process never reaches its first assertion, so the gate and the exit code both catch it while an in-process assertion would not have. `VulkanRenderer.cpp` restored bit-for-bit. **Evidence:** 4/4 on Vulkan, 4/4 on EasyGL, both green as CTests, the Vulkan one under the validation gate. No production change. |
| VULKAN-138 | Prove disposed-buffer behaviour on Vulkan | ✅ | `easygl_disposed_buffer_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_DisposedBuffer`. `VULKAN-392` replaced the dispose-time device stall with fence-gated retirement and closed the *stall* half of this matrix row; the **use-after-dispose** half stayed open, and on this renderer a disposed buffer's `VkBuffer` may still be alive in the retirement queue -- so "it did not crash" is exactly the wrong evidence and the public exception is the right one. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-139 | Prove unknown-stride rejection on Vulkan | ✅ | **Audited, and the answer is that there is nothing to prove yet: the rejection does not exist on this renderer. The row closes on a measurement and hands the fix to `VULKAN-165`.** **Measured, three times over, with the test extended to look everywhere the refusal could legitimately arrive.** A `VertexBuffer(device, 3)` — the convenience constructor, **no `VertexDeclaration`** — given `SetDataRaw(bytes, 3, stride = 28)`: (1) the **upload** does not throw; (2) `BasicEffect::Apply()` + `DrawPrimitives(TriangleList, 0, 1)` does not throw; (3) neither does the `Present()` that replays it, which is where a deferred renderer would decide the layout. No validation message either. On EasyGL the same call refuses at upload: *“unsupported vertex stride 28 without a `VertexDeclaration`; the upload is refused rather than bound as position-only.”* **What happens instead**, from `RequireFaithfulDeclarationEXT`'s own comment: *“the ordinary routes fall back to the colored pipeline's `Position@0 + Color@12` layout for a stride the canonical table does not list”*. So a 28-byte record is drawn as a 16-byte one — a **silent mis-bind**, the class `VULKAN-156`, `VULKAN-163` and `VULKAN-265` each closed elsewhere. The declaration-fidelity gate does not catch it because there is no declaration to be unfaithful to. **Why this row does not fix it, and that is deliberate:** the same fallback is what makes a **position-only stride-12** buffer render correctly here today, by that comment's own account. So "refuse an unlisted stride" is not a one-line change but a decision about which strides may legitimately fall back — and making it inside an audit row, against tests that currently depend on the fallback, is how a fix becomes a regression. **`VULKAN-165`** owns it, with the measurement above and the design question stated. **Done here:** the shared source `easygl_unknown_stride_rejection_test.cpp` is **strengthened** so it can serve either renderer — it now accepts the refusal at upload **or** at draw/present, applies a `BasicEffect` first so it cannot pass on the wrong refusal, and still requires a `NotSupportedException` naming the stride. `EasyGL_UnknownStride_Rejected` passes unchanged in meaning. It is **not** registered on Vulkan: registering it green would require asserting that nothing is refused, which is the defect. `VULKAN-165` registers it. **Evidence:** the three-point probe above; `EasyGL_UnknownStride_Rejected` green on the extended source; `^Vulkan_` **310/310** (unchanged — no Vulkan registration added).
| VULKAN-140 | Prove a genuinely custom `VertexDeclaration` on Vulkan | ✅ | **Already satisfied.** Same discovery as `VULKAN-051`: the batch's registration of `easygl_draw_user_primitives_custom_test.cpp` collided with an existing target. `VULKAN-134` registered it as **`Vulkan_DrawUserPrimitives_CustomVertex`**, and §10.4's row already records *“the 32-bit-with-custom-declaration leg of `Vulkan_DrawUserPrimitives_CustomVertex`”*. Closed as covered. **Evidence:** that test is in the suite and passes.
| VULKAN-141 | Audit `baseVertex` / `startIndex` / `minVertexIndex` / `numVertices` semantics on Vulkan | ✅ | **Audited with a scene that can tell the parameters apart, and the mapping is XNA's with no mismatch — so no row falls out of it.** New CTest `Vulkan_IndexedDrawRange` (`vulkan_indexed_draw_range_test.cpp`), **7/7**. **The scene is what makes the audit worth anything:** four quads, one per screen quadrant, four distinct colours, sharing one 16-vertex buffer and one 24-index buffer, with quad *k*'s indices being `4k+{0,1,2,0,2,3}`. That layout makes the two parameters **separable**: `baseVertex=4` alone moves the *same six indices* to the second quad, and `startIndex=6` alone reaches the second quad by a different route. Landing on one quad by two different routes is what proves the two are not being confused for each other — a test that only moved both at once would pass with them swapped. Both together reach the fourth. **Every leg asserts all four quadrants**, not just the one it expects to light: a draw that lit two quads, or the right quad plus a stray triangle, passes a single-probe check. **`minVertexIndex`/`numVertices` really are hints** — Direct3D 9 needed them, Vulkan does not — and leg E proves it by rendering the tightest legal range and the widest and comparing the two pictures. **What the audit learned that the row did not know:** `numVertices` is **counted from `baseVertex`** and CNA validates it, so `baseVertex=4, numVertices=16` names vertex 19 of a 16-vertex buffer and is refused by name. This file's first version passed exactly that and was told so; leg G now asserts the refusal, so the contract is evidence rather than something the test happens to respect. |
| VULKAN-143 | Prove draw-argument validation on Vulkan | ✅ | Four registrations: `Vulkan_DrawRangeValidation`, `Vulkan_PrimitiveTypeValidation`, `Vulkan_DrawNoVertexBuffer`, `Vulkan_DrawNoIndexBuffer`. None of these had **any** equivalent here, and this renderer defers its draws -- an argument the shared layer fails to reject would be replayed at `Present`, far from the call that made it. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-144 | Bind an arbitrary `VertexDeclaration` instead of selecting the layout from the stride (F-15) | ✅ | **Split, before work started, under §26.5 -- it is larger than one commit by a wide margin.** The original text stands as the statement of the gap and is not repeated here; what changed is that the *fourteen* pipeline factories that each bake their own `VkVertexInputAttributeDescription` array cannot be converted in one reviewable change, and converting them piecemeal without a shared builder would leave two layout mechanisms in the file at once. **The design the split assumes**, recorded so the children are not each free to re-decide it: the attribute set is produced by matching the declaration's elements to the *selected stock program's* inputs **by `(usage, usageIndex)`**, taking each matched element's own byte offset and format -- not by declaration order, which is what EasyGL's `ApplyLayout` uses and what `VertexDeclarationFidelity.hpp`'s own contract explicitly permits a renderer to improve on ("A renderer that maps semantics to locations may bind either order faithfully"). The shared `CNA::Internal::Graphics::StockProgramInput` table type already exists for exactly this and is reused rather than reinvented. A buffer with an **empty** declaration keeps the stride-derived layout: that is the `VertexBuffer(device, count)` convenience constructor, which many existing tests use and which has no declaration to bind from. **Children:** `VULKAN-145` (the builder and its pipeline-key hash), `VULKAN-146` (BasicEffect families), `VULKAN-147` (alpha-test, dual texture, environment map, skinned), `VULKAN-148` (PBR and skinned PBR), `VULKAN-149` (instanced). **This row closes** when all five are ✅ and the cardinality check its original acceptance names -- two declarations of equal stride and different element offsets producing two pipeline entries, not one -- passes. **Preserved from the original:** the explicit by-name refusal stays for anything still unsupported afterwards; multi-stream input remains deliberately `false` (`REMED-GFX-201`); `IVertexBufferRenderer`'s signature and EasyGL are untouched. **CLOSED 2026-09-05.** All five children are ✅ and each one's own cardinality check passes -- two declarations of equal stride and different element offsets leave two pipeline entries, asserted in `Vulkan_DeclaredVertexLayout`, `Vulkan_DeclaredEffectLayout` and `Vulkan_DeclaredInstancedLayout`. **What that actually covers, counted rather than claimed:** of the renderer's **fifteen** `GetOrCreatePipeline*` factories, **eleven** now take a `VulkanVertexInputLayoutEXT` and prefer it over their baked array. The four that do not, and why: `GetOrCreatePipeline2D` and `GetOrCreatePipeline2DMsaa` own SpriteBatch's vertex format outright -- there is no caller declaration to bind, and F-15 does not reach them; `GetOrCreatePipelineDualTex3D` is deliberately **not** converted, because converting it would turn an honest refusal into a wrong picture until its shader has a second UV (F-20, `VULKAN-150`) -- that is the one place `VULKAN-147`'s scope was narrowed, and it is narrowed on purpose; and `GetOrCreatePipeline3D` serves only the legacy no-`GpuDrawParams` `DrawColoredPrimitives` pair, whose own defect this closure found and recorded as F-24 / `VULKAN-155`. **Two things this row did NOT solve, both now owned elsewhere:** what an unsupplied shader input binds to -- answered by `VULKAN-152` after this closure, and not the way that row expected -- and a `Vector4`-spelled `BlendIndices`, still refused where EasyGL binds it (`VULKAN-151`). **Preserved as promised:** the by-name refusal for anything still unsupported, `MultiStreamVertexInput == false`, and `IVertexBufferRenderer`/EasyGL untouched. |
| VULKAN-145 | Build a vertex input layout from the declaration, and key a pipeline on it | ✅ | **Done, and deliberately inert:** no pipeline factory uses it yet, so this row changes no rendered pixel. `modules/renderers/vulkan/include/CNA/Internal/Renderers/Vulkan/VulkanVertexInputLayout.hpp` adds `BuildVulkanVertexInputLayoutEXT(declaration, StockProgramInput*, count)`, which matches each of the selected program's inputs to a declaration element **by `(usage, usageIndex)`** and emits a `VkVertexInputAttributeDescription` at that input's location with the matched element's own byte offset and `VulkanVertexFormatHelper` format. Location `i` is `inputs[i]`, the convention every shader under `src/shaders/` already follows. **Two decisions worth having in writing:** an input the declaration does not supply gets **no** description and a bit in `missingInputMask` -- never a guessed offset, because a wrong offset renders wrong pixels while a missing one is a question the caller can answer; and an element format with no `VkFormat` is reported in a *separate* mask, because "the declaration said nothing" and "the declaration said something unrepresentable" are different problems. `Hash()` folds only what the pipeline bakes -- location, format, offset -- so it is the pipeline-key term `VULKAN-146`+ need. **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_VertexInputLayout` (`vulkan_vertex_input_layout_test.cpp`), **11/11**, pure logic with no device. It proves the two properties the conversion rests on: two **32-byte** declarations whose normal sits at offset 12 vs 20 produce different attribute arrays and different hashes (the stride table gives both the same pipeline -- that is the defect), and listing the same elements in a different order produces the **same** layout, which is what makes semantic matching better than EasyGL's index-based binding and is why compiled XNB data ordering `TextureCoordinate` before `Normal` is not a problem here. It also shows the 28-byte dual-texture record F-15 names by name -- `Position@0, TextureCoordinate0@12, TextureCoordinate1@20` -- is **fully expressible** from its declaration, which is the thing the stride table cannot say. **Suite:** 223/224. |
| VULKAN-146 | Drive the BasicEffect pipelines from the declaration | ✅ | **Landed on the second attempt, after the first was reverted (F-21). The difference is the part the original scope was missing: which stock PROGRAM runs also has to ask the declaration.** `GetOrCreatePipelineFogColored3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineFogTex3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineLitTextured3D` and its `VertexLit` sibling take a `VulkanVertexInputLayoutEXT`, overwrite their baked attribute array with the declaration-derived one when it is complete, and fold `Hash()` into the pipeline key (a new `PipelineKey::vl` field, because `a` is full). The layout is built at **draw** time and snapshotted into `Pending3DDraw`, since the record is replayed at `Present()` by which point the buffer may carry a different declaration. **What the first attempt got wrong:** it made the offsets declaration-driven and left program selection on the stride. Stride 32 is `VertexPositionNormalTexture`'s, and the lit programs take `{aPos, aNormal, aUV}` with **no colour input at all** -- so a Position+Colour vertex padded to 32 reached them and had nothing to bind its colour to. No offset can fix that. **The rule, taken from EasyGL rather than invented:** REMED-GFX-234's -- *a declaration that names no normal cannot be a lit vertex whatever its stride, so ask it; an absent declaration keeps the stride's answer*, which is what every `VertexBuffer(device, count)` relies on. The chosen shape is recorded in the draw (`BasicProgramShapeEXT`) rather than re-derived at replay, and `GetOrCreatePipelineFogColored3D` now takes the real stride for its binding (it hard-coded 16) with that stride folded into its key. **Vulkan therefore joins `TranslatesDeclarations()`** in the shared REMED-GFX-216/234 suite, with a comment saying exactly which families are converted and which still refuse by name. **Evidence, both halves:** the shared suite -- `VertexDeclarationLayoutTest`, `DeclarationGuardTest`, `IndexedDrawDeferredTest` -- is **26 passed, 0 failed**, and the translating control renders all three colliding declarations (`colorPosition16`, `positionTextureColor24`, `positionColorPadded32`), which is the assertion the first attempt could not satisfy. New CTest `Vulkan_DeclaredVertexLayout`, **6/6**: two 24-byte declarations with the colour at 12 vs 20 draw their own colours and get their own pipelines (`0 -> 1 -> 2` cache entries), a buffer with no declaration keeps the stride path, and Position+Colour padded to 32 renders its colour instead of being read as a lit vertex. **Mutation probes from the first attempt still apply and were re-run: layout out of the key -> legs B and C fail with `(0,0,64)`, the UV floats read as a colour; layout in the key but not in the attributes -> only B fails. **Suite:** `-R '^Vulkan_'` 224/225, full `ctest` **9059/9081** -- both numbers, per F-21. |
| VULKAN-147 | Drive the alpha-test, environment-map and skinned pipelines from the declaration | ✅ | **Done, the same way `VULKAN-146` ended up doing it, and with one thing that row did not need.** `GetOrCreatePipelineAlphaTest3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineEnvMap3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineSkinned3D` and its `VertexLit` sibling take the layout, overwrite their baked attributes when it is complete, and carry its hash in the key. Their input tables were read from the shaders themselves. **No shape record was needed here**, unlike the BasicEffect bundle: the family is already decided by the caller's own `needsAlphaTest`/`needsEnvMap`/`needsSkinned` flags and none of these strides is ambiguous, so only the table is selected. **What alpha test makes concrete:** its one vertex shader serves strides 20 and 32 with the UV remapped to location 1, and the factory guessed the offset as `uvOffset = (stride == 32) ? 24 : 12` -- a guess about what a 32-byte record contains, written in the source. The comment stays, next to the call that now replaces it. **The new piece: an integer input cannot take a float attribute.** `BlendIndices` may legally be spelled `Byte4` or `Vector4` (`plans/plan_fx.md` FX-127) and the skinned shader declares `uvec4`; handing it a float attribute is a Vulkan usage error, not a conversion. `BuildVulkanVertexInputLayoutEXT` now compares the element's numeric type against the input's **primary** format -- deliberately not against `alternateFormat`, which records a legal *spelling* and not a bindable one -- and reports a mismatch as unrepresentable, so the caller refuses by name. **That makes `Vector4`-spelled `BlendIndices` a Vulkan refusal where EasyGL accepts it: `VULKAN-151`.** **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_DeclaredEffectLayout` (`vulkan_declared_effect_layout_test.cpp`), **7/7**. Each family draws the same geometry twice at the same stride with the UV element moved from 24 to 12, over a two-texel texture with UVs picked so the two offsets address opposite halves: canonical reads `(230,30,30)`, moved reads `(30,60,230)`, and each pair leaves **two** pipeline entries. `Vulkan_VertexInputLayout` gains leg E2 for the integer-format rule and is **13/13**. **Mutation:** with the attribute override disabled, all three families read `(230,30,30)` for *both* draws -- the stride-derived offset, and the defect itself. **Regression:** all 68 `Vulkan_(AlphaTest|EnvironmentMapEffect|SkinnedEffect|Pbr|BasicEffect|DualTexture|Model)*` tests green, and the full `ctest` measured below. |
| VULKAN-150 | Give the Vulkan `DualTextureEffect` a second UV channel (F-20) | ✅ | **Done, and it is the first row in this plan that changed a SHADER rather than a binding.** `dual_texture3d.vert.glsl` and `dual_texture_colored3d.vert.glsl` gain `in vec2 inUV1` and a fourth varying; `dual_texture3d.frag.glsl` samples `uTexture2` with it instead of with `fragUV`. SPIR-V regenerated with the repo's own `compile_shaders.py` (libshaderc), and the regeneration is **reproducible**: only the three `kDualTexture*Spv` arrays changed in `spirv_shaders.hpp`, nothing else moved. `GetOrCreatePipelineDualTex3D` takes the declaration-derived layout, and its baked 20/24 binding stride became the record's own (`VULKAN-158`'s rule -- see below, it is why this row took two attempts). **The aliasing rule, which is the design decision this row owns:** a record that declares only `TextureCoordinate0` -- every stride-20 and stride-24 record this family has ever been handed -- gets `inUV1` pointed at `TextureCoordinate0`'s **own element**, not at a made-up offset. That reproduces the previous behaviour bit for bit instead of leaving a Vulkan shader input unbound, and it is applied only to `TextureCoordinate1`, only when `TextureCoordinate0` is present. Where neither can be found the layout stays incomplete and the draw goes back through the stride path and the fidelity guard, unchanged. **What this fixes for a game:** `DualTextureEffect` on Vulkan sampled `Texture2` with `TEXCOORD0`. XNA samples it with `TEXCOORD1`. Every lightmapped model with an independent second UV set rendered the wrong picture -- or rather, would have, because the declaration guard refused the record first, which is what F-20 recorded and why this was a skip and not a red test. **Evidence, both halves of the row's stated acceptance:** `Vulkan_DualTextureSlotSamplerContract` now runs its whole `A..M` body -- **106/106** -- where it previously ran 5 palette checks and skipped the rest; and EasyGL's own SAMPLE-073 fixture (`easygl_dualtextureeffect_independent_uv_test.cpp`) is registered for Vulkan as `Vulkan_DualTextureEffect_IndependentUV` and **passes**, reading `(255,0,0)` -- the red that only appears when `Texture` uses its left texel and `Texture2` uses its right one. That also closes the substance of `VULKAN-259`. **Mutation:** with the dual-texture layout builder stubbed to return an empty layout, the slot-sampler contract falls straight back to `[SKIP] A..M: this renderer does not implement DualTextureEffect (... the declaration carries TextureCoordinate0@12 Vector2, which this renderer's native layout for a 28-byte record does not bind at all ...)` and 5/5. **A false start worth recording:** the first attempt looked like a shader bug -- the stride-40 fixture rendered black with the new shader in place. It was not: `GetOrCreatePipelineDualTex3D` baked its binding stride, so the declaration's offsets were read out of records the fetch never reached. That is F-26, it affected three other families too, and it was fixed first as `VULKAN-158`. **Suite:** measured below. |
| VULKAN-151 | Accept a `Vector4`-spelled `BlendIndices` on the skinned route | ✅ | **Done with ONE shader, not the second variant the row proposed, because Vulkan has a format for exactly this.** The five skinned vertex shaders (`skinned3d`, `_color`, `_vertexlit`, `_vertexlit_color`, `pbr3d_skinned`) now declare `in vec4 aBoneIndices` and subscript the palette with `int(...)`. `Vector4` binds natively; **`Byte4` binds through `VK_FORMAT_R8G8B8A8_USCALED`**, which converts integer components to float **without** normalising -- precisely what `glVertexAttribPointer(..., GL_UNSIGNED_BYTE, GL_FALSE, ...)` does, so this is not a Vulkan trick but the conversion EasyGL has always relied on. A bone palette is a handful of entries, far inside float's exact-integer range. **The one risk, measured rather than assumed:** `_USCALED` is not in Vulkan's mandatory vertex-buffer format list. `spikes/vulkan-vertex-format-spike/` asks each physical device directly -- **RADV (AMD 780M) and llvmpipe both answer YES**, for `R8G8B8A8`, `R16G16` and `R16G16B16A16`. The renderer queries it on the device it actually selected and, where it is absent, refuses **by name** and names the way round (`Supply a VertexDeclaration spelling BlendIndices as Vector4, which binds natively.`) rather than letting `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines` fail at `Present`. **The conversion is deliberately narrow:** it is offered only where the declaration named the input's own `alternateFormat` -- a spelling the program's table already sanctions -- so an arbitrary integer element that happens to line up with a float input is still reported unrepresentable, and a float element at an *integer* input still always is, because no format converts that direction. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_VertexInputLayout` is **16/16** -- leg E2 rewritten to the resolved contract (both spellings bind, both keep the declaration's own offset, and a device without `_USCALED` still *reports* rather than binds) and a new leg E3 for the narrowness rule. `Vulkan_DeclaredEffectLayout` gains leg **C2** and is **11/11**: the same skinned quad drawn twice, once with `Byte4` indices and once with `Vector4`, asserted as **equality of the two readbacks** exactly as this row asked -- plus a second assertion that the picture is the sampled texel, because two blank frames are equal too. The bone index used is **1**, and bone 0 is a transform that puts the quad off screen, so a draw that ignored the attribute would render nothing. **A shared test needed updating, and it is worth saying why that is not a weakened assertion:** `GltfRendererPbrFallbackPolicy.EverySkinnedPbrShaderConsumesThePaletteAndInfluenceCount` greps the shader source for `bb.bones[aBoneIndices.y]`, which is now `bb.bones[int(aBoneIndices.y)]` -- the same shape EasyGL's own row in that table has always had. What it asserts is unchanged. It is grepped from disk, so this failure was live in **every** renderer configuration until the row was corrected. **Suite:** measured below. |
| VULKAN-148 | Drive the PBR and skinned-PBR pipelines from the declaration | ✅ | **Converted, and the row's acceptance is corrected: `Vulkan_RenderTarget_EffectSource` cannot go green here, and the reason is `VULKAN-152`.** `GetOrCreatePipelinePbr3D` and `GetOrCreatePipelinePbrSkinned3D` take the layout, override their attributes when it is complete, and carry its hash in the key, with input tables for all five variants (48, 60, 68, 76, 80) read from `pbr3d.vert.glsl` and `pbr3d_skinned.vert.glsl`. **`RequirePbrStrideEXT`'s list is now what a buffer with NO declaration is judged by** -- both the draw-time check (`VULKAN-346`'s) and the two factories' own throws are gated on `!IsComplete()`, so a declaration that supplies every input of the shader its stride selects is not held to 48/60 or 68/76/80. That is a real capability gain: glTF content whose PBR record is padded or reordered now renders instead of being refused. **Why the row's own acceptance was wrong:** `rendertarget_effect_source_test.cpp` draws its PBR legs from a **20-byte position+texture** buffer created as `VertexBuffer(dev, 6)` -- **no declaration at all**. There is nothing to take offsets from, and `pbr3d.vert.glsl` needs a normal and a tangent the record does not contain. EasyGL renders it because an unbound GL attribute takes a constant `(0,0,0,1)`; Vulkan cannot leave an attribute unbound. So this test needs the **default-attribute** mechanism, not the declaration path, and that is `VULKAN-152` -- the same unsupplied-input question `VULKAN-146`'s re-scope raised, now with a concrete second caller. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_DeclaredEffectLayout` gains leg D2 and is **9/9**: a `PbrEffect` draw at stride **64** -- on no list, with four bytes of padding after each of the last three elements, the shape an aligning content pipeline produces -- renders from its declaration, and the *same bytes* with no declaration are still refused by name. Both halves matter; losing the second would turn an honest refusal into a draw from undefined bytes. **A mistake worth recording:** the first attempt gated only the draw-time helper and left both factories' own stride throws in place, so the refusal simply moved to `Present()`. The test caught it, which is what leg D2's *pair* of assertions is for. **Suite:** measured below. |
| VULKAN-152 | Decide what an unsupplied shader input binds to (the question `VULKAN-146` raised and `VULKAN-148` hit) | ✅ | **Decided, and the answer is a refusal rather than the defaults buffer this row originally proposed. The premise that design rested on was wrong.** The row said EasyGL renders `rendertarget_effect_source_test.cpp`'s skinned/PBR legs because an unbound GL attribute takes a constant `(0,0,0,1)`, and that Vulkan therefore had to reproduce that constant to reach parity. It does not: that test carries a compile-time boundary constant, `kSkinnedFamiliesAcceptPositionTexture`, and **EasyGL is already on its declining side** -- with WEBGPU, DIRECTX9, DIRECTX11, DIRECTX12 and LLGL. Six renderers skip those legs by declaring that their skinned and PBR families do not accept a stride-20 `VertexPositionTexture` stream. Vulkan was on the accepting side, which is what made the legs run and fail. **What was actually wrong was not the missing default; it was that Vulkan's answer was inconsistent.** Its PBR pair refused by name; its skinned family reduced the stride to 52, read past the record and drew **nothing** while reporting success -- the defect this row's investigation found, F-25, fixed as `VULKAN-156`. With all three families refusing, this renderer belongs in the list, and the boundary comment there now says so and why. **Why not the defaults buffer, stated as a decision:** it would make Vulkan the *only* renderer that renders this fixture, i.e. reproduce a behaviour EasyGL itself declines, invent a normal and a bone palette the caller never supplied, and add a permanent second vertex binding to every stock pipeline to do it. The row's own question (a) -- "is this XNA's behaviour or GL's?" -- answers itself once the constant is read: it is neither; it is a fixture convenience that most renderers decline. **The guard is not weakened** (question (b)): a *declared* element the program cannot bind is still refused, and a declaration that supplies every input is still honoured whatever the stride -- `Vulkan_DeclaredEffectLayout`'s leg D2 draws PbrEffect at stride 64 for exactly that reason. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_RenderTarget_EffectSource` is **20/20 legs, 0 crashed**, and `ctest -R '^Vulkan_'` is **228/228** -- the dedicated suite is green for the first time since this plan opened. **A skip is not a pass, and this row does not pretend otherwise:** the six skipped family legs are a declared boundary, and the behaviour behind them is asserted positively elsewhere, by `Vulkan_UnsuppliedInputContract` (`VULKAN-156`), which requires each family to *either* render the sampled texel *or* refuse by name and fails if one silently draws nothing. That pairing is the reason the boundary declaration is honest rather than convenient. **Note for `VULKAN-476`:** a green `^Vulkan_` run is one row of the Phase 13 gate, not the gate. |
| VULKAN-149 | Drive the instanced pipeline from the declaration | ✅ | **Converted, and it moved the *program* choice as well as the offsets -- which is the half the row's own text did not anticipate.** `GetOrCreatePipelineInstanced3D` takes the layout, overwrites its per-vertex attributes when it is complete and carries its hash in the key. The per-INSTANCE binding is untouched: its four matrix columns are a separate stream at locations 4..7 on binding 1, appended after the applicator runs (which overwrites from index 0 and resets the count, so ordering there is load-bearing), and `MultiStreamVertexInput` stays `false`. **`PackedColorOffsetForStride` was not only an offset table.** It decided *which of the two vertex shaders ran* -- `instanced_colored3d` at strides 16 and 24, `instanced3d` everywhere else -- so converting the offsets alone would have left REMED-GFX-234's defect in place in both directions: a Position+Colour record at any other stride loses its colour, and a padded position-only record at 16 gets a colour attribute aimed at its own padding. The declaration now answers both, and the shape is read back off the layout it produced (one attribute is position-only, two is coloured -- there is no third shape). An **absent** declaration keeps the stride table, and a declaration that leaves one of the two inputs unsupplied falls back to the stride for the shape *and* the attributes together, because a shader picked from the declaration and fed from the table is the worst of both. **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_DeclaredInstancedLayout` (`vulkan_declared_instanced_layout_test.cpp`), **7/7**. Every record carries a **decoy** colour at offset 12 -- the offset the stride table would have used -- in a different colour from the real one, so a stride-driven implementation cannot agree with a declaration-driven one by accident. Leg A is a stride-20 record with `Color0` declared at 16 (the stride table lists 20 as position+texture, with no colour in it at all); leg B is *the same bytes* with no declaration, which must stay position-only; leg D is a stride-16 declaration naming Position only, which must render white rather than its own padding. **Mutation:** with the builder stubbed to return an empty layout, A is **refused** by the fidelity guard verbatim (`the declaration carries Color0@16 Color, which this renderer's native layout for a 20-byte record does not bind at all`), D renders the decoy `(30,230,30)`, and the cache separates two stride-16 declarations into 1 pipeline instead of 2 -- 3/7. **Regression:** `Vulkan_DrawInstanced_3Instances` and `Vulkan_InstancedVertexColor_Cardinality` green; the shared `VertexDeclarationLayoutTest`/`DeclarationGuardTest`/`IndexedDrawDeferredTest`/`*Instanced*` set is 86 passed / 16 skipped / 0 failed. **Suite:** measured below. **Superseded 2026-09-08:** the `false` report records this row's closure state; `REMED-GFX-203` now packs several public streams of each rate before this declaration-driven path and reports `true` (F-45/§28.4). |
| VULKAN-153 | Stop `CnaInputTests` from being able to hang a whole `ctest` run (F-22) | ✅ | **Split on contact: this row is now the containment half only, and the deadlock itself is `VULKAN-157`.** The two are separable, the containment is worth having whether or not the deadlock is ever diagnosed, and the deadlock needs a stack this machine cannot currently take (**no `gdb` installed**), so bundling them would have parked a one-line fix behind a tooling problem. **Done:** `cmake/UnitTests.cmake`'s registration gains `TIMEOUT 1200`. It had **none**, which is why `ctest` let it run 45 minutes and the `VULKAN-148` baseline could only be finished by killing the process by hand; every other long suite here is bounded. 1200s is generous against the measured range -- **221 s** run alone, **628 s** as the slowest healthy run seen under `-j6`. **Evidence:** the generated `cmake-build-vulkan/CTestTestfile.cmake` line for the test now reads `... LABELS "input" SKIP_RETURN_CODE "77" TIMEOUT "1200" ...`, where before it had no `TIMEOUT` property at all -- the `CTestTestfile.cmake` diff this row's acceptance asked for. **Already exercised in anger:** the `VULKAN-152` full run reaped it at the timeout (`8773 - CnaInputTests (Timeout)`) and finished, instead of stalling. **What this does not do, stated plainly:** it does not fix the deadlock, and a run that hits it still loses that suite's 578 tests. It converts an unbounded stall into a named failure, which is the difference between a baseline that can be taken and one that cannot. |
| VULKAN-163 | Refuse a `Texture3D` binding instead of ignoring it (F-31) | ✅ | **The hole was three times wider than the finding said, and the row's own suggestion about that turned out to be the wrong shape.** It asked me to check whether `BindTextureCube` had the same `{}` default before assuming F-31 was the only hole, and to give a sibling its own row if so. Both siblings do — `VulkanEffectRenderer` overrode **none** of `IEffectRenderer`'s three `Bind*` methods, so `ShaderEffect::SetTexture` was accepted and discarded for **Texture2D, TextureCube and Texture3D alike** — but they are not separate defects. They are one missing override, closed by one fix; three rows would have described one line of code. Recorded here rather than followed blindly. **What changed:** all three are overridden to throw a named `NotSupportedException` that says what this renderer does instead — it supplies a custom effect's texture from the `SpriteBatch` draw that uses it, and has no per-unit binding path. **Nothing regressed because nothing worked:** no test in the suite called `SetTexture` before this row's own, and the call did nothing when it was called, so refusing can only turn a silent nothing into a loud one. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_SamplerAddressW` grows 6/6 → **7/7**; its leg C stops characterising today's behaviour and asserts the refusal, exactly as that leg's comment predicted it would, and a second assertion covers the `Texture2D` overload — because a test that only covered the one the finding named would leave the other two to be rediscovered. **`VULKAN-164` is unaffected**: refusing loudly is not sampling, and a renderer that wants `sampler3D` still has to grow one. |
| VULKAN-164 | Give the Vulkan renderer a `Texture3D` sampling path (F-31) | ✅ | **The sampling path landed in `VULKAN-254`; what this row found is that a sampling path and a *sampled* volume are not the same thing, because two layers above it were still throwing the sampler state away.** `VULKAN-254` put a bound `Texture3D` in descriptor set 1 at `kEffectVolumeBindingBase + unit` and a shader could read it — at a W coordinate inside `[0,1]`, where no address mode is observable. The row's acceptance sentence needs one outside it, and that is what exposed the rest. **Three defects, each fixed where it lives.** (1) **`SpriteBatch::Begin` forwarded the batch `SamplerState`'s filter and its U and V axes and dropped W.** XNA assigns the whole state to `GraphicsDevice.SamplerStates[0]`. The drop is invisible to 2D sampling, which never consults W — which is exactly why it survived — and decides what a `sampler3D` reads. `ISpriteBatchRenderer` gains `SetSamplerAddressModeWEXT`, **additive with a no-op default**, so the eleven other renderers that implement this interface behave exactly as before; widening `SetSamplerAddressMode`'s two-argument signature would have edited every one of them. (2) **This renderer's sprite flush then re-derived W from U anyway.** `ApplySamplerState` sets `addressW = addressU`, which is right for every XNA preset and wrong for a state that set W on its own; `FlushTexture` now applies the batch's W after it, and only when the batch supplied one (`-1` keeps the old derivation for any caller that flushes sprites without going through `Begin`). (3) **The set-1 descriptors were written with the fixed `defaultSampler_`**, so a W that survived (1) and (2) still could not reach a bound volume. They take the batch's slot-0 sampler — and with that, a **fourth** problem became reachable and is fixed here too: the bound set was cached per effect and invalidated only when a *binding* changed, so a second batch with the same textures and a different `SamplerState` reused the first batch's sampler.
