The set now also rebuilds when the sampler changes. **Test:** new `Vulkan_Texture3DAddressW`, **6/6 on llvmpipe and on RADV** — a 1×1×2 volume (slice 0 red, slice 1 blue) sampled at `vec3(0.5, 0.5, 1.25)`, where Wrap gives 0.25 → slice 0 and Clamp gives 1.0 → slice 1. The X and Y axes cannot contribute because there is one voxel on each, and a leg asserts that the two `SamplerState`s differ in W and in nothing else, so the difference is attributable rather than assumed. A third draw repeats the Wrap leg through the **same** effect, which is what makes defect (4) visible. **Mutation, one per fix, each predicted before it was run:** removing (1) or (3) collapses both legs to blue (3/6) — W follows U, so both read the clamp answer; removing (4) collapses them to red (4/6) — the clamp batch samples through the wrap batch's cached set. **The capability profile half of the acceptance, which the row asked for in exactly those words.** New `RendererFeature::Texture3DSampling` (`30`), described as distinct from `Texture3DStorage` because this renderer spent a long time delivering the second without the first — the storage question answered `supported` while a shader reading the volume got nothing, which is F-31 stated as a capability. It is answered by a new `IGraphicsRenderer::SupportsTexture3DSamplingEXT()` whose **default is `false`**: a renderer that has not been measured under-claims rather than over-claims. Vulkan and EasyGL override it to `true`, each with a passing pixel test behind the claim; the five other renderers that implement `BindTexture3D` are left under-claiming and handed to `VULKAN-167` rather than claimed for on a reading of their source. **C ABI:** mirrored as `CNA_RENDERER_FEATURE_TEXTURE_3D_SAMPLING`, which moves `CNA_RENDERER_FEATURE_MAXIMUM` `29 → 30` — an incompatible change by `docs/c-api/ABI_VERSIONING.md`'s own rule, so the ABI goes **`0.23.0 → 0.24.0`** with the release note that document requires, on the precedent it records for `0.8.0`'s five appended capability identities. `tools/c-api/abi_baseline.json` was re-recorded **by hand for the three header constants that changed**, because `--write` refuses to run without the shared library and `CNA_BUILD_C_API` is `OFF` in this build directory; the export list, which is the half that needs the library, is untouched, and `CApiAbiHeaderBaseline` passes. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **340/340** on llvmpipe and on RADV; full `ctest` **9184/9201**, with the 17 failures discriminated rather than waved at: the **12** standing content/lifecycle/net/window/C-API ones, **four** audio-timing cases that were already failing on this machine before this row and still fail run alone (`-j1`), and **one** that passes alone and is therefore a contention flake (`DynamicSoundEffectInstanceTest`). None is new, none is a `Vulkan_*` or `EasyGL_*` test. The full suite was mandatory: `SpriteBatch::Begin` is on the path of every sprite in the suite. **Opened by this row:** `VULKAN-166` (a bound unit takes the batch's sampler, where XNA gives unit *u* `SamplerStates[u]`) and `VULKAN-167` (the five renderers that sample volumes and do not say so). |
| VULKAN-165 | Refuse a declaration-less buffer whose stride the ordinary routes cannot represent | ✅ | **Fixed with rule (c), narrowed twice — and the narrowing is the whole content of this row.** **The hole:** `RequireFaithfulVertexDeclaration` returns immediately when the declaration is empty, correctly, since there is nothing to be unfaithful **to**. That is exactly the case `VertexBuffer(device, count)` + `SetDataRaw(data, count, stride)` produces, so an unlisted stride reached the `PositionColor` fallback and was drawn as a 16-byte record. **The rule, and why each narrowing is there rather than for tidiness:** (1) **only where the fallback invents an attribute.** The `PositionColor` fallback reads a packed colour at offset 12 out of bytes that mean something else — and out of bounds entirely for a record shorter than 16. The `PositionOnly` fallback reads Position at offset 0, which every entry in the canonical table also puts there, so it cannot read the wrong bytes; the instanced routes are therefore left alone. (2) **only where the stride is genuinely unlisted**, asked as `InferredLayoutForStride(stride, RendererRefusesIt).known` — the shared table's own question, not a list repeated in this renderer. (3) **only where there is no declaration.** A buffer that declares its layout is judged by the fidelity guard exactly as before, **including the position-only stride-12 case that guard deliberately admits** — which is the regression `VULKAN-139` refused to risk by fixing this in passing. **Test:** `easygl_unknown_stride_rejection_test.cpp` (strengthened by `VULKAN-139` to accept the refusal at upload **or** at draw/present) is now registered as `Vulkan_UnknownStride_Rejected`. **It failed before this fix and passes after** — `VULKAN-139` recorded the failing measurement, so the before/after is on the record rather than asserted. The refusal names the stride, the route and what to do about it. **One existing test had to be corrected, and the correction is a real improvement.** `Vulkan_DeclaredEffectLayout`'s leg D2 asserted that a declaration-less 64-byte record is refused **by the PBR route's own wording** (`requires vertex stride 48 or 60`). This gate now refuses it earlier and more generally, so the substring no longer matched. D2 now asserts the **contract** — refused, and the message names the stride — which is the general property it was always trying to pin through one route's sentence. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **311/311** with the new registration; full `ctest` in §7.5, mandatory because the gate is on a path every stride-inferring 3D route shares. **Dependencies:** `VULKAN-139` ✅, `VULKAN-144` ✅, `VULKAN-152` ✅.
| VULKAN-166 | A `ShaderEffect`'s bound texture unit takes the batch's sampler, not `SamplerStates[unit]` | ✅ | **The renderer half was two lines; the row is closed on the shared-layer half, which is where the defect actually lived.** Indexing `slotSamplers_[unit]` instead of `slotSamplers_[0]` when writing set 1 is the obvious fix and is not sufficient by itself: `GraphicsDevice::applySamplerStatesToRenderer()` runs from the **draw entry points only**, so in a frame whose only drawing is a `SpriteBatch`, slots 1–15 were never pushed to any renderer at all and `slotSamplers_[1]` held whatever the device was constructed with. That is the unfinished half of Phase 35's Task 293, which added the missing calls to the eighteen `DrawUserPrimitives` overloads and stopped there. **`SpriteBatch` now publishes them, from the two places FNA's `PrepRenderState` runs** — `Begin()` for `SpriteSortMode::Immediate`, which never reaches a flush, and the top of `flushBatch()` otherwise, before its own empty-queue early-out, exactly as `FlushBatch` calls `PrepRenderState` before its `numSprites == 0` return — which is not an accident of FNA's control flow: a `Begin()`/`End()` pair that draws nothing still leaves the device in the batch's render state in XNA, so the publication has to happen even when the queue is empty. **What it costs:** fifteen slots × three calls per batch flush, on the same three entry points every 3D draw already calls sixteen times, and per *batch* rather than per sprite. **Measured on EasyGL later the same day, which is what that argument was standing in for.** The row rests on `SetSamplerAddressModeWEXT` being additive with a no-op default and on slots 1–15 having had no other writer — an argument about eleven renderers this campaign cannot build. `cmake-build-easygl/` turned out to be **idle** (no process holding it, nothing written in two hours), so the caution about that directory was re-checked rather than inherited again: it builds, and `^EasyGL_` runs **319/323**. The four failures are **exactly** §7.5b's recorded pre-existing set — `EasyGL_HandleRelease` and `EasyGL_DeviceDisposeOrder` segfaulting, `EasyGL_StockEffectSamplerContract` and `EasyGL_SamplerComponentIsolation` failing — same names, same kinds, and all four reproduce under `--rerun-failed -j1` exactly as that baseline records. **The two sampler-named ones are the temptation this check exists to resist:** they look like this row's doing and they predate it by a day. **From slot 1 upward, and that is a decision rather than an omission:** slot 0 already has a writer — the batch's own sampler down the `ISpriteBatchRenderer` path — and giving one slot two writers with no ordering between them across twelve renderer families is the shape of both Task 293's bug and `VULKAN-164`'s defect (2). `applySamplerStatesToRenderer` therefore takes a `firstSlot` (defaulted to 0, so every existing caller is unchanged). The consequence — `GraphicsDevice.SamplerStates[0]` still does not hold the batch's state after `Begin()`, where FNA assigns it — is a real divergence and is **opened as `VULKAN-194`** rather than folded in, because assigning it changes what a 3D draw *after* a sprite batch samples with, on every renderer at once. **Test:** new `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_PerUnitSampler` (`modules/renderers/vulkan/examples/vulkan_shader_effect_per_unit_sampler_test.cpp`), **6/6 on llvmpipe and on RADV**. Two 1×1×2 volumes on units 0 and 1 of **one** `ShaderEffect`, sampled at `W = 1.25` — outside [0,1], the only place an address mode is observable, and with one voxel on X and Y so neither can contribute. Volume 0 encodes its slice in the **red** channel, volume 1 in **blue**, and the shader always writes G=128, so a single pixel separates all four worlds: `(255,128,255)` both units on their own state, `(0,128,0)` the same swapped, `(255,128,0)` and `(0,128,255)` the two ways the defect could show. **Mutation-tested, one per half, each predicted before it was run and each caught by a different leg:** the renderer half reverted (`wantSamplers[0]` for every unit) gives `(255,128,0)` and `(0,128,255)` — legs A and B fail; the shared-layer half reverted (no `applySamplerStatesToRenderer(1)`) gives `(255,128,255)` and `(0,128,255)` — legs B and B' fail. **The second mutation is why leg B exists, and it is the finding this row would have missed:** with the publication removed, unit 1 read `Clamp` in *both* legs, because this renderer's `defaultSampler_` clamps W — which is the answer leg A wants. **Leg A alone passes against the unfixed shared layer.** Only the swap distinguishes “unit 1 carried its own state” from “unit 1 got a default that happened to agree”, which is the same trap `VULKAN-050` recorded for `SpriteSortMode::Texture`. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **355/355** on llvmpipe and on RADV; full `ctest` **9204/9216**, whose **12** failures are the standing content/lifecycle/net/window/C-API set of §7.5c exactly — no flake, and nothing in `Vulkan_*`. The full suite was mandatory and not a formality: `SpriteBatch::flushBatch` is on the path of every sprite in the suite. **Docs:** the per-unit rule and its slot-0 exception are in `docs/vulkan-renderer.md`'s ShaderEffect contract, and the CNA-wide finding — Task 293's unreached half — is §8 of `docs/sampler-state-support.md`. **Opened by this row:** `VULKAN-194`. |
| VULKAN-167 | Five renderers sample a `Texture3D` and do not say so | ⛔ | **Status corrected to ⛔ on 2026-09-08 by `VULKAN-237`, and the reason is the row's own:** what is left needs **Magnum, IGL, OpenGL2, OpenGL4 and Sokol builds**, none of which exists in this tree, and each renderer's own plan owns its capability claim. Classification for the Phase-13 gate: `OTHER_RENDERER_BUILD_REQUIRED`. **Not a Vulkan renderer gap** — this renderer binds a `Texture3D`, says so, and is tested for it (`VULKAN-164`); the EasyGL half became `VULKAN-206` and is ✅. **Opened by `VULKAN-164`, and deliberately not answered by it.** `SupportsTexture3DSamplingEXT()` defaults to `false`, and `MagnumEffectRenderer`, `IglEffectRenderer`, `OpenGL2`, `OpenGL4` and `Sokol` all implement `BindTexture3D` with what reads as a real bind. Reading their source is not the same as measuring them, and this campaign's own rule is that a green claim needs a test behind it — so they under-claim (`Unsupported` where the honest answer may be `Supported`) rather than over-claim, until someone runs a volume through each. **The second half this row used to own is gone, and it is closed rather than dropped.** It read: “`ISpriteBatchRenderer::SetSamplerAddressModeWEXT` now carries the batch's W axis to any renderer that wants it, and only Vulkan takes it; EasyGL binds a GL sampler object per unit, so `WrapR` would reach a bound volume there for the cost of one override.” That was a *closable* unit of work and this one is not, so on 2026-09-07 it was split out as **`VULKAN-206`** — a new ID from the free range, not a renumbering — and closed there with a pixel test. What remains here is the five-renderer under-claim only. **Still not this plan's to close alone:** each renderer's own plan owns its claim, and this row exists so the under-claim is on the record with an owner rather than discovered later as a lie. **Classification:** documentation/capability gap, cross-renderer. |
| VULKAN-206 | Carry a `SpriteBatch`'s `AddressW` to a bound volume on EasyGL | ✅ | **Split out of `VULKAN-167` because it was the closable half of a row that cannot close, and closed the same day.** `VULKAN-164` established the XNA rule — XNA assigns the **whole** `SamplerState` to `GraphicsDevice.SamplerStates[0]`, W included — and added `ISpriteBatchRenderer::SetSamplerAddressModeWEXT`, additive with a no-op default so the eleven other renderers were untouched. Vulkan was the only family that took it. **On EasyGL the same defect (2) was present verbatim:** `EasyGLRenderer::ApplySamplerState` sets `WrapR` from `addressU`, with a comment saying why — every XNA 4.0 preset sets all three axes alike — which is true of the presets and false of a `SamplerState` that set W on its own. **One override plus two call sites.** `pendingAddressW_` defaults to `-1`, meaning “the batch supplied no W”, which keeps the W-follows-U derivation for any caller that flushes sprites without going through `SpriteBatch::Begin`; the sprite flush applies it after `ApplySamplerState` when it is set. **Two** sites, not one — the stock flush and the compiled-effect flush — because a custom `sampler3D` reaches the second. **Why unit 0 is the whole answer here, and why this is not one shared test with Vulkan's.** On this renderer `ShaderEffect::SetTexture(0, volume)` binds `GL_TEXTURE_3D` on GL unit 0 while the sprite's own texture occupies `GL_TEXTURE_2D` on the same unit, and one GL **sampler object** at that unit governs both — so the batch's slot-0 sampler is exactly what addresses the volume. Vulkan puts a bound effect texture in descriptor set 1 instead, and `ShaderEffect` takes renderer-specific source by contract (`VULKAN-250`), so `easygl_texture3d_addressw_test.cpp` is a **sibling** of `vulkan_texture3d_addressw_test.cpp` rather than the same file registered twice. **Test:** new `EasyGL_Texture3DAddressW`, **7/7**. A 1×1×2 volume (slice 0 red, slice 1 blue) sampled at `W = 1.25` through one `ShaderEffect` — Wrap gives 0.25 → slice 0, Clamp gives 1.0 → slice 1. One voxel on X and on Y, so neither axis can contribute. `W = 1.5` would have proved nothing: `Wrap(1.5) = 0.5` and `Clamp(1.5) = 1.0` both select slice 1. **Four legs exist to stop a false green:** two in-range controls (`W = 0.25` → red, `W = 0.75` → blue) pin the binding, the uniform and the point filter rather than the address mode; leg D asserts the two `SamplerState`s differ in W and in nothing else, so the difference is attributable; leg C repeats the wrap draw through the **same** effect, which is the sampler-outlived-its-batch failure the Vulkan sibling had to fix. **Mutation-tested, and the answer was predicted before it was run:** with the override storing nothing, every leg that wants Wrap reads `(0,0,255)` — the clamp answer, because W then follows U — so A, B' and C fail at **4/7** while both controls and leg B (which wants clamp anyway) still pass. That asymmetry is the point: a test where the mutation fails everything cannot tell a broken hook from a broken harness. **Evidence:** `^EasyGL_` **321/325** and the full EasyGL-configuration `ctest` **9184/9201**, whose **17** failures are §7.5b's recorded serially-reproducing set exactly — the four `EasyGL_` ones and the thirteen shared content/lifecycle/net/window/C-API ones. The full suite was warranted: every EasyGL sprite flush now makes one extra `ApplySamplerAddressW` call. `cmake-build-vulkan` compiles no `EasyGLRenderer.cpp` object at all, so the Vulkan suite is not a witness here and was not re-run for it. **No capability change:** `SupportsTexture3DSamplingEXT()` was already `true` on this renderer with a passing pixel test behind it (`VULKAN-164`); this row makes an axis of that sampling correct, not newly available. **Classification:** XNA-fidelity gap, EasyGL renderer, closed. |
| VULKAN-194 | `SpriteBatch` does not assign `GraphicsDevice.SamplerStates[0]`, where XNA's `PrepRenderState` does | ✅ | **Opened by `VULKAN-166`, which published slots 1–15 from the sprite path and deliberately left slot 0 alone.** FNA's `PrepRenderState` assigns `GraphicsDevice.SamplerStates[0] = samplerState` (`SpriteBatch.cs:1426`), so after a batch, slot 0 holds the batch's state and keeps holding it after `End()`. CNA carried the batch's sampler to the renderer down the `ISpriteBatchRenderer` path instead and never touched the device's collection, so a game reading `SamplerStates[0]` saw the `LinearWrap` default and — the part with pixels attached — **a 3D draw issued after a sprite batch sampled with whatever the game last set, where XNA samples with the batch's state.** **This row's own acceptance was wrong and a probe corrected it.** It said “the assignment in `SpriteBatch::Begin`”, which is what FNA's source reads like at a glance. Measured on the shipped XNA 4.0 runtime (`spikes/xna-spritebatch-sampler0-spike/`): `before Begin: PointClamp` → `after Begin: PointClamp` → `after End: PointWrap`. For a **Deferred** batch the assignment is **not** visible after `Begin()` at all — `PrepRenderState` runs at the **flush** — and the same probe's 3D draw at `u = 1.25` came back **red**, i.e. the batch's `Wrap` really does reach the next draw. So the fix is not a new publication point: it is the **same two points `VULKAN-166` already publishes from** — `Begin()` for `SpriteSortMode::Immediate`, which never reaches a flush, and the top of `flushBatch()` otherwise. The batch's resolved sampler is retained in `SpriteBatch::effectiveSampler_` because `Begin` is no longer where it is consumed. **Slot 0 still keeps its single renderer-side writer:** `applySamplerStatesToRenderer(1)` is unchanged, and the new line assigns the *device collection* only. The push to the renderer then happens where it already did — `GraphicsDevice`'s draw entry points call `applySamplerStatesToRenderer()` from slot 0 — so the batch's state reaches the sampler on the **next draw**, which is exactly the ordering XNA has and avoids giving slot 0 two writers inside the batch. **Test:** new renderer-agnostic `spritebatch_sampler0_publication_test.cpp`, registered on **both** renderers (`Vulkan_SpriteBatch_Sampler0Publication`, `EasyGL_SpriteBatch_Sampler0Publication`), **9/9 on each**. It asserts the timing half (property after `Begin` and after `End`, for Deferred **and** Immediate) and the behaviour half (a textured 3D quad drawn after the batch) in one file. **Why `u = 1.25` and not `1.5`:** on the 2×1 strip the test samples, `Wrap(1.5) = 0.5` and `Clamp(1.5) = 1.0` both select texel 1 and the leg would pass either way — the same trap that made this spike's first draft print the opposite verdict. At `1.25`, Wrap gives texel 0 (red) and Clamp texel 1 (green). **Three control legs, because the address mode is the only thing under test:** `u = 0.25` → red and `u = 0.75` → green pin the geometry, the texture orientation and the point filter; a **negative control** re-assigns `PointClamp` by hand and re-draws at `1.25`, which must be green — without it the wrap leg would pass on a renderer that ignored the address mode and always wrapped. **Mutation-tested:** with the two assignment lines removed and nothing else changed, the test goes **5/9** — the four failing legs are exactly the four that describe the defect (Deferred-after-`End`, the inherited 3D draw, and both Immediate legs), and every control still passes. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **364/364** and full `ctest` **9213/9225** on the virtual display `:99` (Xvfb), whose **12** failures are §7.5c's standing content/lifecycle/net/window/C-API set exactly; `^EasyGL_` **320/324**, the four being §7.5b's recorded pre-existing set (`EasyGL_HandleRelease` and `EasyGL_DeviceDisposeOrder` segfaulting, `EasyGL_StockEffectSamplerContract` and `EasyGL_SamplerComponentIsolation` failing). The full suite was the point rather than a formality: `flushBatch` is on the path of every sprite in it, and the row's own acceptance warned that goldens taken under the old behaviour might move. **None did.** **Not measured on RADV, and that is recorded rather than glossed:** the only DRI3-capable server available here is an `Xwayland` that maps onto the owner's desktop, and the owner asked for virtual displays. The change is shared-layer C++ with no shader or pipeline component, and it is verified on **two** renderers, which is the two-renderer evidence this row asked for. **Docs:** §8 of `docs/sampler-state-support.md`. **Classification:** XNA-fidelity gap, shared layer, CNA-wide — not a Vulkan-vs-EasyGL parity gap. |
| VULKAN-195 | The plan's derived sections still describe the renderer this campaign started with | ✅ | **Opened and closed by the same audit of §10 against the row statuses it cites, and the finding is that the section's own first word is false.** §10 opens *"Maintained. A row moves only when its 'Vulkan evidence' cell names something a reader can run or open."* **Twenty-six cells carry a gap classification — `TEST_GAP`, `IMPLEMENTATION_GAP` or `SEMANTIC_DIVERGENCE` — whose every named task is now ✅**, and one of the twenty-six (`The 37 GLSL-source sample-shader ports`) is correctly classified because its divergence is permanent, leaving **twenty-five** that describe a state that no longer exists. The first row of the first sub-table is the clearest case: it cites `default: return true` (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:1754`) as this renderer's evidence, and `VULKAN-020` removed that arm entirely — `SupportsCapability` today has one case per member, **no** `default:`, and `-Werror=switch` behind it. **The plan noticed this once already and did not generalise it:** `VULKAN-051`'s own closing text says *"the §10.2 matrix row already cites it — this row was simply never moved."* **Why it matters more than tidiness:** §10 is the artefact this campaign's completion claim rests on, and a reader who trusts it today would count twenty-five open gaps that are closed — the mirror image of the failure mode the campaign is most careful about, and just as wrong. **Acceptance, and the method matters as much as the result:** a cell must **not** move merely because its owning task is ✅ — that is exactly the "a green plan is not a verified renderer" trap. Each of the twenty-five is re-derived from (a) the owning row's recorded evidence, (b) the named test being registered in `modules/renderers/vulkan/examples/CMakeLists.txt` today, and (c) that test passing in the current `^Vulkan_` run. Where a closure did not actually establish parity — `GetShaderDialectEXT`, whose `VULKAN-250` declared the dialect while `VULKAN-264` still owns the enumerator's imprecision — the cell moves to the classification that is true and names the **open** successor, not to `PARITY`. Cells are annotated in the established style (*"was `TEST_GAP` when this matrix was written; closed and re-checked <date>"*) rather than overwritten, so the provenance survives. **Non-goal:** re-auditing the areas themselves. This row moves the matrix onto evidence that already exists; a cell whose evidence turns out not to exist opens its own row instead of being written green. **The same audit re-ran §9.1, and that half is a small correction rather than a rewrite:** all **35** findings have an owner, every owner exists, and only **two** owners are still open — F-08 (`VULKAN-264`) and F-33 (`VULKAN-186`). `VULKAN-472`, the plan's one 🟨, is measured against F-01…F-14 and records **four** remaining; three of those four have since closed (`VULKAN-256` took F-09, `VULKAN-170`/`VULKAN-174` took F-10 and F-11), leaving **one**. That row's text is a dated measurement, so it gets a dated supersession beneath it rather than an edit — the campaign's own rule for a number that has moved. Whether `VULKAN-472` may leave 🟨 is **not** this row's to decide: its acceptance wants a ✅ task or a ⛔ with a reason, and `VULKAN-264` is neither. **Classification:** plan-integrity defect, this file. **Done: all twenty-five re-derived, and the check that says so is the same script that found them —** a cell counts as stale when its classification cell opens with a gap word and every task it names is ✅; the count went **26 → 2**, and both survivors are correct rather than stale (`Custom effect through Begin(effect)` and `The 37 GLSL-source sample-shader ports`, whose dialect divergence is permanent). **Every moved cell names a test that is registered in `modules/renderers/vulkan/examples/CMakeLists.txt` today and passed in the `^Vulkan_` **355/355** run of 2026-09-07 on llvmpipe **and** on RADV** — thirty-one owning rows checked that way, and the Vulkan-evidence column was rewritten alongside the classification wherever it described something that no longer exists (the clearest being `default: return true` at `VulkanRenderer.cpp:1754`, an arm `VULKAN-020` deleted). **Two cells did not simply become `PARITY`, which is the point of the method:** `GetShaderDialectEXT` and `Custom ShaderEffect — dialect declaration` are `PARITY` on *declaring* a dialect and still name the **open** `VULKAN-264` for the enumerator's imprecision; `Runtime MSAA change` became `VULKAN_STRONGER` rather than `PARITY`, because EasyGL's `MsaaChange` still only echoes the request while this renderer really tears down and rebuilds. **`VULKAN-472` gets its dated supersession** and stays 🟨. **No production change, no test added** — this row moves the plan onto evidence that already existed. |
| VULKAN-196 | `EnvironmentMapAmount` above 1 extrapolates past the environment map, where D3D9 saturates it (F-36) | ✅ | **A real defect, found while auditing `VULKAN-186`'s premise rather than by looking for it, and fixed with the real XNA runtime as the oracle.** **The rule:** `plans/plan_fx.md` `FX-123` measured Direct3D 9's saturation of the vertex colour output registers `oD0` **and `oD1`** against real XNA frames. FNA's `EnvironmentMapEffect.fx` writes the blend factor to `vout.Specular.rgb` and `Structures.fxh:156` declares `Specular : COLOR1`, so XNA clamps it before interpolation. `EnvironmentMapAmount` is **not** clamped by its property setter (`EnvironmentMapEffect.cs:283`), so a game can hand the shader a value above 1. **The gap:** `env_map3d.vert.glsl:70` wrote the factor raw. EasyGL clamps it (`EasyGLRenderer.cpp:8423`, with the reasoning in its own comment), so this was a **Vulkan-vs-EasyGL parity gap and an XNA divergence at once** — the exact shape this campaign exists to remove, and one that `VULKAN-188` left behind when it closed F-35 for the vertex-lit family only. **Measured on the real runtime, not inferred:** new spike `spikes/xna-envmap-amount-clamp-spike/`, on the XNA 4.0 runtime in `~/.wine-cna-xna40` (D3D9 through DXVK, AMD Radeon 780M / RADV PHOENIX). A quad with a `(60,60,60)` base texture and a `(200,200,200)` environment cube, `FresnelFactor = 0` so the factor is the amount unmodified, at four amounts: **0.5 → (100,100,100), 1.0 → (200,200,200), 2.0 → (200,200,200), 3.0 → (200,200,200)**. The 0.5 leg is the positive control that separates *saturated* from *the amount does nothing here*; without it the probe prints `INCONCLUSIVE` and stops. **Fixed** by clamping at the **vertex** stage, where D3D9 does it — clamping after interpolation would interpolate the raw value first and give a different gradient, which is the distinction EasyGL's own comment records and `FX-122` settled for the compiled-effect path. `spirv_shaders.hpp` regenerated. **Test:** new renderer-agnostic `modules/graphics/examples/environmentmapeffect_amount_clamp_test.cpp` → **`Vulkan_EnvironmentMapEffect_AmountClamp`**, **3/3 on llvmpipe and on RADV**, with relational legs only (the base colour a lit `EnvironmentMapEffect` produces is not this test's subject): the amount knob is live, 2.0 renders as 1.0, and so does 3.0. **The mutation is the before-state, which is the strongest form there is:** against the unfixed shader the same binary reads **1/3**, with 2.0 and 3.0 at `(255,255,255)` where XNA gives `(200,200,200)`. After the fix CNA reproduces **all four** of the real runtime's values byte-for-byte. **`VULKAN-186` is untouched by this**, checked rather than assumed: the env-map golden still measures `max channel diff 20 of 20`, because its scene never sends an amount above 1 and the clamp is a no-op there. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **356/356** on llvmpipe and on RADV; full `ctest` **9205/9217**, whose **12** failures are §7.5c's standing content/lifecycle/net/window/C-API set exactly — no flake, and nothing in `Vulkan_*`. The full suite was run because a production shader changed, even though the clamp cannot move a scene that stays inside `[0,1]`. **Not registered for EasyGL**, and the reason is the same one `VULKAN-057` records: that build directory belongs to another session on this machine and this campaign does not run in it. The source is renderer-agnostic and its legs are relational, so the registration is one line for whoever owns that tree. **Docs:** §8 of `docs/environmentmapeffect-support.md`, with the four-amount table for real XNA and for this renderer before and after. **Opened by this row:** `VULKAN-197`. |
| VULKAN-197 | The same D3D9 register clamp is missing from the **unlit** diffuse path (F-36) | ✅ | **Opened by `VULKAN-196` as a CNA-wide sibling, and closed for this renderer on a measurement of its own rather than on the one it inherited.** **The rule, measured:** new spike `spikes/xna-diffuse-color-clamp-spike/`, on the XNA 4.0 runtime in `~/.wine-cna-xna40`. A quad with a **grey** `(100,100,100)` texture — never white, or every answer saturates at the output and no leg could fail, which is the trap `FX-123`'s own test records — `LightingEnabled = false`, `Alpha = 1`, at four diffuse values: **0.5 → (50,50,50), 1.0 → (100,100,100), 2.0 → (100,100,100), 3.0 → (100,100,100)**. `BasicEffect.DiffuseColor` has no clamp in its setter (`BasicEffect.cs:117`), so the value reaches the shader; Direct3D 9 saturates `vout.Diffuse : COLOR0` before interpolation and that is what the last two columns are. **Fixed in ten vertex shaders**, each writing `pc.diffuseColor` — or `inColor * pc.diffuseColor` — straight into the varying: `alpha_test3d`, `alpha_test_colored3d`, `colored3d`, `colored3d_legacy`, `colored_textured3d`, `dual_texture3d`, `dual_texture_colored3d`, `instanced3d`, `instanced_colored3d` and `textured3d`. Clamped at the **vertex** stage, where `oD0` is, for the reason `FX-122`/`FX-123` settled: clamping per fragment interpolates the raw value first and gives a different gradient. `spirv_shaders.hpp` regenerated. **Four shaders were deliberately left alone, and reading FNA rather than pattern-matching the code is what stopped an over-clamp — one of which would have been a regression.** `lit_textured3d` is the **per-pixel** lit path, and FNA's `VSBasicPixelLightingTx` writes `vout.Diffuse = float4(1, 1, 1, DiffuseColor.a)`: the material colour is applied in the **pixel** shader there, unclamped, with only the render-target write saturating it. CNA carries the tint through the varying instead, so the two structures do not correspond and clamping it would have made this renderer *more* clamped than XNA. That path needs its own analysis and does not get a clamp on a resemblance. `skinned3d_color` and `skinned3d_vertexlit_color` write `vColor = aColor` — the raw per-vertex colour, unorm in `[0,1]`, with no uniform multiplier — so a clamp there could never fire; `sprite2d` likewise carries a sprite's own colour and no diffuse uniform. `pbr3d*` is a CNA extension with no XNA counterpart, so the D3D9 rule has nothing to say about it. **Test:** new renderer-agnostic `modules/graphics/examples/basiceffect_diffuse_color_clamp_test.cpp` → **`Vulkan_BasicEffect_DiffuseColorClamp`**, **9/9** on llvmpipe and on RADV. Three shader shapes, because one clamp in one shader is not the finding — textured, vertex-coloured, and both — each measured at 0.5 (the positive control, without which “2.0 == 1.0” cannot be told from “`DiffuseColor` does nothing here”), 1.0, 2.0 and 3.0. **The mutation is the before-state:** against the unfixed shaders the same binary reads **3/9** — every one of the three control legs still passes, so the harness is sound and only the clamp legs moved: `T` and `TV` read `(200,200,200)` at 2.0 where XNA gives `(100,100,100)`, and the gradient midpoint reads `(255,255,255)` where XNA gives `(178,178,178)`. **Not a parity gap, and it is now a divergence in the other direction:** EasyGL's `FX-123` fix touched only its two vertex-**lit** programs. Its unlit programs apply `uDiffuseColor` in the **fragment** stage (`FragColor = vc * uDiffuseColor`, `EasyGLRenderer.cpp:7768`), so there is no colour varying for the D3D9 saturate to apply to and it never happens — a different structure with the same divergence, and one this row measured rather than assumed. This renderer is XNA-correct here and EasyGL is not, which the campaign's own tie-break asks for — `XNA wins` — and which is recorded rather than quietly evened out. That half is `plans/plan_fx.md`'s to take, with the spike above as its oracle. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **357/357** on llvmpipe and on RADV; full `ctest` **9206/9218**, whose **12** failures are §7.5c's standing content/lifecycle/net/window/C-API set exactly — no flake, and nothing in `Vulkan_*`. The full suite was mandatory: ten production shaders changed, and between them they carry every unlit `BasicEffect`, `AlphaTestEffect`, `DualTextureEffect` and instanced draw in the suite. |
| VULKAN-198 | Run EasyGL's three unregistered `BasicEffect` sources here and record what this renderer does with them | ✅ | **An audit that cost three build lines and found two real gaps, which is the whole argument for this campaign's register-and-see method.** §10.7's `BasicEffect` cell listed six EasyGL-only tests and pointed at `VULKAN-257`; that row registered the **combination sweep** and nothing else, so the other five sat unexamined behind a `TEST_GAP` label. Three are portable and were run here. **`easygl_basiceffect_default_lighting_test.cpp` passes, 14/14** — it asserts `EnableDefaultLighting`'s exact XNA constants and touches no draw path, so it is registered as **`Vulkan_BasicEffectDefaultLighting`** and closes as coverage. **The other two are refused**, each by name, and the messages are the finding: `easygl_basiceffect_position_normal_test.cpp` (stride **24**, Position@0 + Normal@12) gets *“this VertexDeclaration cannot be represented on the ordinary-**indexed** route … native layout for a 24-byte record does not bind at all”*, and `easygl_basiceffect_lit_vertex_color_test.cpp` (stride **36**, +Colour@24 +TexCoord@28) the same on the **non-indexed** route. Both reproduce on RADV. **The cause is one line:** `needsLitTextured` is `stride == 32 && declaresNormal`, so the lit family exists for exactly one layout, and `REMED-GFX-DECL-GUARD` then refuses rather than reading a normal as colour and UV — the guard working exactly as designed, over a hole it did not create. **Deliberately NOT registered:** a known refusal registered as a CTest is a red test, not evidence, and both sources abort rather than reporting a failure because they are written for a renderer that draws them. `VULKAN-199` and `VULKAN-200` each register their own source when they land — the registration IS the acceptance. **`easygl_basiceffect_world_scale_precision_test.cpp` is the sixth and stays EasyGL-only on the reason `VULKAN-487` already recorded:** it is `FX-124`, a `mediump` fp16 overflow in GLSL **ES**, and this renderer's shaders have no `mediump` default to overflow. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **357/357 → 358/358** on llvmpipe; the three binaries re-run individually on RADV give the same three answers. **No production change.** **Opened by this row:** `VULKAN-199`, `VULKAN-200`, and finding F-37. |
| VULKAN-199 | A lit `BasicEffect` cannot draw a **Position+Normal** (24-byte) vertex at all (F-37) | ✅ | **Fixed, and the acceptance is the registration: `Vulkan_BasicEffect_PositionNormal` runs EasyGL's own `SAMPLE-002` source and passes 2/2 on llvmpipe and on RADV.** **The defect:** `needsLitTextured` was `stride == 32 && declaresNormal`, so the lit family existed for exactly one layout — `VertexPositionNormalTexture`. XNA's Primitives3D vertex is Position@0 + Normal@12, **24 bytes**, the same stride as `VertexPositionColorTexture`, which is precisely why the stride cannot decide this; the draw reached no lit program and `REMED-GFX-DECL-GUARD` refused it by name rather than reading the normal's twelve bytes as a packed colour and a UV. **The selection is now the declaration's answer, not the stride's:** a non-empty declaration whose element set is **exactly** `Position` + `Normal` is the untextured lit layout, at any stride. Asked of the declaration only — without one there is nothing to tell the two 24-byte meanings apart, so the stride's historical answer stands and no existing draw moves. **Set-exact, and the first draft was not, which would have shipped a silent drop.** The looser rule — *names a `Normal`, names no `TextureCoordinate`* — admits Position+Normal+**Colour**, and the layout builder's `IsComplete()` means *every input the shader consumes was supplied*, not *every declared element was consumed*: such a record would have satisfied the two-input untextured program, passed the fidelity guard, and rendered with the vertex colour thrown away without a word. That is `FX-125`'s failure exactly, arriving by the back door of a fix for its sibling. Refusing it until `VULKAN-200` gives the lit family a colour input is the honest state, so `DeclarationIsPositionNormalOnlyEXT` asks the whole-set question. It stays offset-flexible — the declaration-driven layout carries the offsets — where EasyGL's own `positionNormal` test additionally pins both to 0 and 12. **Only the VERTEX stage needed splitting, which is the economy of this row:** the fragment stages already gate their sample on `pc.textureEnabled`, so `lit_untextured3d.vert.glsl` and `lit_untextured3d_vertexlit.vert.glsl` pair with the **existing** fragment shaders and differ from their siblings in exactly two things — no `aUV` input, and `fragUV` written as zero. Vulkan cannot default an absent vertex attribute, which is the whole reason a separate module is needed where OpenGL would leave the attribute unbound. New `BasicProgramShapeEXT::LitUntextured` and `StockInputs::kLitUntextured = { kPos, kNormal }` carry it through the declaration-driven layout builder `VULKAN-144` put there. **`VULKAN-158`'s own warning came due and is discharged in the same change.** That row left `constexpr std::size_t kLitStride = 32` in both factories with the note *“if the selection rule ever widens, this constant becomes the same defect the sibling factories had”* — the rule widened here, so the factories now take the record's own stride and fall back to 32 only when the caller supplies none. **The second half was found by the validation layer and by nothing else, on the first run after the pipeline built:** the descriptor-set and UBO arm was gated on `needsLitTextured` alone, so a pipeline that *statically uses* set 0 was bound with no set bound at all — *“`vkCmdDrawIndexed()`: The VkPipeline … statically uses descriptor set 0, but set 0 is not compatible with the pipeline layout bound with INVALID_EMPTY”*. Same family, same descriptor set, same UBO: the arm takes both flags now. Worth recording because the pixels would have been wrong or absent without a message a reader could act on — this is what `VULKAN-408`'s validation gate is for. **Mutation:** `needsLitUntextured = false` at both draw sites restores the **exact** refusal message, so the selection rule is what unblocked the layout rather than anything incidental. And the before-state is stronger still: `VULKAN-198` measured the refusal on this very source before any of this existed. **The test's second leg is what makes it evidence rather than a smoke test** — reversing the custom normal removes the directional contribution, so the surface is genuinely lit rather than merely painted. **Docs:** a new section in `docs/vulkan-renderer.md` tabulates which vertex layouts a lit `BasicEffect` can draw, including the two still refused and why the rule is set-exact. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **358/358 → 359/359** on llvmpipe and **359/359** on RADV; full `ctest` in four chunks covering all **9220** tests, **14** failures between them — §7.5c's standing **twelve** plus `WaveBankTest.IsInUseFalseSoonAfterCueNaturallyFinishes…` and `ENetDiscoveryServiceTest.MalformedAnnounceDuringSearch…`, the two contention-sensitive cases this machine produces when it is not alone. **None is a `Vulkan_*` test**, and chunk 3 (tests 4801–7200) had no failure at all. The full suite was run in **chunks** (`ctest -I`), because two whole-suite attempts were killed by the harness's low-memory watchdog while another agent's Blender job held the machine — a chunk that dies costs a quarter of the run rather than all of it. **And the reach of this change is bounded by an argument the runs then confirm rather than establish:** every piece of it is inert unless `needsLitUntextured` is true, which happens only for a declaration that is exactly Position + Normal — a declaration the old code **refused**. So no test that passed before can take a different path now: the factories' `kLitStride` still resolves to 32 for every existing caller (`recordStride` is 0 unless the new flag is set), `attrCount` is still 3, `SelectBasicProgramShapeEXT`'s new branch is not taken, and the descriptor arm's added disjunct is false. The `^Vulkan_` set on two devices is therefore the oracle that could actually move, and it is green on both. |
| VULKAN-200 | A lit `BasicEffect` cannot draw a **Position+Normal+Colour+TexCoord** (36-byte) vertex at all (F-37) | ✅ | **Fixed, and the acceptance is the registration: `Vulkan_BasicEffectLitVertexColor` runs EasyGL's own `FX-125` source and passes 3/3 on llvmpipe and on RADV.** **The defect** is `VULKAN-199`'s at a different stride, and this is the one XNA's own content pipeline produces: the stock `ModelProcessor` emits Position+Normal+Colour+TextureCoordinate — 36 bytes — for any mesh carrying a colour channel and sets `VertexColorEnabled` with it. `needsLitTextured` was `stride == 32`, no lit program here had a colour input at all, and `REMED-GFX-DECL-GUARD` refused the draw. `FX-125` found EasyGL dropping such a mesh onto the **unlit** program and rendering `SAMPLE-047`'s sphere as a flat green disc; this renderer refused it instead, which is better and still unusable. **Selection:** a new `BasicProgramShapeEXT::LitColoredTextured` and `StockInputs::kLitColTextured = { kPos, kNormal, kColor, kUv }`, chosen by `DeclarationIsPositionNormalColorTextureOnlyEXT` — set-exact for the reason `VULKAN-199` records, because `IsComplete()` cannot notice a declared element the shader ignores. **Three new shaders, and the split between them is FNA's, not a convenience.** The **per-vertex** family needs only a vertex stage: `VSBasicVertexLightingTxVc` does `vout.Diffuse *= vin.Color` **before** the value reaches `oD0`, so the colour belongs inside Direct3D 9's saturate — `fragLitRGB = clamp((lightSum * diffuse + emissive) * vc.rgb)` and `fragAlpha = diffuse.a * vc.a` — and the existing fragment stage then multiplies the texture by exactly what it already expected. **This renderer's own `skinned3d_vertexlit_color` does it the other way round**, clamping the lit sum and multiplying the colour in the fragment stage afterwards, which is a different picture whenever the sum crosses 1 (1.8 and a colour of 0.5 give 0.9 the right way and 0.5 the wrong way); it was not copied, and whether it is itself a defect is left open on purpose — `SkinnedEffect` has no `Vc` variant in FNA, so XNA has no opinion there and EasyGL's choice is an extrapolation. The **per-pixel** family needs a fragment stage too, because FNA's structure genuinely differs: `VSBasicPixelLightingTxVc` writes `vout.Diffuse.rgb = vin.Color.rgb` and `PSBasicPixelLightingTx` then does `color = tex * pin.Diffuse` followed by `color.rgb *= lightResult.Diffuse`, so the colour multiplies the **whole** lit bracket, emissive included, and its alpha reaches `AddSpecular`'s scale. This renderer carries the material diffuse in `fragTint` and lights per fragment with it, so folding the colour into `fragTint` would scale emissive by it and leave the sum term short. It gets its own varying, applied once, ahead of the specular add. **A second test exists because the first cannot reach that half.** `easygl_basiceffect_lit_vertex_color_test.cpp` sets `PreferPerPixelLighting(false)` **in its own source**, so registering it alone would have shipped the per-pixel shaders untested — the exact shape of claim this campaign refuses. New renderer-agnostic `modules/graphics/examples/basiceffect_lit_vertex_color_perpixel_test.cpp` → **`Vulkan_BasicEffect_LitVertexColorPerPixel`**, the same scene with the flag the other way up. The scene is **ambient-only** by construction (all three directional lights disabled), so the two families are arithmetically identical and one expected pixel — `(51,102,26)`, which is `0.5 × (0.4, 0.8, 0.2)` — applies to both; that is what makes it a test of the colour path rather than of the lighting model. Both tests carry the two negatives that make them evidence: the answer must not be the unlit vertex colour `(102,204,51)`, and must not be lit-but-colourless `(128,128,128)`. **One narrower refusal is left standing, deliberately:** an **unlit** 36-byte record is still refused, because `needsLitColored` requires `lightingEnabled`. Routing it through the coloured shaders' unlit branch would have been free and wrong — that branch does not clamp `inColor * diffuseColor` at the vertex the way `VULKAN-197` just made `colored_textured3d` do, so it would have traded a loud refusal for a quietly inconsistent clamp. Opened as `VULKAN-201`. **The mutation is the before-state, and a second one arrived unasked and is the more useful of the two.** Before: both tests are **refused** by name, the message `VULKAN-198` recorded. During: with the input table written in the RECORD's byte order — `{ kPos, kNormal, kColor, kUv }`, colour at byte 24 before UV at 28 — both tests drew **(64,64,0)** instead of (51,102,26). `BuildVulkanVertexInputLayoutEXT` assigns `location = index in the table`, and these shaders inherit `aUV` at location 2 from their colourless siblings with `aColor` appended at 3, so the record's order bound the UV's two floats to `aColor`: `vec4(uv, 0, 1)` at the centre is (0.5, 0.5, 0, 1), and half of that is exactly (64,64,0). **It passed both negative legs.** It is not the unlit vertex colour and it is not lit-but-colourless, so a test asserting only "not those two" would have called a UV-as-colour bug a pass — which is the argument for leg (a) asserting the exact value XNA's arithmetic predicts, and for `docs/basiceffect-support.md` recording the table's order as the shader's rather than the record's. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **359/359 → 361/361** on llvmpipe and **361/361** on RADV; full `ctest` **in part, and the part is named rather than rounded up**: chunks 1–2 (tests 1–6000, `ctest -I`) show only §7.5c's standing set plus one artefact diagnosed as a **killed process** rather than a defect — `CueTest.GetVariableAfterSetReturnsLocalOverride`, three lines of in-memory arithmetic with no timing, no device and its own process, which passes **3/3 in isolation** and failed while another agent held the machine. **Chunk 3 (6001–9222) has not been run**: the machine went to zero available memory under a storm of parallel mingw linkers belonging to another session, and the harness's watchdog killed the run — and then killed the waiter that was holding for memory. It is recorded as outstanding rather than waved at, and the 361 `Vulkan_*` tests it contains were run twice after the fix anyway. **What that leaves unmeasured is small and boundable by the same argument `VULKAN-199` records:** `needsLitColored` fires only for a declaration that is exactly Position+Normal+Colour+TexCoord **with lighting on** — a draw the old code **refused** — and every other edit is inert when the flag is false (`attrCount` is still 3, the UV offset is still 24, the new shape is not selected, the descriptor arm's added disjunct is false). No test that passed before can take a different path now. **Completed later the same day, and the sentence above is left standing rather than rewritten:** the linker storm passed, chunk 3 ran, and it reports **exactly the five remaining standing failures** (`PacketReaderTest`, `GameWindowPlatformTest`, and the three C-API gates) with **no `Vulkan_*` failure**. All **9222** tests are therefore covered across the three chunks, **14** failures between them — §7.5c's standing **twelve**, plus `SoundBankTest.IsInUseFalse…` (audio-timing, known) and the killed-process artefact diagnosed above. |
| VULKAN-201 | An **unlit** 36-byte Position+Normal+Colour+TexCoord vertex is still refused (F-37) | ✅ | **Closed on the cheaper of the two fixes its own acceptance named, and the more expensive one is now provably the wrong one.** `VULKAN-200` required `lightingEnabled`, so the unlit twin of the stock `ModelProcessor`'s record still reached no shape and `REMED-GFX-DECL-GUARD` refused it. **The fix adds no shader:** `SelectBasicProgramShapeEXT` now routes a declaration that is exactly Position+Normal+Colour+TexCoord to `ColoredTextured`, whose input table (`kColTextured`) names no `Normal` — so that element is bound by nothing and ignored. **Ignoring a declared element is exactly what `VULKAN-199` refused to do, and the distinction is what keeps the rule coherent rather than bent:** reaching this branch means no lit shape claimed the draw, which for this declaration means lighting is **off**, and with lighting off there is no lighting for a normal to feed — the element carries no meaning the program could have used. `VULKAN-199` refused a dropped **Colour**, which the program would have had every reason to use. The rule is still *no element is dropped without a nameable reason*; here the reason is nameable, and it is written at the selection site rather than only here, because the next reader of `kColTextured` against a four-element declaration should see a decision. **Test:** new renderer-agnostic `modules/graphics/examples/basiceffect_unlit_vertex_color_36byte_test.cpp` → **`Vulkan_BasicEffect_UnlitVertexColor36Byte`**, **4/4 on llvmpipe and on RADV**. Leg A is that the record draws its vertex colour at all. **Legs B/B' are a gradient, and they have to be:** a flat quad cannot see the clamp order, because the render-target write saturates either way — the lesson `VULKAN-197` paid for. Left edge white, right edge 20 % grey, `DiffuseColor = 2`, midpoint expected at the **real XNA runtime's (178,178,178)** (`spikes/xna-diffuse-color-clamp-spike/`), which is the midpoint of `saturate(2.0) = 255` and `saturate(0.4) = 102`. That measurement was taken on a 16-byte record with no texture and transfers here unchanged **because the only difference is a multiply by a white texture in the fragment stage** — stated rather than assumed. **Two mutations, and the second is the one that matters.** Removing the routing restores the exact refusal message, which is attribution. Removing `VULKAN-197`'s clamp from `colored_textured3d` moves the midpoint to **(255,255,255)** — and that is the counterfactual `VULKAN-200`'s refusal rested on. When that row declined the free fix on the grounds that *"two unlit coloured draws would disagree about the D3D9 `oD0` saturate purely by stride"*, it was reasoning; this measures it, and the disagreement is **77 levels** wide. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **361/361 → 362/362** on llvmpipe and **362/362** on RADV; full `ctest` in two chunks covering all **9222** tests, **16** failures — §7.5c's standing **twelve** plus the **four** audio-timing cases this machine produces under `-j3`, every one of which was already failing before this campaign. **No `Vulkan_*` failure.**. |
| VULKAN-202 | The declaration guard's refusal contract is asserted on no renderer, and this one refuses (F-38) | ✅ | **Opened by a skip line rather than a failure, which is why it survived three rows that leaned on it.** `ctest` reports `DeclarationGuardTest.ARefusedDeclarationRasterizesNothing` and its three siblings as **Skipped** here, with the reason *"translates colliding declarations instead of refusing them"* — `TranslatesDeclarations()` (`VertexDeclarationLayoutTests.cpp:167`) lists Vulkan. **That is half true, and the half it misses is the one this campaign has been quoting:** this renderer translates every declaration one of its stock input tables can satisfy — `VULKAN-144` built exactly that, and the shared suite's own three colliding cases exercise it — and refuses the rest **by name**. `VULKAN-198` measured two such refusals verbatim; `VULKAN-199`, `VULKAN-200` and `VULKAN-201` each **moved what that boundary accepts**, with no contract test on this renderer that could have caught a regression in it. **Closed with a Vulkan-side contract test rather than by editing the shared predicate**, and that split is deliberate: the predicate is a cross-renderer change that must not alter bgfx's or the GL family's verdicts, and neither can be built here — `VULKAN-203` owns it. **Test:** new `Vulkan_DeclarationRefusalContract` (`modules/renderers/vulkan/examples/vulkan_declaration_refusal_contract_test.cpp`), **6/6 on llvmpipe and on RADV**, driven by a declaration this renderer genuinely refuses — Position + Normal + **Tangent**, which no stock table can satisfy because the tables naming a `Normal` also want a texture coordinate or a colour, and the PBR tables that name a `Tangent` are reachable only for a PBR draw. It asserts the four properties the shared legs would have: the refusal **says why** and names the element it could not bind, the refused draw **rasterizes nothing** (every pixel still the clear colour), the refusal **does not poison** the device or the buffer allocator (the very next draw through a freshly allocated buffer renders exactly), and the **indexed** route reaches the same boundary — plus no validation message. **The mutation is the sharpest of this batch.** Removing `RequireFaithfulDeclarationEXT` from the **non-indexed route only**: leg A reads **(255,255,255)** — the draw rasterized, painting the target from the caller's bytes read as something else, which is precisely *"the boundary is downstream of the work it was supposed to prevent"* in the shared test's own words; leg D still passes, because the indexed route's guard is intact, so the test tells the two routes apart; and **leg E still passes** — the Khronos layer said **nothing**, so the wrong-bytes draw was accepted in silence and only leg A can catch it. **No production change.** **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **362/362 → 363/363** on llvmpipe and on RADV. One caveat recorded rather than dropped from the count: the first `-j3` run after the mutation was reverted read 362/363 with `Vulkan_Sample_MovingQuad3D` failing; it passes **5/5 alone** and 363/363 on re-run, and this row changes no production code at all, so it cannot be the cause — an order/contention flake on that sample scene, not previously recorded, and handed to `VULKAN-204`. Full `ctest` not re-run, on `VULKAN-189`/`VULKAN-192`'s precedent: a registration and a new test cannot move anything outside this renderer's own set. |
| VULKAN-203 | `TranslatesDeclarations()` is a per-renderer boolean over renderers that are not binary (F-38) | ⛔ | **Status corrected to ⛔ on 2026-09-08 by `VULKAN-237`.** Blocked on a **bgfx configuration and at least one GL profile**, because the change is to a harness ten renderers share and the row's own acceptance forbids landing it while only *arguing* that a skip becomes a run. Classification: `CROSS_RENDERER_SHARED_INFRA` **and** `OTHER_RENDERER_BUILD_REQUIRED`. **Not a Vulkan renderer gap** — `VULKAN-202` closed the Vulkan half; what is left is the shared fixture's own question. **Opened by `VULKAN-202`, which closed the Vulkan half and deliberately left this one.** The shared fixture asks *does this renderer translate colliding declarations?* and routes its whole contract on the answer: true skips the four refusal legs, false skips `TheTranslatingRendererStillRendersEveryCollidingDeclaration`. **Vulkan is both**, and so, very likely, is every other entry on that list — a renderer with a declaration translator still refuses what its translator cannot express. The honest question is of a **declaration**, not of a renderer: *does this renderer accept THIS one?* **Acceptance:** the fixture gains a way to find a declaration the renderer under test genuinely refuses — a small candidate list, first refusal wins, skip only if none is refused — and the refusal legs run against that instead of against `kColorPosition16`. The translating leg keeps its three unlit cases unchanged; it is narrow rather than wrong. **The constraint that makes this its own row:** it is a shared harness used by ten renderers, and the change can only ever turn a skip into a run (a renderer that refuses nothing still skips), but *saying* that is not the same as measuring it. It must be run on bgfx's configuration and at least one GL profile before it lands, and whoever lands it must state which were run rather than implying all were. Neither is buildable in this campaign's tree. **Classification:** test-integrity gap, shared harness — not this renderer's, and not this campaign's gate. |
| VULKAN-204 | `Vulkan_Sample_MovingQuad3D` failed once under `-j3` — a census, not a diagnosis | ✅ | **Closed on a measurement that says the opposite of the title it was opened with, and the title is corrected rather than kept.** The row was opened from **one** sighting during `VULKAN-202`: a `^Vulkan_` run read 362/363 with this test failing, and it passed 5/5 alone. **The census the acceptance asked for:** ten consecutive `^Vulkan_` runs at `-j3` on llvmpipe, **10/10 at 363/363**, no failure of any kind. So the frequency is **0 in 10** after the sighting, not the one-in-ten the title guessed — and guessing a rate in a row title from a single observation is the mistake this closure records. **What the sighting most likely was, stated as inference and not as measurement:** it fell inside the same window in which another session's parallel mingw linkers took the machine to **zero available memory**, the harness's watchdog killed three of this campaign's background suites, and `CueTest.GetVariableAfterSetReturnsLocalOverride` — three lines of in-memory arithmetic that **cannot** fail non-deterministically — was reported as Failed and then passed 3/3 in isolation. `ctest` reports a killed process exactly as it reports an assertion failure, and a whole-scene sample test is a fat target for a memory watchdog. That is a coincidence of timing plus a known mechanism, not a proof, and it is written that way. **What this leaves behind, which is the useful part:** a baseline. §7.5c names two order-dependent flakes on this renderer (`Vulkan_FillMode_WireFrame`, `Vulkan_RenderTargetUsage`, tracked since Task 279); this sample scene is **not** a third on the evidence available, and a future sighting now has ten clean runs to be measured against rather than a shrug. **No production change, no test added.** |
| VULKAN-205 | The skinned vertex-colour variants tint the specular highlight, where XNA's own `Vc` effects do not (F-39) | ✅ | **Found by needing an ordering for `VULKAN-200` and discovering the neighbouring implementation had the opposite one; closed on a measurement, and the measurement then overturned a shared test rather than the renderer.** **Two shaders, and the defect differs between them.** `skinned3d_vertexlit_color` applied `vc.rgb` in the fragment stage **after** the specular add and after `VULKAN-188`'s vertex clamp — both halves wrong. `skinned3d_color` is per-pixel and has no vertex clamp to be after, so only the highlight was. **The rule was measured, because XNA has no `SkinnedEffect` `Vc` variant to measure directly:** `spikes/xna-vertex-color-specular-spike/` puts the real runtime's `BasicEffect` in a scene whose diffuse term is exactly zero — ambient 0, every light's diffuse 0, emissive 0 — so that every lit pixel *is* the highlight, and reads **(249,249,249)** for a white vertex colour and **(249,249,249)** for a 20 % grey one. **Fixed** by moving the colour into the vertex stage inside the clamp for the vertex-lit variant (matching what `VULKAN-200` wrote for `BasicEffect`) and folding it into the diffuse ahead of the specular for the per-pixel one, dropping the trailing `outColor.rgb *= vc.rgb` from both. **Test:** two legs added to `Vulkan_SkinnedEffect_VertexColor`, whose scene could not previously see either defect — it sets `SpecularColor` to zero on purpose and keeps the lit sum below 1. **7/10 → 10/10**, and the before-state is the mutation: the highlight read **(44,44,44)** against a white-colour **(219,219,219)** — exactly 0.2 × — and the clamp leg read **(128,128,128)** where XNA's arithmetic gives **(230,230,230)**. Both legs are needed: either passes while the other is broken. **Then the fix broke a sibling, and that is the part worth reading.** `Vulkan_SkinnedEffect_VertexColor_Reused` — EasyGL's own source — failed with **(18,17,15)** where it demands `(0,0,0)` for a *black* vertex colour. Adjusting the test to match the new behaviour would have been quick and wrong, so the probe gained a black leg instead: **black reads (249,249,249) too**. A black vertex colour zeroes the diffuse and leaves the highlight, because `AddSpecular` adds `Specular * color.a` after the colour is folded in and a black colour still carries alpha 1. **The test's claim — *"regardless of the lighting math"* — is true of the diffuse and false of the specular**, and it held only while a renderer multiplied the whole fragment. This is `VULKAN-091`'s precedent (*"the test's depth convention was OpenGL's, and the renderer was right"*), not `VULKAN-057`'s. The leg now switches specular off for that quad alone, so it asserts the half that is true on every renderer and in XNA; quad A is untouched and **both goldens still match at max channel diff 0**, so no image moved. **What is left behind, named rather than implied:** EasyGL still multiplies the whole fragment, so the CNA-wide divergence survives there — F-40, with the probe as its oracle, and not this campaign's to close. **The corrected file is registered by exactly two renderers — EasyGL and Vulkan.** At the time of the commit only the Vulkan side had been run, and the row said so: narrowing an assertion cannot turn a pass into a failure, *but that is an argument and not a measurement*. **Measured afterwards, same day:** `cmake-build-easygl/` turned out to be idle — no process holding it, nothing written in two hours — so the caution this campaign had been carrying about that directory was re-checked rather than inherited again. `EasyGL_SkinnedEffect_VertexColor` builds and runs there: **4/4, both goldens at max channel diff 0**. The argument is now a measurement on both renderers that register the file. **Classification:** XNA-fidelity by analogy on a CNA extension, renderer-local; **not** a parity gap, since EasyGL's skinned program carries no colour input of its own. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **363/363** on llvmpipe and **363/363** on RADV; full `ctest` in two chunks covering all **9222** tests, **17** failures — §7.5c's standing **twelve** plus the **five** audio-timing cases this machine produces under `-j3`, all of which predate this campaign. **No `Vulkan_*` failure.**. |
| VULKAN-168 | A `ShaderEffect` driving an INSTANCED 3D draw | ✅ | **Implemented, and the numbering question the row said was unfixed turned out to be settled already — by EasyGL, in the one test this row is the counterpart of.** `easygl_instancedmodel_shader_test.cpp` states it outright: a per-instance stream's *"attributes continue at the GLSL locations immediately after the mesh declaration"*. So there was nothing to invent; the convention is one rule across both renderers, and this renderer now follows it — per-vertex elements at locations 0..N-1 from binding 0, per-instance elements at N.. from binding 1 at `VK_VERTEX_INPUT_RATE_INSTANCE`. **What changed:** the custom pipeline declares **two** bindings instead of one, and the instance stride and its attribute hash join the pipeline key, so two effects differing only in their instance layout cannot share a pipeline. The record path needed nothing new — setting `useInstanced` alongside `useCustomEffect` reuses the frame instance buffer, its binding and `vkCmdDrawIndexed`'s instance count, all of which are family-agnostic. **`InstanceFrequency` is expanded on the CPU**, exactly as the stock instanced route does and for the same reason: Vulkan 1.1 with `VK_KHR_swapchain` as its only device extension has no vertex-attribute-divisor feature, so binding 1 keeps the implicit divisor of 1 and the grouping is a data-copy concern. **Two refusals kept**, because a custom shader's inputs are not enumerable: a per-instance buffer with no `VertexDeclaration`, and one whose element format has no `VkFormat`. And the range check that the last record the draw needs is inside the buffer. **Test:** `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_3D` leg E turns from a refusal into an acceptance in this commit, as the row required — two instances, two different per-instance records, two different halves of the target, **6/6 on llvmpipe and on RADV**. **Mutation, two:** binding 1 at `VK_VERTEX_INPUT_RATE_VERTEX` gives `(34,136)` and `(8,34)` — the per-instance record interpolated across the mesh, which is EasyGL's own documented mutation for its test; starting the instance locations at 0 instead of after the mesh declaration gives the clear colour and two validation messages. **One thing found while writing the test, and recorded rather than smoothed over:** the leg first used the five-element mesh from legs A–C, and the layer warned four times that *"Vertex attribute at location N not consumed by vertex shader"* — because a declaration-driven layout declares every element the buffer has, and only SPIR-V reflection, which this renderer deliberately does not do, could know which of them the shader reads. That is legal Vulkan and a warning rather than an error, but it fails `VULKAN-408`'s output gate, so the leg uses a position-only mesh and the interaction is stated here for the next reader. |
| VULKAN-169 | Let `SaveAsPng`/`SaveAsJpeg` save a texture whose pixels are only on the GPU (F-32) | ✅ | **Fixed in the shared layer, where the defect is, and both of the row's open questions were answered by measurement rather than by choosing.** All four save routines (`SaveAsPng` and `SaveAsJpeg`, stream and filename) now take their pixels from one helper, `gatherPixelsForEncode`, which follows **exactly the precedence `GetData` already uses**: the renderer's readback for a render target, the CPU shadow otherwise. That precedence is not a preference, it is the rule `GetData` states beside its own copy of it — a shadow left by a temporary `SetData` staging buffer is not authoritative once something has rendered into the target. **No renderer can regress**, and that is structural rather than hopeful: the readback arm runs only when `gpuOnlyContent_` is true, and a renderer whose `GetData` returns false falls through to precisely the old code. The only reachable change is a throw becoming a save. **Question 1, caching:** the readback is deliberately **not** written into `cpuPixels_`. It would make a second save cheaper and make it stale the moment anything rendered into the target again — the exact defect the precedence exists to prevent. **Question 2, a texture that is neither uploaded nor drawn into:** measured, not decided. A fresh `Texture2D` already carries a zero-filled shadow, so it saves transparent black, which is what XNA's zero-initialised texture holds; leg D asserts it so that a constructor that stopped allocating that shadow would be caught here rather than in a game's screenshot. **Test:** `VULKAN-178`'s probe becomes the assertion it was written to make room for — moved to `modules/graphics/examples/` and registered on **both** renderers, because a one-renderer result could not tell a shared fix from a Vulkan-shaped one. `Vulkan_Texture2D_SaveAsRenderTarget` and `EasyGL_Texture2D_SaveAsRenderTarget`, **5/5 each**: the target saves, the PNG decodes back to the colour that was drawn, saving after `GetData` gives the same answer, and the fresh texture still saves zeros. **Mutation:** with the readback arm disabled both render-target legs fail with the original *"no CPU-side pixel data available"*. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **345/345** on llvmpipe; full `ctest` **9190/9206**, which was mandatory here — `Texture2D` is on the path of every test in the suite that touches a texture, which is most of them. Its 16 failures are the 12 standing ones plus the four audio-timing cases, and one fewer than the previous full run. |
| VULKAN-185 | A golden CTest must report how much of its tolerance it used | ✅ | **Implemented, and it found something on its first run.** `CompareGoldenImage` now tracks the maximum per-channel difference over the whole region and prints it on the **`[PASS]` line** — `(max channel diff N of M allowed)` — not behind an environment variable, for the reason this plan applies to every other gate: a boundary that does not print its measurement is a claim rather than a measurement. **The second decision the row named — whether to lower a tolerance in the same commit — is `no`**, deliberately: this row makes drift *visible*, and re-tuning thirteen scenes on the strength of one run is how a documentation change becomes a flaky-test change. The numbers are recorded here instead, so the row that lowers one can cite them. **Measured, all thirteen on Vulkan:** `GoldenImage_Smoke` 0/0; `BasicEffect` 0/8; `DualTextureEffect` 0/8; `BlendState_Additive` 0/10; `TextureFilter_Linear` **1**/10; `AlphaTestEffect` 0/20; `PbrEffect` and `SkinnedPbrEffect` 0/35, 0/25, **1**/20, **1**/30; `RasterizerState_CullMode` 0/30; `SkinnedEffect` 0/40; `DepthStencilState_WriteEnable` 0/60; `SpriteBatch_Rotation` 0/60 — **and `EnvironmentMapEffect` 20 of 20**. Twelve of thirteen scenes are within one channel step of the golden and their tolerances are pure insurance; one is sitting exactly on its limit. **That one is finding F-33, and it is not a Vulkan defect:** EasyGL, which authored the PNG, measures `20 of 20` against it as well, so the image no longer describes what either renderer draws. `VULKAN-186` owns it. **Shared harness**, so both renderers were run: `^Vulkan_` **345/345** with the new output, and the EasyGL twins of two goldens rebuilt and run by hand (`0 of 8`, `20 of 20`). No mutation is recorded because there is nothing to mutate — the change adds a printed number and cannot alter a verdict; what would have hidden the defect is the code that was there before. |
| VULKAN-186 | The `EnvironmentMapEffect` golden no longer describes either renderer (F-33) | ⛔ | **Status corrected to ⛔ on 2026-09-08 by `VULKAN-237`.** Blocked on an **`OPENGL2` build**, for the reason this row diagnosed itself: re-capturing the shared golden from EasyGL would move the defect to a renderer this tree cannot run rather than remove it. Classification: `OTHER_RENDERER_BUILD_REQUIRED`. **Not a Vulkan renderer gap** — the scene passes on Vulkan *and* on EasyGL today; what is stale is a shared PNG, and step (1) of the ordered plan below is a defect in OpenGL2 owned by its own plan. **Opened by `VULKAN-185`'s first measurement.** The scene passes at `max channel diff 20 of 20` on Vulkan **and on EasyGL**, which authored the PNG — so the drift is shared, the golden is stale, and a single further channel step turns both gates red at once. **Do not simply re-capture it.** The question this row exists to answer is which of the two is right: the image, or what both renderers draw today. `VULKAN-260` changed this very effect on Vulkan (Fresnel per vertex, not per fragment) and `plans/plan_graphics.md` may have moved EasyGL's own env-map path since the capture; a re-capture without that history would freeze whatever is there now. **Intended:** locate what changed (the golden's own commit against the effect's), decide whether today's render is correct, then either fix the renderer or re-capture with `CNA_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1` **and lower the tolerance in the same commit** to whatever the corrected scene actually measures — a tolerance of 20 over a difference of 0 is what let this hide. **Cross-renderer**, since the PNG is shared: whatever lands must be measured on both. **Classification:** stale test fixture, shared. **Diagnosed 2026-09-07, and deliberately not fixed here — the reason is a third renderer.** **The cause is dated:** the PNG was captured on **2026-07-09** (`bfc0e3734`, Task 469); `dc33a6d58` (**2026-08-28**, `FX-123`/`FX-124`) changed EasyGL's built-in lit shaders to match D3D9's vertex-colour clamp and fragment precision, and `EnsureEnvMapped3DProgram` is one of the seven programs it touched. So the golden predates a deliberate *correctness* fix, and what both renderers draw today is the corrected picture — the image is what is wrong. **Why re-capturing is not a one-line job:** `opengl2_environmentmapeffect_golden_test.cpp` compares against the **same PNG** with the same tolerance of 20, and its own vertex shader still reads `vFresnel=(uFresnelEnabled>0.5)?pow(...)*uEnvMapAmount:uEnvMapAmount` where EasyGL's now reads `vFresnel=clamp(...)`. OpenGL2 therefore still renders the **pre-fix** way and still matches the **old** golden; re-capturing from EasyGL would leave that renderer judged against an image only its tolerance can reach — moving the defect rather than removing it. There is no OPENGL2 build directory in this tree and this campaign does not create one, so the check cannot be run here. **What this row now needs, in order:** (1) OpenGL2's env-map program gets the same clamp — that is a defect in OpenGL2, evidenced above, and belongs to its own plan; (2) then re-capture the golden with `CNA_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1` from EasyGL; (3) then lower the tolerance in all three sources to what the corrected scene measures, which on the two renderers built here is expected to be 0 or 1. Doing (2) before (1) is the trap this note exists to stop. |
| VULKAN-187 | Register this renderer's `adapterQueries` so `GraphicsAdapter` stops under-reporting (F-34) | ✅ | **Registered — one hook, and the reason only one is registered is the interesting half.** **The problem the hook has to solve** is that `GraphicsAdapter::QueryRenderTargetFormat` runs *before* any `GraphicsDevice` exists, so it reaches a renderer through a plain function pointer with no instance behind it. EasyGL answers with a static table, and says why in its own comment: an adapter query runs before there is a context whose extensions can be probed. Vulkan has the same problem and a better answer available: **the first `VulkanRenderer` constructed publishes its physical device's colour∩depth sample-count mask**, and the clamp answers from that. Before any device has existed it still returns 0 — unchanged, and honest, because creating a `VkInstance` just to answer a query is a much larger promise than this row is making. **Only `clampMultiSampleCount` is registered.** The format hooks are deliberately left null: leg B of `Vulkan_AdapterQueryContract` shows the shared table and this renderer's own verdict already agree that a `Bgr565` render target falls back to `Color`, and adding a second answer where the first is right is how the two come to disagree later. **Test:** that probe's leg C becomes an assertion in this commit, as the row required — `exact=true selectedMultiSampleCount=4` where it read `exact=false … 0` this morning. It asks for **4×** rather than each device's maximum on purpose, because llvmpipe offers 4× and RADV 8× and the leg has to mean the same thing on both; it passes on both. **Mutation:** with the `adapterQueries` line removed from the descriptor, leg C returns to `0`. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **348/348** on llvmpipe; the four affected tests re-run on RADV. |
| VULKAN-188 | Vulkan's two vertex-lit programs interpolate an UNCLAMPED colour (F-35) | ✅ | **Fixed, and the defect was proved by an existing test before a line changed.** `easygl_basiceffect_vertex_color_clamp_test.cpp` is renderer-agnostic — a `BasicEffect` scene, `DrawUserPrimitives`, a backbuffer read — so it is **registered** here as `Vulkan_BasicEffectVertexColorClamp` rather than rewritten, and on the unfixed renderer it reported exactly the number the EasyGL row predicted for the bug: **`got=(153,153,153)` where D3D9 gives `(102,102,102)`**. The scene is built so the two answers are computable by hand — left vertices lit 2.0, right vertices lit 1.0, a 0.4 grey texture, so the centre reads 0.4×1.0 clamped and 0.4×1.5 unclamped — which is why a single pixel is enough. **The fix is six lines in three shaders**, not two: `lit_textured3d_vertexlit.vert.glsl`, `skinned3d_vertexlit.vert.glsl` and its `_color` sibling each write **two** per-vertex colour outputs, the lit RGB and the specular RGB, and D3D9 saturates `oD0` *and* `oD1`. Clamping only the one the test measures would have left the specular half of the same defect in place. `spirv_shaders.hpp` regenerated with `compile_shaders.py`. **The mutation is the before-state**, recorded above rather than manufactured: the test failed with the unclamped midpoint and passes with the clamped one. **Nothing else moved**, which was the risk worth checking: a clamp changes every per-vertex-lit draw in the suite, including the golden scenes — `^Vulkan_` **349/349** on llvmpipe and **348/349** on RADV, goldens included, because those scenes never drive the light sum past 1. The single RADV failure is not a pixel: `Vulkan_GoldenImage_Smoke` died with *"CNA::Platform: VULKAN does not support VulkanSurface"* — a surface-creation failure on the scaffolding display under `-j3` — and passes **3/3** run alone, `max channel diff 0 of 0`. That message is **not** matched by `VULKAN-013`'s skip guard, which is `VULKAN-193`. **Found by `VULKAN-487`'s re-audit**, which is the argument for running that gate rather than tabulating it. |
