That is the acceptance this row was written with, met without touching a fixture. **Mutation-checked on BOTH branches of the condition, because a conditional feature has two:** (1) mapping `Bgra4444` to the core-1.0 near-twin `VK_FORMAT_B4G4R4A4_UNORM_PACK16` — which allocates and uploads perfectly happily — turns leg C red with `expected (255,0,0) got (0,255,255)` and `expected (0,255,0) got (255,0,255)`, the exact channel rotation the two layouts differ by, while legs A and B stay green; (2) forcing `formatA4R4G4B4Supported_ = false` after the probe — a device without the extension — turns leg C back into the **refusal** assertion, still 3/3, and `UnsupportedFormatConstructionTest.EverySurfaceFormatEitherWorksOrThrowsClearly` follows it. So neither branch is untested and neither can silently become the other. **One line in the shared sweep**: `Bgra4444` is the single entry in `EverySurfaceFormatEitherWorksOrThrowsClearly` derived from the renderer rather than named, because its promotion is a device fact — naming it would be wrong on every device without the extension, omitting it wrong on every device with one. Everything else there stays named, so the sweep does not become a tautology against `Texture2D`'s own gate. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_Packed16Format` **3/3 with all three legs now pixel-verified**; `Vulkan_SurfaceFormatClassification` **39/39 → 42/42**, verdicts **6 Supported, 0 Unsupported, 21 Defer**; `^Vulkan_` **258/258**. Full `ctest` in §7.5. **Dependencies:** `VULKAN-170` ✅, `VULKAN-173` ✅.

---

## 18. Phase 6 — Render targets (`VULKAN-210`–`VULKAN-249`)

Vulkan is the **stronger** renderer in most of this area (§10.6). The tasks here close the few
EasyGL-only rows and audit the parts neither renderer proves.

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-210 | Prove the bound-MSAA-target alpha case on Vulkan | ✅ | **Not a Vulkan requirement as written — and the attempt to register it found a real defect, which is now `VULKAN-216`.** **Why the source cannot run here:** `easygl_gfx164_bound_msaa_alpha_test.cpp` resolves `glGenFramebuffers`, `glBindFramebuffer`, `glFramebufferTexture2D` and `glGetFramebufferAttachmentParameteriv` through `SDL_GL_GetProcAddress` and asserts *“OpenGL attachment diagnostics are available”* and *“complete native GL error drain is clean”*. Those are assertions about an OpenGL context, not about the GFX164 contract. §28 point 3 allows a `TEST_GAP` to close on a written justification that it is not a Vulkan requirement; this is one. **The GFX164 contract itself is covered here** by `Vulkan_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve`, `_RenderTargetCube_MsaaResolve`, `_MsaaFace`, `_MRT_MsaaResolve`, `_MsaaFirstReadback` and `_MsaaDepthContract` — six tests against EasyGL's one, which is why §10.6 already reads `VULKAN_STRONGER` for this area. **What the attempt found, and it is the reason this row was worth attempting rather than reading:** the first failing leg is `creation: requested 4x selects supported multisample storage -- applied=0`. A `RenderTarget2D` asking for **4x** on a device whose own back buffer is not multisampled gets **0**, because `VulkanRenderTargetRenderer`'s constructor computes `wantsMsaa = requestedMultiSampleCount > 0 && owner_->sampleCount_ > VK_SAMPLE_COUNT_1_BIT` — the target's sample count is tied to the **device's**. In XNA a render target's `MultiSampleCount` is its own. **`VULKAN-216`** owns that.
| VULKAN-211 | Prove `RenderTargetCube` depth-format fidelity on Vulkan | ✅ | `easygl_rendertargetcube_depthformat_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_RenderTargetCube_DepthFormat`. `Vulkan_RenderTarget2D_DepthFormatFidelity` covers the 2D half; `CreateRenderTargetCube` takes the same `depthFormat` parameter, and `VULKAN-215` found this class lagging its 2D twin for the third time on exactly this kind of question. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-212 | Prove the plain two-attachment MRT case on Vulkan | ✅ | **Not a Vulkan requirement as written, and the reason is the one `VULKAN-256` already records.** `easygl_mrt_test.cpp` is not a plain two-attachment draw: it builds two **`ShaderEffect`s from GLSL source** — the first failing leg is *“four-output ESSL 3.00 MRT effect compiles”* — and one of them uses `gl_SampleMask`, an ESSL 3.20 core output. This renderer's custom-effect intake takes **SPIR-V words** and refuses source by name (`VULKAN-250`), so the test cannot run here for the same reason the 37 GLSL sample-shader ports cannot: the dialect, which `plans/plan_csl.md` owns. §28 point 3's written justification applies. **The contract the row wanted is covered**, and by more than EasyGL has: `Vulkan_MRT_MixedFormats` (two attachments, different formats), `Vulkan_MRT_MsaaResolve` (four slots and a partial sample mask), `Vulkan_CubeMrtBinding` and `Vulkan_RenderTargetCube_PluralMRT`. §10.6 already reads `VULKAN_STRONGER` for MRT on that basis. **What is genuinely missing is a SPIR-V MRT effect**, which is `VULKAN-255`'s territory (a custom effect driving a draw with its own layout), not a second registration of a GLSL one.
| VULKAN-213 | Audit render-target lifetime across deferred work end to end | ✅ | **All three shapes the row named are now covered, and none of them found a hole.** New `Vulkan_RenderTargetLifetimeShapes` (`modules/renderers/vulkan/examples/vulkan_rendertarget_lifetime_shapes_test.cpp`), **5/5**, alongside the four tests the row credits. **The shapes:** (A) a target drawn into and then **disposed** with its pass still queued -- no readback and no `Present` in between, so the pass executes after the wrapper is gone; (B) a target written and then **sampled** in the same frame, disposed immediately after the draw that sampled it, so the source dies between its use and its execution; (C) a `RenderTargetCube` disposed with three faces rendered, three never touched, and all of that work pending. **How each is judged, which is the part worth stating:** none of the three can be judged by a pixel -- the target is gone, so there is nothing to read. What they are judged by is that the frame completes, the **next** frame renders correctly, and no validation message appears. A witness target that is filled and read back after each shape is what turns "did not crash" into "still works", and it doubles as the flush that forces the disposed target's queued pass to actually execute. Leg D repeats the probe in the frame **after** all three, because that is when the queued work has really run; leg E reads the message count in-process, so a message is localized to this test rather than to the whole binary, and says explicitly that the `vkDestroyDevice` half belongs to the `VULKAN-408` gate. **Mutation-tested, and the first attempt was a no-op worth recording:** freeing the views collected by `EvictSampledViewFromCaches` immediately changes nothing, because at that point in `ReleaseVulkanResources` the view list is still empty -- the target's own views are appended below it. Moving the mutation after the collection, so every framebuffer and view is destroyed at once instead of behind the frame fence, **segfaults** the test (exit 139) after `[Vulkan Validation] vkCmdBeginRenderPass(): pRenderPassBegin->framebuffer Invalid VkFramebuffer Object`. So the test does guard REMED-GFX-166's rule for these three shapes, and the retirement is load-bearing for all of them. `VulkanRenderer.cpp` restored bit-for-bit. **No remediation row opened**, per §26 rule 3: no hole was found, and inventing one would be worse than recording that the existing lifetime design already covers these arrangements. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **337/337**, no validation output. No production change. |
| VULKAN-214 | Audit `RenderTargetUsage::PreserveContents` across a swapchain recreation | ✅ | **Tested, and the row's stated premise is corrected rather than assumed.** New renderer-agnostic `modules/graphics/examples/rendertarget_preserve_across_resize_test.cpp` → **`Vulkan_RenderTargetPreserveAcrossResize`** (**4/4**) and **`EasyGL_RenderTargetPreserveAcrossResize`** (**3/3**), the fourth leg being Vulkan-only. **Premise corrected: “a resize destroys and rebuilds render passes” is not what happens.** `RecreateSwapchain` destroys the swapchain, its image views, its framebuffers and the backbuffer depth resources; `renderPass_`/`renderPassMsaa_` are explicitly permanent (`CleanupSwapchain`'s own comment says so) and the four render-TARGET pass caches are not touched at all. So a live `PreserveContents` target's framebuffer keeps pointing at a pass that is still there, which is why this passes. The row was right that it was untested and right to ask; it was wrong about the mechanism, and a later reader deciding what to tear down on a resize needs the accurate version. **Three legs plus a guard against a vacuous pass.** A: before any resize, the preserved target holds the first draw -- the control, without which B measures `PreserveContents` itself rather than the resize. B: after the resize, rebinding the SAME target and drawing into the OTHER half leaves the first half's texels intact. C: a `Texture2D` created before the resize still reads back its own pixels, which separates "render targets specifically" from "every resource". D (Vulkan only): `GetSwapchainRecreateCountEXT()` really went up -- **3 → 5** -- because A–C would all pass on a build where the resize quietly did nothing, and that is the one way this test could lie. **Driven through `GraphicsDeviceManager::ApplyChanges()` rather than by resizing the native window**, for two reasons and the second decided it: the GDM path is synchronous, so there is no compositor-throttling deadline of the kind `easygl_real_window_resize_test.cpp` has to carry; and `cmake/PlatformRatchet.cmake` **refused the first version at configure time** for naming the windowing library in a `modules/graphics/` example -- correctly, and the refusal is recorded here because it is a real constraint on how tests in this directory may be written, including in its comments (the audit is a text scan, not a compile-time one). **Mutation-tested against the row's own hypothesis:** making `RecreateSwapchain` destroy the two RT render-pass caches -- the thing the row assumed it already did -- reproduces exactly the failure the row feared: `[Vulkan Validation] vkCmdBeginRenderPass(): pRenderPassBegin->renderPass Invalid VkRenderPass Object`, with the process stopping after leg A. So the test would have caught it, and the `VULKAN-408` gate would have caught it twice. `VulkanRenderer.cpp` restored bit-for-bit. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **335/335**, no validation output; both new tests green as CTests. No production change. |
| VULKAN-215 | `RenderTargetCube.DepthStencilFormat` reports the request, and no interface lets it report anything else | ✅ | **Fixed at all three levels, and the interface change is additive so no other renderer is touched.** **Observed:** `RenderTarget2D` asked the renderer and stored the answer (`RenderTarget2D.cpp:105`-`111`); `RenderTargetCube` stored the raw constructor argument (`RenderTargetCube.cpp:64`) and asked nobody, because `IRenderTargetCubeRenderer` declared no applied-format virtual at all. An override written for the Vulkan cube during `VULKAN-348` failed to compile for exactly that reason. **Done:** `IRenderTargetCubeRenderer` gains `GetAppliedDepthStencilFormatEXT` with the **same identity default** its 2D twin has — every renderer that does not substitute keeps today's behaviour without a line changing — `VulkanRenderTargetCubeRenderer` overrides it from the cube's own `depthVkFormat_`, and `RenderTargetCube`'s constructor asks it, one line below where it already asks for the multisample count. **The third time this class has lagged its 2D twin the same way**, which is recorded in the code: `MOD-107` was the surface format, the multisample count was the second. **The test could not be written the obvious way, and finding that out is the substance of this row.** The first draft asked for `Depth16` and its **control leg failed**: `PickDepthFormat` honours `Depth16` verbatim wherever `D16_UNORM` is supported, so nothing was substituted and the cube leg would have passed by comparing a request with itself. `Depth24` failed the control too — llvmpipe supports `X8_D24_UNORM_PACK32` as well. **On this device no request is substituted at all**, so no reachable case existed in which the report could differ. New `SetDepthFormatPreferredUnsupportedForTestEXT` forces the fallback, the same way `SetSwapchainOutOfDateForTestEXT` forces a state the window manager owns; it affects render targets only, since the back buffer's `FindDepthFormat()` never offers those two formats. **Without the control leg both earlier drafts would have been reported green and written up as verified.** **Test:** `Vulkan_AppliedFormats` grows 3/3 → **5/5**. Leg D is the control — `RenderTarget2D` must show the substitution, proving the environment really substitutes — and leg E asserts the cube reports what its 2D twin does. **Mutation-checked:** with the cube's ask removed, E fails as `cube 2, flat 3` while D still passes, so the failure names the cube rather than render targets in general. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **250/250**, full `ctest` **9088/9107** with **19** failures — none new, none in `Vulkan_*`, and the two load-sensitive cases from the previous row did not recur. The full suite is mandatory here: the change adds a virtual to a shared renderer interface and alters a shared XNA class. |
| VULKAN-216 | A `RenderTarget2D`'s MultiSampleCount is tied to the device's, not its own | ✅ | **Fixed: a render target's sample count is now its own, on `RenderTarget2D` and `RenderTargetCube` alike, and the pixel test proves the samples are real rather than reported.** New `Vulkan_RenderTargetOwnMsaa` (`modules/renderers/vulkan/examples/vulkan_rendertarget_own_msaa_test.cpp`), **6/6**, on a device created WITHOUT multisampling and deliberately never touching `GraphicsDevice::RecreateRendererForMultiSampleCount()` — the scaffolding hook `vulkan_rendertarget2d_msaa_test.cpp` needs precisely because of the behaviour this row removes. **What changed.** Both constructors now call `PickSampleCount(physicalDevice_, requestedMultiSampleCount)` for themselves instead of gating on `owner_->sampleCount_ > 1`, and both draw-time pipeline selections (`useMsaaPipe` for sprites, `drawMsaa` for 3D) lost their `&& sampleCount_ > 1` half — that half was the same decision seen from the other side, and it was already capable of binding a single-sampled pipeline inside a multisampled render pass. **What it cost, which is the part the row predicted and the part worth reading:** the renderer no longer has ONE MSAA sample count, so (a) the two MSAA render-pass caches are keyed by **(depth format, sample count)** rather than by depth format alone — render-pass compatibility requires attachments to agree on sample count, so a 4x and a 2x target at the same depth format genuinely need two passes; (b) `PipelineKey` gained an `ms` field; (c) `pipelineSampleCount_` records, once per `RecordCommandBuffer` segment, what `msaa == true` currently means, read through `MsaaSamplesForPipelinesEXT()` by all fourteen pipeline factories rather than threaded through fourteen signatures — it is a property of the pass, not of the draw; and (d) `VulkanEffectRenderer::GetOrCreatePipeline` gets an explicit `samplesOverride`, because it builds at `SpriteBatch::End()` time, before any segment exists. **A use-after-free removed rather than introduced:** `ApplyMultiSampleCount` used to destroy both RT MSAA pass caches on a device-wide count change. Those passes no longer bake `sampleCount_`, and every live multisampled target holds a framebuffer built against one — so the teardown is gone. It was unreachable before only because a target could not have MSAA unless the device did; per-instance counts break that coupling, so the coupling in the teardown had to go with it. **Three regressions found by the suite and each fixed at its cause, not worked around.** (1) `Vulkan_PipelineCacheBound` leg C went from +0 to **+30** pipelines: `SampleCountToInt(VK_SAMPLE_COUNT_1_BIT)` returns **0**, not 1 (its `default:` arm — its callers use 0 for XNA's "no MSAA"), and `NarrowSampleMaskEXT` reads `samples <= 0` as "do not narrow", so VULKAN-162's mask normalization silently switched off. The old code dodged it with a literal `msaa ? SampleCountToInt(...) : 1`; the fix names the rule instead — `RasterSampleCountEXT()` — and applies it at all fourteen sites. **The same trap was live one function away** in `VulkanEffectRenderer::GetOrCreatePipeline`, which passed `SampleCountToInt(sampleCount)` for a possibly-1x count; fixed with the same helper, since leaving a defect this row's own measurement exposed would be worse than the one-line change. (2) and (3) `Vulkan_RenderTargetCube_GetDataContract` and `_Usage` failed on their own declared contract — `msaaCubeTargets=false` no longer describes this renderer, so both shared contract blocks were corrected to `true` (and `msaaPreserves` to `true`, which is REMED-GFX-141's LOAD variant of the MSAA pass, and which the suite then confirmed rather than the row assuming). **Mutation-tested, and one mutation was NOT caught — recorded rather than hidden.** Restoring the `&& owner_->sampleCount_ > 1` gate fails legs A, B and D and leaves C and E passing, exactly as intended. But forcing `key.ms = 1` everywhere **passes 6/6**, and the reason is a real limit of this hardware, not of the test: the `msaa` bit already lives in `PipelineKey::a`, so the field only disambiguates two counts that are **both** greater than one, and llvmpipe here offers only 1x and 4x through `framebufferColorSampleCounts & framebufferDepthSampleCounts` — leg E measures that directly, a 2x request applying as 0. `ms` is kept because the Vulkan spec requires it on any device offering two or more multisample counts, and this note is here so that a later session does not "simplify" it away on the strength of a green suite. What would catch it: a device offering 2x and 4x, with two targets at those counts drawn in one frame. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **331/331**, full `ctest` re-run because two shared contract files changed. No `[Vulkan Validation]` output anywhere — which is itself load-bearing here, since a pipeline whose `rasterizationSamples` disagree with its render pass is exactly what the `VULKAN-408` gate reports. |

---

## 19. Phase 7 — Effects and shader-driven ordinary rendering (`VULKAN-250`–`VULKAN-299`)

Compiled `.fx` bytecode behaviour is **`plans/plan_fx.md`'s** (`FX-065`, `FX-110`, `FX-112`;
`CNA_VULKAN_COMPILED_EFFECTS`). Cross-dialect shader portability is **`plans/plan_csl.md`'s**. This
phase owns Vulkan's own stock-effect and `ShaderEffect` surface.

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-250 | Declare the shader dialect the Vulkan renderer actually requires | ✅ | **Declared: `ShaderDialectEXT::GlslVulkan`.** It is not device- or build-dependent -- this renderer has exactly one custom-effect intake and `CompileProgram` rejects anything that is not SPIR-V words. **And the review the row asked for concluded the enumerator is genuinely imprecise, so the coordination row is opened rather than waved at:** `IGL`'s Vulkan backend reports the **same** `GlslVulkan` and takes GLSL **source** (`IglEffectRenderer::CompileProgram` -> `igl::ShaderStagesCreator::fromModuleStringInput`, and `igl_spritebatch_shadereffect_test.cpp` gates on exactly this value before handing it a GLSL string), while this renderer takes compiled **bytecode**. An application that queried the dialect and acted on it would still have to guess which of the two to send -- which is the one thing this query exists to prevent. Narrowing it needs a new enumerator, a C-ABI change through `MapShaderDialect` (`modules/c-api/src/CnaCApiGraphics.cpp:144`), and that is **`VULKAN-264`**. **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_ShaderDialectContract` (`vulkan_shader_dialect_contract_test.cpp`), **4/4** -- the dialect is declared and is `GlslVulkan`; ordinary Vulkan-flavoured GLSL source of exactly the shape IGL compiles is **refused** here (`SPIR-V size must be a multiple of 4 bytes`) rather than accepted and drawn from nothing; and the ambiguity itself is asserted, so a future enumerator that resolves it turns this leg red and says so instead of leaving a stale comment behind. That a valid SPIR-V pair is accepted and renders is not re-proved -- `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_SpirV` owns that end to end with a real tinted draw. **Suite:** 219/220. |
| VULKAN-264 | Coordinate a shader-dialect enumerator that distinguishes source from bytecode (F-08's tail) | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-08 across the C++ renderer contract and the native C ABI.** `ShaderDialectEXT::SpirV` is appended after the six existing source dialects, so ordinals 0–6 do not move. Native CNA Vulkan reports it; IGL's Vulkan backend retains `GlslVulkan`, which is correct because IGL compiles source through `fromModuleStringInput`. The C ABI appends `CNA_SHADER_DIALECT_SPIRV = 7`, advances `CNA_SHADER_DIALECT_MAXIMUM`, and bumps the experimental ABI from 0.24.0 to 0.25.0 with release notes and an updated measured-header baseline. `MapShaderDialect` remains exhaustive with no `default`. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_ShaderDialectContract` **5/5** asserts the resolved distinction and still proves GLSL refusal by SPIR-V content rather than byte length; `Vulkan_CapabilitySnapshot` **10/10** asserts `SpirV`, source execution false and every source-dialect profile row unsupported; C and C++ ABI header targets compile with strict warnings; `CnaCApiGraphics.cpp` and the Vulkan-aware `EffectSmoke.c` each compile as their exact CMake object target. The header-only ABI gate is current at 222 structs, 350 scalar types and 1,569 constants. A full C-API link was deliberately not used as evidence: enabling the shared library makes the whole dependency closure PIC and requested 673 rebuild steps; it was stopped after 36 and replaced with the two exact object targets. |
| VULKAN-265 | Refuse the four array uniform setters until `VULKAN-252` implements them | ✅ | **Scope, stated precisely because the first draft of this row got it wrong:** `VULKAN-252` has owned *implementing* the array setters since the planning session, and this row does not do that. What `VULKAN-027`'s contract table found is what `VULKAN-252` leaves untouched while it waits — the calls are **silently accepted**, so a caller cannot tell an unimplemented path from a working one. This row closes that interim, and is a predecessor of `VULKAN-252` rather than a duplicate. **Observed:** `IEffectRenderer` declares four array setters whose bodies are `{}` (`IGraphicsRenderer.hpp:931`–`947`). `VulkanEffectRenderer` overrode the six scalar/vector setters (`VulkanRenderer.hpp:373`–`378`) and none of the four, presenting itself as a working effect renderer and then discarding every array upload without a word. **Classification:** implementation bug — a silent no-op on a reachable public call, the same class as F-31. **Reachability, checked rather than assumed:** `ShaderEffect` lives in `modules/graphics`, the XNA-public module; its array setters carry the `CNAEXT` marker but no `CNA_CNAEXT` build guard (`ShaderEffect.hpp:95`–`128`, forwarding at `ShaderEffect.cpp:77`), so the call compiles and runs in every build. **EasyGL evidence:** overrides all four (`EasyGLRenderer.hpp:516`–`522`) and uploads them. **Vulkan evidence:** the `ShaderEffect` payload is a fixed 128-byte push-constant block with fixed slots — one `mat4`, one `vec4`, eight floats (`VulkanRenderer.hpp:450`–`452`) — and no shader reflection, so an array of arbitrary length has nowhere to go. That is why the setters were never written, and it is the same reason `VULKAN-252` says a uniform buffer is required. **Intended behaviour, by §5's authority order:** XNA has no opinion — these are CNA extensions — so the governing rule is this project's own, that unsupported behaviour refuses explicitly rather than pretends (`CLAUDE.md`, Platform Boundary). **Done:** new `RefuseEffectUniformArrayEXT(setter, name, count)` (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:4074`), deliberately separate from `RefuseEffectTextureBindEXT` because the limit it reports is a different one, and four one-line overrides that call it. **Test, and why it discriminates:** `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_SpirV` gains four legs asserting the throw **plus the control that was already there** — the red centre pixel proves the same `fx` still reaches the shader through `SetUniformVec4`, so four throws cannot be explained by an effect that is broken end to end. **Mutation-checked:** restoring the silent no-op on `SetUniformMat4Array` alone turns the test red with `SetUniformMat4Array was accepted and silently ignored` while the control still reports `centre=(255,0,0) bg=(0,255,0)` — so the leg fails for the defect it claims, and for that defect only. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_SpirV` still passes, `^Vulkan_` **238/238**. **Consequence handed to a new row rather than assumed away:** three CNAEXT passes call these setters, so with `CNA_CNAEXT=ON` they now throw where they previously fell silent. That build does not exist here, so it was **not** verified — `VULKAN-266` owns it. **Explicit non-goal:** the uniform-buffer implementation, which stays with `VULKAN-252`. **Superseded 2026-09-07 (`VULKAN-252`):** the refusal this row installed is gone, replaced by the implementation it was holding the place for. What survives is the rule — a setter never falls silent — and the two refusals that are still real, a count past the block's 72 elements and a null array with a non-zero count. |
| VULKAN-266 | Determine whether the three CNAEXT passes can reach the now-refusing array setters on Vulkan | ✅ | **Closed by runtime measurement on 2026-09-08; no production change was needed.** The existing Vulkan build was temporarily configured with `CNA_CNAEXT=ON`, and the three exact registered examples named by this row were built and run under Xvfb on llvmpipe. `CNAEXT_ShadowMap` initialised Vulkan and exited at its documented 3D/shader/shadow-support gate; `CNAEXT_ClusteredLights` refused its GLSL payload as non-SPIR-V and exited at `ExecutesShaderEffectSourceEXT`; `CNAEXT_Ssao` ran its 4096-texel disabled-pipeline identity control with **0 differing texels**, then refused the depth-normal/SSAO GLSL payloads and exited at its shader-support gate. All three returned CTest's intentional skip code rather than throwing, crashing or silently running a broken pass. The array setters therefore remain unreachable through these source-shader CNAEXT passes on native Vulkan, while direct compiled-SPIR-V array delivery remains covered by `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_UniformArrays` (`VULKAN-252`). **Exact validation:** `xvfb-run -a ctest --test-dir cmake-build-vulkan -R '^(CNAEXT_Ssao|CNAEXT_ShadowMap|CNAEXT_ClusteredLights)$' -V -j1` — 3/3 safe capability skips, zero failures. **Historical reason this row existed:** `VULKAN-265` replaced silent array-setter no-ops with explicit refusal before `VULKAN-252` implemented arrays; source reading could not prove whether these three passes reached the calls. The runtime configuration now supplies that missing evidence. **Non-goal:** adding GLSL-source compilation to native Vulkan; its public contract is compiled SPIR-V (`VULKAN-264`). |
| VULKAN-251 | Prove `ShaderEffect` uniform delivery through `SpriteBatch` on Vulkan | ✅ | **Done with **no new SPIR-V**, which is a simplification found by reading rather than a shortcut — and the row's real requirement turned out to be sharper than "author the twin".** **What the row asked for:** that the drawn colour be a *function* of the uniform, so a dropped uniform fails. **What was already there:** `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_SpirV` set `uColor` **once**, to red, and asserted the centre was red. That does catch a uniform dropped *entirely* — the shader would draw the untinted white texture and `G <= 50` would fail — but it cannot catch a value delivered to the wrong push-constant slot, or with only one channel arriving, because "reddish" satisfies it either way. One value is not a function. **Done:** two batches with two distinct values into two regions — red on the left, blue on the right — and both are asserted. The existing tint shader already reads `uColor`, so the "author the SPIR-V twin" step the row anticipated was unnecessary; recording that is more useful than quietly skipping it. **Checked before relying on it:** two batches in one frame do not collapse onto the last value, because `End()` copies the effect's push constants into the batch's snapshot (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:1355`) — the same snapshot-at-submission discipline `VULKAN-132` measured for vertex buffers. Had that not been so, this test would have been measuring a defect instead of the uniform. **Mutation-checked:** making `SetUniformVec4` write a constant tint regardless of its arguments turns the right-hand region red and fails the leg — **and would have passed the single-value check completely**, which is the clearest statement of what this row added. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **253/253**. Full `ctest` not re-run: the change is confined to one test source. |
| VULKAN-252 | Implement the array uniform setters on `VulkanEffectRenderer` | ✅ | **Implemented as four uniform-buffer ranges in descriptor set 1, and the row's own warning was the first thing that had to be obeyed: the refusal came out in the same commit as the feature.** **The design decision, and it is the row.** Set 1 gains **four** bindings, one per element type — 12 `float`, 13 `vec2`, 14 `vec3`, 15 `mat4` — rather than one block holding all four. With a single block a shader wanting only a bone palette would have had to declare the three arrays it does not use, in the right order, or silently read the wrong offset; with four, it declares `layout(set = 1, binding = 15) uniform Mat4Array { mat4 uBones[72]; };` and reads the right bytes. They are four **ranges of one buffer**, so the split costs descriptors, not allocations. **72 elements each**, which is XNA's own `SkinnedEffect.MaxBones`, so the array a custom effect is most likely to want fits exactly; the whole block is 8448 bytes against the 16384 every Vulkan device must allow, and the device's actual `maxUniformBufferRange` is checked once rather than discovered as a validation error. **Visible to both stages**, because a bone palette is read in the vertex shader — fragment-only would have made the headline use case impossible. **Three lessons this renderer had already paid for, applied rather than re-learned.** (1) The buffer is built at `SpriteBatch::End()` and captured by value into the batch snapshot, beside the bound textures and the push constants, so a later write cannot change what an already recorded batch reads. (2) A new buffer is allocated only when the **contents** changed, and the old one is retired on the same fence as the descriptor set that names it — the set is the only thing that still references it. (3) The bindings are written **unconditionally**, with zeros for an effect that never set an array, because `VULKAN-253` measured what an unwritten binding in a statically used set does. **Refusals kept where they are real:** a count past 72 and a null array with a non-zero count are refused **by name**; only the push-constant refusal is gone. **Test:** new `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_UniformArrays`, **5/5 on llvmpipe and on RADV**. Its two shaders are built so a plumbing mistake cannot look like success — the vector one takes one channel from each of the three bindings at a **different element index** in each (1, 2, 3), and the matrix one reads element 0 column 0 row 0, element 1 column 1 row 1, element 2 column 2 row 2, so a transposed matrix, a wrong element stride and a wrong element index each fail independently. **Mutation, three, each predicted first:** packing the vector elements tightly instead of padding them to std140's 16 bytes fails leg C only (`(0,0,0)`); never refreshing the buffer fails leg B only, which is exactly the stale-descriptor shape `VULKAN-164` had to fix for the sampler; giving every binding the same whole-buffer range fails A, B and C and leaves D and E green. **One existing test changed, and the row said it would have to:** `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_SpirV`'s four refusal legs now assert the opposite — each setter is accepted, and one past the capacity is still refused by name — because leaving them would have failed the feature this row adds. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **341/341** on llvmpipe and on RADV. Full `ctest` **not re-run**: every changed line is inside `VulkanEffectRenderer` and its two examples, so nothing outside this renderer's own set can move. **`VULKAN-266`'s premise moves with this row** — the three CNAEXT passes it is about no longer meet a throw, provided their arrays fit in 72 elements — and it is annotated rather than closed, because that is its own verification to do. |
| VULKAN-253 | Implement `IEffectRenderer::BindTexture` on `VulkanEffectRenderer` | ✅ | **Implemented: a custom effect can now sample a texture it was explicitly bound, and the pixels prove it.** New `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_BoundTexture`, **4/4 on llvmpipe and on RADV**. **The design decision, and it is the whole row:** bound textures live in **descriptor set 1**; the `SpriteBatch` texture stays at **set 0**. Set 0 changes per sprite and set 1 is fixed for the batch, so one shared set would have meant rebuilding a descriptor set for every sprite in the batch. A shader reaches a bound texture as `layout(set = 1, binding = <unit>) uniform sampler2D`, and every existing SPIR-V shader keeps working because set 0 is untouched. That numbering differs from EasyGL's, where unit 0 IS the sprite texture -- `ShaderEffect` takes renderer-specific source by contract (`VULKAN-250`), so the divergence is documented rather than papered over. **Four sampler units**, allocated through `VULKAN-181`'s growing pool, with the set built at `SpriteBatch::End()` and captured **by value** into the batch snapshot -- the same rule the effect's pipeline and push constants already follow, so a texture unbound or disposed after `End()` cannot change what the recorded frame samples. The previous set is retired rather than freed, because a recorded frame may still name it. **Two holes found by mutation and closed, not worked around.** (1) Writing only the bound units left the rest `VK_NULL_HANDLE`: the layer reported three `pImageInfo[0].imageView is VK_NULL_HANDLE` per set. Every binding is now written, the unbound ones with the renderer's own white 1×1 -- so a shader sampling a unit nothing was bound to reads white rather than undefined memory, and the default texture is created on this path because nothing had ever needed it there. (2) Building the set **only when something was bound** meant a shader that declares `set = 1` and gets no binding drew with set 1 unbound: `vkCmdDrawIndexed(): The VkPipeline … statically uses descriptor set 1, but all sets 0 to 1 [were not bound]`, then a segfault. The set is now unconditional -- one descriptor per effect, and the whole class of failure is gone. **The test is built so that failure reads as white**, because white is what an unbound unit produces: the sprite's own texture is white and the effect samples only set 1, so blue can only come from the binding. With `BindTexture` mutated to store nothing, legs A and B fail with `(255,255,255)` and nothing crashes. **One existing test changed, and it had to.** `Vulkan_SamplerAddressW`'s leg C asserted that binding a `Texture2D` to a `ShaderEffect` is **refused by name** -- `VULKAN-163`'s contract, correct until this row replaced it. It now asserts the pair that must hold: the volume still refused, the flat texture accepted. Leaving it would have failed the feature this row adds, which is exactly what `VULKAN-252`'s row warns about for the array setters. **And one gap in `VULKAN-013`'s guard, found by this row's own verification run and fixed in the same commit:** a RADV suite run came back with **55 subprocess aborts**, every one of them `CNA::Platform: AcquireSubsystem(Video) failed: x11 not available` -- the scaffolding display had died. That is precisely the environment-versus-regression confusion `VULKAN-013` exists to prevent, and it is further from "a device that WAS created" than the two cases already matched, since no device is reached at all. The guard now covers it: exit **77** with `[SKIP]`, verified against `DISPLAY=:9999`. Re-running with the display restored gives **339/339**. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **339/339 on llvmpipe and 339/339 on RADV**. |
| VULKAN-254 | Implement `BindTextureCube` / `BindTexture3D` for custom effects on Vulkan | ✅ | **Both implemented on `VULKAN-253`'s set-1 machinery, and the one design question they raise is answered rather than worked around.** `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_BoundTexture` grows 4/4 → **6/6, on llvmpipe and on RADV**. **The question: can a cube and a volume share the four bindings a `sampler2D` uses?** No -- a descriptor's **view type** must match the dimensionality the shader declares, so a 2D filler cannot stand in for an unbound `samplerCube`, and sharing one range would have made every unused unit a usage error rather than a white texel. So set 1 gives each sampler KIND its own range: bindings 0–3 `sampler2D`, 4–7 `samplerCube`, 8–11 `sampler3D`, with unit `u` of each kind at its own base + `u`. Twelve descriptors per effect, which is the price of never having a mismatched one. **Which meant a third filler.** The 2D white already existed and `VULKAN-163`'s environment-map path had built a white cube; a **1×1×1 white `VK_IMAGE_TYPE_3D`** did not exist anywhere and is added here, created and torn down beside its cube twin. **Two SPIR-V shaders written for this**, compiled from the GLSL kept in the test's own comments through `libshaderc`: one sampling `samplerCube` at binding 4, one sampling `sampler3D` at binding 8. Each is fed a blue texture through an effect whose sprite is white, so -- as in `VULKAN-253` -- a binding that never arrives reads white and the failure is unmistakable. **`Vulkan_SamplerAddressW`'s leg C changed for the second time in two rows, and the second change is the interesting one.** `VULKAN-253` had already turned its `Texture2D` half from "refused by name" to "accepted"; this row does the same for the volume half. What the leg now asserts is what it was really written for and what has been true throughout: a binding is **acknowledged**, never dropped in silence. `VULKAN-163` made that true by naming a refusal; these two rows make it true by doing the work. The refusal helper is deleted with its last caller, and the comment above it says what replaced it. **The array refusal is deliberately NOT deleted with it** -- an array still has nowhere to live in a fixed 128-byte push-constant block, and that is `VULKAN-252`'s row to close. Removing it in this commit would have been the easy tidy and the wrong one. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **339/339 on llvmpipe and 339/339 on RADV**. |
| VULKAN-255 | Support a `ShaderEffect` driving a 3D draw with a custom vertex layout on Vulkan | ✅ | **Implemented for the ordinary and the indexed route, refused by name for the instanced one, and the measurement that opened it is worth stating first: a game that applied a `ShaderEffect` and issued a 3D draw got its geometry drawn by a STOCK program, silently.** The 3D path never looked at the custom effect at all — `GetOrCreatePipeline` baked the `SpriteBatch` vertex input and a triangle list, and `DrawPrimitivesEx` selected a stock family by stride. That is the same silent mis-bind `VULKAN-156`, `VULKAN-165` and `VULKAN-265` each removed elsewhere. **Where the hook goes is the load-bearing decision.** It runs at the TOP of `DrawPrimitivesEx` and `DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx`, before `REMED-GFX-DECL-GUARD`, because that guard asks whether the stock attribute table can represent the caller's declaration — a question about a shader this renderer wrote, not about the game's. Measured: with the hook removed, this row's own test does not merely render wrong, it is **refused** by that guard (*"the declaration states Tangent0@24 Vector3 but this renderer's native layout reads that semantic as Vector4"*). It branches on **`params.customEffectRequested`**, the shared layer's own per-draw signal and what EasyGL reads, not on `activeCustomEffect_` — that is sticky state a `SpriteBatch` clears at `End()`. **The layout convention is EasyGL's**, deliberately: location = the element's own index in the `VertexDeclaration` (Task 1080's rule), because a custom shader's inputs are not enumerable and there is no semantic table to match against. A buffer with **no** declaration is refused by name rather than falling back to the stride table, which for a custom shader would be a guess about someone else's program. **Three things a 3D draw needs that a sprite batch does not**, all now in the pipeline key so the two families cannot collide in one cache: the vertex layout (by hash), the topology, and the rasterizer state (cull mode and fill mode). A fourth was found by the validation gate rather than by reading — the 3D replay sets depth bias before every draw, so a 3D pipeline that does not declare `VK_DYNAMIC_STATE_DEPTH_BIAS` is a validation error in its own right, exactly as `VULKAN-058` found for the stencil trio. **The pipeline is built at DRAW time**, unlike every stock family's: a 3D draw already knows its render target, and the deferred replay must not reach into an effect the game may have disposed. The handle survives that disposal because the effect's destructor retires its pipelines on the frame fence. **The transform, and this is a real decision rather than plumbing:** a 3D draw arrives carrying the world, view and projection the effect's own `IEffectMatrices` properties hold, and EasyGL delivers them to a custom shader **by name** — reflection this renderer does not have. They go into the one matrix slot the push-constant block has, column-major like every stock shader here, **unless the game called `SetUniformMat4` itself**, in which case the game wins. Dropping them instead would have been one more silence. **Test:** new `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_3D`, **6/6 on llvmpipe and on RADV**, on the same five-element 48-byte declaration EasyGL's own custom-layout proof uses, with one attribute per output channel so a wrong offset is one wrong channel. **Mutation, three, each predicted:** removing the hook makes the draw refuse rather than render; binding every attribute at offset 0 gives `(0,63,0,0)`; never filling the matrix slot gives the clear colour, which is what the leg's own message says it would mean. **Leg E earned its keep twice:** written without a per-instance stream it passed for the wrong reason — `DrawInstancedPrimitivesEx` falls back to an ordinary indexed draw when no instance stream is bound, so nothing was refused because nothing was instanced. With a real instance binding it reaches the refusal. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **342/342** on llvmpipe and on RADV. Full `ctest` **not re-run**: every changed line is in this renderer and its examples. **Opened by this row:** `VULKAN-168` (the instanced route). |
| VULKAN-256 | Record the GLSL-source shader-test divergence rather than porting 37 tests | ✅ | **Recorded — and the recording found that the divergence was not actually enforced, which is why this row changed code as well as documentation.** **The defect, and it is a real one:** `CompileProgram` refused a non-SPIR-V payload with *"SPIR-V size must be a multiple of 4 bytes"* — a **length** check. `Vulkan_ShaderDialectContract` leg B had been passing on it since `VULKAN-250`, by the accident that its GLSL source's byte count is not a multiple of four. **Measured** by padding that same source with spaces: `vkCreateShaderModule` **accepted the text** on llvmpipe (the validation layer objected; the call still returned `VK_SUCCESS`), so the effect reported itself valid and would have drawn from GLSL bytes read as instructions. The check is now the **SPIR-V magic word**, per stage, with a message that names what the payload has to be and how to produce it. Leg B2 pins it: the padded source is refused, and the message contains `SPIR-V`. **Mutation:** with the magic test disabled, B2 reports *"accepted -- the refusal was an accident of length"* and two `[Vulkan Validation]` lines appear. **The documentation half, which is what the row asked for:** `docs/vulkan-renderer.md` gains a section stating the contract (`GlslVulkan` names the source language the bytecode was compiled *from*, not what this renderer compiles), the capability-mapping table below, and a *Writing a `ShaderEffect` for this renderer* guide — the push-constant slot map, the set-1 binding map for 2D/cube/volume textures and the four array ranges, the location = declaration-index rule for a 3D draw, where the transform comes from, and that clip space is Vulkan's. **The row's own closing condition was that the SPIR-V equivalents exercise capability rather than convenience, and that is checked here rather than asserted:** each of the eight capabilities the 37 EasyGL tests need is mapped to the Vulkan test that proves it — uniforms (`Vulkan_ShaderEffect_SpirV`), bound 2D/cube/volume textures (`Vulkan_ShaderEffect_BoundTexture`), sampler state on a volume (`Vulkan_Texture3DAddressW`), array uniforms (`Vulkan_ShaderEffect_UniformArrays`), a 3D draw with a custom layout (`Vulkan_ShaderEffect_3D`), and MRT from a custom shader, which turned out to be covered already by `Vulkan_MRT_MsaaResolve`'s four-output effect. **Two are genuinely not available and say so** rather than approximating: an instanced draw with a custom effect (`VULKAN-168`, refused by name) and translating the source itself (`plans/plan_csl.md`). **Evidence:** `Vulkan_ShaderDialectContract` 4/4 → **5/5**; `^Vulkan_` **342/342** on llvmpipe and **341/342** on RADV. The one RADV failure is `Vulkan_OcclusionQuery_Precision`, which **passed on RADV in this session's own earlier run** and fails 6 times out of 6 when run alone half an hour later, with no code between the two runs that it can reach: this row's only production change is a payload check inside `ShaderEffect` compilation, and that test never constructs one. Handed back to `VULKAN-182`, which returns to 🟨. |
| VULKAN-257 | Close the `BasicEffect` evidence gaps on Vulkan | ✅ | `easygl_basiceffect_combinations_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_BasicEffectCombinations`, the combination sweep. On this renderer each combination selects a **different pipeline** out of a different cache (`pipelinesLitTextured3D_`, `pipelinesFogTex3D_`, …), so a sweep is a test of program selection here and not only of shading. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-258 | Close the `AlphaTestEffect` evidence gaps on Vulkan | ✅ | Three registrations: `Vulkan_AlphaTestModes`, `Vulkan_AlphaTestEffect_AlphaCutout`, `Vulkan_AlphaTestEffect_Properties`. Vulkan covered the compare-function sweep, fog, null texture and vertex colour; the cutout and property surfaces were EasyGL-only. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-259 | Prove `DualTextureEffect` independent UV sets on Vulkan | ✅ | **Already satisfied.** Same discovery again: `easygl_dualtextureeffect_independent_uv_test.cpp` was registered as **`Vulkan_DualTextureEffect_IndependentUV`** by `VULKAN-150` (finding F-20), whose whole subject was that `DualTextureEffect` samples `Texture2` with `TEXCOORD1` and that this renderer's shader had one UV input and used it for both samplers. The registration's own comment in the CMakeLists says so. This row asked for the test that closing F-20 already added. Closed as covered. **Evidence:** that test is in the suite and passes.
| VULKAN-260 | Prove the `EnvironmentMapEffect` Fresnel **gradient** on Vulkan | ✅ | **A real rendering defect, found by registering the test and fixed in the shader — the same defect Task 1112 fixed on EasyGL, still present here.** **Observed:** `Vulkan_EnvironmentMapEffect_Fresnel_Gradient` failed three of its four legs on the first run with `got=(200,100,50)` at every sample point — the environment-map colour at full strength, i.e. **`fresnelFactor = 1` everywhere**, where a gradient from 0.941 to 0.352 belongs. **Diagnosis:** `env_map3d.frag.glsl` computed the blend factor **per fragment** — `viewAngle = dot(E, N)` from the interpolated-and-renormalized normal and eye vector. XNA's `EnvironmentMapEffect.fx` evaluates `ComputeFresnelFactor` in the **vertex** shader, from each vertex's own un-interpolated normal and eye vector, writes the scalar to `vout.Diffuse.a`, and lets the rasterizer Gouraud-interpolate **that**. The two stop being equivalent the moment a triangle's vertices carry different normals. **Why the failure was flat rather than merely wrong, which is what made it legible:** on this fixture's quad — top edge normal `(0,0,1)`, bottom edge `(1,0,0)`, eye at the origin — the interpolated normal along the vertical centre line has no component along the interpolated eye vector, so the per-fragment `dot` is **0** at every sample and `pow(1-0, k)` is 1. The defect erased the gradient completely instead of distorting it. **Fixed:** the computation moves to `env_map3d.vert.glsl` as a `location = 4` varying, with `EnvironmentMapAmount` folded in **there** because FNA folds it there too (`VSEnvMap` writes `ComputeFresnelFactor(...) * EnvironmentMapAmount`, `VSEnvMapNoFresnel` writes the amount alone). The fragment shader reads the interpolated scalar. `spirv_shaders.hpp` regenerated with the committed `compile_shaders.py`. **Authority, per §5 and per this row's own instruction:** the expected values are **hand-derived from XNA's formula** in the test's header, not taken from EasyGL's output — EasyGL was the suspect that produced this test in the first place (`plan_dx9.md`'s D9-A6 oracle cross-check). Measured after the fix: `(188,94,47)`, `(165,83,41)`, `(70,35,18)` against hand-derived `fresnel × (200,100,50)` of `0.941`, `0.823`, `0.352`. **The pre-existing `Vulkan_EnvironmentMapEffect_Fresnel` did not catch this**, and that is the lesson worth keeping: it proves the term **exists**, and a term that is constant 1 exists. Only a gradient shaped to discriminate per-vertex from per-fragment can tell them apart. **Evidence:** the four legs pass; the other ten `EnvironmentMapEffect` tests are unchanged; `^Vulkan_` **309/309**. Full `ctest` in §7.5 — mandatory here, because a regenerated `spirv_shaders.hpp` is linked into every test in the configuration.
| VULKAN-261 | Close the `SkinnedEffect` evidence gaps on Vulkan | ✅ | Three registrations: `Vulkan_SkinnedBones`, `Vulkan_SkinnedEffectVector4BoneIndices`, `Vulkan_SkinnedEffect_BoneDeformation`. The bone-index leg matters most here: `VULKAN-151` had to settle **two spellings of `BLENDINDICES`** on this renderer, and a `Vector4` index set is the shape that distinguishes them. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-262 | Prove PBR material maps on Vulkan | ✅ | `easygl_pbr_material_maps_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_Pbr_MaterialMaps`. `Vulkan_Pbr_TextureSlots` proves slot **mapping** and `Vulkan_PbrEffect_Golden` proves the BRDF; the per-map contribution -- normal, metallic-roughness, occlusion and emissive each changing the result -- is the gap, and it is the one that catches a slot bound to the right descriptor but sampled into the wrong term. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-263 | Prove `Effect::Clone` and `CurrentTechnique` on Vulkan | ✅ | `Vulkan_EffectClone` and `Vulkan_EffectCurrentTechnique`. Mostly shared logic, with one property that is not: a cloned effect must not share renderer-side **pipeline or descriptor state**, and on this renderer both are cached per effect. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.

## 20. Phase 8 — Model, content and glTF renderer-facing behaviour (`VULKAN-300`–`VULKAN-329`)

Parser-side glTF/`.cnj` correctness stays with `plans/plan_gltf.md` and `plans/plan_cnj.md`. The
question here is only whether the Vulkan renderer executes the same already-parsed scene and
material semantics.

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-300 | Prove basic `Model` drawing on Vulkan | ✅ | `easygl_model_draw_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_ModelDraw_RedQuad`. The seven `ModelJsonReader`/hierarchy/skinned-playback sources already ran here; this one -- the simplest possible `Model.Draw` -- did not, which is the usual way a smoke test goes missing. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-301 | Prove tangent handedness and mirrored tangents on Vulkan | ✅ | `Vulkan_Gltf_TangentHandedness` and `Vulkan_Gltf_MirroredTangent`. **Both aborted on the first run** with `Cannot open file: tests/assets/gltf/tangent-handedness`, and the cause is neither the renderer nor the test: `cna_register_renderer_test` defaults `WORKING_DIRECTORY` to the **build** directory, while these fixtures are addressed relative to the repo root. EasyGL's own registrations pass `WORKING_DIRECTORY "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"` and these now do too. Recorded because a registration that aborts on a missing fixture looks exactly like a renderer defect. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-302 | Prove glTF sampler wrap and per-map texture transforms on Vulkan | ✅ | `Vulkan_Gltf_SamplerWrap` and `Vulkan_Gltf_TextureTransformPerMap`, with the same repo-root `WORKING_DIRECTORY` fix `VULKAN-301` records. The per-map transform is the interesting one on this renderer: each map's transform has to reach its own sampler slot, not the effect's shared one. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-303 | Prove glTF alpha blending, transmission ordering and base-colour factor × texture on Vulkan | ✅ | **All three registered and green on the first run.** `Vulkan_Gltf_AlphaBlend`, `Vulkan_Gltf_TransmissionOrdering` and `Vulkan_Gltf_BaseColorFactorTexture`. **The middle one is why this row was worth running rather than arguing**, and the row said so: transmission ordering is a **draw-order** property, and this renderer's command model is deferred — every batch and 3D draw is recorded and replayed at `Present`, with `RecordCommandBuffer` deciding the order. A shared-code argument ("the importer produces the same draw list") cannot settle whether that list is replayed in the same order, and REMED-GFX-129/-140/-143 exist because it has not always been. **Registration note carried from `VULKAN-301`:** these three take `WORKING_DIRECTORY "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"`, because their fixtures are addressed relative to the repo root and `cna_register_renderer_test` defaults to the build directory. **Evidence:** 3/3, `^Vulkan_` **329/329**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only.
| VULKAN-304 | Prove skinned PBR under non-uniform joint scale on Vulkan | ✅ | `Vulkan_Gltf_SkinnedPbrNonUniformJoint`, same `WORKING_DIRECTORY` fix. Non-uniform joint scale is where a normal matrix taken as the plain world matrix stops being equivalent, so it is the leg that separates a correct skinned normal from one that merely looks lit. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.

---

## 21. Phase 9 — Device, presentation and lifecycle (`VULKAN-330`–`VULKAN-369`)

Vulkan has no GL context loss, and this phase does **not** translate EasyGL's context-recovery
machinery. It compares the observable CNA contract.

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-330 | Implement `SetPresentationMode` on Vulkan (F-03) | ✅ | **Implemented, and the mutation check caught a silent behaviour change hiding inside the fix — which is the part of this row most worth reading.** **Observed:** `void SetPresentationMode(int) override {}` (`VulkanRenderer.hpp:1477`), the only empty override body in either renderer, so a game selecting `Letterbox` silently got Vulkan's uniform height-derived scale. Same class as F-31: the call is accepted and discarded. **Done:** the mode is stored, `GetPresentedRectEXT` computes the presented rectangle, and it is applied wherever "full target" is resolved **for the back buffer** — the pass-level default *and* both replay sites. **Two replay sites, not one:** every batch and every 3D draw overrides the viewport from its own captured state (REMED-GFX-062), so changing only the pass default would have letterboxed nothing, and changing only the 3D site would have letterboxed 3D draws while sprites kept the whole image. **Render targets are deliberately untouched:** a presentation mode describes how the virtual resolution reaches the *window*, not how a game's own targets are laid out. **The algorithm is EasyGL's, reproduced rather than shared.** `EasyGLSurfaceState::GetDefaultViewportRect`'s own comment names it the established reference for these semantics, and the modes must mean the same thing on both renderers or implementing them here is pointless. Duplicated because this row's non-goal forbids touching the shared contract or another renderer; the duplication is now three-way (OpenGL2, SdlGpu, EasyGL, Vulkan) and is worth folding into a shared helper by whoever is allowed to. **The default was wrong and the mutation check is what exposed it.** `presentationMode_` was first initialised to `Letterbox`; with the no-op body restored the test still passed, because the *default* was doing the letterboxing. `GraphicsDevice::SetPresentationMode` forwards to the renderer **only when a game calls it** (`GraphicsDevice.cpp:3499`-`3503`), so the renderer's default is load-bearing — and EasyGL's is `FixedHeightDynamicWidth`, which is exactly the height-derived scale this renderer already had. `Letterbox` as the default would have silently letterboxed **every game that never sets a mode**: an unrequested behaviour change for all existing content, hidden inside a fix for something else. Corrected to `FixedHeightDynamicWidth`. **Test:** `Vulkan_PresentationMode` (`vulkan_presentation_mode_test.cpp`), **5/5**. The virtual resolution is set to half the drawable's height, so `Letterbox` has bars to produce: measured 800×240 at (0,120) inside an 800×480 drawable. One probe near the top edge separates the modes — a bar under `Letterbox`, geometry under `Stretch`. **Leg C is what stops leg B passing for the wrong reason:** a renderer that drew nothing, or clipped everything away, would satisfy "the top edge is background" while implementing nothing at all. **Mutation-checked, after the default was fixed:** restoring the no-op fails leg A (the rectangle is the full 800×480) and leg B2 (the top edge is geometry), while both `Stretch` legs still pass. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **253/253**. **An inconsistency this row INTRODUCES, stated plainly rather than filed as a distant follow-on:** `TransformWindowToLogical`/`TransformLogicalToWindow` map window coordinates through the height-derived scale alone (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:11281`-`11305`), where EasyGL maps them through the presented rectangle — subtracting its origin and scaling by its size (`EasyGLRenderer.cpp:757`). Before this row that was consistent, because there were no bars to ignore. **After it, a game using `Letterbox` renders correctly and maps input wrongly**, offset by the bar height. `GetDefaultViewportRect` likewise still reports the full drawable. Both are `VULKAN-331`, whose dependency on this row is now satisfied and which must land before `Letterbox`/`Overscan` are usable rather than merely visible. Shipping the rendering half first is deliberate — it is independently testable and independently correct — but it is half, and saying so is the difference between a staged change and a regression nobody wrote down. **Full-suite evidence, gathered in four numeric chunks rather than one run:** **9091/9110**, **19** failures — 7 audio/content, 7 lifecycle, 0 in `4801-6400`, 5 net/platform/C-API — all in the known set, none new, and **no `Vulkan_*` test among them**. The chunking is not a shortcut: four consecutive whole-suite runs were OOM-killed by another session's compile spikes on this shared machine, twice at `-j2`, once at `-j3` and once even at `-j1`, each losing everything. Splitting by test number (`ctest -I`) made a kill cost one chunk instead of the lot, and the union of the chunks is the same set of tests as one run over the same tree. |
| VULKAN-331 | Implement `GetDefaultViewportRect` on Vulkan (F-04) | ✅ | **Closes the inconsistency `VULKAN-330` introduced, which is why it was done immediately rather than left in the backlog.** **Two consumers needed the real rectangle, not one.** The row named `GraphicsDevice::UpdateViewportFromWindow`, and that half is a one-line override forwarding to `GetPresentedRectEXT` — the framework already splits logical from physical correctly (`GraphicsDevice.cpp:3421`-`3429`: `Viewport.Width/Height` stays the game's virtual size while the physical rectangle goes to the GPU). **The half the row did not name is the one that mattered more:** `TransformWindowToLogical`/`TransformLogicalToWindow` mapped through the height-derived scale alone. That was equivalent while no mode produced a smaller rectangle; once `VULKAN-330` made `Letterbox` real it meant **a game rendered correctly and picked wrongly**. Both now map through the presented rectangle exactly as `EasyGLSurfaceState`'s do — subtract the origin, scale by the size, in client units via `displayScale`. **Test:** `Vulkan_PresentationMode` grows 5/5 → **8/8**. D asserts `GetDefaultViewportRect` reports the presented rectangle. E takes a window point one pixel from the top — inside the bar — and asserts it maps **outside** the logical area: measured `y = -119`. F asserts `logical → window → logical` round-trips, which catches a fix applied to one direction only. **Mutation-checked:** restoring the height-only transform makes E read `y = 0.5` — the bar point mapping *inside* the game area, so a click on the letterbox would be delivered as a click on the game — and F reads `(50, 85)` instead of `(100, 50)` because the two directions no longer agree. **Why E is the leg worth having:** "the viewport rectangle is right" is checkable by reading; "a click on the bar is not a click on the game" is the thing a player would notice, and it is what the height-only transform got wrong. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **253/253**, and the full `ctest` recorded on this row covers `VULKAN-330` as well — one serial run over a tree containing both halves, stated here and in that row so neither looks as though it skipped it. **Full-suite evidence, gathered in four numeric chunks rather than one run:** **9091/9110**, **19** failures — 7 audio/content, 7 lifecycle, 0 in `4801-6400`, 5 net/platform/C-API — all in the known set, none new, and **no `Vulkan_*` test among them**. The chunking is not a shortcut: four consecutive whole-suite runs were OOM-killed by another session's compile spikes on this shared machine, twice at `-j2`, once at `-j3` and once even at `-j1`, each losing everything. Splitting by test number (`ctest -I`) made a kill cost one chunk instead of the lot, and the union of the chunks is the same set of tests as one run over the same tree. |
| VULKAN-332 | Implement `SetSwapInterval` on Vulkan (F-05) | ✅ | **Implemented, and the mechanism was already there.** `swapInterval_` was read once, at swapchain creation, so `GraphicsDeviceManager.SynchronizeWithVerticalRetrace` + `ApplyChanges()` -- which does reach `SetSwapInterval` through `GraphicsDevice::Reset` -- changed nothing. `CreateSwapchain` already picks its `VkPresentModeKHR` from `swapInterval_` and this renderer already rebuilds its swapchain on every resize; the override records the request and reruns that path when the interval actually differs. `IGraphicsRenderer`'s own comment naming Vulkan as a renderer that "cannot change VSync at runtime" described the old implementation, not the API, and is corrected in place. **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_SwapInterval` (`vulkan_swap_interval_test.cpp`), **7/7**, stable over three consecutive runs. The oracle is the **live swapchain's own `VkPresentModeKHR`** -- written only by `CreateSwapchain`, so it cannot move unless the swapchain was genuinely rebuilt -- observed going FIFO -> IMMEDIATE -> FIFO through the public `ApplyChanges()` route, with a drawn quad read back after each rebuild. `easygl_present_interval_test.cpp` is also registered (`Vulkan_PresentInterval`, 5/5) for the renderer-agnostic round-trip half. **Two things learned the hard way, both now written into the test:** (1) the recreation *counter* is useless as an oracle -- `Reset` also re-applies the virtual resolution and presentation format, and the same no-change `ApplyChanges()` cost 0 recreations before a draw and 1 after one, so asserting it tests `Reset`'s bookkeeping rather than this; it is printed, not asserted. (2) The first version had an **escape hatch that excused the defect**: FIFO is the only present mode Vulkan guarantees, so it inferred "this surface has no unsynchronised mode" from the mode not changing -- and with `SetSwapInterval` reverted to its no-op the test still passed 7/7. The branch now turns on a *measured* fact, `SupportsUnsynchronisedPresentModeEXT()`, read from the surface's own present-mode list. **Mutation probes, both run against the fixed test:** the pre-task no-op gives 6/7 (vsync off never leaves FIFO); recording the interval without rebuilding gives 5/7 (the mode lags the request in both directions -- worth noting, because `Reset`'s other rebuilds would eventually apply a recorded interval, just at the wrong time). **Suite:** 222/223. |
| VULKAN-333 | Implement `GetSwapIntervalEXT` on Vulkan | ✅ | **Implemented, and the row's registration instruction is deliberately NOT followed -- with the reason, per §26.6.** `GetSwapIntervalEXT()` now returns `swapInterval_`, so the `-1` that means "this renderer does not record what it was asked for" -- the honest answer while `SetSwapInterval` was the inherited no-op -- is gone. **Why `easygl_graphicsdevicemanager_vsync_test.cpp` is not registered on Vulkan:** its driver half is `SDL_GL_GetSwapInterval`, which has no meaning on a window created for Vulkan. Registered verbatim, its check A (`interval == 0` with vsync off) would pass **vacuously** on the zero left in an untouched `int`, and check B would take the skip path -- which is precisely the failure mode that file's own header documents as the reason `REMED-GFX-243` existed at all ("deleting the very fix this file was written to guard left it reporting PASS and SKIP"). Registering it here would recreate that, one renderer over. **What is done instead:** the two questions it asks are asked in `Vulkan_SwapInterval` with the probe that means something on this renderer. Its `P1`/`P2` forwarding half becomes legs `E`, read through `IGraphicsRenderer&` rather than the concrete type -- the same cross-renderer seam -- and its GL driver half becomes `VULKAN-332`'s `GetAppliedPresentModeEXT()` assertion, which is a real driver-side observation rather than a CNA-internal echo. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_SwapInterval` is now **10/10**: the interval is not `-1`, `SynchronizeWithVerticalRetrace=false` reads back as `0` and `true` as non-zero, alongside `VULKAN-332`'s present-mode legs. **Suite:** 222/223. |
| VULKAN-334 | Decide and document the Vulkan answer to device loss | ✅ | **Audited, decided, implemented as far as it can honestly go, and the refusal is written down rather than left as an absence.** **What CNA promises:** `GraphicsDevice` carries XNA's `DeviceLost`, `DeviceResetting` and `DeviceReset` events, `GraphicsDeviceStatus`, and `ContentLost` on tracked resources — all delivered by the renderer through `GraphicsRendererCreateArgs::deviceEventCallback`. **What Vulkan did:** nothing. `VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST` appeared **zero times** in `VulkanRenderer.cpp`, the callback was never stored, and a lost device would have surfaced as whichever generic failure the next call raised — `"vkQueueSubmit failed"` if it was lucky. Nine of the ten renderers were in that state; only DirectX9 (`D9-34`) called the callback. **What it does now:** every call that can report the loss is checked — `vkQueueSubmit`, `vkQueuePresentKHR`, `vkAcquireNextImageKHR` and both fence waits — and one helper turns it into an answer: the loss is reported **once** to the shared layer, so a game's own `DeviceLost` handler runs and `GraphicsDeviceStatus` becomes `Lost`, and the call then fails with a message naming the entry point and stating that no reset is attempted. **The refusal, with its reason:** a lost `VkDevice` cannot be recovered — the specification requires destroying it and every object made from it — so a `Reset` would mean rebuilding every live `Texture2D`, `RenderTarget2D`, `Effect` and buffer wrapper underneath the game. That is a different feature from D3D9's `Reset`, and this renderer does not have it. The row's explicit non-goal was a GL-shaped loss/restore state machine built by analogy; there is none. **Test:** new `Vulkan_DeviceLostContract`, **4/4**. The loss is **injected**, because no driver here exposes a way to provoke a real one inside the process — the hook replaces the `VkResult` of one check and everything after it is the production path, the same technique `VULKAN-390` and `VULKAN-161` used for allocation failure. Its legs are the contract: the handler runs and the status changes (A), the message names the cause and the refusal (B), the event fires **once** however many later calls fail (C), and a control proves none of that is true before the injection (D). **Mutation:** with the callback call removed, leg A reports `events=0 status=0`. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **347/347** on llvmpipe. Documented in `docs/vulkan-renderer.md`. |
| VULKAN-335 | Prove `PresentationParameters` round-trip on Vulkan | ✅ | **Registered, and one leg had to be corrected rather than satisfied — it asserted the ABSENCE of substitution, which is a property of EasyGL and not a contract.** **Observed on registration:** four legs passed and `DepthStencilFormat matches requested value` failed. The test requests `DepthFormat::Depth24` and asserts the stored value is `Depth24`. **Diagnosis, and it is a deliberate design rather than a defect:** this renderer's back-buffer depth image is always stencil-capable — `FindDepthFormat()` prefers `VK_FORMAT_D24_UNORM_S8_UINT` since Task 870, precisely so `DepthStencilState.StencilEnable` can work at all — so a request for `Depth24` genuinely yields `Depth24Stencil8`, and `VULKAN-348` deliberately made the renderer **report what is in effect rather than what was asked for**. Reporting `Depth24` would describe a stencil buffer that exists as absent. **Corrected the test, not the renderer** (§12): the leg now asserts `pp.DepthStencilFormat == GetAppliedDepthStencilFormatEXT(Depth24)` — *“PresentationParameters stores what the device applied”*, which is the real contract and which on a renderer that never substitutes is the same statement the old assertion made. A **second** leg was added so neither can pass alone: the applied format must not be `DepthFormat::None`, i.e. it must describe a depth buffer that exists. Without it a renderer reporting `None` for everything would satisfy the first. **Cross-checked on EasyGL:** `EasyGL_PresentationParameters` rebuilt and green, unchanged in meaning — there applied == requested == `Depth24`. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_PresentationParameters` **6/6** (was 5 legs, now 6), `^Vulkan_` **310/310**. Full `ctest` not re-run: one shared test source, verified on both renderers that register it.
| VULKAN-336 | Prove backbuffer resize on Vulkan | ✅ | `easygl_backbuffer_resize_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_BackbufferResize`. This is the path `VULKAN-404` found serving the **pre-resize frame** reinterpreted at the new width, so the row is closed with a public-API test over the same ground rather than only the fixture that found it. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-337 | Prove real window resize on Vulkan | ✅ | **Audited, and it found that `FixedHeightDynamicWidth` is only half implemented here. The row closes on the measurement; `VULKAN-350` owns the fix.** **Measured:** `easygl_real_window_resize_test.cpp` resizes the real SDL window through `SDL_SetWindowSize`, bypassing `GraphicsDeviceManager`, and polls until the X11/Xvfb event has propagated. On Vulkan **three of its four checks pass** — the viewport width follows the window, `PresentationParameters.BackBufferWidth/Height` deliberately do not, and `GameWindow.ClientSizeChanged` fires. The one that fails is the first: *“Viewport height stays pinned to `PreferredBackBufferHeight` … (FixedHeightDynamicWidth mode)”*. **Diagnosis, from the shared layer's own split.** `GraphicsDevice::UpdateViewportFromWindow()` reads **two** things from the renderer: the **logical** size from `GetViewportSize()`, which becomes `GraphicsDevice.Viewport.Width/Height`, and the **physical** rectangle from `GetDefaultViewportRect()`, which is what is pushed to the GPU. `VULKAN-330`/`VULKAN-331` implemented the presentation mode in the **second** — the presented rectangle, both replay sites and both coordinate transforms. `VulkanRenderer::GetViewportSize` still returns `surfaceInfo_.drawableSize` unmodified, so the **logical** height follows the window instead of staying pinned to the virtual resolution. Half the mode. **Not fixed here, and the reason is blast radius rather than difficulty.** `GetViewportSize()` is read by `ReadBackbuffer`'s region clamp, by `SpriteBatch`'s ortho divisor and by every `GetBackBufferData` element-count check — `VULKAN-404` is a recent reminder of what a wrong size on that path costs. Changing it inside an audit row, with no test registered to hold the other consumers, is how a fix becomes a regression. **`VULKAN-350`** owns it. **The registration is deliberately withheld** for the same reason `VULKAN-139`'s was: registering it green is impossible, and registering it red is not a closure. `VULKAN-350` registers it. **Evidence:** three of four checks pass on Vulkan today, the fourth reproduces every run; `^Vulkan_` **311/311** (unchanged — nothing registered).
| VULKAN-338 | Prove repeated swapchain recreation, zero-sized and minimized windows on Vulkan | ✅ | **Twenty-two back-to-back recreations, a minimized drawable with a frame presented in that state, and a restore -- with a `RenderTarget2D`, a `Texture2D` and a `VertexBuffer` alive across all of it.** New `Vulkan_SwapchainChurn` (`modules/renderers/vulkan/examples/vulkan_swapchain_churn_test.cpp`), **6/6**. Six legs: the `PreserveContents` target still holds its pre-churn marker; the `Texture2D` still reads back its own texels; the `VertexBuffer` still **draws** (readable is not usable -- a buffer whose memory was retired under it passes a count check and fails this one); the recreate count really rose (**3 → 50**, well past the twenty-two cycles, because each `ApplyChanges` drives more than one); no validation message from the churn itself, read in-process so a message is localized to this test rather than to the whole binary; and a zero drawable does not recreate the swapchain. **The minimized state is delivered the way the platform delivers it**, `OnSurfaceChanged` with a 0×0 drawable on the same surface, which needed one small read-only addition -- `GetSurfaceInfoEXT()`, because `OnSurfaceChanged` rightly refuses a fabricated `RendererSurfaceInfo` whose `windowId` does not match, so a test has to start from the real one. The frame after that is then **presented** while the renderer still holds a swapchain for a window that is no longer visible, which is the case the row named and nothing covered. **A limit of the test, measured and stated rather than left implied by a green run:** `RecreateSwapchain`'s own `if (w == 0 || h == 0) return;` is **not falsifiable in this environment**. Deleting it leaves all six legs passing, because `CreateSwapchain` prefers `caps.currentExtent` and falls back to `surfaceInfo_.drawableSize` only after clamping it to `[minImageExtent, maxImageExtent]` -- so a zero in `surfaceInfo_` can never reach `vkCreateSwapchainKHR`, and the X11 surface under Xvfb always reports a real `currentExtent`. The guard earns its keep on a platform whose surface reports a genuinely zero `currentExtent` while minimized, which Windows does. Deleting `OnSurfaceChanged`'s guard instead also leaves six green, because `RecreateSwapchain`'s own guard then catches it: leg F asserts the **composite** contract, and holds while either guard stands.
