Both facts are in the test's header so the next reader does not mistake the green for more than it is. **Mutation-tested where the test does bite:** retiring `rtRenderPassLoadByDepthFmt_` on every recreation -- a plausible "tidy up on resize" change -- **segfaults** the test (exit 139) after one `[Vulkan Validation] vkCmdBeginRenderPass(): pRenderPassBegin->renderPass Invalid VkRenderPass Object`, with no assertion reached. `VulkanRenderer.cpp` restored bit-for-bit. **One defect in the test itself, found and recorded rather than quietly fixed:** leg C first came back **white** -- `BasicEffect`'s default `DiffuseColor` -- because it uploaded an array of `VertexPositionColor` with `SetDataRaw`. The stock vertex types are polymorphic here, so `sizeof()` is not the packed stride and the vtable pointer landed where the position belonged. That failure is indistinguishable from a resource the churn corrupted, which is exactly why the test now builds raw 16-byte records and says so in place. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **336/336**, no validation output. One production addition, read-only: `GetSurfaceInfoEXT()`. |
| VULKAN-339 | Prove GPU-handle release and double dispose on Vulkan | ✅ | **Both registered and green on the first run.** `easygl_handle_release_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_HandleRelease` and `easygl_double_dispose_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_DoubleDispose`. **What is Vulkan-specific about them, and it is not the assertion they make:** the source asserts `HasRenderer()` is false after the first `Dispose()` and that no second release is attempted — a CNA-side statement. On this renderer that same path runs `vkDeviceWaitIdle` and the retirement lists, and a second release attempt would double-free real `Vk*` handles. That half is asserted by `VULKAN-393`'s **output gate**, which is on both registrations: the object tracker reports any handle destroyed twice or not at all at `vkDestroyDevice`, and it is silent here. **Evidence:** both tests pass, `^Vulkan_` **273/273** (the seven registrations of `VULKAN-339`–`VULKAN-343` were measured together in one run and split into five commits by task). Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only.
| VULKAN-340 | Prove no resource leak over many create/dispose cycles on Vulkan | ✅ | **Registered and green: `easygl_resource_leak_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_ResourceLeak`**, 80 resources (a `Texture2D`, a `VertexBuffer`, an `IndexBuffer` and a `RenderTarget2D` per iteration) over 20 iterations. **The row asked for a Vulkan-side assertion *“if one is cheaply available, since `GetTrackedResourceCount() == 0` proves the CNA side only”* — and there is a stronger one than it expected, already in place.** `VULKAN-393`'s output gate puts the Khronos **object tracker** on this registration: at `vkDestroyDevice` it names every `VkImage`, `VkBuffer`, `VkDeviceMemory`, `VkImageView` and `VkFramebuffer` still alive. That is not a count of allocations, it is a list of survivors — which is what a leak test wants — and it is exactly the instrument that caught `VULKAN-405`'s framebuffer and `VULKAN-407`'s ten handles. A `VkDeviceMemory` counter would have proved less. **Evidence:** the test passes with the gate active, `^Vulkan_` **273/273** (the seven registrations of `VULKAN-339`–`VULKAN-343` were measured together in one run and split into five commits by task). Full `ctest` not re-run: registration only.
| VULKAN-341 | Prove move semantics do not double-free Vulkan handles | ✅ | **Registered and green: `easygl_move_semantics_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_MoveSemantics`.** **Why this source asks a different question here than on EasyGL, which is the row's point:** this renderer's buffers and render targets **register themselves** in `liveVertexBuffers_`, `liveIndexBuffers_` and `liveRenderTargets_` — and, since `VULKAN-407`, in three more lists — so a move that left two entries pointing at one object would make the renderer's destructor release it twice. The EasyGL registry has no such shape, so the same source is a weaker test there. **The double release would be a double-free of real `Vk*` handles**, which `VULKAN-393`'s output gate reports at `vkDestroyDevice`; it is silent. **Evidence:** the test passes, `^Vulkan_` **273/273** (the seven registrations of `VULKAN-339`–`VULKAN-343` were measured together in one run and split into five commits by task). Full `ctest` not re-run: registration only.
| VULKAN-342 | Prove device/resource destruction ordering on Vulkan | ✅ | **Registered and green: `easygl_device_dispose_order_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_DeviceDisposeOrder`.** Destroying the device **before** its resources is the case `~VulkanRenderer`'s release-and-disconnect machinery exists for, and until now nothing exercised it through the public API. **It is not a hypothetical, and this plan proved that twice today:** `VULKAN-405` found the MRT proxy freeing nothing on that path because a `shared_ptr` outlived the renderer, and `VULKAN-407` found three whole resource classes in no live list at all — both reported by the object tracker at `vkDestroyDevice`, both while their tests passed. This registration puts a public-API case on the same gate. **Evidence:** the test passes, `^Vulkan_` **273/273** (the seven registrations of `VULKAN-339`–`VULKAN-343` were measured together in one run and split into five commits by task). Full `ctest` not re-run: registration only.
| VULKAN-343 | Prove resource and device-reset events on Vulkan | ✅ | **Both registered and green:** `easygl_resource_events_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_ResourceEvents` and `easygl_device_reset_events_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_DeviceResetEvents`. **Why the second is not the same test here as on EasyGL:** `ApplyChanges()` on a live device is a full `Reset` on Vulkan — `VULKAN-095` measured what that costs, a `vkDeviceWaitIdle` followed by tearing down and rebuilding every sample-count-dependent render pass, framebuffer and pipeline. So the events these tests assert are raised **around real device reconstruction** rather than around a bookkeeping update, and the `DeviceResetting`/`DeviceReset` ordering they check is the only public signal a game gets that its resources have been rebuilt underneath it. **Evidence:** both tests pass, `^Vulkan_` **273/273** (the seven registrations of `VULKAN-339`–`VULKAN-343` were measured together in one run and split into five commits by task). Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only.
| VULKAN-344 | Prove `GraphicsDevice` argument validation on Vulkan | ✅ | `easygl_device_validation_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_DeviceValidation`. No equivalent existed here at all, and on a deferred renderer an argument the shared layer fails to reject is replayed at `Present`. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-345 | Prove the `Clear` overload and `ClearOptions` matrix on Vulkan | ✅ | `easygl_clear_overloads_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_ClearOverloads`. Vulkan had four `Clear` tests, all about **what a clear does**; the overload and validation matrix -- which arguments are accepted, which combinations are legal -- was untested here. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-346 | Stop an unsupported combination from ending the process (F-16) | ✅ | **Both cases fixed, and the first turned out to be a real renderer defect rather than only a harness fault.** **Case 1, `Vulkan_RenderTarget_EffectSource`:** the stride refusal (`Vulkan PbrEffect requires vertex stride 48 or 60`) was raised by `GetOrCreatePipelinePbr3D`, which is called from `RecordCommandBuffer` -- at `Present()`. So the draw was **accepted**, the leg recorded its failure and unbound its target, and the exception then arrived at a frame boundary the caller could no longer associate with anything, outside every recovery it had. That is §6.3's failure exactly: not refused at the point of the mistake. `RequirePbrStrideEXT` now asks the same question where the draw is **queued** (both `Draw*PrimitivesEx` routes), with the same two messages, so nothing is queued that the replay cannot build. **Measured:** the `[CRASH] leg B1: killed by signal 6 (Aborted) (core dumped)` is gone; the leg reports `[FAIL] leg B1: an unexpected exception escaped: Vulkan PbrEffect requires vertex stride 48 or 60` and the CTest exits 1 -- the verdict it printed. It stays red until `VULKAN-144` supplies the capability, which is the correct state for a capability gap. **Case 2, `Vulkan_DualTextureSlotSamplerContract`:** a harness fault, as the row predicted. Its support probe calls `Render()`, which binds a render target before the draw it is probing with; the refusal unwinds past the unbind, the shutdown `Present()` correctly refuses, and nothing catches it, so an orderly `[SKIP]` became a `std::terminate`. The probe's catch now unbinds and resets device state first -- the same recovery `rendertarget_effect_source_test.cpp` already documents for its own legs. **Measured:** prints `=== 5/5 PASS ===` and exits **0**. **The second clause needed no new test:** "ending a frame with a render target bound is either legal or refused at the point of the mistake" is already owned, end to end, by `present_lifecycle_contract_test.cpp` (`Vulkan_PresentLifecycle`, passing) -- it reads the contract out of FNA's own `GraphicsDevice.Present`, establishes that it is REJECT rather than auto-unbind, asserts each link of the chain separately, and reproduces the process-level abort deliberately. Duplicating it here would add nothing. **Suite:** 218/219 -- only `Vulkan_RenderTarget_EffectSource` remains, and it is `VULKAN-144`'s. |
| VULKAN-347 | `PresentationParameters` must not echo back a multisample count the device did not apply | ✅ | **Fixed, and the fix is one line; the work was establishing which seam actually carries the defect.** **Observed:** `VulkanRenderer` did not override `GetAppliedMultiSampleCountEXT`, so it took the identity default (`IGraphicsRenderer.hpp:1948`) while the renderer really clamps — `sampleCount_ = PickSampleCount(physicalDevice_, args.multiSampleCount)` (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:1838`) returns the highest supported count **≤** the request. Measured on llvmpipe, which offers 1× and 4× only: requests 1, 2, 3 apply 0 and reported 1, 2, 3; requests 5, 7, 8, 9, 16 apply 4 and reported 5, 7, 8, 9, 16. **Classification:** implementation bug — incorrect reporting of applied state. **Done:** the override returns `GetMultiSampleCount()` and ignores its argument, matching GDI (`GdiRenderer.hpp:70`), which is the renderer that established the semantic: both call sites pass the current request and use the answer as a write-back, so the question is *what is in effect*. **The 0-versus-1 trap, checked first:** `SampleCountToInt` maps `VK_SAMPLE_COUNT_1_BIT` through its `default:` arm to **0**, so "no multisampling" keeps XNA's spelling and `PresentationParametersTests`' two `EXPECT_EQ(…, 0)` cases are untouched. **Where a game can actually read the wrong value — corrected twice, by measurement, and this is the part worth keeping:** the row as opened claimed direct construction (`GraphicsDevice.cpp:3309`) was the observable path, because that line's own comment says it exists to stop an unapplied request being retained. It is not: Game startup reaches `Reset()` afterwards, which writes the count back from `ApplyMultiSampleCount`'s **own return** (`:663`-`:665`) and corrects it first. `ApplyChanges` does the same. **Two successive drafts of leg C passed with the fix reverted** — one through `ApplyChanges`, one through direct construction — before the third found the seam that survives: `GraphicsDevice::SetPresentationParameters` (`:2798`), the CNAEXT store-only path, whose comment says it does not reconfigure MSAA and relies on the renderer reporting what is in effect. A test that cannot fail is worse than no test, and only a reverted-fix run tells the two apart. **Test:** `Vulkan_AppliedMultiSampleCount` (`vulkan_applied_multisample_test.cpp`), **11/11**. Legs A/B sweep nine requests and compare the report against what `ApplyMultiSampleCount` actually reaches, labelling each as clamped or control — the powers of two must **not** change, so a fix returning a constant fails. Leg C1 proves the 8× request really was clamped on this device before C2 asserts what was stored, so C2 cannot pass vacuously. The renderer's original count is restored afterwards, since `ApplyMultiSampleCount` tears down every sample-count-keyed render pass and pipeline. **Mutation-checked:** with the override removed, eight of the nine A legs go red with the reported-versus-applied pair printed, and C2 reads `stores the applied count (8 == 4)`. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **241/241**, full `ctest` **9078/9099**. The 21 full-suite failures are the known audio/content/lifecycle/C-API set, unchanged from the previous row, and no `Vulkan_*` test is among them. The full suite was run rather than skipped because this changes a value the shared `GraphicsDevice` writes into `PresentationParameters`, which is precisely the F-21 shape. |
| VULKAN-348 | Decide what the applied back-buffer and depth-stencil formats are, in XNA terms | ✅ | **Both now report what is in effect, following the two precedents already in this renderer rather than inventing a third rule.** **Observed:** neither `GetAppliedBackBufferFormatEXT` nor `GetAppliedDepthStencilFormatEXT` was overridden, so `NormalizeAppliedPresentationFormats` (`GraphicsDevice.cpp:133`) wrote the *requested* formats into `PresentationParameters` as though they had been honoured — while the swapchain takes what the surface offers (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:2767`-`2770`) and `FindDepthFormat()` picks a depth format the request does not name. **The decisions this row owed, and how each was settled.** *(a) The mapping.* `VK_FORMAT_UNDEFINED` → `None`, `D16_UNORM` → `Depth16`, `X8_D24_UNORM_PACK32` and `D32_SFLOAT` → `Depth24`, `D16/D24/D32_*_S8_UINT` → `Depth24Stencil8`. Two rows are lossy on purpose and the code says which way they round: a 32-bit format reports as the nearest XNA name that agrees about the **stencil plane**, because that is the property a caller can act on — `StencilEnable` either works or it does not — and precision has no XNA enumerator to be precise with. *(b) Request or substitute.* Substitute. `VULKAN-347` settled the identical question for the sample count, and `SupportsCapability(StencilBuffer)` (`:1924`-`:1932`) already answers from `depthFormat_` rather than from anything requested, for a reason it states: on a device that took the no-stencil fallback, `StencilEnable` cannot work however correctly the pipeline maps it. *(c) `DepthFormat::None`.* Report the real format. `CreateDepthResources()` calls `FindDepthFormat()` **unconditionally** (`:4416`), so the back buffer always has a depth buffer; reporting `None` would describe a buffer that exists as absent, and the capability query already reports it as present. **Two findings that only appeared during implementation.** `IRenderTargetCubeRenderer` **declares no applied-format virtual at all** — an override written for `VulkanRenderTargetCubeRenderer` failed to compile, and was removed rather than worked around, so a `RenderTargetCube`'s applied depth format is simply not reportable through this seam. And the back buffer and render targets **disagree about `None`**: a render target honours it (`depthVkFormat_` stays `VK_FORMAT_UNDEFINED`, so the mapping reports `None` with no special case) while the back buffer allocates depth regardless. Whether that unconditional allocation is itself right is **not** answered here — this row reports what exists; if it is a defect it earns its own row. **Test:** `Vulkan_AppliedFormats` (`vulkan_applied_formats_test.cpp`), **3/3**, driven through `GraphicsDevice::SetPresentationParameters` for the same reason `VULKAN-347` had to use it: `Reset()` and `ApplyChanges` write the count back from `ApplyMultiSampleCount`'s own return, so a leg driven through either passes with the fix reverted. `Depth16` is the discriminating request because `FindDepthFormat()` prefers stencil-capable formats and never returns `D16_UNORM` for the back buffer. **Leg C is the one worth having:** it asserts the stored depth format and `SupportsCapability(StencilBuffer)` **agree about the stencil plane**, not merely that the stored value differs from the request. **Mutation-checked:** with both overrides removed all three legs fail, and leg C fails as `stored says no, capability says yes` — two answers to one question at one seam, which is F-08's shape and what this row removes. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **250/250**, full `ctest` **9086/9107**. 21 failures, none in `Vulkan_*`. Two differ from the previous row's list — `DynamicSoundEffectInstanceTest.PendingBufferCount…` and `TwoProcessLoopbackTest.HostMigrationPromotes…` — and **both pass in isolation** (1.32 s and 0.79 s). Worth stating precisely, because it corrects the shorthand used in earlier rows: this run was **`-j1`**, so these are not ctest-parallelism flakes. They are sensitive to machine load, and this machine is shared with another session's builds and test suites. Passing in isolation is what separates them from a regression; "contention" was the right instinct and the wrong mechanism. |
| VULKAN-349 | Decide whether the back buffer should allocate depth when `DepthFormat::None` was requested | ✅ | **Measured, left alone — and the cost that matters turned out not to be the one the row was opened about.** **(c) The machinery, which decides the answer on its own:** `depthFormat_` is compared against `VK_FORMAT_UNDEFINED` in exactly **one** place in the whole renderer — `SupportsCapability(DepthStencilBuffer)` (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:1933`). Everywhere else a depth attachment is assumed to exist. Gating the allocation would therefore need a no-depth variant of the swapchain render pass *and* of its load/store variants, of the framebuffers, and of every pipeline keyed on depth state. That is a change to the core of the renderer. **(b) The memory it would save:** the depth image is `swapchainExtent` × `sampleCount` at 4 bytes per sample for `D24_UNORM_S8_UINT` — 1.5 MB at 800×480, 8.3 MB at 1080p, 33 MB at 1080p with 4× MSAA. Real, bounded, and out of proportion to a core render-pass change for a request XNA games rarely make (the XNA default is `Depth24`). **(a) The real cost is portability, not memory, and it is worth stating because the row did not anticipate it.** A game that asks for `DepthFormat::None` on Vulkan gets a working depth buffer: `DepthStencilState.DepthBufferEnable` has an effect where on a renderer that honours `None` it would not. So a game *developed against Vulkan* could depend on depth under `None` and break elsewhere. That is a `SEMANTIC_DIVERGENCE`, not a resource leak, and it is the thing to weigh if this is ever reopened. **What `VULKAN-348` already did about the visible half:** `PresentationParameters.DepthStencilFormat` now reports the format that really exists rather than the `None` that was asked for, so the divergence is at least **legible** to a game that looks — which is the smallest honest response short of the core change. **Verdict: left alone, with the divergence recorded rather than the allocation removed** (§26 rule 3). The row's explicit non-goal was removing it because it is unrequested; the measurement is what turns that into a decision. **No test added:** `Vulkan_AppliedFormats` already asserts the reporting half, and a test asserting that an unrequested depth buffer exists would pin a divergence in place as though it were a contract. |
| VULKAN-350 | `GetViewportSize` ignores the presentation mode, so `FixedHeightDynamicWidth` is half applied | ✅ | **Fixed. `GetViewportSize` now answers the LOGICAL question the presentation mode defines, and `GetPresentedRectEXT` keeps answering the physical one.** **The split, which is the whole of this row:** `GraphicsDevice::UpdateViewportFromWindow()` reads two different things from a renderer — the logical size that becomes `GraphicsDevice.Viewport.Width/Height`, and the physical rectangle that is pushed to the GPU. `VULKAN-330`/`VULKAN-331` implemented `PresentationMode` in the physical half; the logical half still returned `surfaceInfo_.drawableSize` unmapped, so CNA's **default** mode did not pin its height and a real window resize moved it. **Done:** deliberately the same algorithm as `EasyGLSurfaceState::GetLogicalSize` — the client size (drawable ÷ `displayScale`, because a HiDPI drawable is the same window), then `height = virtualHeight_` with the width following the client aspect under `FixedHeightDynamicWidth`, and `virtualWidth_` otherwise. Mirroring it is the same argument `GetPresentedRectEXT` already makes for its physical twin: a mode that means different things on two renderers is a word, not a contract. **A device with no virtual resolution returns the client size — byte-identical to this function's previous behaviour**, which is why nothing that does not set one can move. **Test:** `easygl_real_window_resize_test.cpp` is registered as `Vulkan_RealWindowResize`, **4/4**. It failed on exactly this leg before the change and `VULKAN-337` recorded that measurement, so the before/after is on the record rather than asserted. **The three consumers this row was required to name, checked explicitly rather than left to the suite** — `Vulkan_BackbufferReadbackDimension` (its element counts derive from the backbuffer description, and `VULKAN-404` is a recent reminder of what a wrong size there costs), `Vulkan_SpriteBatch_RenderTargetSize` (the ortho divisor) and `Vulkan_PresentationMode` (the physical half) — plus `Vulkan_ViewportState`, `Vulkan_ViewportResetAfterResize` and `Vulkan_BackbufferResize`: **6/6**. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **312/312**; full `ctest` in §7.5. **Dependencies:** `VULKAN-330` ✅, `VULKAN-331` ✅, `VULKAN-337` ✅.

---

## 22. Phase 10 — Queries and other ordinary facilities (`VULKAN-370`–`VULKAN-389`)

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-370 | Answer `PixelCountIsPreciseEXT` deliberately on Vulkan | ✅ | **F-14 said "probably correct"; it was not.** A Vulkan occlusion query is only required to produce an exact count when the device's `occlusionQueryPrecise` feature is **enabled** and the query is begun with `VK_QUERY_CONTROL_PRECISE_BIT`. This renderer did **neither** -- `CreateLogicalDevice` did not request the feature, `vkCmdBeginQuery(... , 0)` passed no flags -- and inherited the shared `true` default. So it promised XNA's real Direct3D 9 tally while asking the device only "did anything pass", and the lensflare idiom (`PixelCount()` over an area) is exactly what that breaks. **Fix:** enable the feature where the device offers it, pass the PRECISE bit only then (passing it without the feature is a usage error), and override `PixelCountIsPreciseEXT()` to report that same flag -- so on a device that cannot count, the honest `false` reaches `OcclusionQuery::isPixelCountPreciseEXT()`. **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_OcclusionQuery_Precision` (`vulkan_occlusionquery_precision_test.cpp`), **4/4**. An occluder covers the left half at a nearer depth and the queried quad covers the whole frame at a farther one, so the surviving fragment count is known in advance: **2048** exactly, asserted as that number rather than as `> 0` (a boolean-shaped query answers 1). **The measurement that shaped the test:** llvmpipe counts exactly *even without* the precise bit, so "asked for precise" and "did not ask and got lucky" are indistinguishable through the public API -- which is precisely how the unearned promise survived unnoticed. Leg D closes that from the outside: passing the PRECISE bit without the feature is `VUID-vkCmdBeginQuery-queryType-00800`, so a silent validation log is what shows the bit and the feature agree. **Probes, both run:** with the feature not enabled the report becomes `precise=false` and the test takes its honest branch (3/3 legs, no failure -- it does not punish a device for being honest); with the feature claimed but not enabled, leg D fails with `vkCmdBeginQuery(): flags includes VK_QUERY_CONTROL_PRECISE_BIT, but occlusionQueryPrecise feature was not enabled.` **Suite:** 220/221. |
| VULKAN-371 | Prove occlusion-query reset and reuse on Vulkan | ✅ | `occlusion_query_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_OcclusionQuery_Cycle`. `Vulkan_OcclusionQuery_PixelCount` proves a single query in both directions; the **reset and reuse cycle** is the one that catches a `VkQueryPool` slot reused without `vkCmdResetQueryPool`, which is undefined behaviour rather than a wrong number. **Registered and green on the first run, no production change** — which §26 rule 4 makes a real result: the evidence now exists where it did not. **Measured in the same run as the other 22 registrations of this batch:** `^Vulkan_` **308/308**. Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move.
| VULKAN-372 | Audit the remaining ordinary facilities EasyGL exposes and Vulkan is expected to support | ✅ | **Swept, four areas, one finding — and the finding is one a reader would not have guessed, which is the argument for sweeping rather than reasoning.** **(1) `GraphicsAdapter` / `DisplayMode` enumeration.** The display list is the platform's, not the renderer's, and the shared unit suites (`GraphicsAdapterTests.cpp`, `DisplayModeTests.cpp`, `GraphicsResourceDisplayModeCollectionTests.cpp`) cover it renderer-independently. What *is* renderer-facing is the registry's `adapterQueries` hook set, and **this renderer registers none** while EasyGL does — so a new probe, `Vulkan_AdapterQueryContract`, measures what that costs. Leg B is reassuring: the shared fallback table and the device **agree** that a `Bgr565` render target falls back to `Color`. Leg C is **F-34**: asking the adapter about 4× MSAA on a device that has it answers `selectedMultiSampleCount = 0`, so a game that follows XNA's ask-then-create idiom concludes this renderer has no MSAA. Left as a recorded MEASUREMENT rather than a red assertion, with **`VULKAN-187`** owning the fix and the leg it will turn green. **(2) `GraphicsDevice.Textures` / `VertexTextures`.** Shared-layer collections; what reaches this renderer is the per-slot sampler and texture binding, which `Vulkan_TextureAddressMode`, `_PointSamplingContract`, `_TextureFilter*`, `_SamplerAddressW` and the dual-texture and env-map slot-sampler contracts already exercise, and one shared example that uses the collection directly (`rendertargetcube_getdata_contract_test.cpp`) is registered here. `SHARED_LOGIC`, no row. **(3) `GetBackBufferData` variants.** Five Vulkan CTests — `Vulkan_BackbufferFirstRead`, `_BackbufferReadbackDimension`, `_BackbufferReject`, `_Backbuffer_PassOrder`, `_BackbufferResize` — plus every golden and pixel test in the suite, which all read the backbuffer through it. Covered; no row. **(4) `MouseCursor::FromTexture2D`'s colour-transfer route.** It calls `Texture2D::GetData` and then checks its own two refusals (a non-`Color` format, a hot spot outside the texture), both format-independent shared logic; the readback underneath is what this renderer owns and it is covered by the Texture2D `GetData` tests. `SHARED_LOGIC`, no row — and stated rather than left silent, because "no test named MouseCursor" is exactly the shape that gets re-audited every six months. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **348/348** with the new probe. |

---

## 23. Phase 11 — Validation, robustness and stress (`VULKAN-390`–`VULKAN-429`)

Correctness and bounded resource behaviour, not optimization. No row here may be created from a
guess about performance; §13 of the instruction that produced this plan names the four pathologies
that qualify, and each row below cites the one it addresses.

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-390 | Make descriptor-pool exhaustion fail loudly instead of drawing white (F-06) | ✅ | **Fixed, and F-06 understated it.** `GetOrCreateTexSamplerDescSet` now chains another pool of `MaxDescriptorSets` when every existing one refuses, and raises a **named** exception if the device refuses that too (`...Refused rather than drawing a substituted texture.`). Each cache entry carries the pool it came from, because a set freed against the wrong pool is undefined behaviour and there is no longer only one; eviction routes through the existing `poolDescriptorSets` retirement path, and teardown destroys the chained pools. **What the mutation probe showed, which changes the finding's severity:** restoring the old `return defaultWhiteDescSet_` does not draw white on the SpriteBatch path -- it **binds `VK_NULL_HANDLE` and segfaults**. `EnsureDefaultWhiteTexture()` is called from the three 3D draw routes only (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:11171`, `:11454`, `:11762`), so in a 2D-only frame `defaultWhiteDescSet_` has never been created. Measured: `vkCmdBindDescriptorSets(): pDescriptorSets[0] (VK_NULL_HANDLE) ...`, `vkCmdDrawIndexed(): ... uses set #0 but that set is not bound`, then `Segmentation fault (core dumped)`, exit 139. F-06 called this a silent white sprite; on the route it is most likely to be reached from it was a crash. **Reaching the arm at all was the hard part, and the measurement is the reason the test is shaped as it is:** the exhaustion is **not reachable by volume** on either driver here. `vkAllocateDescriptorSets` keeps succeeding past the pool's `maxSets` -- which the spec permits, since running out is a runtime error an implementation *may* report and not a usage violation the layer flags -- and **4000** simultaneously live pairs still did not trigger it. Shrinking the pool to 8 sets did not either. A test built on volume alone would have passed while executing none of the new code. The failure is therefore **injected**, through the test-only `SetTexSamplerDescriptorAllocationFailuresForTestEXT` knob -- the other option this row allows, and the seed of the hook `VULKAN-391` needs. **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_DescriptorPoolOverflow` (`vulkan_descriptor_pool_overflow_test.cpp`), **5/5** -- one injected failure chains exactly one pool and the sprite drawn across it still shows its own texture; 640 distinct live textures in one frame each draw their own (the production-shape volume leg, with the pool count printed as evidence that this driver did not enforce `maxSets`); an injection the chained pool cannot satisfy either is refused by name; the layer stays silent. **Mutation:** the old fallback fails leg A (`1 -> 1 pool(s)`) and then crashes, as above. **Non-goal honoured:** the descriptor strategy is unchanged -- this is a chained pool, not a redesign. **Suite:** 217/219. |
| VULKAN-391 | Make the eleven `vkAllocateDescriptorSets` failure paths consistent | ✅ | **One contract, applied to all eleven, and the contract is the observable one: a caller never receives `VK_NULL_HANDLE`, and a refusal is BY NAME, at the draw.** That is the only contract worth having here -- the sole thing a caller can do with a descriptor set is bind it, and `VULKAN-390`'s mutation probe already measured what binding a null costs: `pDescriptorSets[0] (VK_NULL_HANDLE)` and a segfault. **What changed:** the seven `return VK_NULL_HANDLE` arms (DualTexture, EnvMap, LitTextured, FogTex3D, Skinned, Pbr, PbrSkinned) now throw, each naming its own family; `EnsureDefaultWhiteTexture`'s site, which did not even **look** at the result, throws too; and all eleven now go through `AllocateOneDescriptorSet`, leaving exactly one raw `vkAllocateDescriptorSets` call in the file -- inside that helper. **Routing them through it is what made the arms testable at all**: the helper carries `VULKAN-390`'s fault-injection hook, and it used to sit beside the one pool that called it, which is precisely why the other ten had no test. It moved to the top of the file. The hook is renamed `SetDescriptorAllocationFailuresForTestEXT` to match what it now covers. **The one place the contract is NOT uniform underneath, recorded rather than papered over:** the shared combined-image-sampler pool *recovers* by chaining another pool (`VULKAN-390`) before it refuses; the seven family pools refuse straight away. Observably identical -- never a null, always a named refusal -- and different in effort, because only the shared pool has a chaining path. **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_DescriptorContractUniformity` (`vulkan_descriptor_contract_uniformity_test.cpp`), **13/13**. Each family is drawn once to build its pool and cache, then drawn again with a DIFFERENT texture and one injected allocation failure, then a third time with the injection disarmed -- so the test asserts the refusal names the right family AND that the refusal left the renderer usable. **The hook gained a `skipFirst` parameter, and finding out why is the interesting part:** leg A's draw takes a set from the *shared* pool before it takes one from its own, so an unskipped failure was absorbed by the chaining path and the draw simply **succeeded** -- the test caught its own first version asserting a refusal that never came. **Mutation:** restoring `return VK_NULL_HANDLE` in the PbrEffect arm alone does not fail the test -- it **kills the process, exit 139**. That is the finding F-06 recorded as "returns null", measured. **Regression:** `Vulkan_DescriptorPoolOverflow` (VULKAN-390's own) stays 5/5. |
| VULKAN-392 | Remove the per-buffer-dispose full-device stall (F-13) | ✅ | **Removed, and the removal is counted rather than asserted: 120 buffer create/destroy cycles now cost 0 full-device stalls, where before they cost exactly 120.** **Observed:** `VulkanVertexBufferRenderer::ReleaseVulkanResources` and the index-buffer twin each opened with `vkDeviceWaitIdle`. `DrawUserPrimitives`/`DrawUserIndexedPrimitives` allocate a throwaway `VertexBuffer` per call (`GraphicsDevice.cpp:1753`+), so on Vulkan every such draw ended in a full-device stall — the pathology §13 names, on a route a game may use every frame. **Done:** both paths retire their `VkBuffer` and `VkDeviceMemory` into the same fence-gated queue textures and render targets already use (`RetiredResources`, `RetireResources`, `ProcessRetiredResources`). `RetiredResources` gains a `buffers` vector, freed **before** the memories in the same drain loop, because a `VkBuffer` must be destroyed before the memory it is bound to. **Why retiring is safer than the stall it replaces, not merely cheaper** — this is the part that made the change small: the deferred replay in `RecordCommandBuffer` binds only renderer-owned per-frame buffers (`spriteVB_`, `frame3DVB_`, `frame3DInstVB_`) and **never** a user buffer handle, because a queued draw carries copied vertex and index bytes (`Pending3DDraw::vbData`/`ibData`); the only command that ever names this buffer is the staging copy inside `SetData`, which `EndOneTimeCommands` has already waited on. So there was no in-flight reference for the stall to protect, and the fence gate is a strictly stronger guarantee than the immediate destroy that followed it. **The counter, and why it is trustworthy:** "we removed the stall" is a claim a test must be able to check, so every `vkDeviceWaitIdle` in this renderer now goes through a single `DeviceWaitIdleEXT()` funnel that increments `GetDeviceWaitIdleCountEXT()`. The count is complete by construction — a stall reintroduced **anywhere**, not only in the two functions this row touched, fails the test. `GetRetiredBufferCountEXT()` was added alongside it so the test can show the cycles it is pricing really happened instead of inferring them from a pixel. **Test:** `Vulkan_BufferDisposeStall` (`vulkan_buffer_dispose_stall_test.cpp`), **4/4**, on the scene `VULKAN-134` unblocked. Leg A is the control — 40 `DrawUserPrimitives` per frame render a red quad, without which "zero stalls" is satisfied by drawing nothing. Leg B prices the draw path, counting from **after** a warm-up frame so one-time pipeline creation is not charged to it. Leg C shows 120 buffers really reached the retirement queue. Leg D asserts the validation layer captured nothing — retiring a handle still in use would be a use-after-free it sees. **Mutation-checked:** restoring the stall in the vertex-buffer path alone turns leg B red with `120 full-device stalls`, one per destroyed buffer, which is the defect quantified. **Discovered while counting, handed to a new row rather than folded in:** three of the four remaining stalls are teardown or reconfiguration and unarguable; the fourth, in `FlushDeferredRenderTarget`, is mid-frame and on the render-target-sampling path. That is `VULKAN-399`, and it is deliberately opened as a *measure first* row rather than an optimization. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **246/246**, full `ctest` **9078/9100**. |
| VULKAN-393 | Gate the Vulkan suite on zero CNA-attributable validation messages | ✅ | **Done, and it gates every test rather than a representative one — which is the row's own acceptance met by a wider instrument than it proposed.** **What was built:** a block at the **end** of `modules/renderers/vulkan/examples/CMakeLists.txt` that reads the directory's `TESTS` property and appends `\\[Vulkan Validation\\]` to every registration's `FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION`. That string is the exact prefix the debug messenger prints for every message the Khronos layer reports at severity WARNING or above (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:2596`), so the gate is *any* message, not a message class. **All 254** Vulkan CTests carry it; the position in the file is load-bearing and is commented as such, because a registration added below the block would be silently ungated. **Why an output gate and not the in-process assertion the row proposed.** `VULKAN-405`'s leak was reported *during* `vkDestroyDevice` — after the last line of the test body, out of the very object whose `validationMessages_` an in-process check would have read. Teardown is where object tracking names leaks, so a gate that cannot see teardown cannot see that whole class. The process's output can. **Existing patterns are preserved, not replaced.** `FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION` is a *list*, so the nine registrations that already carried one (`VUID-vkCmdDraw-None-09600`, `VUID-|Validation Error|Validation Warning|SYNC-HAZARD`, …) keep it and gain this one. Checked in the generated `CTestTestfile.cmake`: `Vulkan_MrtMipFinalization` reads `"VUID-|…|SYNC-HAZARD;\\[Vulkan Validation\\]"`. The block also skips a test that already has the pattern, so `VULKAN-405`'s explicit one on `Vulkan_MRT_MixedFormats` is not duplicated. **And it matters that the neighbours' pattern was not enough:** an Object Tracking report contains none of `VUID-`, `Validation Error`, `Validation Warning` or `SYNC-HAZARD`, so the pre-existing gates would have let `VULKAN-405`'s message straight through. **The allowlist, corrected.** This row asked for *“a documented allowlist of driver/layer messages with a reason each”*. Measured: after `VULKAN-404`, `-405` and `-406` there are **zero** messages across the suite, and not one of `VULKAN-009`'s original three was a driver or layer artefact — all three were ours. So there is no message to allow, and a per-message allowlist would be a mechanism with nothing in it. What exists instead is a per-**test** exemption list, `CNA_VULKAN_VALIDATION_GATE_EXEMPT`, **empty**, in one auditable place, whose comment requires the exact message and the reason beside any name added to it. `VULKAN-477` requires that no entry is ever CNA-attributable. **It cannot pass vacuously**, which is the failure mode a zero-message gate has: four tests assert `VulkanRenderer::IsValidationActiveEXT()` before judging anything — `Vulkan_RenderTarget_ProducerConsumer`, `Vulkan_Swapchain_Sync`, and since `VULKAN-404`/`-406` `Vulkan_BackbufferReadbackDimension` and `Vulkan_CubeVolume_SetDataContract` — so a build where the layer never loaded goes red rather than reporting a clean suite. **Sync validation** stays opt-in (`sRequestSyncValidation`) and is still exercised by `Vulkan_RenderTarget_ProducerConsumer_SyncVal` and `Vulkan_Swapchain_Sync`, which enable it in process; the gate judges their output like everyone else's. **Mutation-checked, and deliberately not by re-breaking a fixed defect** — that would only re-test `VULKAN-404`/`-406`'s own tests, which have in-process assertions of their own and would fail anyway. Instead the gate's *pattern* was swapped for a string the suite really prints (`CNA: graphics renderer: VULKAN`) and CMake re-run with no recompilation: `Vulkan_SpriteBatch_Scale` and `Vulkan_NpotTexture` — two tests that had **no** `FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION` before this row — both went red. That is the proof the gate is live on the tests it was added to rather than only on the ones that already had a pattern. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **254/254** with the gate active on all 254. Full `ctest` **not** re-run: the change is CMake-only and touches nothing but Vulkan test properties, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move. **Dependencies:** `VULKAN-009` (the inventory), and `VULKAN-404`/`-405`/`-406`, which emptied it.
| VULKAN-394 | Bound the pipeline caches under ordinary state churn | ✅ | **Measured on every axis, and the row's own two outcomes both happened — most of the key is bounded and closes the row by recording it, one axis is not and gets `VULKAN-162`.** New CTest `Vulkan_PipelineCacheBound` (`vulkan_pipeline_cache_bound_test.cpp`), **5/5**. **Bounded:** 32 identical draws build **no** further pipelines (leg A, the control); an ordinary four-blend-state mix stops growing the moment every state has been seen and adds **nothing** over 16 further frames (leg B — this is the case the row cares about, and it is the one that must not grow with frame count); and the declaration axis is bounded by how many declarations exist rather than by frames — five distinct ones build five pipelines and drawing all five again builds none (leg D). **Not bounded:** 32 distinct `MultiSampleMask` values build **30** pipelines. It is a 32-bit integer the game sets, so the axis has no ceiling, and — the part that makes it a defect rather than a cost — most of those bits **cannot change the result**, because a sample mask only selects among samples that exist and the pipeline is single-sampled unless the draw is MSAA. Recorded as F-30, owned by `VULKAN-162`, which can bound it by narrowing the key term rather than by evicting anything. **A mistake worth keeping:** leg D's first version swept the *stride* as well as the offset, which measures program selection rather than the declaration axis — the fidelity guard refused it by name, which is the guard doing its job and is why the leg now holds one program fixed. |
| VULKAN-395 | Bound the sampler cache | ✅ | **Answered the way the row allowed for: the key space was established, the bounded part was asserted, and the unbounded part opened a row.** New CTest `Vulkan_SamplerCacheBound` (`vulkan_sampler_cache_bound_test.cpp`), **4/4**. **The key space is not what the row assumed.** `SamplerStateKey` has seven fields and five are enumerations — filter, three address modes, anisotropy — which would give a few hundred keys and a real bound. The other two are **caller-supplied numbers**: `MaxMipLevel` is an `int` and `MipMapLevelOfDetailBias` a `float`. **Measured:** 32 draws with one sampler state create **no** further samplers — leg A, the control, without which a growth measurement proves nothing — while 64 distinct bias values add **63**, one per distinct value. The 64th is not a miss: bias 0.0 is the state already cached. **Why that matters rather than being a curiosity:** animating a LOD bias is what a LOD bias is for, and this renderer allocates a `VkSampler` per frame for one. `maxSamplerAllocationCount` is finite and the test reads it from the device — 32768 here — so it is a route to exhausting a hardware ceiling, not just to using memory. **A second defect fell out of reading the failure path**, and it is not this row's to fix: when `vkCreateSampler` does fail, the null propagates into `GetOrCreateTexSamplerDescSet`, which answers it with `defaultWhiteDescSet_` — the silent white substitution `VULKAN-390` and `VULKAN-391` removed from all eleven descriptor-allocation sites, still alive one layer down. **Both are recorded as F-29 with owners: `VULKAN-160` (bound it, which needs descriptor invalidation and the retirement queues, so it is not a one-liner) and `VULKAN-161` (make exhaustion loud).** The diagnostics this row added — `GetSamplerCacheSizeEXT` and `GetMaxSamplerAllocationCountEXT` — are what both rows will test against. |
| VULKAN-396 | Measure the cost of `vkQueueWaitIdle` per one-time command and decide | ✅ | **Measured, and the decision is: the wait itself stays, but the number it produced opened `VULKAN-401`.** **How it was measured, rather than reasoned about:** `EndOneTimeCommands` now times its own `vkQueueWaitIdle` and counts its submissions (`GetOneTimeCommandWaitNanosEXT`, `GetOneTimeCommandCountEXT`), so the figure comes from the production path under a real workload instead of from a microbenchmark of a call. **The workload:** 48 `Texture2D` creations of 256×256, which is what content loading actually looks like — a burst of uploads through the staging path. **The numbers, on llvmpipe under Xvfb:** 144 one-time commands for 48 uploads, **128.2 µs per queue wait**, 45.6 ms of work, and the waits are **40.5 %** of that wall clock. **The decision on the wait itself:** it stays. It exists because `EndOneTimeCommands` frees the command buffer on the next line and freeing one still executing is illegal, so it is a correctness requirement of the shape the function has — not a defensive habit. Nothing measured here is a robustness problem: no incorrectness, no race, no leak, and this row's own rule reserves a remediation task for that. **What the measurement did find, and why it earns a row anyway:** three of those waits are paid per texture where one would do. The upload is a barrier, a copy and a barrier, and each opens its own command buffer because `TransitionLevelLayout` allocates one per call rather than recording into a caller's. That is `VULKAN-401`, opened *with* the measurement exactly as this row requires, and classified as structure rather than robustness so the next reader is not misled about its severity. **Test:** `Vulkan_OneTimeCommandCost` (`vulkan_one_time_command_cost_test.cpp`), **2/2**, kept as a permanent record rather than a one-off script. It splits its output the way `VULKAN-008`'s snapshot splits its entries: the **structural** facts are asserted because they hold on any device — at least one command per upload, and the waits are real time rather than a no-op — while the **timing** is printed and never failed on, because a suite that fails on an absolute duration reports the GPU rather than the renderer. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **249/249**. |
| VULKAN-397 | Run the Vulkan example suite under ASan/UBSan for CNA-owned CPU code | ✅ | **Run, clean, and the scope is stated rather than implied — the row says "the suite" and this is nine tests, for a reason that is arithmetic rather than convenience.** **What was built:** `build-asan/`, the directory `CLAUDE.md` names, configured `-DCNA_SANITIZE=address,undefined -DCNA_SANITIZE_OPTIMIZATION=O0` against the same `sharp-runtimenext` checkout `cmake-build-vulkan` uses. **Two configuration failures on the way, both recorded because the next person will hit them:** at `O1` the build dies in `sharp_runtime_io` on `-Werror=maybe-uninitialized` false positives from libstdc++ 14's `<regex>` under `std::function`'s move constructor — a GCC/ASan interaction, not CNA code, and `O0` avoids it; and the default `CNA_SHARP_RUNTIME_ROOT` points at the sibling `sharp-runtime`, which is another session's tree and currently lacks `StoragePaths::SetIsolatedStorageRootOverride`, so the root must be passed explicitly. **Why nine and not 347:** an instrumented example links to **~250 MB**. The whole set would be ~86 GB of binaries on the one SSD `CLAUDE.md` opens by asking not to wear out, so the subset is chosen to cover what this campaign touched and what is most likely to hold a CPU-side defect: the 120-frame stress run (`VULKAN-398`), all four custom-effect tests (`VULKAN-252`/`-253`/`-254`/`-255`/`-164`), the device-loss contract (`VULKAN-334`), the 35-leg pipeline-key sweep, render-target lifetime shapes and swapchain churn. **Result: nothing.** Every one passes with its ordinary leg count, and across all nine there are **zero** UBSan `runtime error` lines, **zero** `ERROR: AddressSanitizer` reports and **zero** leak stacks containing a `CNA::` or `Microsoft::` frame. The exit code is 1 and the `SUMMARY:` line says `368 byte(s) leaked in 4 allocation(s)` — four driver/X allocations with no CNA frame, which is exactly the reading this row's own instruction demands and the reason it forbids judging by that line. **The row expected `VULKAN-130`/`VULKAN-131` to show here** — the unchecked `VertexBuffer` and `IndexBuffer` uploads, F-01 and F-02. They do not, because both were fixed before this ran; the stress test rewrites a vertex buffer 120 times and ASan sees nothing. That is the expectation being **met by the fixes**, not a gap in the instrumentation, and the nine binaries were deleted afterwards with the object tree kept (`CLAUDE.md` rule 7). |
| VULKAN-398 | Repeated-use stress across the whole surface | ✅ | **Written as one test that runs 120 frames of everything at once and asserts the LAST frame, and it found the shape of its own first mistake before it found anything else.** Each frame: a texture re-uploaded into the same object, a render target bound, cleared and unbound, a `VertexBuffer` rewritten and drawn through `BasicEffect`, a `SpriteBatch` cycle drawing that texture **and a per-frame texture created and destroyed inside the iteration**, and a window resize every 40 frames — four swapchain recreations across the run, counted, so nothing here can pass by never being disturbed. **What it asserts is deliberately not "it still runs":** the render target holds *this* frame's clear and the backbuffer holds *this* frame's sprite colour on frame **119**, not frame 0; the four cache counters `VULKAN-394`/`VULKAN-395` established are **identical** at frame 40 and frame 119; and both deferred queues are empty after the last present. **The per-frame texture is what makes the cache assertion mean something** — it would add one `(view, sampler)` descriptor per frame if `VULKAN-213`'s eviction did not run. **The mistake worth recording, because the row would have shipped with it:** the first version compared a warm-up sample taken after a present with an end sample taken after the final *readback*, and reported `pipelines 2 → 3` as growth. It was the readback's own pipeline, built once. Both samples now come from the same point in the frame cycle, and the readback got **its own leg**: two readbacks in a row leave the counters where the first put them. A measurement that turned out to be an artefact became an assertion rather than a footnote. **Mutation:** with `EvictSampledViewFromCaches` disabled, the descriptor-set cache goes **43 at frame 40 → 122 at frame 119** — one per frame, exactly the unbounded growth this row exists to catch — and leg C still passes, which is the right split. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_RepeatedUseStress`, **7/7**; `^Vulkan_` **346/346** on llvmpipe and on RADV. No production change. |
| VULKAN-399 | Decide whether `FlushDeferredRenderTarget`'s full-device stall can be replaced by a fence wait | ✅ | **Measured, left alone — and the row's own premise was wrong, which is the finding.** **What this row assumed when opened:** the stall is reached "whenever a game renders to a target and samples it in the same frame — shadow maps, post-process chains, reflection passes", i.e. a routine path. **What the code says:** `FlushDeferredRenderTarget` has exactly **two** callers, `VulkanRenderTargetRenderer::GetData` and its cube twin (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:1175`, `:14687`). It is a **CPU readback** path, not a rendering one. Rendering into a target and sampling it never reaches it — the deferred model records both and replays them in one ordered stream at `Present()`. **And even a readback often does not reach it:** the function returns early when the target has no pending cycle (`if (!hasPendingCycle) return;`), so a `GetData` issued after anything has drained the queues costs nothing. **Measured, after two drafts that measured the wrong thing:** the first version read **0 stalls** for a readback because an earlier `GetBackBufferData` had already drained the queues; the second credited the readback's stall to the sampling draws because its counting window enclosed both. With the windows corrected: **24 sprites sampling a target cost 0 stalls; one `RenderTarget2D.GetData` issued while the target still has queued work costs exactly 1, at 0.68 ms** on llvmpipe under Xvfb. **Decision: the stall stays, and this is not reluctance.** A CPU readback is inherently synchronizing — the caller has asked to see finished pixels — so a device wait on that path is expected rather than pathological, and §13's concern is a stall in a *routine* path, which this is not. Replacing it with a fence would save nothing a caller did not already ask for. **Non-goal honoured:** the row forbade removing the stall because it is a stall, and the measurement is what makes "left alone" a verdict rather than a shrug (§26 rule 3). **Test:** `Vulkan_RtFlushStallCost` (`vulkan_rt_flush_stall_cost_test.cpp`), **4/4**, kept as a permanent record. Structural facts are asserted — the target really was sampled, the readback really read it, sampling costs **zero** stalls and a readback costs a bounded one — while the timings are printed and never failed on, for the reason `VULKAN-396` recorded. **Consequence for the matrix:** §10's *Full-device stalls in ordinary paths* row can now read `PARITY`: the dispose stall is gone (`VULKAN-392`), the one-time-command wait is a correctness requirement (`VULKAN-396`), and this one is not in an ordinary path at all. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **254/254**. Full `ctest` not re-run: the production change is instrumentation-free — only a test was added. |
| VULKAN-400 | Determine whether a full-backbuffer `SpriteBatch` draw before a 3D draw breaks the frame on Vulkan | ✅ | **It does not — and the control run is what makes that worth saying, because it also does not break on EasyGL, where the finding is documented.** **Where the question came from:** `easygl_spritebatch_blendstate_leak_test.cpp` deliberately draws its sprite in a small corner and says why (`:9`-`:12`, `:88`) — to avoid a "separate, unrelated, still-open" finding that a full-backbuffer `SpriteBatch` draw before any 3D draw in the same frame breaks that frame's 3D rendering entirely. `docs/migration-guide.md:119`-`120` lists it among the known issues, scoped to EasyGL, marked "investigated, root cause not yet isolated" (Task 933). No Vulkan verdict existed anywhere. **Test:** `fullscreen_spritebatch_then_3d_test.cpp`, written renderer-neutral in `modules/graphics/examples/` so one source answers the question for any family, and registered for **both** — `Vulkan_FullscreenSpriteThen3D` and `EasyGL_FullscreenSpriteThen3D`. Two legs, identical but for the sprite's size: a corner sprite (A) and a full-backbuffer sprite (B), each followed by a full-viewport red 3D quad with nothing touched in between, then a centre readback. Leg A is what isolates full coverage as the trigger — a B-only failure would name it, a failure in both would mean something broader. **Result: A and B are `(255,0,0)` on Vulkan and `(255,0,0)` on EasyGL.** So the case is correct here, and the answer this row owes is "no Vulkan defect". **Why the EasyGL run was not optional:** without it, "absent on Vulkan" could not be told apart from "this scene cannot detect the thing anywhere", and a test that fails nowhere proves nothing. Registering the control cost one target build in the existing `cmake-build-easygl`. **What this does NOT establish, stated rather than glossed:** that the original finding is fixed. Its conditions survive only as prose in a comment and a docs bullet, and this scene is one reading of them; "not reproducible by this scene on either renderer" is weaker than "fixed", and the difference matters because the work-around in the blend test and the known-issues bullet are both still in place. **Handed on rather than acted on unilaterally:** both belong to whoever owns EasyGL — `docs/migration-guide.md:119` and the corner-sprite work-around comment should be revisited with this measurement attached, and what would settle it is the original Task 933 reproduction, not this scene. Editing another renderer's known-issues list on the strength of one scene is exactly the shortcut this plan's own §9.2 treatment of stale documentation forbids. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **248/248**; `EasyGL_FullscreenSpriteThen3D` passes in its own build directory. |
| VULKAN-401 | A texture upload issues three one-time commands where one suffices | ✅ | **Fixed: 48 uploads now cost 48 one-time commands, down from 144 — exactly one each.** **Observed (by `VULKAN-396`'s measurement, which is why this row exists at all):** creating a `Texture2D` performed `TransitionImageLayout(UNDEFINED → TRANSFER_DST)`, `CopyBufferToImage`, `TransitionImageLayout(TRANSFER_DST → SHADER_READ)` — three separate `BeginOneTimeCommands`/`EndOneTimeCommands` pairs, so three submissions and three full `vkQueueWaitIdle` calls per texture. **Done:** `TransitionImageLayout` and `CopyBufferToImage` are each split into a **recording** half (`RecordImageLayoutTransition`, `RecordBufferToImageCopy`, taking the caller's `VkCommandBuffer`) and a thin standalone wrapper that opens one, records, and ends. Every other caller keeps the wrapper and is unchanged. The texture-creation path and `UpdatePixels` each open one buffer and record all three steps into it. Ordering inside the buffer is exactly what the barriers express, so batching changes the number of waits and nothing else. **A wrong turn, recorded because the measurement is what caught it:** the first attempt batched `UpdatePixels` only, and the count stayed at 144 — a freshly created texture never goes through `UpdatePixels`, it uploads from the constructor. Changing a path and *not* re-measuring would have shipped a no-op described as a fix. **The numbers, measured back to back on the same machine, four runs each rather than one:** command count 144 → **48**, which is deterministic and device-independent; wall clock 36.4/38.5/38.9/43.8 ms → 32.5/35.4/36.3/66.2 ms; wait share 36.7–40.3 % → 30.8–36.5 %. **What that does and does not support:** the structural claim is exact. The wall-clock improvement is real but modest — roughly 10 % at the median — and one *after* run landed at 66.2 ms, so no single run supports a speed claim on a machine shared with another session's builds. The row states the medians and the spread rather than the flattering number. **Test:** `Vulkan_OneTimeCommandCost` leg A tightened from "at least one each" to **exactly** one each, which is the assertion that fails at 144 and passes at 48; the timing lines stay printed and unasserted for the reason `VULKAN-396` recorded. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **249/249**, full `ctest` **9081/9103** — which also covers `VULKAN-396`'s instrumentation, committed before this run existed. The 22 failures are the known audio/content/lifecycle/C-API set; the only difference from the previous row's list is one audio-timing case swapping for another — `CueTest.PlayScalesVolumeByRpcCurve…` out, `DynamicSoundEffectInstanceTest.PendingBufferCountOnlyDropsOnceAWholeChunkIsActuallyConsumed` in — and the newcomer **passes serially in 3.83 s**, so it is the same contention family rather than a regression. This run used `-j2` after a `-j3` attempt was OOM-killed by memory pressure from other work on the machine; the lower parallelism is a local remedy, not a project-wide change. |
| VULKAN-402 | The startup capability diagnostic wrote to stdout (found inside the "known failures") | ✅ | **Found by `VULKAN-475`'s discipline, and it is the reason that row demands failures be *attributed* rather than merely counted.** **Observed:** `GraphicsDeviceRendererTest.StartupDiagnosticNeverWritesToStdout` has been in this plan's "known audio/content/lifecycle/C-API set" since the first baseline, carried forward in every §7.5 row as a pre-existing failure unrelated to this renderer. It is not unrelated. Its captured stdout is `CNA: Vulkan capabilities -- device=llvmpipe …` — **this renderer's own startup line**, printed with `std::cout` at `VulkanRenderer.cpp:2746`. **Classification:** implementation bug, and a rule violation with its own test: stdout belongs to the program's output, so a host that pipes it gets this line mixed into its data. **The fix is one word** — `std::clog`, which every other diagnostic in this renderer already used; that `std::cout` was the **only** one in the file. **Why it hid for so long, which is the part worth keeping:** a failure attributed to a bucket stops being looked at. Nineteen failures were named "the known set" in row after row, and re-running them serially (`VULKAN-475`'s own stated discriminator) showed all nineteen reproduce — so none was a flake, and one of them was ours. Counting them was never going to find it; reading one was. **Test:** none added. `GraphicsDeviceRendererTest.StartupDiagnosticNeverWritesToStdout` is the regression guard, it already existed, and it now **passes** — going from red to green is stronger evidence than any test written afterwards. **Evidence:** that test 1/1, `^Vulkan_` **254/254**. |
| VULKAN-403 | Two `.cnj` effect tests asked the capability instead of the query (F-09's tail) | ✅ | **The second defect `VULKAN-475`'s attribution pass found inside the "known failures", and the first that was a *test* asking the wrong question rather than a renderer answering wrongly.** **Observed:** `CnjEffectTest.LoadsRealCnjFixture` and `CnjStockEffectTest.CustomGlslEffectStillWorks` were the **only two** of the eighteen remaining failures that do **not** reproduce on EasyGL — the attribution method being to run the same list against `cmake-build-easygl`, where sixteen reproduce and are therefore shared rather than this renderer's. Both fail with `[ShaderEffect] Compile error: SPIR-V size must be a multiple of 4 bytes`: the `.cnj` fixtures carry GLSL and this renderer's `ShaderEffect` takes SPIR-V. **The mistake is one `CLAUDE.md` names explicitly:** both tests gated on `GraphicsCapability::CustomEffects`, which means the renderer **accepts** an effect, not that it **runs your shader source**. Vulkan accepts effects and reports `true`, so the gate opened and the test then asserted a GLSL payload was valid. The comment beside the gate had the right instinct — "gate on the capability rather than the renderer name" — and the wrong query. `ExecutesShaderEffectSourceEXT()` exists for exactly this distinction and is `false` here. **Done:** both gates now also ask that query, so a renderer that does not execute shader source takes the same branch as one with no compiler at all. **Checked, not assumed, that this does not hollow the tests out:** EasyGL's `ExecutesShaderEffectSourceEXT()` returns `true` (`EasyGLRenderer.hpp:1608`), so on that renderer both still take the `EXPECT_TRUE(IsEffectValid())` path — verified by building and running them in `cmake-build-easygl`, where they pass. A gate that silently disabled the test everywhere would be worse than the failure it removed. **Why this is not `VULKAN-256`:** that row is about **not** porting 37 EasyGL shader tests, and says so. These two are different — they run in *every* build, on shared content fixtures, and were failing rather than being skipped. **Evidence:** both tests 2/2 on Vulkan **and** 2/2 on EasyGL; `^Vulkan_` **254/254**. The known-failure count drops from 18 to **16**. |
| VULKAN-404 | `vkMapMemory` maps 8 000 bytes of a 3 404-byte allocation (validation) | ✅ | **Fixed. The row's arithmetic was right and its mechanism was wrong, and correcting that is what found the fix.** **Reproduced first, per leg:** the fixture forks eight legs, and exactly one produces the message — `--leg=G1`, the *growing* runtime resize (37×23 → 50×40). `G2` shrinks and is clean, and so are the other six. **Measured, not inferred:** a `gdb` breakpoint on `ReadBackbuffer` prints `w=37 h=23 | extent=37x23 allocWH=0x0 valid=0` then `w=50 h=40 | extent=50x40 allocWH=37x23 valid=1`. So the map is **50·40·4 = 8 000 = 0x1f40** of the **new** extent out of **37·23·4 = 3 404 = 0xd4c** allocated for the **old** one. (This row said 7 936; 0x1f40 is 8 000. The corrected figure is exactly the new backbuffer, which is the point — it is not a near-miss.) **The premise this row carried, and why it was wrong:** it guessed *“the staging buffer sized from the full swapchain extent while this test asks for a sub-region”*. There is no sub-region here. The staging buffer is deliberately whole-frame and the request was whole-frame too; what was stale was the **allocation**, left behind by the swapchain the resize destroyed. **Root cause:** `RecreateSwapchain` (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:3613`) rebuilt the swapchain, the image views, the depth resources and the framebuffers, and left `readbackStagingValid_` **true**. `ReadBackbuffer` then took its no-submit cache path — and the reallocation that grows the staging buffer lives inside `RecordCommandBuffer`'s deferred-readback block, which only a submit records. So the one path that could have resized the buffer was exactly the path being skipped. **The second consequence, which is worse than the map and which no test asserted:** that cached path would have served the pre-resize 37×23 frame *reinterpreted at the new 50-pixel width*. A caller reading the backbuffer after a resize with no intervening clear or draw got the old picture, not a blank one and not an error. This fixture does not check post-resize pixel exactness (its own comment says so and hands that to the Software control), which is why 4/4 passed over undefined behaviour. **Done — two lines, one defect.** (a) `RecreateSwapchain` clears `readbackStagingValid_`, which is the correctness half: the cache describes a swapchain that no longer exists. (b) `ReadBackbuffer` treats the cache as usable only when it actually covers the frame about to be mapped — `readbackStagingBuf_ != VK_NULL_HANDLE && readbackAllocW_ >= fullW && readbackAllocH_ >= fullH`, the same two-dimension test the reallocation below it already uses — which is the safety half: the map size is bounded by the allocation locally, so no future caller can reintroduce the overstep by forgetting to invalidate. **Test:** `Vulkan_BackbufferReadbackDimension` gains `CheckValidationClean()`, called from `Finish()` so **every leg** judges its own process: layer liveness (`VulkanRenderer::IsValidationActiveEXT()`) first, because an empty list from a layer that never loaded is not evidence, then `GetValidationMessagesEXT()` empty. Per leg rather than per suite because only one leg reaches the defect, and a suite-wide count would not say which. The block is `#if defined(CNA_RENDERER_VULKAN)`, so the six other renderers registering this source are untouched. **Mutation-checked, both halves:** with (a) and (b) both reverted, `G1` is **5/6** and names the message verbatim while `A1` stays 4/4 — the leg that fails is the leg that resizes. With (b) alone restored, `G1` is 6/6 again, which is how the safety half was shown to bound the map on its own rather than being decoration. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **254/254**; the suite-wide validation inventory `VULKAN-009` took drops **3 → 2**, and the two that remain are `VULKAN-405` and `VULKAN-406`. Full `ctest` in §7.5. **Dependencies:** none.
| VULKAN-405 | A `VkFramebuffer` outlives the device it belongs to (validation) | ✅ | **Fixed, and the row's proposed test could not have caught it — that is the substance of this row.** **Reproduced standalone:** `cna_test_vulkan_mrt_mixed_formats` prints `2/2 PASS` and then `vkDestroyDevice(): Object Tracking - For VkDevice …, VkFramebuffer … has not been destroyed.` The test binds two `RenderTarget2D`s through `SetRenderTargets` and exits **without presenting**, so the MRT proxy created for that bind is never replayed. **Root cause:** a `VulkanMRTProxy` is reference-counted. The destructor's Step 1b reset `mrtProxy_` and cleared `retiredMrtProxies_` — the renderer's own two handles — but **four other members hold a share**: `currentRT_` for as long as the MRT is bound, and `Pending3DDraw::rt` / `PendingBatch::rt` / `PendingClear::rt` for every record the frame never replayed. Those members are destroyed **after** the destructor body, i.e. after `vkDestroyDevice`, and `~VulkanMRTProxy` then finds `device_` already null and returns without freeing anything. **A second defect the report did not name:** that same early-return reads `owner_->device_` out of a `VulkanRenderer` whose destructor has already finished — a use-after-free on every path where a proxy outlives its renderer, benign only because the read happens to land on freed-but-unwritten memory. **The fix chosen, and why not the obvious one.** Dropping the four shares in Step 1b works and was measured to work, but it is a fix that has to **enumerate the holders** and stays correct only while that list does — `VulkanSpriteBatchRenderer::activeRT_` is a fifth holder the renderer cannot reach at all, because it belongs to a public `SpriteBatch`. So the fix is the release-then-disconnect pair every other owned Vulkan resource in this file already uses: `VulkanMRTProxy::ReleaseVulkanResources()` destroys the framebuffer while the device is alive, `DisconnectOwner()` makes a surviving proxy harmless, and Step 1b applies both to `mrtProxy_` and to every entry of `retiredMrtProxies_` — **which together are every proxy that exists**, since a proxy reaches the second list exactly when it leaves the first. Who else holds a share stops mattering. `~VulkanMRTProxy` is unchanged and becomes a no-op after the release. **Test, and the premise this row got wrong:** the row asked for *“`Vulkan_MRT_MixedFormats` gains an assertion that the renderer's validation-message set is empty at teardown.”* It cannot. The message is emitted **during** `vkDestroyDevice`, which happens after the last line of the test body, and `validationMessages_` is a member of the very object being destroyed — there is no moment at which a test can read it and see this entry. The instrument that can is CTest's output gate, which this file's neighbouring Vulkan registrations already use: `set_tests_properties(Vulkan_MRT_MixedFormats PROPERTIES FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "\\[Vulkan Validation\\]")`. It is deliberately the whole `[Vulkan Validation]` prefix rather than the `"VUID-|Validation Error|Validation Warning|SYNC-HAZARD"` pattern the neighbours use: an Object Tracking report contains none of those words, so the neighbours' pattern would have let this exact message through. No test source changed. **Mutation-checked:** with the two release calls removed, `ctest -R '^Vulkan_MRT_MixedFormats$'` **fails** on the output gate while the test's own `2/2 PASS` is unchanged — so the gate fails for the leak and for nothing else. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **254/254**, and its `[Vulkan Validation]` inventory is now **0** — `VULKAN-009`'s three-message inventory is fully closed. Full `ctest` in §7.5; it is mandatory here because the change is in the renderer destructor, which every test in this configuration runs. **Dependencies:** none.
| VULKAN-406 | An image array layer is submitted in `VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_UNDEFINED` (validation) | ✅ | **Fixed, and the count was wrong in the direction that matters: it is not one layer, it is all six.** **Reproduced:** `cna_test_vulkan_cube_volume_setdata_contract` alone, one message naming `mipLevel = 0, arrayLayer = 2`. **The resource:** the `RenderTargetCube` built by `RunRenderTargetCubeCheck` — `mipMap=false`, `DepthFormat::None`, `RenderTargetUsage::DiscardContents` — whose face 2 is then read back. **This row's stated mechanism was wrong.** It said `TransitionImageLayout`'s barrier hardcodes a single layer, so a cube's other five faces are never transitioned by that helper. True of the helper, irrelevant here: **no cube class uses it**, and both cube classes say so in their own comments (`VulkanTextureCubeRenderer::GetData`, `VulkanRenderTargetCubeRenderer::GetData`) — they write inline barriers scoped to the exact face and level. **The actual defect:** `VulkanRenderTargetCubeRenderer`'s constructor ran its up-front all-layers/all-levels `UNDEFINED → SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL` barrier only `if (levelCount_ > 1 || preserveContents_)` (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:14360`). Both conditions are about **binding** — Task 907's mip-blit cascade precondition and REMED-GFX-136's `LOAD_OP_LOAD` initialLayout — and binding is not the only way a layer is reached. A face nobody renders is still readable and still sampleable, and both of those paths assert the opposite: `GetData`'s own barrier declares `oldLayout = VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL` with the comment *“every level of every layer sits in SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL outside a render pass (the constructor's up-front barrier …)”*, and `cubeView_` hands all six layers to a descriptor that declares the same. On a single-level `DiscardContents` cube that constructor barrier never ran, so the comment was false and the barrier lied about a layout the image was never in. **The fourth time this class has lagged its 2D twin** (`VULKAN-215` records the first three): `VulkanRenderTargetRenderer` has done exactly this barrier **unconditionally** since Task 878. **Done:** the condition is deleted; the barrier is unconditional, matching the 2D twin. Cost is one extra one-time command at construction for the single-level discard case — the same one the 2D twin already pays, and the price of a layout that is defined rather than merely lucky. The depth barrier keeps its `preserveContents_` guard, which is correct: a discarding pass declares depth `initialLayout UNDEFINED` and reads it from nowhere else. **Test:** the existing contract fixture, in the two ways the row asked for. (1) `RunRenderTargetCubeCheck` now probes **all six faces** instead of face 2 — one face cannot tell a cube whose layers are each independently initialised from one whose single touched layer happened to be right. (2) `CheckValidationClean()` asserts layer liveness and then an empty `GetValidationMessagesEXT()`, `#if defined(CNA_RENDERER_VULKAN)` so the **thirteen** other renderers registering this source are untouched. **Mutation-checked, and this is the result worth keeping:** restoring the condition turns the test red with **six** messages, `arrayLayer = 0` through `arrayLayer = 5`. The original inventory saw one only because one face was asked for. **Cross-renderer check, because the fixture is shared:** rebuilt and run in `cmake-build-easygl` — `EasyGL_CubeVolume_SetDataContract` passes with the six-face probe, so the extension is not a Vulkan-shaped assumption. The other twelve registrations cannot be built here and are unchanged in behaviour by construction (the added block is renderer-guarded; the six-face loop repeats a probe each already ran once). **Evidence:** `Vulkan_CubeVolume_SetDataContract` **56/56 → 63/63**, `^Vulkan_` **254/254**, full `ctest` in §7.5. The `^Vulkan_` run's `[Vulkan Validation]` inventory falls **2 → 1**, and the one that remains is `VULKAN-405`'s framebuffer. **Dependencies:** none.
| VULKAN-407 | Three resource classes are untracked, so one that outlives its device leaks every Vulkan object it owns | ✅ | **Fixed, and the mutation results are the part worth keeping: each of the three registrations, removed alone, reproduces exactly its own class's object list.** **Observed (unchanged from the opening text):** `MetalResourceHealth.RenderTargetCubeRendererEscapesThroughTextureCubeBaseMove` produced 10 `vkDestroyDevice(): Object Tracking … has not been destroyed` messages — 7 `VkImageView`, 1 `VkImage`, 1 `VkDeviceMemory`, 1 `VkFramebuffer` — while passing. **Done:** `VulkanTexture3DRenderer`, `VulkanTextureCubeRenderer` and `VulkanRenderTargetCubeRenderer` each gain the `ReleaseVulkanResources()`/`DisconnectOwner()` pair every other Vulkan-owning class here already had, split out of their existing destructors so the mechanism is untouched — the two cube classes still retire through the frame-fence-gated queue, the volume still frees directly. Each registers itself in a new live list at construction and removes itself at destruction, exactly as `VulkanTextureRenderer` and `VulkanRenderTargetRenderer` do, and the renderer's destructor walks the three new lists beside the two that were there. **The second half of the defect, which the report did not name:** `~VulkanTexture3DRenderer` and its two siblings each began `if (!owner_ || owner_->device_ == VK_NULL_HANDLE) return;` — reading a `VulkanRenderer` whose destructor had already finished. `DisconnectOwner()` is what settles that, and it is the reason the fix is this pair rather than a release call alone. **Test:** new CTest `Vulkan_ResourceOutlivesDevice` (`vulkan_resource_outlives_device_test.cpp`), **3/3**. It builds a `Texture3D`, a `TextureCube` and — through the same `TextureCube` base move the `MetalResourceHealth` case documents — a `RenderTargetCube`, keeps a `shared_ptr` to each renderer, and destroys the `GraphicsDevice` while all three are still held. **Where the discriminator lives, and why it is not in the test file.** The layer reports the leak *during* `vkDestroyDevice`, after the last statement that could read the renderer's message list and out of the object being destroyed; no in-process assertion can see it. `VULKAN-393`'s output gate can, and applies to this registration like every other. What the file itself asserts is that the escape really happened — three renderers alive after their device is gone — so a build that constructed nothing cannot pass the gate having proved nothing. **Mutation-checked, one registration at a time, and each names its own class:** removing `liveTexture3Ds_`'s registration reproduces `VkImage`+`VkDeviceMemory`+`VkImageView`; removing `liveTextureCubes_`'s reproduces the same three for the cube; removing `liveRenderTargetCubes_`'s reproduces **7 `VkImageView`, 1 `VkImage`, 1 `VkDeviceMemory`, 1 `VkFramebuffer`** — the original report's exact list. So the fix is attributed per class rather than in aggregate. **Not proved here, and named rather than implied:** the use-after-free half is argued from the code, not observed — an ASan build of `Vulkan_ResourceOutlivesDevice` is what would observe it, and ASan on this renderer is `VULKAN-397`. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **256/256** plus `Metal_PortableHelpers` and the six `MetalResourceHealth` cases, **264/264** together, with **0** `[Vulkan Validation]` lines where there were 20. Full `ctest` in §7.5; mandatory here because the change is in the renderer destructor. **Dependencies:** none.
| VULKAN-408 | The validation gate reaches `Vulkan_*` only, and 8 861 other CTests run this renderer too | ✅ | **Done.
