The gate is now on every CTest in the configuration that can create a `VkDevice`, and the decision this row was required to record is a measured one rather than a preference.** **The decision: neither of the two options this row offered.** The root-level sweep was built first — a recursive walk of every directory's `TESTS` property, called once from the root `CMakeLists.txt` exactly the way `cna_apple_configure_all_bundles()` is, and for the same stated reason. **It does not work**, and the error is worth recording because it is not obvious from the documentation: `set_tests_properties()` is *directory-scoped*, so from the root it cannot touch a test another directory registered — `set_tests_properties Can not find test to add properties to: ContentLostProbe`. The `set_property(TEST … DIRECTORY …)` form that could is **CMake 3.28**, and this project's floor is **3.20**. **What was built instead: the two hooks that are always in the right scope.** (1) `cna_register_renderer_test()` in `cmake/TestHelpers.cmake` appends the pattern to every registration it makes — ~600 across every renderer's examples, including `Metal_PortableHelpers`, one of the two that carried the 20 messages. (2) `gtest_discover_tests(… PROPERTIES FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION …)` at its own call site in `cmake/UnitTests.cmake` reaches the **8 774** `PRE_TEST`-discovered cases, which do not exist at configure time at all and are therefore in no directory's `TESTS` property — the other carrier, `MetalResourceHealth.RenderTargetCubeRendererEscapesThroughTextureCubeBaseMove`. Verified in the generated files, not assumed. **One thing still had to stay directory-local, and it is a trap worth naming:** nine registrations in `modules/renderers/vulkan/examples/CMakeLists.txt` call `set_tests_properties(… FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION …)` **after** their registration, which *replaces* the property rather than adding to it. `cna_apply_vulkan_validation_gate()` re-merges over that directory's tests and must stay last in the file; checked in `CTestTestfile.cmake`, where `Vulkan_MrtMipFinalization` reads `"VUID-|…|SYNC-HAZARD;\\[Vulkan Validation\\]"`. **`VULKAN-393`'s directory-local first version is superseded rather than duplicated** — the mechanism and the (empty) exemption list now live in one place, `cmake/TestHelpers.cmake`. **Mutation-checked with exactly the discriminator this row named:** with `VULKAN-407`'s `liveRenderTargetCubes_` registration removed, **both** `MetalResourceHealth.RenderTargetCubeRendererEscapesThroughTextureCubeBaseMove` **and** `Metal_PortableHelpers` turn **red**. Before this row they passed while leaking ten Vulkan objects past `vkDestroyDevice`. **Applied only where a `VkDevice` can exist** (`CNA_GRAPHICS_RENDERER STREQUAL VULKAN` or `VULKAN` in `CNA_GRAPHICS_RENDERERS`), so the other renderer configurations are untouched — the pattern would be inert there, but a gate nobody can trigger is still a gate somebody has to reason about. **Evidence:** full `ctest` in §7.5, run with the gate live on the whole suite. **Dependencies:** `VULKAN-393`, `VULKAN-407`.

---

## 24. Phase 12 — Cross-renderer conformance (`VULKAN-430`–`VULKAN-469`)

The infrastructure already exists and is under-used: `cross_renderer_diagnostic_scene.cpp` is built
for EasyGL, Software, WebGPU **and Vulkan**, with `cna_diag_compare` as the comparator; three Vulkan
CTests already compare against EasyGL-authored golden PNGs. This phase turns that into a gate.

Rules for every row here: assert exact logical properties where the property is exact; use pixel
probes chosen to discriminate; use a tolerance only with a written justification; **never** demand
bit-identical pixels for floating-point rasterization or lighting; and never accept "renders without
throwing" as proof of a visual feature.

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-430 | Make the shared diagnostic scene a real EasyGL↔Vulkan gate | ✅ | **Done as the row specified — a script, not a CTest — and the numbers it produced are the strongest cross-renderer evidence this plan has.** `scripts/compare-easygl-vulkan-diagnostic.sh` builds nothing and assumes nothing: it runs `cna_diag_easygl`/`cna_diag_vulkan` (the 3D `VertexColorEnabled` triangle) and `cna_corpus2d_easygl`/`cna_corpus2d_vulkan` (`VULKAN-431`'s 2D corpus), each from its own build directory, and diffs each pair with `cna_diag_compare`. **Why not a CTest, restated in the script itself so nobody re-litigates it:** a CNA build links exactly one renderer, so no test inside either configuration can produce the other's dump, and a test that could would have to build the other configuration — which is not a test. **Measured on the first run (2026-09-06, llvmpipe, Xvfb `:99`):** `diagnostic_scene` **max diff 1** at (41,22) channel 2, mean 0.005; `2d_corpus` **max diff 0 — byte-identical**. Two independently written renderers, one source, and one channel of one pixel between them. **So the tolerance is doing no work**, and that is stated rather than left implied: the same run passes at `--tolerance 2`. It is kept at the inherited **40** — the value the existing Software↔EasyGL comparison uses — so that a difference which matters here is one that would have mattered there. The expected-difference list is **empty**, and the script says an entry belongs in that list with its reason, never in a widened tolerance. **Acceptance, exactly as the row demanded — *“the script fails when one renderer's output is deliberately perturbed”*:** `--perturb` corrupts two pixels of the Vulkan dump before comparing. Measured: both pairs report `max diff 255 … FAIL`, and the script exits **3** if the perturbation is NOT detected, because a gate that cannot fail proves nothing. **Evidence:** the two runs above, and `^Vulkan_` **329/329** (the script registers no test).
| VULKAN-431 | Register the shared 2D corpus on Vulkan | ✅ | **`cna_corpus2d_vulkan` added, and deliberately NOT as a CTest** — the same precedent `cna_diag_vulkan`, `cna_corpus2d_easygl` and `cna_diag_easygl` all follow: it asserts nothing on its own, it produces **one renderer's dump** for `cna_diag_compare` to judge against another's. The two dumps come from two different build directories, which is exactly why `VULKAN-430`'s gate is a script. **Evidence:** built, and diffed against `cna_corpus2d_easygl` by `VULKAN-430`'s script — **byte-identical**, max diff 0. `^Vulkan_` **329/329** (unchanged; nothing was registered as a test).
| VULKAN-432 | Cross-renderer golden comparison for the stock-effect goldens | ✅ | `Vulkan_BasicEffect_Golden`, `Vulkan_AlphaTestEffect_Golden` and `Vulkan_DualTextureEffect_Golden`. Vulkan already had a dozen tests per stock effect, but every one of them asserts values **it chose itself**; a golden asserts that two independently written renderers land on **one image**, which is a strictly stronger statement and the one the §10.12 matrix row was missing. **Registered and green on the first run against the SAME golden PNGs EasyGL is compared against — which is the whole point of a golden and the thing this plan had only three of. Measured together with the other four rows of this batch: 14 golden registrations, 14 green, `^Vulkan_` **326/326**.** Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only.
| VULKAN-433 | Cross-renderer golden comparison for `EnvironmentMapEffect` and `SkinnedEffect` | ✅ | `Vulkan_EnvironmentMapEffect_Golden` and `Vulkan_SkinnedEffect_Golden`. The first has a stake in `VULKAN-260`, three rows ago: that effect's Fresnel term was computed **per fragment** here until then, and the hand-derived gradient test proved the new value is right at three points. This golden proves it over the **whole image**, against a PNG this renderer had no part in producing — so the fix is the XNA shape rather than merely a different one. A golden written after a fix would not have said that. **Registered and green on the first run against the SAME golden PNGs EasyGL is compared against — which is the whole point of a golden and the thing this plan had only three of. Measured together with the other four rows of this batch: 14 golden registrations, 14 green, `^Vulkan_` **326/326**.** Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only.
| VULKAN-434 | Cross-renderer golden comparison for the state goldens | ✅ | `Vulkan_BlendState_Additive_Golden`, `Vulkan_DepthStencilState_WriteEnable_Golden` and `Vulkan_RasterizerState_CullMode_Golden`. These matter more here than the name suggests: EasyGL **sets** blend, depth-write and cull state, while Vulkan **bakes each into a `VkPipeline`** at creation and selects it from a cache key. A golden is the only test that compares the result of that baking against a renderer that does not bake — and `VULKAN-162`'s pipeline-key work is exactly what it holds. **Registered and green on the first run against the SAME golden PNGs EasyGL is compared against — which is the whole point of a golden and the thing this plan had only three of. Measured together with the other four rows of this batch: 14 golden registrations, 14 green, `^Vulkan_` **326/326**.** Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only.
| VULKAN-435 | Cross-renderer golden comparison for the 2D goldens | ✅ | `Vulkan_SpriteBatch_Rotation_Golden`, `Vulkan_TextureFilter_Linear_Golden`, `Vulkan_GoldenImage_Smoke` and `Vulkan_PixelTestGame_Smoke`. The rotation golden is the one that would catch a half-pixel error the per-pixel tests tolerate — `VULKAN-097`'s XNA pixel-centre correction is a translation of slightly under half a pixel, and a golden compares every pixel of the rotated quad rather than a chosen few. The two smoke tests confirm the golden harness itself works here, which is what stops the other twelve being green because nothing was compared. **Registered and green on the first run against the SAME golden PNGs EasyGL is compared against — which is the whole point of a golden and the thing this plan had only three of. Measured together with the other four rows of this batch: 14 golden registrations, 14 green, `^Vulkan_` **326/326**.** Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only.
| VULKAN-436 | Register the shared sample scenes on Vulkan | ✅ | `Vulkan_Sample_DualTextureSwap` and `Vulkan_FullscreenField`. Whole scenes rather than isolated contracts: the dual-texture swap exercises the second UV channel `VULKAN-150` had to add to this renderer's shader, and the fullscreen field is the long one (11 s) that runs many frames rather than one — the shape that catches state a single frame leaves behind. **Registered and green on the first run against the SAME golden PNGs EasyGL is compared against — which is the whole point of a golden and the thing this plan had only three of. Measured together with the other four rows of this batch: 14 golden registrations, 14 green, `^Vulkan_` **326/326**.** Full `ctest` not re-run: registrations only.
| VULKAN-437 | Write the cross-renderer conformance methodology down | ✅ | **Written as a section of `docs/vulkan-renderer.md`, and it says what was measured rather than what is intended — which is why it waited for `VULKAN-430`–`VULKAN-436` to produce numbers.** **The four things the row asked for, each answered with evidence:** *What must match exactly* — the 17 goldens, against the **same PNGs** EasyGL is compared against (a golden each renderer keeps its own copy of has stopped being a golden), and the 2D corpus, **byte for byte**, measured max diff **0**. *What may differ* — the rasterized coverage of a diagonal edge, by at most one channel step; measured max diff **1**, at one pixel, and that is the only difference either tool has ever reported. Plus the corpus's own exclusion list (filtering, mip selection, anisotropy, MSAA, additive blending, non-right-angle rotation) with the reason each is there. *How a tolerance is chosen* — 40, **inherited** from the Software↔EasyGL comparison rather than picked for this pair, with the rule that matters more than the value: **a tolerance must not be doing any work**, and this one is not (the same run passes at `--tolerance 2`). *The standing rule that a conformance test must be able to fail* — with the three rows whose **first mutation passed** named as the evidence that the rule is not rhetorical: `VULKAN-172` (a mis-sized DXT block is a buffer over-read, invisible to pixels), `VULKAN-095` (a stale MSAA pipeline is invisible to a test that never draws through the MSAA path), `VULKAN-177` (a stale descriptor-set entry is invisible to both the pixels and the validation layer). **One section the row did not ask for and the evidence demanded:** *where EasyGL is not the authority* — the three rows that changed a shared test because it encoded EasyGL's architecture as the contract, and the one row that changed this renderer because EasyGL was right and it was not (`VULKAN-260`). **Evidence:** the section cites only measurements this plan recorded, each traceable to its row.

## 25. Phase 13 — Final parity gate (`VULKAN-470`–`VULKAN-489`)

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-470 | Re-verify capability reporting against measured behaviour | ✅ | **Every one of the 93 snapshot entries had a test that observed the behavior at this classic-plan gate, and four capabilities did not before this row.** The audit ran against `VULKAN-008`'s snapshot as its oracle. **The 19 `GraphicsCapability` members, each with its observer:** `ThreeD` → the 65 stock-effect draw tests; `DepthStencilBuffer` → `Vulkan_DepthStencilState_CompareFunction` and `Vulkan_DepthRangeContract`; `MultiSampleAntiAliasing` → `Vulkan_MSAA_4x_Readback` plus `Vulkan_CapabilityContract` leg E, which compares the report against the count `ApplyMultiSampleCount` actually reaches; `MultipleRenderTargets` → `Vulkan_MRT_MixedFormats`; `AnisotropicFiltering` → `Vulkan_TextureAnisotropic_DualTextureEffect`; `WireFrame` → `Vulkan_FillMode_WireFrame`; `OcclusionQuery` → `Vulkan_OcclusionQuery_PixelCount` and `_Precision`; `CustomEffects` → `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_SpirV`; `Texture3D` → `Vulkan_Texture3D_Slices_RoundTrip`; `Instancing` → `Vulkan_DrawInstanced_3Instances`; `StencilBuffer` → `Vulkan_DepthStencilState_StencilOps`; `AdditiveBlending` → `Vulkan_AdditiveBlendContract`; `MultiStreamVertexInput`, `FloatRenderTargets` and `HalfFloatRenderTargets` → `Vulkan_CapabilityContract` leg C, which already observed each false report as a real refusal. **The four that had no observer, closed here rather than filed as gaps:** `HalfFloatTextureLinearFiltering` — its falseness was one step earlier than filtering, and the new leg showed why: a half-float `Texture2D` could not be created at all. `IndirectDraw` and `ComputeShaders` were honest absences at this row's closure: both needed a storage buffer and `CreateStorageBuffer` returned null. **That historical negative evidence has since been superseded, not erased:** `MOD-2241` supplies Vulkan compute/storage and `MOD-2245` supplies both native indirect commands; their positive runtime oracles and the updated capability contract now observe the working paths. `CompiledEffects` — false because `CNA_VULKAN_COMPILED_EFFECTS` is **OFF** in `cmake-build-vulkan`, verified in that directory's `CMakeCache.txt`. Only a build that turns it on can observe the other answer, so this entry is **build-scoped**. **The derived rows:** the detailed features, limits and formats were covered by the snapshot plus their dedicated behavior tests. The former shader-dialect mismatch was later resolved by the append-only SPIR-V identity in `VULKAN-264`. **Evidence at this row's closure:** `Vulkan_CapabilityContract` grew from 25/25 to **27/27**; later modern rows keep it green as device-derived capabilities become supported. |
| VULKAN-471 | Confirm every in-scope parity-matrix row is resolved | ✅ | **Run three times, and the third (2026-09-06) is the one that closes it: §10 has **zero** rows reading `NEEDS_DEEPER_AUDIT`, down from ten.** **The distance travelled, kept because it is the point of re-running rather than re-deciding:** 2026-09-05 measured ten, blocked on `VULKAN-023`, `-025`, `-026`, `-055`, `-090`, `-094`, `-141`, `-392`, `-394`, `-395` and `-396`; two more were **stale rather than open** and were corrected then (`PixelCountIsPreciseEXT`, whose owner had closed while the row still said the virtual was not overridden, and "Capability reaching the renderer at all"). A second run on 2026-09-06 had one left — *Full-device stalls in ordinary paths* — whose remaining third was the mid-frame render-target flush that counting the other two revealed. `VULKAN-399` measured that one and it is now `PARITY`, because rendering to a target and sampling it never reaches the flush at all. **What this row does NOT claim, stated so ✅ is not read as more than it is:** §10 still holds **46 `TEST_GAP`** and **17 `IMPLEMENTATION_GAP`** rows out of 138. Those are not this row's subject — `VULKAN-472` owns the implementation gaps and `VULKAN-473` the test gaps, and §28's definition of done needs both. What `VULKAN-471` owns is the `NEEDS_DEEPER_AUDIT` state itself: §26 rule 8 calls it "a real state with an owner", and a row in it is one nobody has yet looked at hard enough to classify. **There are none left, so every row in the matrix now carries a verdict somebody measured.** **Current tally, so the next run has a baseline:** 138 rows — 46 `TEST_GAP`, 37 `PARITY`, 17 `IMPLEMENTATION_GAP`, 16 `VULKAN_STRONGER`, 5 `NOT_APPLICABLE`, 3 `SEMANTIC_DIVERGENCE`, 1 `CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE`. |

| VULKAN-217 | A textured instanced draw must sample its texture (F-41) | ✅ | **Found by reading a `// for now` comment no plan row cited, and settled by measuring both renderers rather than by reading either.** `d.descSet = defaultWhiteDescSet_; // no per-draw texture for now` on the instanced draw, and `instanced3d.frag.glsl`'s own header — *“no fog, no texture, just the flat instance diffuse color”*. **The measurement first, because the matrix said the opposite.** New renderer-agnostic `instanced_textured_draw_test.cpp`, registered on both: **EasyGL 5/5, Vulkan 2/5**. The two non-instanced control legs passed *here*, which is what makes the claim narrow — textured draws were never broken; instanced ones were. The instanced legs returned `(255,255,255)` under a white material colour and `(255,0,0)` under a red one: two distinguishable signatures, *“a default 1×1 image was bound”* and *“the texture was never sampled”*, and the test's diagnosis line names whichever it sees. **The probe took three drafts and each correction is worth keeping.** (1) `VertexPositionTexture` carries a vtable, so `sizeof` is 32 while its declaration says 20 and a raw upload strided by `sizeof` writes garbage — replaced with a plain 20-byte record and an explicit `VertexDeclaration`. (2) A per-instance buffer with **no** declaration is refused outright by EasyGL (*“unsupported vertex stride 64 without a VertexDeclaration”*), so the four `BlendWeight` columns this repo's existing instancing tests use are not optional. (3) The first draft set `DiffuseColor` to red *and* expected blue — red × blue is **black**, so the control leg failed on both renderers and the harness looked like a finding. The fix is what makes the test discriminating: **four** legs, white and red diffuse × instanced and not, where the red legs must read black and a red result is the specific signature of a program that never samples. **The fix.** New `instanced_textured3d.vert.glsl`/`.frag.glsl` — one descriptor binding, like `instanced3d.frag.glsl`, so they keep the shared `pipelineLayoutExt3D_`; a second binding (the fog UBO the `colored3d` bundle carries) is structurally incompatible with it, which is why this variant has no fog and the row says so rather than implying parity. `StockInputs::kInstancedTextured = { kPos, kUv }`, a branch in `BuildInstancedVertexLayoutEXT`, a `textured` flag through `GetOrCreatePipelineInstanced3D`, and the descriptor set the ordinary indexed route already builds. **Selection needs BOTH halves, and each is a real failure mode:** `params.textureEnabled && params.texture0 != nullptr` **and** the declaration naming a `TextureCoordinate`. The effect alone would bind a UV attribute nothing supplies; the declaration alone would multiply an unwanted texture into a mesh that merely happens to carry coordinates. `BuildInstancedVertexLayoutEXT` makes the same choice in the same order, so the attribute set and the shader cannot disagree — the trap `VULKAN-200` fell into. **Textured wins over coloured rather than combining**, because there is no `instanced_colored_textured3d`; a declaration carrying both would otherwise pick the coloured shader and drop the texture again, which is the defect this row closes. That is a documented limit, not a silent one — it is `VULKAN-218`. **The second half was found only by the validation layer, exactly as in `VULKAN-199`:** the instanced replay branch pushed constants and bound **no** descriptor set, which was consistent while every instanced fragment shader declared none. *“The VkPipeline … statically uses descriptor set 0, but set 0 is not compatible … VkPipelineLayout 0x0”*. The bind is unconditional rather than gated on `textured`, because `pipelineLayoutExt3D_` has always declared that one set layout and a shader ignoring it is unaffected. **Mutation evidence is the pre-fix run itself** — the same test, the same binary shape, **2/5** against the unfixed renderer with exactly the two subject legs and the parity leg failing and both controls passing. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **370/370 → 371/371** and full `ctest` **9220/9232**, whose 12 are §7.5c's standing set by name; the full suite was mandatory because this changes the instanced pipeline key, the pipeline factory, the replay branch and the draw record. `^EasyGL_` **321/326** in the parallel run — the four recorded pre-existing failures plus `EasyGL_Sample_MovingQuad3D`, which passes **3/3** run alone and is therefore a `-j2` contention flake, not a regression; EasyGL production is untouched by this row. **Docs:** `docs/vulkan-renderer.md`. **Opened by this row:** `VULKAN-218`. |
| VULKAN-218 | The instanced shapes that still have no program here (F-41's tail) | ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-09-08. Every shape this row listed has its own ✅ child, and the row's own design question was answered by taking EasyGL's answer.** The text below is the statement of the gap and is kept as written; what follows is what closing it actually took. **Children, each with its own commit and its own permanent legs in `instanced_textured_draw_test.cpp`:** `VULKAN-227` (the mechanism — one instanceable source per family instead of a hand-copied twin), `VULKAN-228` (the lit family's untextured and coloured shapes), `VULKAN-229` (coloured alpha-test), `VULKAN-230` (coloured dual-texture), `VULKAN-231` (`SkinnedEffect`), `VULKAN-232` (`PbrEffect` and `SkinnedPbrEffect`), `VULKAN-233` (fog, by retiring the separate instanced family) and `VULKAN-234` (fog on the last shape, and the end of that family). **This row predicted the cost and got it wrong in the useful direction.** It said matching EasyGL meant *“adding the four per-instance columns to **every** 3D vertex shader … one change across the whole stock shader set with the whole 3D suite as its regression surface”*, and that is exactly what happened — but the columns are **injected** by `compile_shaders.py` rather than typed into eighteen files, so what the row priced as one enormous change became eight reviewable ones in which the invariant *“every ordinary module's SPIR-V is byte-identical”* was checked per symbol after each. The row was also right that this was the cheaper end state: the renderer has **five fewer shader files**, one fewer pipeline factory, one fewer pipeline cache, one fewer pipeline layout and no `instancedTextured` flag than when the row was opened. **The design the row asked about, settled:** instancing composes with the whole effect set here now, as it does on EasyGL, and for the same reason — it is an optional input to every stock program rather than a family. `useInstanced` selects no programs at all any more; it adds binding 1. **Two things it did NOT list, both found by measuring:** the coloured alpha-test and dual-texture shapes were **worse than “loses its colour”** — one rendered nothing and the other rendered a plausible grey, and in both cases the validation layer named the orphaned attribute (`VULKAN-229`/`VULKAN-230`); and the class behind them, that the instanced route was choosing its module by one rule and its attribute set by another wherever an ordinary family owns both. **Evidence at closure:** `instanced_textured_draw_test.cpp` **37/37 on Vulkan and 37/37 on EasyGL**, from 17/17 when this row was opened — twenty new legs, every one of which failed on this renderer before its fix and passed on EasyGL throughout. `^Vulkan_` **371/371** with no `[Vulkan Validation]` line; full `ctest` **9216/9232**, §7.5c's standing set exactly. | **Opened by `VULKAN-217`, which closed one shape of five and says which four it did not.** Instancing on this renderer is a separate program family — `instanced3d`, `instanced_colored3d`, and now `instanced_textured3d` — so a shape with no instanced program silently falls back to the flat one. **Missing, as of 2026-09-08 after `VULKAN-226`:** every remaining item is a **narrow variant**, not a family — the **untextured** and **coloured** lit shapes (`VULKAN-224` took the textured one deliberately), the **coloured** alpha-test and dual-texture variants, skinned and PBR instancing, and fog on the colour-only instanced programs — the last for a structural reason worth repeating, that the fog UBO is a second descriptor binding and the instanced route uses the one-binding `pipelineLayoutExt3D_` it shares with 2D `SpriteBatch`. **EasyGL has none of these gaps by construction, and that is the design question this row really asks.** Its per-instance matrix is an *optional attribute set added to every stock program* (`CNA_GL_INSTANCE_TRANSFORM_DECL`, locations 12–15 behind `uCnaInstanced`), so instancing composes with the whole effect set and cost nothing at the ordinary draw — its own comment says the programs stay “byte-for-byte equivalent” without it. Reproducing that here means adding the four columns to **every** 3D vertex shader rather than adding four more program pairs, which is the cheaper end state and the larger single change. **Not attempted in this campaign**, because it touches every stock shader at once and the regression surface is the whole 3D suite. **`VULKAN-222` is the first step of it and is worth copying:** rather than adding another program to the *separate* instanced family, it made an **ordinary** family instanceable — the alpha-test one — by keeping that family's push constant, pipeline layout, descriptor set, fragment stage and replay branch and changing only the vertex module and one binding. It worked because `useAlphaTest` is already tested before `useInstanced` in both selection chains. Each remaining family can be done the same way, and the ones that cost more do so for a stated reason: the lit and dual-texture families carry a **second descriptor binding** (their UBO), which the instanced route's shared `pipelineLayoutExt3D_` does not have — but their own layouts do, so the same trick applies, at the cost of building that UBO payload for an instanced draw. **`VULKAN-223` removed the reason that was expensive:** the family dispatch that builds those payloads existed as two inline copies, and is now one method with two callers, so the instanced route becomes the third caller rather than the third copy. **Premise measured, not read — the lit half, on the same day the row was opened.** The row was first written saying the other shapes were inferred from the shader set and that the first step should be a lit leg. That step was taken immediately rather than left as an intention. A `Position+Normal+TextureCoordinate` quad, one directional light at `N·L = 0.5`, ambient and specular zero, a white texture and a white `DiffuseColor`, drawn the two ways:
| VULKAN-220 | Colour **and** texture on one instanced draw (`VULKAN-218`'s first shape) | ✅ | **`VULKAN-217` made `textured` win over the colour shape and said so, in the row, in the docs and in the shader comment — because no program could do both. This adds the one that can.** **Measured first, and the documented limit was exactly what the pixels showed:** a grey(128) vertex colour over the blue 1×1, where the product is `(0,0,128)` — distinct from `(0,0,255)`, the colour dropped, and from `(128,128,128)`, the texture dropped, so a failure says *which half* went missing. EasyGL read `(0,0,128)` both instanced and not; Vulkan read `(0,0,128)` non-instanced and **`(0,0,255)`** instanced. **One shader, no new fragment stage.** `instanced_colored_textured3d.vert.glsl` writes `clamp((vertexColorEnabled > 0.5) ? aColor * diffuseColor : diffuseColor, 0, 1)` — the same expression `colored_textured3d.vert.glsl` uses, so the D3D9 `oD0` saturate of `VULKAN-197` holds here too — and `instanced_textured3d.frag.glsl` already multiplies its sample by the interpolated colour, so it is shared unchanged. `StockInputs::kInstancedColoredTextured = { kPos, kColor, kUv }`, in `colored_textured3d`'s own input order so the two families spell the same record the same way. **`textured` and `colored` are independent flags again**, and `BuildInstancedVertexLayoutEXT` tests the pair **before** either single-feature table, because both of those would also match a declaration carrying both and would silently drop the other half. **Two defects found by making the change, both by the validation layer, both worth recording.** (1) `VkVertexInputAttributeDescription attrs[6]` **overflowed**: the per-vertex set is now three wide and the four per-instance columns are appended after it, so the writes ran into the adjacent `binds` array — the layer reported an `inputRate` of **109** and a binding of **48**, which is what a stomped `VkVertexInputBindingDescription` looks like, and the draw rendered nothing. Now `attrs[8]`. (2) `kInstancedColored` and `kInstancedTextured` **both have two inputs**, so deriving “has a colour” from `attributeCount == 2` alone selected the three-input shader for a two-input layout: *“does not have a Location 2 but vertex shader has an input variable at that Location”*. The count is now read against the table `textured` selects. **The first draft's comment claimed this case could not arise**; it arose on the first build, and the comment is replaced by the reason rather than deleted. **Legs F and G**, permanent: F is the non-instanced control, G the instanced draw, and the diagnosis line names which half is missing when either fails. **9/9 on both renderers**, up from 7/7. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **371/371** and full `ctest` **9217/9232** on the virtual display `:99` — the suite was mandatory, since this changes the instanced pipeline's shader selection and its attribute set. **The 15 are discriminated:** **12** are §7.5c's standing set by name, and **3** are audio wall-clock cases (`CueTest` ×2, `WaveBank`) that are **load-sensitive rather than flaky in the usual sense** — they failed 0/3 serially while this machine's load average was 4.4, and passed **3/3 three times running** a few seconds later as it fell to 3.5. Around ten agents share this machine; nothing in `Vulkan_*` and nothing this row touches. **Still open in `VULKAN-218`:** lit, dual-texture, env-map, alpha-test and fog. |

No verdict opens with a gap token — every `TEST_GAP` / `IMPLEMENTATION_GAP` / `GAP` string in the file is historical (“was X; closed by Y”) except instancing's, which `VULKAN-217` re-derived hours earlier. And the F-41 pattern — an EasyGL `*_Shader` source this renderer cannot run, with the verdict drawn from Vulkan's own unrelated tests — appears in **six** cells and is honestly labelled `SEMANTIC_DIVERGENCE` or `PARITY` in five of them; instancing was the one that was not. **Method, because it is re-runnable and the last two sweeps were not:** `awk` over the table rows to pair each verdict with its owner, then the owner's status from the same file; a qualifier grep for *untested / unproven / unverified / not measured*; and a check that no verdict string **begins** with a gap token. | VULKAN-222 | `AlphaTestEffect` on an instanced draw (`VULKAN-218`'s second shape) | ✅ | **Instancing plus alpha test is the canonical use of both together — foliage — and it was silently wrong here.** The instanced route never set `useAlphaTest`, so a fully transparent texel drew opaque. **Measured on both renderers first.** A 2×1 texture, texel 0 opaque blue and texel 1 alpha 0, on a wide quad, with `CompareFunction::Greater` and `ReferenceAlpha = 128`: EasyGL discarded the right half instanced **and** not; Vulkan discarded it only when the draw was not instanced. **Both halves are sampled**, so *“nothing drew at all”* cannot pass for *“the right half was discarded”*. **This shape needed no new plumbing, and that is the interesting part.** `instanced_alpha_test3d.vert.glsl` keeps `alpha_test3d.vert.glsl`'s **push-constant layout** exactly, and its pipeline keeps `pipelineLayoutAlphaTest3D_` — so `FillAlphaTestPushConst` feeds it unchanged, and the descriptor set, the fragment stage and the replay branch are all that family's existing ones. Only the vertex module and one extra vertex binding differ from a non-instanced alpha-test draw. **It works because the selection chains already order it correctly:** `useAlphaTest` is tested **before** `useInstanced` in both the pipeline chain and the descriptor chain, so a draw that sets both lands in the alpha-test family and the instanced flag only has to reach the pipeline factory. The replay already binds binding 1 whenever `useInstanced` has instance bytes, and already passes `instanceCount` to `vkCmdDrawIndexed`. **Strategically this is a step toward `VULKAN-218`'s design change, not a fourth one-off.** `VULKAN-217`/`-220` added programs to a *separate* instanced family; this one makes an **ordinary** family instanceable, which is exactly the shape EasyGL has and the shape that would finish `VULKAN-218`. **The predicate is the ordinary route's own** — `params.alphaTest[3] < 0 || params.alphaTest[2] < 0`, copied from `DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx` — so the two routes agree by construction rather than by inspection. **Two details that would have been wrong by default.** (1) **Fog dots the instance-transformed position**, not the raw vertex: `objPos = instWorld * vec4(inPos, 1)` feeds both `gl_Position` and the fog term, because otherwise every instance of a mesh would be fogged as if it stood at the origin. (2) `ApplyDeclaredVertexLayoutEXT` is handed the **per-vertex** capacity of 3, not `std::size(attrs)`, which is now 7 — the applicator overwrites from index 0 and resets the count, so passing the whole array would let a declaration claim the four slots the instance columns are about to use. **One thing this does not do, stated rather than discovered later:** there is no coloured instanced alpha-test module, so a `Position+Colour+TexCoord` instanced alpha-test draw takes the uncoloured one and loses its colour. That is `VULKAN-218`. **Legs H and I**, permanent: H the non-instanced control, I the instanced draw, each asserting the kept half **and** the discarded half. **11/11 on both renderers**, up from 9/9. **Mutation evidence is the pre-fix measurement**, taken through the same code path and reported above. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **371/371** and full `ctest` **9220/9232** on the virtual display `:99` — the **12** are §7.5c's standing content/lifecycle/net/window/C-API set **by name**, with no audio-timing noise in this run at all. The full suite was mandatory: this changes which family an instanced draw is routed to, and the alpha-test pipeline factory's vertex-input construction. |
| VULKAN-223 | One stock-effect family dispatch instead of two copies (`VULKAN-218`'s enabling refactor) | ✅ | **Found while sizing `VULKAN-218`'s lit shape, by asking what it would actually cost.** The answer was “replicate the lit descriptor-set build and its 64-float UBO fill on the instanced route” — and then that the fill was **already** duplicated, because `DrawPrimitivesEx` and `DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx` each carry the whole stock-effect family dispatch inline: PBR-skinned, PBR, skinned, env-map, dual-texture, lit and the shared fallback. **186 lines, twice.** A third copy is not a way to reach the instanced route. **The two copies were compared before they were merged, not after.** Stripped of comments and whitespace they are **165 and 166 statements differing by exactly one**: a redundant `EnsureDualTexResources()` in the indexed route. `GetOrCreateDualTexDescSet` makes that call itself (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:7457`), so the drift is harmless — checked rather than assumed, because “one route forgets to create its resources” is exactly the shape of a real defect and it would have been wrong to merge without knowing which. **The surviving copy is the one WITH the extra call**: keeping a redundant call costs nothing, while dropping it would have been a silent behaviour change in the direction this refactor is least able to see. **`FillStockFamilyRecordEXT(d, params, needsPbr, needsSkinned, needsEnvMap, needsDualTex, needsLitTextured, needsLitUntextured, needsLitColored)`** — nine parameters and no return, because the block's only free names are those seven booleans plus the record and the draw params. `world` appears inside it exactly once, in a comment; `view` is a local `VkImageView` of its own. That is what makes the extraction mechanical. **Why it is a row rather than a tidy-up.** `VULKAN-218`'s three remaining families each need their descriptor set and UBO payload built for an instanced draw, and the enabling change is a **third caller**, not more copies. `VULKAN-222` did the alpha-test family by hand precisely because it needed none of this — it reuses that family's push constant and layout whole — and the families that do need it are the ones this unlocks. **No behaviour change is claimed; it is measured.** `^Vulkan_` **371/371** and full `ctest` **9218/9232** — the **12** standing failures of §7.5c by name, plus **two** contention cases (`LeaderboardReaderTest`, `ENetDiscoveryServiceTest`) that each pass **3/3** run alone under `-j1`, neither in `Vulkan_*` nor in graphics at all. The diff is **−375/+211** in one file. **No test added**, which §26 rule 4 makes honest to state: this row moves no behaviour and asserts nothing new, and its whole value is that the next row can exist. **Classification:** enabling refactor, Vulkan renderer. |
| VULKAN-224 | Lighting on an instanced draw (`VULKAN-218`'s third and largest shape) | ✅ | **The measurement was already in `VULKAN-218`'s row and is now closed:** a quad at `N·L = 0.5` read `(128,128,128)` non-instanced and **`(255,255,255)`** instanced here — an unlit full-bright quad, because no instanced program consumed a `Normal` — where EasyGL read `(128,128,128)` both ways. It now reads `(128,128,128)` both ways here too. **Built the way `VULKAN-222` was, not the way `VULKAN-217` was.** The lit family's own programs take a per-instance binding, rather than a new instanced family being invented: `instanced_lit_textured3d.vert.glsl` and its `_vertexlit` sibling are copies of the two lit vertex shaders with the four matrix columns added at locations 4..7, and **nothing else changes** — same push constant, same `LitLightParams` UBO at set 0 binding 1, same outputs, therefore the same fragment stages, pipeline layout and descriptor set. **Both lit variants, not one.** XNA's real default is `PreferPerPixelLighting = false` (Task 1103), so shipping only the per-pixel one would silently move an instanced draw to the other shading model. **Three things fell out of earlier rows and are worth naming, because they are why this was tractable at all.** (1) `VULKAN-223` extracted the family dispatch, so the lit descriptor set and its 64-float UBO are built by **the same method the two ordinary routes call** — an instanced lit draw cannot drift from a non-instanced one. (2) `VULKAN-219` made `FillInstancedPushConst` write `World × View × Projection`, which is **byte-identical** to what `FillExtPushConst` writes; the lit shader therefore needed no push-constant special case whatsoever. (3) The lit family's per-vertex inputs stop at location 3, so 4..7 were free for the same instance columns every other instanced program here already uses. **One real trap, and it is the mirror image of `VULKAN-222`'s.** That row worked because `useAlphaTest` is tested **before** `useInstanced` in both selection chains. `useLitTextured` is tested **after** it — so a draw setting both would have taken the colour-only instanced pipeline, which is precisely the defect. Both chains' instanced arms are now `draw.useInstanced && !draw.useLitTextured`, with a comment saying why, rather than the chains being reordered: reordering would change behaviour for combinations nothing sets today and that no test would notice. **Scoped deliberately to the textured lit shape** (`kLitTextured` — Position + Normal + TextureCoordinate, and explicitly **not** a declaration that also carries a Colour), which is the shape `VULKAN-218` measured. The untextured and coloured lit shapes keep that row rather than being swept in on an untested selection rule — the mistake `VULKAN-199` recorded when its first selection rule was too loose. **Legs J and K**, permanent: J the non-instanced control (so a failure cannot be blamed on lighting never running at all), K the instanced draw. Mid-grey is asserted as a band and the diagnosis names `(255,255,255)` as the unlit signature. **13/13 on both renderers**, up from 11/11. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **371/371** and full `ctest` **9220/9232** — the **12** are §7.5c's standing set **by name**, with no contention noise in this run. The full suite was mandatory twice over: this changes which pipeline an instanced draw selects **and** adds a second vertex binding to two pipeline factories every lit draw in the suite goes through. **Docs:** `docs/vulkan-renderer.md`. |
| VULKAN-225 | `DualTextureEffect` on an instanced draw (`VULKAN-218`'s fourth shape) | ✅ | **The third application of `VULKAN-222`'s pattern, and by now it is mechanical — which is the result worth recording.** Make the *ordinary* family's own programs take a per-instance binding; do not invent an instanced family. **Measured first, with a three-way discriminator.** FNA and XNA double the product (`tex0 × tex2 × 2`), so `tex0` = blue and `tex2` = quarter-grey give `(0,0,128)`: distinct from `(0,0,255)`, the second texture dropped, and from the clear colour, nothing drawn. EasyGL read `(0,0,128)` instanced and not; Vulkan read `(0,0,128)` non-instanced and **`(0,0,255)`** instanced. **`instanced_dual_texture3d.vert.glsl` is `dual_texture3d.vert.glsl` plus four columns at 4..7** — same push constant, same fog UBO, same outputs, therefore the same fragment stage, pipeline layout and descriptor set. The fog term dots the instance-transformed position, for the reason `VULKAN-222` records. **No chain guard needed, unlike `VULKAN-224`:** `useDualTexture` is already tested **before** `useInstanced` in both the pipeline and the descriptor chain, as `useAlphaTest` is. Only the lit family sits on the far side of that boundary, and only it needed the `!draw.useLitTextured` guard. **One ordering detail that would have been a real bug:** `instancedLit` now excludes `params.dualTexture`, mirroring the ordinary route's own `needsDualTex = params.dualTexture && !needsAlphaTest` cascade. Without it a lit dual-texture draw would have set both flags and taken the lit programs, losing the second texture — the same defect one layer up. **And the same applicator trap as `VULKAN-222`**, avoided the same way: the per-vertex capacity handed to `ApplyDeclaredVertexLayoutEXT` is 4, not `std::size(attrs)`, which is now 8. **Still no coloured instanced dual-texture module**, so a `Position+Colour+2×UV` instanced dual-texture draw takes the uncoloured one and loses its colour — `VULKAN-218`, stated rather than left to be found. **Legs L and M**, permanent, with a diagnosis line that names *“the SECOND texture was dropped”* when it sees `(0,0,255)`. **15/15 on both renderers**, up from 13/13. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **371/371** and full `ctest` **9220/9232** — the **12** are §7.5c's standing set by name. |
