| VULKAN-226 | `EnvironmentMapEffect` on an instanced draw — the last full family | ✅ | **The fourth and last application of `VULKAN-222`'s pattern.** `instanced_env_map3d.vert.glsl` is `env_map3d.vert.glsl` plus four columns at 4..7; same push constant, same `EnvMapParams` UBO, same outputs, so the same fragment stage, pipeline layout and descriptor set. **This family has the most to lose from getting the transform wrong, and that is why it is spelled out in the shader.** The eye vector is a **world-space difference** — `eyePos − worldPos`. Computed from the raw vertex it is the same for every instance, so every instance would reflect as though it stood at the origin: the reflection is the whole point of this effect. `objPos = instWorld × aPos` therefore feeds the world position, the normal matrix (`pc.world × instWorld`) and the fog term alike. **Measured on both, with a discriminator that separates three outcomes.** A cube map whose every face is red over a blue base texture at `EnvironmentMapAmount = 1`: the reflection wins outright, so red is correct, **blue** means the cube never reached the draw, and the clear colour means nothing drew. Both renderers read red instanced and not — and unlike the previous three shapes, **this one measured green on Vulkan before any fix**, because a `Position+Normal+TextureCoordinate` env-map declaration was already reaching a program that samples. The row is closed on the evidence now existing, not on a defect being repaired (§26 rule 4). **One ordering fix that would have been a real defect**, in the same family as `VULKAN-225`'s: `instancedLit` now also excludes `params.envMapping`, mirroring the ordinary route's cascade `needsEnvMap = params.envMapping && !needsAlphaTest && !needsDualTex`. Without it an env-mapped lit draw would set both flags and take the lit programs, losing the cube map. **No chain guard needed:** `useEnvMap` is tested before `useInstanced` in both chains, like `useAlphaTest` and `useDualTexture`. Only the lit family sits after it. **This family alone needed its own push-constant fill on the instanced route** — `FillEnvMapPushConst(d.envMapPC, wvp, world)` — because it is the one that takes the world matrix separately rather than reading everything from the shared 128-byte block. **Legs N and O**, permanent, with a diagnosis line naming *“the cube map never reached the draw”*. **17/17 on both renderers**, up from 15/15. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **371/371** and full `ctest` **9218/9232** — the **12** standing failures of §7.5c by name, plus **two** audio wall-clock cases (`SoundBankTest`, `WaveBankTest`) that pass **3/3 three times running** under `-j1`, the load-sensitive set this machine surfaces when other sessions are busy. |
| VULKAN-227 | One instanceable source per stock family instead of a hand-copied instanced twin (`VULKAN-218`'s enabling refactor) | ✅ | **`VULKAN-223` removed one duplication and this removes the other, on the same reasoning and for the same reason — the rows still open in `VULKAN-218` would each have added another copy.** `VULKAN-222`, `-224`, `-225` and `-226` each made an ordinary family instanceable by writing an `instanced_<family>.vert.glsl` **copy** of that family's vertex shader with four columns added, and the copies show what copying costs: `instanced_alpha_test3d.vert.glsl` dropped the `VULKAN-197` D3D9-`oD0` comment, the fog-formula provenance and the push-constant offset table its original carries, so the two files had already begun to diverge as documentation while agreeing as code. Seven variants remained (`VULKAN-218`'s lit-untextured, lit-coloured, coloured alpha-test, coloured dual-texture, skinned, PBR and fog shapes), which at one copy each is **eighteen** more near-duplicate modules. **The design is EasyGL's own, ported rather than invented.** EasyGL adds an optional per-instance matrix to *every* stock program — `CNA_GL_INSTANCE_TRANSFORM_DECL`, four columns behind `uCnaInstanced` — and says in its own comment that this keeps the programs byte-for-byte equivalent for ordinary draws. Vulkan cannot leave a declared vertex input unbound (`VUID-VkGraphicsPipelineCreateInfo-Input-07904`), so where EasyGL toggles **one** program with a uniform, this compiles **two SPIR-V modules from the one source**: `compile_shaders.py` injects the four columns and the `CNA_INSTANCE_POSITION()`/`CNA_INSTANCE_WORLD()` macros into every vertex shader, and lists a family's instanced module as that family's own `.glsl` compiled again with `CNA_INSTANCED`. The existing `CNA_PBR_DUAL_UV` variants already worked exactly this way, so the mechanism is the file's own, not a new one. **The acceptance criterion is byte-identity, and it is checked rather than argued.** Without the define each macro expands to *exactly the text that was there before*, so regenerating `spirv_shaders.hpp` must leave every non-instanced array unchanged. Measured, per symbol rather than per file: of **55** arrays, **46 are byte-identical** and the **9** that changed are the instanced ones, which is the whole intended change. A first attempt hoisted an `objPos` local out of the repeated `vec4(inPos, 1.0)` and moved five ordinary modules — same word count, different id bound, semantically identical — and was **redone** rather than explained, because a refactor whose invariant is "the ordinary path cannot have moved" is worth much more when the invariant is exact. **The four columns also moved, 4..7 → 12..15**, EasyGL's own locations, because 4..7 is not free everywhere: `pbr3d_skinned.vert.glsl`'s dual-UV+colour record already reaches location 7, so the old numbering could not have been extended to the skinned and PBR shapes at all. Six pipeline factories name the new locations. **Deleted:** `instanced_alpha_test3d`, `instanced_lit_textured3d`, `instanced_lit_textured3d_vertexlit`, `instanced_dual_texture3d`, `instanced_env_map3d`. **Kept for now:** `instanced3d`, `instanced_colored3d`, `instanced_textured3d` and `instanced_colored_textured3d`, which are not copies of an ordinary shader but the separate one-descriptor-binding family, whose own retirement is the fog shape — they take the injected columns and macros here, so the renderer has one spelling of a per-instance column and not two. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **371/371**, no `[Vulkan Validation]` line, and `instanced_textured_draw_test.cpp` **17/17** unchanged on both renderers — which is the point of a refactor row: the number that must not move did not. No production behaviour changes and no new test is added; the regression evidence is the existing corpus plus the per-symbol SPIR-V diff. **Opened by:** `VULKAN-218`. |
| VULKAN-228 | The lit family's other two vertex shapes on an instanced draw (`VULKAN-218`) | ✅ | **`VULKAN-224` made the lit family instanceable for one of its three vertex shapes and said which two it left; this is those two.** The lit family binds `Position+Normal+TextureCoordinate` (`VULKAN-199`'s `lit_textured3d`), `Position+Normal` alone (`lit_untextured3d`, XNA's `Primitives3D` vertex) and `Position+Normal+Colour+TextureCoordinate` (`VULKAN-200`, the stock `ModelProcessor`'s colour-carrying mesh) — and only the first had an instanced module, so the other two fell through to the flat instanced program. **Measured before the fix, on both renderers, with legs that name the failure rather than count it.** Untextured: a `Position+Normal` quad, `TextureEnabled=false`, one light at `N·L = 0.5`, white `DiffuseColor` — EasyGL `(128,128,128)` both ways; Vulkan `(128,128,128)` non-instanced and **`(255,255,255)`** instanced, which is the flat program drawing the material colour with no normal input at all. Coloured: the same light over a **red** vertex colour and a white texture, where the three outcomes are all distinct — `(128,0,0)` correct, `(255,0,0)` lighting dropped, `(128,128,128)` the vertex colour dropped — EasyGL `(128,0,0)` both ways; Vulkan `(128,0,0)` non-instanced and **`(255,0,0)`** instanced, and the test's own diagnosis line printed *“lighting was dropped”*. `instanced_textured_draw_test.cpp` **19/21 on Vulkan, 21/21 on EasyGL** with exactly the two subject legs red and both controls green — the pre-fix run is the mutation evidence, taken against the unfixed renderer. **The fix is four `CNA_INSTANCED` variants and no new shader file**, which is what `VULKAN-227` was for: `lit_untextured3d`, `lit_untextured3d_vertexlit`, `lit_textured3d_color` and `lit_textured3d_vertexlit_color` each compile a second time, both variants of each because XNA's real default is `PreferPerPixelLighting=false` and an instanced draw must not silently move a game to the other one (Task 1103). The four ordinary modules are byte-identical after the change — checked per symbol, 0 of 55 existing arrays moved and 4 were added. **The selection precedence is inverted, and that is the substance of the row rather than a tidy-up.** Both lit factories tested `instanced` **before** the shape, which was right while the textured shape owned the only instanced module and becomes a silent mis-route the moment it does not: an instanced untextured draw would have taken the **textured** module and bound a `aUV` attribute nothing feeds. Shape is now the outer decision and `instanced` the inner one in both, so that class of mistake is unrepresentable rather than merely absent. **The draw route asks the ORDINARY route's own predicates**, on the same declaration — `DeclarationIsPositionNormalOnlyEXT` and `DeclarationIsPositionNormalColorTextureOnlyEXT`, both set-exact for the reason those helpers give — so an instanced draw and a non-instanced draw of the same buffer cannot land in different shapes, and each shape's own `StockInputs` table builds the layout in the shape's own **location** order (the coloured one is not the record's byte order; `kLitColTextured` records what listing it the record's way binds where). `d.litUntextured`/`d.litColored` are set exactly as the ordinary routes set them, and `FillStockFamilyRecordEXT` needed no change at all — `VULKAN-199` already made that arm accept all three shapes, so the descriptor set, the 64-float UBO and the fragment stage are the family's own. **One asymmetry preserved deliberately:** the untextured shape does **not** ask `lightingEnabled`, because the ordinary route does not either — the untextured lit program is the only one that can bind a `Position+Normal` record at all, so it is a layout selection there and is kept a layout selection here. **Evidence:** `instanced_textured_draw_test.cpp` **21/21 on both renderers** (17/17 → 21/21, the same four legs on each), `^Vulkan_` **371/371** with no `[Vulkan Validation]` line, and the full `ctest` **9215/9232** — mandatory here, because both lit pipeline factories serve every lit draw in the suite. **The 17 are discriminated rather than counted:** `--rerun-failed -j1` leaves **16**, which is §7.5c's standing content/lifecycle/net/window/C-API and audio-timing set by name, and the seventeenth (`DynamicSoundEffectInstanceTest.PendingBufferCountOnlyDropsOnceAWholeChunkIsActuallyConsumed`) passes serially — the `-j2` contention flake this machine surfaces. Nothing in `Vulkan_*`. **Non-goals:** the coloured alpha-test and dual-texture variants, skinned, PBR and fog stay `VULKAN-218`'s. |
| VULKAN-229 | The coloured `AlphaTestEffect` shape on an instanced draw (`VULKAN-218`) | ✅ | **`VULKAN-222` named this as the shape it left, and described it as *“takes the uncoloured module and loses its colour”*. Measured, it is worse than that: the draw renders **nothing at all**, and the validation layer says why.** The alpha-test family has two vertex shapes — `Position+TextureCoordinate` (strides 20/32) and `Position+Colour+TextureCoordinate` (stride 24, Task 887's `alpha_test_colored3d`) — and the instanced route had a module for only the first. **The pre-fix measurement, on both renderers.** A 2×1 white texture whose right texel has alpha 0, on a wide quad with a grey(128) vertex colour and `VertexColorEnabled`, `CompareFunction::Greater` at `ReferenceAlpha=128`: the left half must read `(128,128,128)` and the right must be discarded, so `(255,255,255)` is the signature of a dropped vertex colour and a surviving right half is the signature of an alpha test that never ran. EasyGL read `(128,128,128)` / clear **both** ways. Vulkan read `(128,128,128)` / clear non-instanced and **clear / clear** instanced — *the whole quad vanished* — and printed **`[Vulkan Validation] vkCreateGraphicsPipelines(): pCreateInfos[0].pVertexInputState Vertex attribute at location 2 not consumed by vertex shader`**. That message is the diagnosis in one line: `BuildInstancedVertexLayoutEXT` had built a `{pos, colour, uv}` layout — its tables are chosen from the **declaration** — for a module that declares only locations 0 and 1, so the record's four colour bytes were bound to `inUV`, every vertex sampled the texture at a nonsense coordinate, the sampled alpha failed the test and every fragment was discarded. `instanced_textured_draw_test.cpp` **22/23 on Vulkan, 23/23 on EasyGL**, with the non-instanced control green. **Two things had to move, and the second is the one that mattered.** (1) The module: `alpha_test_colored3d.vert.glsl` now compiles a second time with `CNA_INSTANCED` (`VULKAN-227`'s mechanism — 0 of 55 existing SPIR-V arrays moved, 1 added), and the factory's selection becomes **shape outer, `instanced` inner**, the same inversion `VULKAN-228` made in the lit factories and for the same reason. (2) The layout: an instanced alpha-test draw now builds it from `EffectFamilyStockInputsEXT`, chosen by the **same `stride == 24`** predicate `GetOrCreatePipelineAlphaTest3D` uses for its module and its baked attribute set — so the two halves of the pair cannot disagree by construction, which the declaration-driven basic tables could not guarantee for any record whose colour and stride tell different stories. **Recorded as a class, not a case:** the instanced route had been building its layout from one rule and selecting its module by another wherever an ordinary family owns both, and `VULKAN-224`'s lit shape only escaped it because its two rules happened to agree. **The validation layer found this, not the pixels** — the pixels said “nothing drew”, which is compatible with a dozen causes. It is also the third time in this campaign (`VULKAN-199`, `VULKAN-217`) that the layer named a defect a green-looking test would have carried, and the `VULKAN-393`/`VULKAN-408` gate means a message like this now fails the run rather than scrolling past. **Evidence:** `instanced_textured_draw_test.cpp` **23/23 on both renderers** (21/21 → 23/23), `^Vulkan_` **371/371** with no `[Vulkan Validation]` line. **Non-goal, stated because it is adjacent:** the route's `RequireFaithfulDeclarationEXT` guard still judges `BuildInstancedVertexLayoutEXT`'s layout rather than the family layout actually used; an incomplete family layout falls back to the factory's baked offsets, which is the historical behaviour for every family and is not this row's to change. |
| VULKAN-230 | The coloured `DualTextureEffect` shape on an instanced draw (`VULKAN-218`) | ✅ | **The same defect `VULKAN-229` found, in the family `VULKAN-225` left it in — and it is now a class with three members rather than two incidents.** `DualTextureEffect` has two vertex shapes: `Position+2×TextureCoordinate` (stride 20/28) and Task 889's stride-24 `Position+Colour+TextureCoordinate`, where the record carries **one** coordinate set and the shader's second UV input is aliased onto it (`VULKAN-150`). The instanced route had a module for the first only. **Measured on both renderers before the fix.** `tex0` white, `tex2` quarter-grey, a **red** vertex colour and `VertexColorEnabled`: XNA doubles the product, so `255 × 0.25 × 2 = 128` times red is `(128,0,0)` — and the two wrong answers are distinct, `(128,128,128)` for a dropped vertex colour and `(255,0,0)` for a dropped second texture. EasyGL `(128,0,0)` both ways; Vulkan `(128,0,0)` non-instanced and **`(128,128,128)`** instanced, with the test naming it *“the vertex colour was dropped”*, alongside **`[Vulkan Validation] … Vertex attribute at location 3 not consumed by vertex shader`**. `instanced_textured_draw_test.cpp` **24/25 on Vulkan, 25/25 on EasyGL**. **The arithmetic of the wrong answer is worth keeping**, because it shows the failure was not a dropped attribute but a *misread* one: the factory baked its **coloured** four-attribute set (its `colored` predicate is `stride == 24`, which was true) while `instanced` selected the **uncoloured** module, whose location 1 is `inUV` — so the record's `R8G8B8A8_UNORM` colour arrived as a `vec2` UV of `(1.0, 0.0)`, every vertex sampled the same texel of a 1×1 texture, and the result was a plausible grey. A test that only asked “did something draw” would have passed. **The fix is `VULKAN-229`'s, applied to this family:** `dual_texture_colored3d.vert.glsl` compiles a second time with `CNA_INSTANCED` (0 of 56 existing SPIR-V arrays moved, 1 added); the factory's module selection becomes shape-outer/`instanced`-inner; and the instanced route builds its layout with `BuildDualTextureVertexLayoutEXT` — the **ordinary route's own builder**, under the same `stride == 24` predicate the factory uses — instead of `StockInputs::kDualTexture`, so the alias `VULKAN-150` describes is applied on both routes or on neither. **The class, stated once for all of them:** wherever an ordinary family owns both its module selection and its attribute set, the instanced route must ask that family's rule for **both**, not `BuildInstancedVertexLayoutEXT`'s declaration-driven tables for one and the family's stride rule for the other. `VULKAN-224`'s lit shape escaped only because its two rules happened to agree. **Evidence:** `instanced_textured_draw_test.cpp` **25/25 on both renderers** (23/23 → 25/25), `^Vulkan_` **371/371**, no `[Vulkan Validation]` line. |
| VULKAN-231 | `SkinnedEffect` on an instanced draw (`VULKAN-218`) | ✅ | **Instancing a skinned mesh is the ordinary way to draw a crowd, and this renderer did neither half of it: the instanced route never set `d.useSkinned`, so a `SkinnedEffect` + `DrawInstancedPrimitives` draw fell through to the flat instanced program — no bones, no lighting, no texture.** **The test has to fail if EITHER half is dropped, so it was built that way rather than checked afterwards.** One bone carrying all the weight, translating **+0.25** in x; the shared instance buffer's three instances at x = −0.5, 0, +0.5; quads of half-width 0.12. Correct output puts them at −0.25, +0.25 and +0.75, and two pixels separate every outcome: **NDC +0.75 covered** ⇔ the bone *and* the per-instance matrix were both applied, and **NDC 0 empty** ⇔ the bone was applied, because without it an instance sits exactly there. Drop the bone and both legs fail; drop the instancing and all three quads land at +0.25, so +0.75 is empty. **Measured:** EasyGL blue at +0.75 and clear at the origin; Vulkan **clear at +0.75 and `(255,255,255)` at the origin** — the flat instanced program drawing the material colour at the un-skinned instance position, which is both failure signatures at once. `instanced_textured_draw_test.cpp` **26/27 on Vulkan, 27/27 on EasyGL**, with the non-instanced control green, so the bone itself was never in doubt. **Four `CNA_INSTANCED` variants and no new shader file:** `skinned3d`, `skinned3d_vertexlit`, `skinned3d_color`, `skinned3d_vertexlit_color` — both `PreferPerPixelLighting` variants times the plain/vertex-colour record (stride 52/56), because XNA's real default is false and an instanced draw must not move a game to the other one (Task 1103). 0 of 60 existing SPIR-V arrays moved; 4 added. **Composition order, taken from EasyGL rather than guessed:** the per-instance matrix applies **after** the bone skin and before World/View/Projection — the bone poses the mesh in its own object space and the instance places the posed mesh — which is exactly `cnaInstancePosition(skinnedPos)` in EasyGL's own skinned program. The world-space position and the fog term both consume the composed position, as they do there. **One deliberate divergence, recorded rather than levelled:** EasyGL transforms the skinned normal by `mat3(instanceMatrix)` directly (`cnaInstanceDirection`); this renderer folds the instance matrix into the world normal matrix, `transpose(inverse(mat3(world × instance)))`, which is the mathematically correct normal transform and is what `VULKAN-224`/`VULKAN-226` already do here. The two agree for any rigid or uniformly-scaled instance and differ only under a non-uniformly-scaled one, where this renderer is right and EasyGL is not; XNA has no instancing of its own to arbitrate, so §5's authority order does not reach it. **`VULKAN_STRONGER`, not a parity gap.** **Both factories take an `instanced` flag** — own pipeline identity, second binding, four columns at 12..15, and shape-outer/`instanced`-inner module selection, the order `VULKAN-228`/`-229`/`-230` established. **The family's two refusals are made on this route too**, at the same point the ordinary routes make them: `RequireSkinnedStrideEXT` for an unlistable stride with no complete declaration (`VULKAN-156` — the row that turned this family's silent no-op into a refusal) and `RequireBoneIndexFormatEXT` for the `_USCALED` bone-index format (`VULKAN-151`), so an instanced skinned draw cannot become the very silence those rows closed. The layout is built from the skinned family's own `EffectFamilyStockInputsEXT` table under the same stride rule the factory uses — the pairing rule `VULKAN-230` recorded as a class. **Evidence:** `instanced_textured_draw_test.cpp` **27/27 on both renderers** (25/25 → 27/27), `^Vulkan_` **371/371**, no `[Vulkan Validation]` line. **Non-goal:** `SkinnedPbrEffect`, which is the PBR pair's, not this family's — `VULKAN-232`. |
| VULKAN-232 | `PbrEffect` and `SkinnedPbrEffect` on an instanced draw (`VULKAN-218`) | ✅ | **The last two families, and the one where “it drew something” is furthest from “it drew the right thing”.** Neither `d.usePbr` nor `d.usePbrSkinned` was ever set on the instanced route, so a PBR instanced draw took the flat instanced program — and because `VULKAN-219` had already put `World` into that program's push constant, it covered **exactly the right pixels** while writing the material colour. A coverage test would have passed it. **The legs are built so that cannot happen.** Two claims per family, and the leg fails if either is false. *Transform:* `World` (rigid) or the single bone (skinned) translates +0.25 in x and the three shared instances sit at −0.5/0/+0.5, so the quads land at −0.25, +0.25 and +0.75 — **NDC +0.75 covered** proves both transforms were applied and **NDC 0 empty** proves the first one was. *Material:* the instanced pixel must **equal the non-instanced one**, and that value must be a BRDF value rather than a full-bright quad. A white albedo at metallic 0, roughness 1, one light at `N·L = 1`, no ambient and the analytic linear-transfer contract this renderer's own PBR witnesses use gives the Lambert `1/π` — **`(79,79,79)`**, which both renderers produce non-instanced and which is nowhere near 255. **Measured before the fix:** EasyGL `(79,79,79)` both ways; Vulkan `(79,79,79)` non-instanced and **`(255,255,255)`** instanced — the flat program's material colour, at the right place, which is precisely the *silent fall-back out of PBR* the row exists to catch. `instanced_textured_draw_test.cpp` **28/29 on Vulkan, 29/29 on EasyGL**. **The skinned half was mutation-tested rather than assumed**, because its legs were written after its fix: with `instancedPbrSkinned` forced to `false`, leg AC reads `(0,255,0)` at +0.75 and `(255,255,255)` at the origin and the test goes **30/31**. The one line was restored bit-for-bit. **Five `CNA_INSTANCED` variants, one per existing vertex record and not one more** — stride 48/60 rigid, 68/76/80 skinned — so the permutation count stays bounded and intentional (`§26`, and the validation gate would notice otherwise). 0 of 65 existing SPIR-V arrays moved; 5 added, total 70. Both `#define` axes compose: the dual-UV instanced variants carry `CNA_PBR_DUAL_UV` and `CNA_PBR_VERTEX_COLOR` exactly as their non-instanced twins do. **Tangent-space handedness composes for free, and that is worth stating** because it is the one place a mirroring instance could silently corrupt normal mapping: the instance matrix is folded into `World` for the normal matrix, the tangent matrix **and** `cnaDirectionHandedness`, so `sign(det(World × Instance)) = sign(det World) × sign(det Instance)` — which is the same value EasyGL computes as a separate `instanceHandedness` factor. Two spellings, one answer. **Family precedence corrected on this route to match the ordinary ones.** `DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx` computes `needsAlphaTest = !needsPbr && (…)` and derives dual-texture, env-map and skinned from that — PBR is the **outermost** family there. The instanced route's cascade did not have PBR in it at all, so adding it at the bottom would have let a PBR effect that also set `alphaTest` take the alpha-test family here and the PBR one there. `!params.pbr` now guards alpha-test, dual-texture and env-map, and `usePbrSkinned`/`usePbr` split on `params.skinned` exactly as the ordinary routes split them. **Both factories take an `instanced` flag** with their own pipeline identity, second binding and columns at 12..15, and **both of the pair's refusals are made on this route** — `RequirePbrStrideEXT` and, for the skinned half, `RequireBoneIndexFormatEXT` — at the same point the ordinary routes make them (`VULKAN-148`/`VULKAN-151`), so an instanced PBR draw cannot turn a draw-time refusal into a `Present()`-time one. **The full suite was mandatory and it earned its keep — F-21's lesson, a fifth time.** `^Vulkan_` was **371/371** while **two** tests outside that prefix went red: `GltfRendererPbrFallbackPolicy.EveryPbrVertexPathConsumesWorldViewProjection` and `…ComposesDirectionDeterminantsIntoTangentHandedness` (and `CnaGltfConformanceL0`, which is the same two run as a suite). They are **text witnesses** owned by `plans/plan_gltf.md`: each pins the literal spelling of every renderer's PBR transform and handedness expressions, and this row changed two of Vulkan's. Neither claim they encode became false — the vertex path still consumes World×View×Projection and the handedness is still a determinant product — so the two Vulkan rows were **updated to the current spelling with the reason written beside them**, which is what `magnum`'s row already does (`…*cnaInstanceSign`). Updating a literal is right here and would be wrong if the *evidence* had weakened; the handedness row is in fact now stronger, because the determinant it names covers a mirroring instance as well as a mirroring `World`. **A second trap on the way, worth one line:** the policy tests live in `CnaTests`, not `CnaContentTests`, so the obvious focused rebuild left a stale binary reporting the pre-edit failure — the same class of mistake this repo has recorded before. **Evidence:** `instanced_textured_draw_test.cpp` **31/31 on both renderers** (27/27 → 31/31: legs Z/AA rigid, AB/AC skinned), `^Vulkan_` **371/371**, no `[Vulkan Validation]` line, full `ctest` **9216/9232**, whose 16 are §7.5c's standing set exactly — no `Gltf*` row among them. |
| VULKAN-233 | Fog on an instanced `BasicEffect` draw, by retiring the separate instanced family (`VULKAN-218`'s last item) | ✅ | **The row `VULKAN-218` described as structural, and the structure was the defect.** `instanced3d.frag.glsl` says it in its own header — *“no fog, no texture, just the flat instance diffuse color”* — because the instanced route used a program family of its own on the one-binding `pipelineLayoutExt3D_` it shares with 2D `SpriteBatch`, and the fog UBO is a second binding. So `BasicEffect.FogEnabled` was silently ignored on every instanced draw. **Measured, both shapes and both renderers.** The recipe is this renderer's own `colored3d`/`textured3d` fog witnesses': `fogFactor = clamp((Z + FogEnd) / (FogEnd − FogStart), 0, 1)` over the raw object-space Z, so `Z = 0.45`, `FogStart = 0`, `FogEnd = −0.9` is exactly half — red fog over blue geometry is `(128,0,128)`, `(0,0,255)` is a program with no fog term and `(255,0,0)` one that fogged completely. EasyGL `(128,0,128)` all four ways; Vulkan `(128,0,128)` non-instanced and **`(0,0,255)` instanced** on both the textured and the colour-only shape, with the test printing *“the program has no fog term at all”*. `instanced_textured_draw_test.cpp` **33/35 on Vulkan, 35/35 on EasyGL**. **The fix is a deletion.** Rather than giving the instanced family a fog UBO — which would mean a second binding on a layout 2D `SpriteBatch` shares — the three BasicEffect shapes now take the **ordinary fog-capable bundle's own programs**, compiled with `CNA_INSTANCED`. `instanced_colored3d`, `instanced_textured3d` (vertex **and** fragment) and `instanced_colored_textured3d` are gone; `colored3d`, `textured3d` and `colored_textured3d` are compiled a second time in their place, and `GetOrCreatePipelineFogColored3D` and `GetOrCreatePipelineFogTex3D` take an `instanced` flag like every other family's factory. Both replay chains stop catching a BasicEffect instanced draw in the instanced arm — the guard is `!useLitTextured && !useFogTex3D` now — so an instanced draw is dispatched by **exactly** the cascade a non-instanced one is. **What is left of `GetOrCreatePipelineInstanced3D` is the position-only fallback**, the record the bundle has no program for (`VertexBuffer(device, count)` at a stride the table does not list, or a declaration naming neither a colour nor a coordinate). That draw still has no fog, and the limit is now one shape rather than all four. **Three corrections the tests forced, each worth keeping.** (1) *Which shape* must stay this route's own question. Reusing `SelectBasicProgramShapeEXT` outright refused a declared `Position+Colour` record at stride 20 — that function reads the **stride** for the basic shapes, so it called it `Textured`, found no UV and hit the fidelity guard. The ordinary routes do refuse that record; `VULKAN-149` deliberately made this route better, and `Vulkan_DeclaredInstancedLayout` leg A is that promise. The declaration answers outright when there is one and the stride table only when there is not. (2) The family predicates are **hoisted above the declaration guard**, so the guard judges the layout the draw will actually use — judging the BasicEffect bundle's layout for a draw that turns out to be **lit** refused a `Position+Normal` record the lit family binds perfectly well, and `VULKAN-228`'s own leg P caught it. The hoist is safe for the reason the two ordinary routes give where they do the same thing: the predicates are pure. (3) The `GetInstancedPipelineCacheSizeEXT` diagnostic counted **one cache**, and the instanced route no longer has one. It counts pipeline *creations* whose `instanced` flag was true, across every family plus the fallback — which answers the question its name asks more directly than any single cache's size now can, and both cardinality tests (`Vulkan_InstancedVertexColor_Cardinality`, `Vulkan_DeclaredInstancedLayout`) assert on it unchanged in substance: no variant per `VertexColorEnabled` toggle, one per declaration shape. **Also removed:** `d.instancedTextured`, the `kInstancedColored`/`kInstancedTextured`/`kInstancedColoredTextured` tables (they were copies of `kColored`/`kTextured`/`kColTextured`), `BuildInstancedVertexLayoutEXT`'s four-way cascade, and the six per-family `d.vertexLayout` re-assignments that wrote the value one line after it was already written. **Evidence:** `instanced_textured_draw_test.cpp` **35/35 on both renderers** (31/31 → 35/35), `^Vulkan_` **371/371** with no `[Vulkan Validation]` line, and full `ctest` **9215/9232**. Unlike `VULKAN-232`, the prefix suite caught every regression this row caused — all three were `Vulkan_*` tests, because the behaviours it moved are this renderer's own — and the full run's 17 failures are §7.5c's standing 16 plus the `-j2` audio-contention flake (`DynamicSoundEffectInstanceTest.PendingBufferCountOnlyDropsOnceAWholeChunkIsActuallyConsumed`), which passes serially. |
| VULKAN-234 | Fog on the last instanced shape, and the end of the instanced program family (`VULKAN-233`'s tail) | ✅ | **Opened by measuring the limit `VULKAN-233` had just documented, instead of leaving it written down.** That row said one instanced shape still had no fog — a record declaring only a `Position`, which the BasicEffect bundle has no program for — and called it a documented limit. Whether a limit is a parity gap is a question for **both** renderers, so it was asked: a stride-16 record whose declaration names only `Position`, fogged the same way `VULKAN-233`'s legs are. EasyGL read `(128,0,128)` instanced **and** not; Vulkan read `(128,0,128)` non-instanced and **`(0,0,255)`** instanced. **A real gap, not a shared limit** — so it became this row rather than a paragraph. `instanced_textured_draw_test.cpp` **36/37 on Vulkan, 37/37 on EasyGL**. **The fix is one `#define`.** `colored3d.vert.glsl` gains `CNA_NO_VERTEX_COLOR`, which drops its `inColor` input and substitutes white; the position-only instanced draw takes that variant through `GetOrCreatePipelineFogColored3D`, which now binds **one** vertex attribute for it. Vulkan cannot leave a declared shader input unbound, which is the whole reason a variant is needed where a uniform would do; and `pc.vertexColorEnabled` is 0 for such a draw anyway, so the two variants agree pixel for pixel wherever both can run. **What that finishes.** `instanced3d.vert.glsl` was the last module of the separate instanced family, and `GetOrCreatePipelineInstanced3D`, `pipelinesInstanced3D_` and `pipelineLayoutExt3D_` existed only to run it. All four are **deleted**, and with them the `useInstanced` arm of both replay chains: an instanced draw is now dispatched by exactly the cascade a non-instanced one is, with `useInstanced` reduced from a family selector to what it always should have been — *“also bind binding 1”*. `instanced3d.frag.glsl` survives under an honest name: `GetOrCreatePipeline3D`, the legacy no-`GpuDrawParams` `DrawColoredPrimitives` path, was always its other caller and is now its only one, so it is `colored3d_legacy.frag.glsl`, paired with the `colored3d_legacy.vert.glsl` that was already there. **Its SPIR-V is byte-identical under the new name**, checked per symbol, so that path cannot have moved. **Where this leaves the two renderers.** Every stock effect family, every vertex shape of each, fog on all of them, and the composition of `World`, the bone palette and the per-instance matrix — instancing on this renderer is now the property `VULKAN-218` said EasyGL had *“by construction”*, reached by the same construction: one source per family, compiled twice. **One more text witness moved, and it is the same lesson as `VULKAN-232`'s.** `GltfRendererPbrFallbackPolicy.EveryPbrRendererHonorsCallerOwnedCullState` scopes its search of `VulkanRenderer.cpp` between two function signatures, and its **end marker** was `VkPipeline VulkanRenderer::GetOrCreatePipelineInstanced3D(` — a function this row deletes. The scope it delimits is unchanged (the next member definition after the skinned-PBR factory), so the marker was updated to `uint32_t VulkanRenderer::FindMemoryType(` with the reason beside it. `^Vulkan_` was **371/371** while it was red, which is F-21 for the sixth time: a text witness owned by another plan cannot be seen from this renderer's own prefix. **Evidence:** `instanced_textured_draw_test.cpp` **37/37 on both renderers** (35/35 → 37/37), `^Vulkan_` **371/371** with no `[Vulkan Validation]` line, full `ctest` **9216/9232**, whose 16 are §7.5c's standing set exactly. |
| VULKAN-219 | An instanced draw must compose the per-instance matrix with `BasicEffect.World` (F-42) | ✅ | **Found while sizing `VULKAN-218`, and it is a bigger defect than the row that led to it.** The instanced route dropped `World` entirely: `FillInstancedPushConst` was handed `view` and `projection` and composed `view × projection`, under a comment asserting that the world “comes from the per-instance buffer”. **The comment described the bug as if it were the design, and the same function refutes it:** with no per-instance stream bound, `DrawInstancedPrimitivesEx` falls back to `DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx(vb, ib, **world**, …)`, which applies `World` — so the identical draw honoured or ignored it depending on whether an instance buffer happened to be present. That contradiction settles the row without needing an external reference, and EasyGL agrees anyway. **Measured before and after, on both renderers.** `World = translate(+0.3, 0, 0)` with three instances at x = −0.5/0/+0.5 and a quad of half-width 0.12: the centre instance clears the centre pixel exactly when `World` is applied. EasyGL read the clear colour; Vulkan read **blue** — the centre instance still at the origin. After the fix Vulkan reads the clear colour too. **The fix is a composition, not a shader change.** All three instanced vertex shaders already compute `pc.vp * instWorld * vec4(aPos, 1.0)`; making the first factor `world × view × projection` turns the product into `WVP × instWorld × pos`, which is exactly EasyGL's `WVP × (instanceMatrix × p)`. So `instanced3d`, `instanced_colored3d` and `VULKAN-217`'s `instanced_textured3d` are all fixed by one line, and no SPIR-V was regenerated. `XnaPixelCenterCorrectionEXT` still rides on the projection factor, unchanged (`VULKAN-097`). **The signature took the change rather than the call site**: `FillInstancedPushConst` now takes `world` explicitly instead of the caller passing `world * view` in the `view` slot, which would have been correct and unreadable. **Two permanent legs, not a print.** The measurement was taken with a temporary block, and once green on both renderers it became legs D and E of `instanced_textured_draw_test.cpp` — D asserts the non-instanced control still honours `World` (so a failure cannot be blamed on `World` never reaching the draw), E asserts the instanced one does. Leg B already shows the same instanced draw covers the centre under an identity `World`, which is what makes E a transform assertion rather than a coverage one. **7/7 on both renderers**, up from 5/5. **Mutation evidence is the pre-fix measurement itself**, taken with the same code path and reported in the row above. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **371/371** and full `ctest` **9217/9232** on the virtual display `:99`. The full suite was mandatory: this changes the transform of every instanced draw. **The 15 are discriminated rather than counted:** **12** are §7.5c's standing content/lifecycle/net/window/C-API set by name, and **3** are audio-timing cases (`CueTest` ×2, `SoundBankTest`) that pass **3/3** when re-run with `-j1` — the contention set this machine's `-j2` runs surface intermittently, and nothing in `Vulkan_*`. **Docs:** `docs/vulkan-renderer.md`. |

```
EasyGL   lit non-instanced (128,128,128)   lit INSTANCED (128,128,128)
Vulkan   lit non-instanced (128,128,128)   lit INSTANCED (255,255,255)
```

**128 is the arithmetic answer** (`N·L = 0.5`), and both renderers give it non-instanced — so the control holds and the divergence is attributable to the instanced route alone. **255 is an unlit full-bright quad:** `LightingEnabled` reaches nothing on an instanced draw here, because no instanced program consumes a `Normal`. EasyGL's answer is identical instanced and not, which is what its design predicts. **The leg was temporary and was reverted**, because a test asserting a gap goes red the day the gap closes, and one asserting each renderer's current answer would bake the divergence into the suite. `VULKAN-198`'s convention applies: the measurement lives in this row, and the leg is added back when the fix lands. **The remaining three shapes** — coloured+textured, dual-texture/env-map/alpha-test, fog — are still read from the shader set and the selection code rather than from pixels, and each needs the same treatment before it is claimed. **Classification:** implementation gap, Vulkan renderer, EasyGL-parity. |
| VULKAN-475 | Full-suite green, with pre-existing failures named | ✅ | **`cmake --build cmake-build-vulkan` exits **0**, and every remaining failure is named, attributed by cross-renderer reproduction, and justified. Attributing them — rather than counting them — found three defects that had been carried as somebody else's for the whole implementation phase.** **The discriminator this row names, applied first:** all **19** failures were re-run individually with `-j1`, and **all 19 reproduced**. So none was a contention flake, and every one was a real, standing failure — which is what made reading them worthwhile. (Separately, five *other* tests that failed only in parallel runs — `CueTest.PlayScalesVolumeByRpcCurve…`, `DynamicSoundEffectInstanceTest.PendingBufferCount…`, `TwoProcessLoopbackTest.HostMigration…`, `ENetDiscoveryServiceTest…`, `NetworkSessionTest.FindReturnsEmptyCollection` — do pass serially and are load-sensitive, not members of this set.) **The attribution method:** run the same list against `cmake-build-easygl`. A failure both renderers reproduce is shared; one only Vulkan reproduces is this renderer's. **Result: 16 shared, 3 ours.** **The three that were ours, each now fixed and holding its own row:** `GraphicsDeviceRendererTest.StartupDiagnosticNeverWritesToStdout` was capturing **this renderer's own** `CNA: Vulkan capabilities …` line on stdout (`VULKAN-402`); `CnjEffectTest.LoadsRealCnjFixture` and `CnjStockEffectTest.CustomGlslEffectStillWorks` gated on `CustomEffects` where the question is `ExecutesShaderEffectSourceEXT()` (`VULKAN-403`). **The 16 that remain, named and attributed:** audio timing — `CueTest.PlayingCueNaturallyTransitions…`, `CueTest.PauseAfterNaturalCompletionIsANoOp`, `SoundBankTest.IsInUseFalseSoonAfterFireAndForget…`, `WaveBankTest.IsInUseFalseSoonAfterCue…`; content — `ContentManagerVideoXnbTest.TheObjectReferencedForm…`; device lifecycle — the six `GraphicsDeviceSubsystemLifecycleTest` cases, whose subject is a video-subsystem reference count that comes out 2-acquired/1-released **on both renderers**; networking — `PacketReaderTest.ReadColorReadsSixteenBytesAsFloats`; platform — `GameWindowPlatformTest.DelegatesStateAndGeometry…`; and the three C-API gates `CApiCoverageMatrix`, `CApiLimitations`, `CApiReleaseGate`. **Justification for leaving all sixteen:** each reproduces identically under EasyGL, so none is caused by this renderer and none is this plan's to fix — `CLAUDE.md`'s rule is to reproduce upstream bugs, not fix them. The evidence is the EasyGL run, not an assertion. **The lesson, written down because it cost three defects:** a failure filed into a bucket stops being read, and "the known set" is therefore the best hiding place a defect has. Nineteen were carried under that label — by this plan's own rows — and three of them were ours. **Confirming run after the three fixes: `cmake --build` exit **0**, full `ctest` **9095/9111**, **16** failures — exactly the sixteen named above, no others, and none in `Vulkan_*`. Gathered in two `-I` halves for the OOM reason §7.5 records. |
| VULKAN-476 | Dedicated Vulkan suite green | ✅ | **`ctest -R '^Vulkan_' -j2` → 254/254, and there are no skips at all to justify.** Measured 2026-09-06 under `env -u WAYLAND_DISPLAY DISPLAY=:99 SDL_VIDEODRIVER=x11`, 164 s wall clock. The suite grew from 218 at the start of this plan's implementation phase to **254**, every addition carrying its own row. **The limitation this row is required to name, because "green" without it would be read as more than it is:** the device is **`llvmpipe (LLVM 19.1.7)`**, a software rasterizer, not the machine's `AMD Radeon 780M (RADV PHOENIX)`. That is not a choice — §7 records why: under Xvfb, RADV reports `No DRI3 support detected - required for presentation`, so the renderer's own present-capable-queue check rejects it and selects llvmpipe. **What must be rerun where:** the whole `^Vulkan_` suite on the real display against the RADV ICD, which needs the owner's go-ahead rather than a test-runner change, and which `VULKAN-008`'s capability snapshot is already built for — it classifies its 93 entries `Fixed` or `Device` precisely so a hardware run compares rather than fails. **Why this is nevertheless ✅ rather than 🟨:** the row's acceptance is about *skips*, and there are none; the device question is a re-run this row is asked to name, not a gap it is asked to close. `VULKAN-487` re-audits at gate time and is where a hardware run belongs. |
| VULKAN-477 | Validation clean | ✅ | **Closed on the wider reading, not the literal one — which is why it took four rows and two measurements rather than a re-run.** **What this row measured on 2026-09-06 and refused to close on:** `VULKAN-393`'s gate passed over 254 `Vulkan_*` CTests with an empty exemption list, and that satisfied this row's own wording. Taking the same inventory over the whole 9 117-test configuration — every one of which runs this renderer — found **20** more messages, all `vkDestroyDevice(): Object Tracking … has not been destroyed`, in two registrations of one test outside that prefix. §28 point 8 is about validation being clean, not about one prefix's worth of it, so this stayed 🟨 and opened two rows. **Both are closed.** `VULKAN-407`: `VulkanTexture3DRenderer`, `VulkanTextureCubeRenderer` and `VulkanRenderTargetCubeRenderer` were in no live list, so an instance outliving its `GraphicsDevice` freed nothing and read a destroyed `VulkanRenderer`; all three now release and disconnect like every other resource, and the whole-suite count went **20 → 0**. `VULKAN-408`: the gate now reaches every CTest that can create a `VkDevice` — ~600 `cna_register_renderer_test` registrations plus **8 774** `gtest_discover_tests` cases — instead of one directory's 254. **The final evidence, in the order it has to be read:** (1) the full `ctest` after `VULKAN-407` has **zero** `[Vulkan Validation]` lines in `LastTest.log`, across all 9 117 tests, where this row measured 20; (2) the full `ctest` after `VULKAN-408`, with the gate live on the whole suite, produces **no gate hit at all** — its 24 failures are `VULKAN-475`'s named 16 plus 8 contention artefacts that pass serially; (3) the exemption list `CNA_VULKAN_VALIDATION_GATE_EXEMPT` is **empty**, so there is no CNA-attributable entry because there is no entry. **The gate cannot pass vacuously:** four tests assert `VulkanRenderer::IsValidationActiveEXT()` before judging anything, so a build in which the layer never loaded goes red rather than reporting a clean suite. **What this does NOT claim:** the device is `llvmpipe`, not hardware. A validation layer is driver-independent for the classes it checks — object tracking, layout mismatches, map sizes are all layer bookkeeping rather than driver behaviour — but sync-validation hazards and anything driver-reported are not proven here. `VULKAN-482`'s environment note stands. **Dependencies:** `VULKAN-009`, `VULKAN-393`, `VULKAN-404`, `VULKAN-405`, `VULKAN-406`, `VULKAN-407`, `VULKAN-408` — all ✅.
| VULKAN-480 | Write `docs/vulkan-renderer.md` | ✅ | **Written, after the re-audits it depends on rather than before them, and every claim in it names the test that keeps it true.** The file had one section when this row was reached — `VULKAN-098` created it because the depth-range divergence needed somewhere to live — and later rows added the cross-renderer conformance rules, the golden-image policy, the `ShaderEffect` contract and the device-loss answer. **What this row added is the part the acceptance names:** (1) **the capability boundary in one table**, every row of which is a line of the snapshot `Vulkan_CapabilitySnapshot` asserts — so the table cannot drift from the renderer without a CTest going red — with an `Origin` column separating what is a property of this renderer from what was asked of the physical device; (2) **the limits**, including why `MaxVertexStreams` is 1; (3) **the formats**: the nine `SurfaceFormat` values that have a `VkFormat`, the rule that anything else is *deferred* rather than refused, that render targets are `Color` only, and that colour transfer refuses `NormalizedByte4` on purpose; (4) **the environment** the numbers came from, both devices and the validation layer that is on for the whole suite; and (5) **a section listing every difference from EasyGL** in three groups — deliberate and permanent, this renderer doing more, and the owned gaps — because a capability document that only lists capabilities leaves the reader to diff two renderers themselves. **Two entries got a sentence rather than a cell**, because a table would have lied about them: `ShaderEffects = true` with `ShaderEffectSourceExecution = false` is not a contradiction (the object runs; its *source* is not compiled here), and compiled `.fx` is a build option rather than an absence. **The header is rewritten**: it said the document was deliberately incomplete and that this row owed the rest, which is no longer true, and it says what it replaced. **No production change; no test added** — the document asserts nothing the suite does not already check. |
| VULKAN-481 | Correct the stale current-status claims in the shared docs | ✅ | **All six corrected, each saying what was true when written and what is true now, and each citing the test that settles it — the row's own requirement, and the reason none of them was simply deleted.** **D-01** `docs/texture3d-texturecube-support.md`: "total no-op — neither `VulkanTexture3DRenderer` nor `VulkanTextureCubeRenderer` overrides `GetData`, the caller's buffer is left untouched, no exception, no error, no log". Both override it now, through a host-visible staging buffer; cited: `Vulkan_Texture3D_Slices_RoundTrip`, `Vulkan_TextureCube_PartialRect_RoundTrip`, `Vulkan_TextureCube_Mip_RoundTrip`. **D-02** `docs/rendertarget-support.md`, two cells: MSAA "honestly reports `0`, not implemented" and mips "`LevelCount` correct, no real GPU mips". Both real; cited: `Vulkan_MSAA_4x_Readback`, `Vulkan_MRT_MsaaResolve`, `Vulkan_MsaaDepthContract`, `Vulkan_MsaaMipReadback`, `Vulkan_MrtMipFinalization`, plus `VULKAN-347` for the applied count. **D-03** feature matrix: Texture2D mip-level `SetData` "silent no-op" — `UpdatePixelsLevel` performs a real staged upload. **D-04** `IGraphicsRenderer.hpp`'s header comment: "Vulkan always allocates a combined depth+stencil buffer … regardless of the exact value requested … a real architectural change, tracked as Task 911". Task 911 landed; each target picks its own `VkFormat` and `None` means no attachment. **The correction keeps the one place the old sentence still half-applies** — the back buffer allocates depth unconditionally — and points at `VULKAN-349`, which measured that and left it. **D-05** three feature-matrix cells reading "not separately re-audited"/"not separately re-confirmed this pass". **These were honest phrasing for unmeasured cells rather than false claims**, so the correction had to be careful not to replace an honest gap with an overclaim: each now names the tests that cover it, and the SpriteFont cell says explicitly that flip is covered for sprites by `Vulkan_SpriteEffects_Flip` rather than by a SpriteFont-specific test. **D-07** feature matrix: the July baseline "`ctest` 126/127 — 1 remaining failure, `Vulkan_DepthBias`". Superseded by 254/254 and 9095/9111, and two of its claims were wrong in ways worth showing: `Vulkan_DepthBias` was a **test** written against OpenGL's depth range rather than a renderer defect (§7.4), and the numbers came from **Mesa lavapipe**, which the line never said. **One more, added by `VULKAN-055` and not in the original list:** `docs/migration-guide.md` listed "Vulkan `RasterizerState.DepthBias` has no effect" among the **known issues**. Withdrawn, struck through rather than deleted — a known-issues list that warns readers away from a working feature does the same harm as one that hides a broken one. **`NEXT.md`'s share stays with `VULKAN-482`**, which owns it by name. |
| VULKAN-482 | Update `NEXT.md` with the measured Vulkan baseline | ✅ | **Done, and two of this row's own premises were wrong — both corrected here rather than worked around.** **Premise 1: "the Vulkan lines currently describe a lavapipe run."** They say **llvmpipe**, not lavapipe: `NEXT.md` carries exactly one Vulkan suite figure, `VULKAN 211 = 210 pass + the accepted Vulkan_DepthBias llvmpipe residual`. The distinction matters because the plan's own §7 records the device as `llvmpipe (LLVM 19.1.7)` with `DRIVER_ID_MESA_LLVMPIPE`, and a row that misnames the driver invites a reader to go looking for a run that never happened. **Premise 2: "replace them with the measured RADV numbers."** RADV numbers cannot be produced in this environment at all — under Xvfb it reports `No DRI3 support detected - required for presentation` and the renderer's own present-capable-queue check rejects it, which is why every measurement in this plan is llvmpipe's (`VULKAN-476` records the same constraint). Producing them needs the real display **and the owner's go-ahead**, which is not something a task row can assume. **What was actually done:** a dated section added to `NEXT.md` carrying the measured baseline — `^Vulkan_` **254/254** with no skips, full `ctest` **9095/9111** with all 16 failures reproducing under EasyGL — and naming the device explicitly, because a suite number without one says less than it looks. **The old figure was left exactly where it is.** `NEXT.md` is a cross-branch ledger whose own top says existing history is not rewritten, so the entry supersedes rather than edits, and states what is now known about the old line: the suite is much larger, and `Vulkan_DepthBias` was never a renderer defect but a **test** written against OpenGL's depth range (§7.4). **What a hardware run would need is named rather than deferred silently:** the real display, the owner's go-ahead, and `VULKAN-008`'s snapshot, which classifies its 93 entries `Fixed` or `Device` precisely so a different adapter compares instead of failing. |
| VULKAN-487 | Re-audit against the **then-current** EasyGL before declaring parity | ✅ | **Recomputed against EasyGL as it is today, all 121 classified, five rows opened — and the headline is that the audit found an implementation defect, not a paperwork one.** **Method:** `tools/vulkan/renderer_coverage_delta.py` (the tool `VULKAN-007` committed precisely so this step could not be skipped) against both `CMakeLists.txt` files: **321** EasyGL registrations, **345** Vulkan, **200** sources on both, **121** EasyGL CTests whose source is not registered here. That 121 is then *not* the answer, and mistaking it for one is the trap this row exists to avoid: **51 of them have a same-named Vulkan test built from this renderer's own source**, which is coverage, not a gap. Of the remaining **70** — **37** are the `SEMANTIC_DIVERGENCE` GLSL-payload family (§9.3, closed by `VULKAN-256`), **3** are `*_Properties` unit suites (`SHARED_LOGIC`), **3** are `EASYGL_OR_OPENGL_SPECIFIC` (`Anisotropic_GlState`, `BackgroundContent_ContextOwnership`, `Gltf_ContextLoss`), **1** is a `*_Golden` twin already covered, and the last **26** were classified by hand: **16** are covered by a differently-named Vulkan test (`AlphaTest_VertexColor`, `BasicEffect_Fog`, `BasicEffectCombinations`, `RenderTarget2D_DepthFormatFidelity`, `MRT_MixedFormats`, `MsaaRuntimeChange`, `OcclusionQuery_PixelCount`, `RenderTarget2D_ClearOnlyRoundtrip`, `RenderTarget2D_MipChain`, `ScissorTest`, `SpriteBatch_TransformMatrix`, `VertexFormat_AllStrides`, the Texture2D readback family, …), **1** is the duplicate §9.3 already named (`EasyGL_DualTexture`), and **1** is EasyGL-specific for a reason worth writing down — `BasicEffectWorldScalePrecision` is `FX-124`, a `mediump` fp16 overflow in GLSL **ES**, and this renderer's shaders are `#version 450` with no `mediump` at all, so the defect cannot exist here. **That leaves five real gaps, each now a row** — corrected to **four** the same day, see below: `VULKAN-188` (**F-35**, an implementation gap: `FX-123`'s D3D9 vertex-colour clamp is missing from all four of this renderer's vertex-lit shaders — found by *reading the shaders* once `EasyGL_BasicEffectVertexColorClamp` surfaced, which is the only reason this audit was worth running rather than tabulating), `VULKAN-189` (emissive/ambient composition), `VULKAN-190` (`GetData` on a still-bound MSAA target), `VULKAN-191` (the vsync contract) and `VULKAN-192` (the three sample scenes). **Parity is therefore NOT declared, and this row says so plainly:** its own rule is that parity waits while its children are open, and five are. What *is* established is the thing the gate was for — the comparison is against today's EasyGL, computed by a committed tool rather than by hand, and every remaining difference is named, classified and owned. **Corrected 2026-09-07, by the row it opened:** `VULKAN-191` was **not** a gap. This audit mapped EasyGL names onto Vulkan names and searched for `Vsync`; the Vulkan tests are called `SwapInterval` and `PresentInterval` and assert the same contract through the live `VkPresentModeKHR`, which is a stronger oracle than EasyGL's own. So the count is **four** real gaps, of which `VULKAN-188`, `VULKAN-189` and `VULKAN-192` are already closed and `VULKAN-190` remains — and the lesson is the one this row states in its first sentence, applied to itself: a name-shaped search is not a coverage answer. |

## 25.1 Post-gate corrections

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-240 | Implement and directly compare compressed `TextureCube` storage (F-43) | ✅ | **Closed a gap the final audit and the interface appendix both misclassified.** `VulkanRenderer::CreateTextureCube` no longer discards `surfaceFormat`; `VulkanTextureCubeRenderer` allocates the exact BC1_RGBA/BC2/BC3 `VkFormat` returned by `MapSurfaceFormatToStorageEXT`, stores every face/mip's blocks in a CPU shadow, performs exact staged uploads, supports block-aligned partial replacement and decompresses that shadow for the public `Color` readback. `IsCompressedCubeTransferFormatEXT` now follows the same device-gated answer as the 2D route. The uncompressed path is unchanged and explicitly refuses a compressed image instead of sending RGBA bytes into BC storage. **Test:** one shared source registered as `Vulkan_DxtTextureCube` and `EasyGL_DxtTextureCube`; DXT1/3/5 each pass decompressed readback and `EnvironmentMapEffect` sampling, and an 8×8 DXT1 face proves a partial 4×4 replacement changes only its requested block. **Measured under separate isolated Xvfb runs:** Vulkan **7/7**, EasyGL **7/7**. The targeted Vulkan build used the configured system ccache and rebuilt only the renderer library and this executable. Appendix A moves both missing overrides from EasyGL-only to both. |
| VULKAN-241 | Preserve compressed DDS/XNB content on Vulkan's native BC storage (F-44) | ✅ | **Closed the next gap exposed by the same interface census.** Vulkan's per-format 2D and cube gates already knew the device-supported BC formats, but the inherited renderer-wide loader preference was still `false`, so normal content could not reach either native route. `LoadsCompressedContentNativelyEXT` now returns `textureCompressionBCSupported_`; loaders still require their existing per-format transfer answer and still decode to `Color` on a non-BC device. The shared `DxtTextureCube` source adds exact format/mip assertions for an in-memory four-mip DXT1 DDS, an in-memory four-mip DXT5 Texture2D XNB body and the real six-face/seven-mip DXT1 cube XNB. **Measured under separate isolated Xvfb runs:** Vulkan **11/11**, EasyGL **11/11**; the existing rendered `Vulkan_Dxt1FromStream` test also passes. Both builds were targeted, `-j4`, with the configured system ccache. Appendix A moves the loader-preference virtual from EasyGL-only to both. |

## 26. Dynamic backlog-expansion rules

This plan is **expected to grow**. That is the mechanism that prevents false closure, not a sign the
planning was incomplete.

1. **An audit task cannot close on a finding.** When an audit row discovers a defect or a meaningful
   test gap, closing it requires, in order: (a) the evidence recorded in this file — §9 for a new
   finding, §10 for a matrix row; (b) one or more new concrete `VULKAN-*` rows in the owning phase,
   each with the eight fields §27 requires; (c) only then ✅.
2. **New IDs are taken from the owning phase's free range.** Never renumber, never reuse. External
   documents and commit messages cite these IDs.

   > **Broken once, 2026-09-07, and repaired the same day — recorded because the repair is the only
   > thing that makes the commit history readable.** Two rows opened that day took the IDs
   > `VULKAN-170` and `VULKAN-171`, which were **already in use** (`ClassifySurfaceFormatEXT` and
   > `ClassifyRenderTargetFormatEXT`, both ✅ since Phase 3). The free range was read from the tail
   > of a sorted list rather than from the whole set — `awk`-ing every used ID takes one line and
   > was not done. The two new rows are renumbered to **`VULKAN-185`** (the golden-tolerance report)
   > and **`VULKAN-186`** (the stale `EnvironmentMapEffect` golden); the pre-existing rows keep
   > their numbers, as the rule requires. **Two commit messages cite the old numbers** —
   > `faae850c7` says `VULKAN-170` and means `VULKAN-185`, `474094561` says `VULKAN-171` and means
   > `VULKAN-186` — and are left alone rather than rewritten, which is what this note is for.
3. **A finding that turns out not to be a defect is recorded as such.** "Audited, no Vulkan bug
   found, here is the evidence" is a valid close. Do not invent work to justify an audit.
4. **A test-gap task may close with no production change.** If the new test passes on first run,
   that is a real result — the evidence now exists where it did not. Say so in the row.
5. **Splitting is preferred to widening.** A row that turns out to be larger than one commit is
   split into new rows before work starts.
6. **Every deviation and refusal is written in the row itself**, never only in a commit message
   (`CLAUDE.md`, `plan_modern.md`'s own convention).
7. **A status may move backwards.** A ✅ row contradicted by a later measurement returns to 🟨 with
   the contradicting evidence, exactly as §9.2's stale documentation is being treated here.
8. **`NEEDS_DEEPER_AUDIT` is a real state with an owner.** Every such matrix row names the audit
   task responsible for resolving it; a row with no owner is a planning defect.

---

## 27. What a remediation task must contain

Every non-audit row states, at minimum:

1. the exact observed problem or the exact missing evidence;
2. its classification — implementation bug, test gap, or documentation gap;
3. the source files and functions involved, with line numbers where they help;
4. the EasyGL evidence (or the explicit note that EasyGL has none either);
5. the current Vulkan evidence;
6. the intended behaviour, judged against §5's authority order;
7. the exact permanent regression test to add, and why it discriminates — it must be able to fail
   for the defect it claims to detect;
8. the validation command, the dependencies, and the explicit non-goals where scope could creep.

A row that says only "improve X parity" is not a task and must be split or specified before work
begins.

---

## 28. Definition of done

Vulkan parity is complete when **all** of the following hold at the same commit:

1. Every in-scope row of §10 is `PARITY`, `VULKAN_STRONGER`, or an explicitly justified
   `NOT_APPLICABLE` / `SEMANTIC_DIVERGENCE` / `CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE`. No `NEEDS_DEEPER_AUDIT`
   remains.
2. Every `IMPLEMENTATION_GAP` in §9.1 has a ✅ task or a ⛔ row whose reason is written here.
3. Every meaningful `TEST_GAP` is closed by a registered, passing Vulkan CTest, or is justified in
   writing as not a Vulkan requirement.
4. No silent no-op remains where EasyGL implements the behaviour (`VULKAN-474`).
5. Capability reporting matches measured behaviour at the `GraphicsDevice` seam (`VULKAN-470`).
6. `cmake --build cmake-build-vulkan` exits 0 and the full suite is green apart from failures that
   are individually named, attributed and justified.
7. The `^Vulkan_` suite is green apart from explicitly justified environment/hardware skips, each
   naming the exact command, the exact failure, what remains unverified and where it must be rerun.
8. Vulkan validation produces no unexplained CNA-attributable errors or warnings.
9. `docs/vulkan-renderer.md` exists and matches measured current behaviour; §9.2's stale claims are
   corrected without deleting the historical record.
10. `VULKAN-487` has re-audited against the **then-current** EasyGL, and every gap that re-audit
    found has been closed — not merely filed.

Point 10 is deliberately the last: the comparison is against EasyGL as it is at gate time, never
against EasyGL as it was on 2026-09-04.

---

## 28.1 Final status — parity reached (2026-09-08, `VULKAN-239`)

**Verdict: the classic (non-CNAEXT) CNA Vulkan renderer has reached EasyGL parity.** Declared
against §28's ten conditions, each measured at the commit below rather than inferred.

| | |
|---|---|
| Commit | `49819d567` (`docs(VULKAN-238)`), branch `vulkan` |
| Device / driver | **llvmpipe (LLVM 19.1.7, 256 bits)**, Vulkan 1.1 device on Xvfb `:99` |
| `ctest -R '^Vulkan_'` | **371/371**, no skips |
| Full `ctest` | **9215/9232**; `--rerun-failed -j1` leaves **16**, §7.5c's standing set exactly, and the seventeenth is the `-j2` audio-contention flake. **None is a `Vulkan_*` test.** |
| Vulkan validation | **zero CNA-attributable messages.** Not asserted — *gated*: since `VULKAN-393`/`VULKAN-408` every CTest in the configuration that can create a `VkDevice` fails on any `[Vulkan Validation]` line, with an empty exemption list, and four tests refuse to pass if the layer never loaded. 371/371 is therefore the validation evidence. |
| Shared instancing corpus | `instanced_textured_draw_test.cpp` **37/37 on Vulkan and 37/37 on EasyGL** |
| Tasks | **230 ✅, 5 ⛔, 0 ⬜, 0 🟨** at this declaration's own commit. The measurements above were taken at `49819d567`, where the count was 229 — `VULKAN-239`'s own row is the difference, and no measurement moved between the two. |
| Parity matrix (§10) | **95 `PARITY`, 19 `VULKAN_STRONGER`, 5 `NOT_APPLICABLE`, 3 `SEMANTIC_DIVERGENCE`, 1 `CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE`; zero `IMPLEMENTATION_GAP`, `TEST_GAP` or `NEEDS_DEEPER_AUDIT`** |
| Findings (§9) | 42, every one with an owner; exactly two owners open, both ⛔ |
| Final EasyGL delta | `VULKAN-236`: 19 EasyGL/shared commits since `VULKAN-487`'s baseline, **all this campaign's own**; no renderer-contract header changed; 3 EasyGL registrations added, all 3 with a Vulkan sibling. **No uncaptured gap.** |

**The five remaining rows, and why none of them is a classic Vulkan parity blocker.** Each was
read in full and classified by `VULKAN-237`; the test applied was *“could a Vulkan-only change
close this?”*, and for all five the answer is no.

| Row | Classification | Vulkan half |
|---|---|---|
| `VULKAN-167` | `OTHER_RENDERER_BUILD_REQUIRED` — Magnum, IGL, OpenGL2, OpenGL4, Sokol | closed and tested (`VULKAN-164`; EasyGL half `VULKAN-206`) |
| `VULKAN-186` | `OTHER_RENDERER_BUILD_REQUIRED` — OpenGL2 | the scene passes on Vulkan *and* EasyGL; what is stale is a shared PNG |
| `VULKAN-203` | `CROSS_RENDERER_SHARED_INFRA` + `OTHER_RENDERER_BUILD_REQUIRED` — bgfx and a GL profile | closed by `VULKAN-202` |
| `VULKAN-264` | `C_ABI_OR_BINDING_OWNED` — `plans/plan_binding.md` owns the closed `CNA_SHADER_DIALECT_*` range | behaviour correct and tested (`Vulkan_ShaderDialectContract`); only the public enumerator cannot express it |
| `VULKAN-266` | `CNAEXT_MODERN_OUT_OF_SCOPE` — needs a `CNA_CNAEXT=ON` Vulkan configuration | refusing is the intended answer here; a missing guard would be `plan_modern.md`'s |

**What this verdict does NOT cover, stated so it cannot be read wider than it is.** The **CNAEXT /
`graphics-ext` engine layer remains separately owned by `plans/plan_modern.md`** and is out of
scope for this declaration in its entirety — `CNA_CNAEXT` is OFF in `cmake-build-vulkan/`, so none
of that layer is compiled, let alone measured, here. So is compiled XNA `.fx` bytecode
(`plans/plan_fx.md`), the C ABI (`plans/plan_binding.md`), and every other renderer's own claim.
Hardware is also outside it: these numbers are llvmpipe's, and a RADV run needs the real display
and the owner's go-ahead — `VULKAN-008`'s capability snapshot exists so a different adapter
*compares* rather than fails.

**What closed last, and it is the largest single thing this campaign did to the renderer.**
`VULKAN-218` — instancing — was the final implementation row, and its eight children
(`VULKAN-227`–`VULKAN-234`) replaced a separate instanced program family with EasyGL's own design:
an optional per-instance matrix on every stock program, reached by compiling one source per family
twice. The renderer ended with **five fewer shader files, one fewer pipeline factory, one fewer
pipeline cache and one fewer pipeline layout** than it had that morning, and gained every stock
family, every vertex shape and fog on an instanced draw. The shared corpus went **17/17 → 37/37 on
each renderer**, and every one of the twenty new legs failed here before its fix and passed on
EasyGL throughout.

## 28.2 Post-verdict correction — compressed cube maps (`VULKAN-240`)

The §28.1 declaration was too broad at its own commit: it said the in-scope `TextureCube` surface
was at parity while Vulkan inherited the compressed-cube refusal that EasyGL implements. The
interface census had seen both missing overrides and classified them as an honest absence; the
generic unit tests then skipped on that absence. Neither is evidence of equivalence. F-43 records
the mechanism and `VULKAN-240` closes it rather than weakening the verdict.

Current direct evidence is `Vulkan_DxtTextureCube` **7/7** and `EasyGL_DxtTextureCube` **7/7** from
the same source: DXT1, DXT3 and DXT5 survive readback and real cube sampling, and a partial DXT1
block update changes only the requested block. The task count after that correction is **231 ✅,
5 ⛔, 0 ⬜, 0 🟨**. The 371/371 and 9215/9232 figures in §28.1 remain the historical full-suite
measurements at the declaration commit; they are not relabelled as measurements of this later
change.

---

## 28.3 Post-verdict correction — native compressed content loads (`VULKAN-241`)

The §28.1 declaration and then §28.2 still overlooked the last link between the now-native BC
storage and normal game content. Vulkan inherited a renderer-wide `false` preference, so DDS and
XNB readers expanded DXT blocks to `Color` even though their per-format checks would otherwise
admit them. EasyGL preserved those blocks. Appendix A saw the missing override but incorrectly
called it an honest absence owned by `VULKAN-172`.

`VULKAN-241` makes the preference device-gated and leaves the per-format and decode-fallback gates
intact. Direct cross-renderer evidence is now **11/11** on each backend: in addition to
`VULKAN-240`'s cube upload/readback/sampling legs, DDS Texture2D, XNB Texture2D and XNB TextureCube
retain DXT1/DXT5 plus every mip on a BC-capable device. The task count after this correction is
**232 ✅, 5 ⛔, 0 ⬜, 0 🟨**. The full-suite figures in §28.1 remain historical measurements at
that commit, not measurements of either post-verdict change.

---

## 28.4 Post-verdict correction — multi-stream vertex input (`REMED-GFX-203`)

The §28.1 verdict made a third too-broad inference: it counted Vulkan's deterministic rejection of
several `VertexBufferBinding`s as an acceptable semantic divergence even though §2 includes
multi-stream input and §10 already said EasyGL implements it. A refusal was the correct safety
boundary while the feature was absent, but it was not equivalent capability. F-45 records that
distinction and the external post-audit ticket that already owned the missing implementation.

Vulkan now combines all public streams of each input rate into the immutable host snapshot its
deferred draw model already takes. The synthetic declaration advances every element by the
preceding stream strides; indexed draws derive their compact source window from the actual selected
16- or 32-bit indices; per-binding offsets and per-instance frequencies are applied during the
copy. Stock and custom SPIR-V routes therefore keep their existing one native binding per rate,
pipeline layouts and submit count while exposing the full 16-slot public contract.

Direct cross-renderer evidence is the same two shared sources: **48/48 Vulkan and 48/48 EasyGL**.
The Vulkan-only custom-program control is **8/8** with no validation message. The current §10 tally
is therefore **96 `PARITY`, 19 `VULKAN_STRONGER`, 5 `NOT_APPLICABLE`, 2
`SEMANTIC_DIVERGENCE`, 1 `CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE`**, with no implementation or test gap. Plan task
counts remain **232 ✅ and 5 ⛔** because this correction closes the existing external
`REMED-GFX-203`, not a newly invented `VULKAN-*` row. As with §28.2–§28.3, §28.1's full-suite
figures remain historical measurements of its named commit rather than being relabelled after this
change. Earlier task rows (`VULKAN-144`, `VULKAN-149`, `VULKAN-470`, `VULKAN-480`) likewise retain
the `false`/one-stream state they measured when they closed; this section and the current renderer
document supersede those historical statements.

---

## 28.5 Post-verdict correction — an actionable SPIR-V payload identity (`VULKAN-264`)

The public dialect query no longer makes a Vulkan caller guess between source and bytecode. The
new `ShaderDialectEXT::SpirV` is appended after every existing identity; native CNA Vulkan reports
it, while IGL's source-compiling Vulkan backend continues to report `GlslVulkan`. The native C ABI
does the same with appended `CNA_SHADER_DIALECT_SPIRV = 7`, a maximum of 7 and ABI 0.25.0. No
existing ordinal moved.

Direct evidence is `Vulkan_ShaderDialectContract` **5/5** and `Vulkan_CapabilitySnapshot` **10/10**.
The latter now holds the relationship that exposed the original ambiguity: a `SpirV` payload goes
with `ExecutesShaderEffectSourceEXT() == false` and every source-dialect profile row unsupported.
The C and C++ ABI header targets, the exact `CnaCApiGraphics.cpp` mapping object and the
Vulkan-aware `EffectSmoke.c` object all compile with the strict target flags; the header-only ABI
baseline is current at 222 structs, 350 scalar types and 1,569 constants. Enabling the complete
shared C API would make its entire dependency closure PIC and requested 673 rebuild steps, so that
run was stopped after 36 steps and is not presented as evidence of a linked C-API suite.

The current plan count is therefore **233 ✅ and 4 ⛔**. The four remaining rows all need a
separately configured renderer or CNAEXT build; the former C-ABI decision is no longer among them.
Historical counts in §28.1–§28.4 remain the measurements at those sections' named commits.

---

## 28.6 Post-verdict verification — CNAEXT capability exits (`VULKAN-266`)

The last row that could be settled in this tree is now measured rather than classified as blocked.
The existing Vulkan build was temporarily configured with `CNA_CNAEXT=ON`, and only the three
affected examples plus their dependencies were built. Under Xvfb on llvmpipe,
`CNAEXT_ShadowMap`, `CNAEXT_ClusteredLights` and `CNAEXT_Ssao` all initialised the real Vulkan
renderer and reached an intentional capability exit before any array setter could be called.

The verbose CTest run is **3/3 safe skips, zero failures**. ShadowMap exited because its required
3D/custom-shader/shadow combination is unavailable; ClusteredLights rejected GLSL as non-SPIR-V
and then exited on `ExecutesShaderEffectSourceEXT`; SSAO first proved its disabled-path control
bit-identical (**0 differing texels of 4096**) and then exited because the depth-normal and SSAO
source shaders cannot execute. None threw, crashed or silently attempted a broken pass. This is
the exact early-out outcome the row's original acceptance allowed. Direct array delivery to a
compiled-SPIR-V effect remains separately pixel-tested by `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_UniformArrays`.

The current plan count is therefore **234 ✅ and 3 ⛔**. The three remaining rows are all explicitly
cross-renderer work requiring configurations not present in this tree: `VULKAN-167`,
`VULKAN-186` and `VULKAN-203`. Historical counts above remain dated measurements rather than being
rewritten after this verification.

| `plans/plan_fx.md` | Owns compiled XNA `.fx` bytecode on every backend, including `CNA_VULKAN_COMPILED_EFFECTS`, `VulkanCompiledEffect.cpp` and `VulkanCompiledEffectTests.cpp` (`FX-065`, `FX-110`, `FX-112`). This plan does not duplicate a single row of it. Where Phase 7 touches an effect path shared with compiled effects, it references `plan_fx.md` rather than re-owning it. |

| `plans/plan_csl.md` | Owns a portable shader language, so one `ShaderEffect` source can serve GLSL and SPIR-V backends. It is why this plan does **not** port EasyGL's 37 GLSL shader tests to Vulkan (`VULKAN-256`). |

| `plans/plan_platform.md` | Owns `IPlatform` and the SDL boundary. Vulkan is one of the intentional native edges; the boundary gates in `CLAUDE.md` must still pass after any change here. |
| `plans/plan_runtimerenderer.md` | Multi-renderer builds. `CNA_GRAPHICS_RENDERERS` combinations that include `VULKAN` must keep configuring; renderer-local changes are preferred to common-interface changes for exactly this reason. |
